Liber Psalmorum
by Anselm of Laon and his school

## THE PROLOGUE OF BLESSED JEROME ON THE BOOK OF PSALMS.

[n: ( Vide Operum ejus tom. VIII. )]



## PROTHEMATA ON THE PSALTER.

[0841A] [n: (AUG.)] This book is entitled among the Hebrews the Book of Hymns, for this reason, that a hymn is where words and praise are held together with a song, which is found in the psalms. It is also entitled, according to some, the Book of Soliloquies: which seems to be so done for this reason, that what a man searches out with himself, and learns from none save from the truth presiding within him, is called a soliloquy, for example, in the manner in which blessed Augustine searched out with himself concerning the nature of the soul and concerning the nature of God, in the same way the Prophet may be said to have searched out with himself concerning the incarnation of the Son of God, and concerning the repairing of the human race. King David and prophet, when he was reigning prosperously, wishing to magnify the worship of God, when at last he was bringing back to Jerusalem the ark that had been given back by the Philistines and had for a long time [0841B] afterward wandered in uncertain seats, went before it naked with the harp [n: (II Reg. VI)]: but he had also chosen four thousand men who should sing psalms not only with the voice but also with musical instruments: over whom he set four, Asaph, Idithun, Eman, Ethan, or the sons of Core, who, falling away from the crime or sacrilege of their father, whom the fire had consumed [n: (Num. XVI)], stood on most devoted in the worship of God.

[n: (HIERON.)] The psaltery is a certain musical instrument put together in the shape of the letter Δ: and from that musical instrument (by which the present work was set to measure) it took its name to itself. The psaltery is called among the Hebrews *nebel* (נבל), among the Greeks ψαλτήριον, but among the Latins *organum.* Many say that David [0841C] did not make all the psalms, because certain of them are entitled in the person of others. But, as blessed Jerome says, they are not to be listened to in this. For David composed all the psalms: he moreover appended them, wherein he had many helpers, over whom he set four, namely Eman, Ethan, Asaph and Idithun. By divine counsel this was done, that the psalms should be entitled in the persons of others, either for the interpretation of the names only, or for the business in hand, or for both. For the interpretation of the names only, as in the person of Idithun; for the business, as in the person of Aggeus and Zacharias; for both, as in the person of Solomon; Idithun, Asaph, the sons of Core and the rest of this kind were not authors of the psalms, but were set over the craftsmen who sounded the psalms [0841D] in three ways: either by the human voice, or by musical instruments, or commonly in both ways. And this in two manners, either by the instrument after the voice, or by the voice after the instrument: whence their names, that they might take [0842A] honour from the ministry, are inserted in the titles, chiefly for the signifying of things. But a psalm is the sound of the instrument, a canticle the singing of the human voice in the praise of God; a canticle of a psalm, when the voice comes after the instrument, a psalm of a canticle, when the instrument comes after the voice.

[n: (CASS.)] It is commonly asked: why are the psalms read in the Church before other Scriptures? This is for the reason that of old among the Hebrews this custom obtained in the temple of God. Or, because the psalms contain more of mysteries than the other Scriptures, and say many things briefly, in which the whole law and the prophets and the Gospel consist.

[n: (CASS.)] Prophecy is a divine inspiration or revelation, which declares the outcomes of things, whether by deeds or by words, with unmoving [0842B] truth, whence prophecy is *a vision,* the prophet *one that sees.* Now this grace has been given in many ways: either through the works of men, as were the ark of Noe and the sacrifice of Abraham, and the passage of the Red Sea; or through births, as of Esau and Jacob, who signified things to come; or through speaking angels, as they spoke to Abraham, to Lot and to others; or through visions, as to Isaias and Ezechiel, and the rest; or through dreams, as to Solomon, Joseph and Daniel; or through the cloud and the voice from heaven, as to Moses; or in yet other ways. David, however, by none of these, but taught inwardly by a heavenly breathing, with the Spirit speaking in him, was filled full, as in I Kings: « The Spirit of the Lord was directed into David [n: (I Reg. XVI)]. » And the Lord: « If David in spirit calls him Lord, how is he [0842C] his son? » [n: (Matth. XXII.)] But the Holy Spirit was so given to the prophets that at times he withdrew for their sins and returned appeased; and as Peter says: « Prophecy was not at any time brought by human will, but, inspired by the Holy Spirit, holy men of God spoke [n: (II Pet. I)]. » Nor are those strangers to the gift of prophecy to whom is given the faculty of understanding or of interpreting the Scriptures.

[n: (HIERON.)] There are many kinds of anointed ones: the patriarchs were called anointed, as: « Touch ye not my anointed [n: (Psal. CIV)]; » in the Parables, all who came out of Egypt are called anointed. The chrism is made in Exodus, whence the priests are anointed; there is another ointment, with which kings are anointed; but this in two manners, for if it is David and Solomon, that is, *strong of hand* and [0842D] *peaceable,* one is anointed with the horn; if Jehu and Azahel, with the cruse, which is an earthen vessel. But Cyrus also, king of the Persians and Medes, who released Israel, is an anointed one, according to that word: « Thus saith the Lord to my anointed [0843A] Cyrus [n: (Isa. XLV)], » which the words that follow do not permit to be taken of the Saviour, as some suppose. There is a prophetic ointment, with which Elias is commanded to anoint Eliseus for a prophet [n: (III Reg. XIX)]; above all is the spiritual ointment, which is called the oil of gladness, wherewith Christ was anointed above his fellows, who have the chrism of baptism. And if any man lose it, let him not despair; because concerning the separating chrism it is written in Leviticus [n: (Lev. XIII)], that when any man's leprosy has been cleansed, the priest shall put oil for him in his left hand, and the rest of the things that are said there, and let him himself be called the anointed of God. But this remains, that the same man is often anointed, lest there be occasion of ruin to the negligent. For it is enough for the leper that, after the first ointment has been lost, he be anointed a second time; but he is often anointed who is always [0843B] going forward: and, from the oil of the leper, he comes to the ointment of the people; thence to the priestly; from that to the chrism of the bishop; from the bishop to the king; from the king to the patriarchs; from the patriarchs to Christ.

[n: (CASS.)] As prophecy comes about in diverse ways, so also at diverse times. There is therefore one concerning the present, as Elizabeth: « Whence is this to me, that the mother of the Lord should come? etc. » [n: (Luc. I.)] Concerning the past, as Moses: « In the beginning God created heaven and earth [n: (Gen. I)]. » Concerning the future, as: « Behold a Virgin shall conceive, etc. [n: (Isa. VII)]. »

Again, one prophecy is according to the foreknowledge of God, which must in all ways be fulfilled, as: « Behold a Virgin shall conceive, etc. » Another is according to threatening, as: « Forty days, and Ninive shall be overthrown [n: (Jonae III)]. »

[0843C]It is therefore to be noted that the prophet sometimes speaks indiscriminately of things to come as of things present; sometimes of things past, sometimes also properly of things to come: which is done for this reason, that he says all things at the dictation of the Holy Spirit, to whom all things are known, whether past or future, as though present.

[n: (REMIG.)] This book consists of a hundred and fifty psalms, not without the reason of a deeper mystery. For this number is most renowned for mysteries. For it is made up of eighty and seventy, whose beginning and signification come from eight and seven. For the eightfold signifies the eighth, the resurrection: because, whereas there are six ages of the living, the seventh of the dying, the eighth will be of those that rise again. But by the sevenfold is signified the time of this life, which is carried on by the repetition of seven [0843D] days. Rightly therefore has this book been composed of such a number of psalms, because it thus teaches us so to work and to live in the sevenfold of this life that in the eighth of the resurrection we may obtain the robe of glory. Or else because by the eightfold the New Testament is signified: for the fathers of the New Testament serve the ogdoad, observing the eighth day of Christ's resurrection, that is, the Lord's day, the octaves of the saints, awaiting the eighth of the resurrection of the dead. But by the sevenfold the Old Testament, because the fathers of the Old Testament served the hebdomad, that is, the sevenfold, observing the seventh day, the seventh week, the seventh year and the fiftieth, which is woven of seven weeks of years, that is [0844A] the jubilee: whence Solomon: « Give portions to seven, and also to eight [n: (Eccl. XI)]. » Well therefore has this book been published with this number of psalms, whose parts contain the doctrine and the commandments of both Testaments.

[n: (AUG.)] Again, by three fifties a threefold division of the psalms is made, for the signifying of the three states of the Christian religion: the first of penitence, in which also the first fifty of the psalms is ended, namely: « Have mercy on me, O God; » the second of righteousness, in which also the second fifty: « Mercy and judgment I will sing to thee; » the third the state of eternal life, in whose praise the third fifty is finished, namely: « Let every spirit praise the Lord. »

[n: (ID.)] The Book of Psalms is one, not five, as is read in the Acts of the Apostles: « As it is written [0844B] in the book of Psalms » [n: (Act. I)]. Jerome nevertheless says five books, that is, five divisions which are made by « so be it, so be it. »

The order of the psalms disagrees with the order of the history, because David sinned with the wife of Urias, and sang of penitence: « Have mercy on me, O God, » before his son persecuted him and he sang: « Lord, why are they multiplied; » but they are believed to have been so ordered by Esdras the prophet at a divine prompting.

The matter is the whole Christ, bridegroom and bride. The intention, to conform to Christ the new man those men who were deformed in Adam. The manner of treating: sometimes he deals with Christ according to that which is the head, sometimes according to the body, sometimes according to both. According to that which is the head, in three ways: for either according [0844C] to the divinity, as: « With thee is the beginning, etc.; » sometimes according to the humanity, as: « I have slept, etc., » sometimes by transumption, as when he uses the voice of the members, as there: « Far from my salvation, etc. » Again, of the Church in three ways: sometimes according to the perfect, sometimes according to the imperfect, sometimes according to the evil: who are in the Church in body, not in mind; in number, not in merit; in name, not in the divine power.



## PSALM ONE.

VERS. 1.-- « Blessed is the man who hath not gone away, etc. » [n: (AUG., Enarr. in Psal., tom. III.)] The order, Adam went away when he withdrew from God; he stood, when he was delighted with sin; he sat, when, confirmed in pride, he could not return, save as set free by this man, who had none of these things. [0844D] Adam went away when he consented to the persuasion of the devil; he stood, while he ate the apple; he sat, while he excused himself: « The woman whom thou gavest me seduced me. » [n: (Gen. III)] [var: Vulg. Gn 3:12 *Mulier, quam dedisti mihi sociam, dedit mihi de ligno, et comedi*, "The woman, whom thou gavest me as a companion, gave me of the tree, and I did eat"; the printed *me seduxit* imports the seducing that the Vulgate assigns to the serpent at Gn 3:13.]. After the likeness of exiles, who, while they are on the road, are more easily called back, while they now stand, with more difficulty: at length with the greatest difficulty, when they have there been made masters and lords. [n: (REMI.)] There is sinning by thought, by act, and by words in teaching. [n: (CASS.)] The impious man is so against God, the sinner against himself, the pestilent man against his neighbour. « In the chair, giving an example of sinning. » [n: (CASS.)] The throne is of kings, the tribunal of judges, the chair properly of teachers. [n: (Ibid.)] It is as though he said: You who have fallen through the first Adam, be ye conformed to this second one: because the blessed man is the contrary of the first

[0845A]VERS. 2.-- « But in the law. » AUG. [n: (CASS.)] Here he is shown full of every good in himself. [n: (AUG.)] It is not perfect praise to flee the things to be blamed, unless the things to be praised follow. In the order in which they grew in the first man, they are taken away in the second.

« In the law. » [n: (Ibid.)] He who is in the law acts according to the law, he who is under the law is acted upon according to the law: the one is free, the other a slave. [n: (Ibid. AMBR.)] One thing is the law which is written for him that serves, another that which is beheld with the mind by him who has no need of letters. « The will. » [n: (CASS.)] That there be no weariness of labour, nor idleness, but he shall meditate always. The will of sense is under the law; of reason, in the law; of divinity, above the law.

VERS. 3.-- « And he shall be. » [n: (Ibid.)] Here he is shown useful to us, giving the fruit of life, and leaves, and overshadowing us, [0845B] « Blessed is the man. But in the law. And he shall be. » The full definition of the blessed man. All these things belong not to every blessed man, but to Christ alone. « As a tree. » [n: (Ibid.)] After the likeness of the tree of life which is in paradise, whereof obedient man was to have eaten. « A tree. » [n: (Ibid.)] In paradise there is the tree of life, a tree unto life, and the tree of the knowledge of good and evil. « Of waters. » [n: (AUG.)] The waters are the Holy Spirit, according to that word: « He that shall have believed in me, out of his belly shall flow rivers of living water [n: (Joan. VII)], » and it is against the heats of the vices. [n: (CASS.)] Or, by water, the wisdom of God, by which the inner man is restored, as thirst by water. [n: (AUG.)] Or the mortal race, as Job: « Many waters, many peoples, » because they flow down into death, as waters into the sea. « He shall give. » [n: (Ibid.)] To give pertains to the rational sense and to the will of the one offering. [0845C] « And the leaf. » [n: (Ibid.)] As the leaves cover the fruit, so the words of the Lord guard his promises. « And all things. » [n: (Ibid.)] Why go through them one by one.

VERS. 4.-- « Not so the impious. » Here by the vengeance upon the wicked he terrifies the adverse, the dread of these makes what has gone before more welcome. « From the face of the earth. » [n: (AUG.)] Of eternal stability, because as this earth nourishes and holds up a man, so that other earth holds up the inner man.

VERS. 5.-- « Therefore they shall not rise again. » Because the wind casteth them away, that is, therefore, because they have been given over to a reprobate sense: therefore they do not rise again in the first resurrection, that they may judge themselves in counsel, that they may set the wills of God before their own. Or in the council, that is, in the harmonious multitude of the just, the impious idolaters, or apostates. Sinners, false Christians: or else therefore they do not rise again in the time to come, [0845D] that they may judge, or that, being judged, they may rest in the council, that is, in the college of the just. « In judgment, etc. » In the judgment there will be four orders: some shall judge and shall not be judged, as the most perfect; some shall neither judge nor be judged, as the damned unbelievers; some shall be judged and shall be saved, as the moderately good; some shall be judged and shall perish, as the evil faithful. « In the council. » [n: (AUG.)] In the eternal good, to follow which was counsel to the saints. « Or in the council, » in the judgment, that they may judge together with them; for there is judgment when different sins are punished with different penalties; and it is said from this by likeness. « They rise again. » What resurrections these are: of the soul, from sins in the present; of the body, from death in the time to come, whereby it shall be made impassible and immortal.

[0846A]VERS. 6.-- « For the Lord knoweth, etc., » That is, these do not rise again in the soul, because God does not approve their works, but those of the just only.



## PSALM II.

« A psalm. » The modulation that is made by the touching of ten strings is the good work that is done by the keeping of the ten commandments. « David. » [n: (CASS.)] Strong of hand, or fair of aspect, is Christ, who warred down the powers of the air, upon whom the angels also desire to look.

VERS. 1.-- « Why have the nations raged, etc. » [n: (CASS.)] This psalm is the second in order, the first in title; whence in the Acts of the Apostles it is called the first. First he rebukes the persecutors and threatens them, [0846B] and foretells as a prophet. « Vain things. » [n: (Ibid.)] To hold him in death; or, that the world should not go away after him.

VERS. 2.-- « Kings. » [n: (Ibid.)] The former, who slew the infants, is Herod, the latter, he who consented with Pilate in the death of Christ. Kings and princes. [n: (Ibid.)] He could also, according to Ambrose, speak in the plural of one man. For only king Herod and prince Pilate are shown by the voice of Peter, in the Acts of the Apostles, to have conspired against Christ.

VERS. 3.-- « Let us break asunder. » [n: (AMB.)] Or they came together, saying: Let us break asunder their reasonings, that is, of the Lord and of his Christ, or of Christ and of the disciples, by which they wish to bind us to themselves. [n: (AUG.)] And although it might be said otherwise, it fits more openly those who are unwilling that the yoke of Christ be laid upon them. « Bonds. » The promises of eternal life, [0846C] and the threatenings of hell, by which they wish to subjugate us to themselves. « Let us cast away. » Their reasonings being destroyed, let us shake off his dominion from us. [n: (Ibid.)] The yoke, that is, the law: but « he that dwelleth in the heavens. »

VERS. 4.-- « He shall laugh them to scorn. » [n: (Ibid.)] As when he rose again with the guards set: « He shall deride, » when they were scattered through the world. The derision of God is the force of the foreknowledge which God gives to his saints, that, seeing the name of Christ to be in all nations hereafter, they may understand that those men meditated vain things.

VERS. 5.-- « He shall speak to them. » [n: (Ibid.)] He opens it more plainly when he says, he shall trouble them. The anger of God is a motion that arises in a soul which knows the law of God, when it sees that law passed over by an evil man, through which motion many things are avenged: or, the anger of God is the darkening of the mind of the transgressors [0846D] of the law.

VERS. 6.-- « But I. » [n: (CASS.)] Secondly, he brings in his words concerning his almighty kingdom and his unspeakable generation, where we learn of his divinity and his humanity. « King. » [n: (ID.)] So also are the kings of the earth appointed by him. « Preaching upon Sion, » so also are the prophets, « The Son, » so also is Israel, « I have begotten thee. » This to Christ alone.

VERS. 7.-- « The Lord hath said. » The person of him that reigns. « My Son. » [n: (AUG.)] Therefore do I give thee the nations for an inheritance rather than to any obedient servant: because thou art Son by nature, and consubstantial by an ineffable begetting. « This day. » [n: (Ibid.)] Although it might be taken of the day on which he was born according to man, yet, since "this day" signifies [0847A] a presence, let it be taken of the everlasting generation of the wisdom of God.

VERS. 8.-- « Ask. » [n: (AUG.)] It pertains to the taking up of man: which was done for the salvation of men, and according to which he intercedes for us. « An inheritance. » [n: (CASS.)] Inheritance is from lord (herus), because in it he holds dominion with free power.

VERS. 9.-- « Thou shalt rule them. » [n: (AUG.)] Partly thou shalt rule, partly thou shalt break, according to that word: « Behold, this one here is set for the ruin and for the resurrection of many [n: (Luc. II)], » so that he who is just may be justified still: and he who is in filth shall be defiled still [n: (Apoc. XXII)]. » « A rod of iron. » [n: (CASS.)] The power of Christ is the rod of iron; the sinner is the potter's vessel, which is both of clay and easily broken.

VERS. 10.-- « And now, ye kings, understand. » Now, [0847B] that is, by the things that have been said. [n: (CAS.)] Thirdly, on account of these things laid open, he admonishes all to obey humbly, and persuades them from the useful, that they perish not, and from the honourable, because blessed are they that trust in him. In this, after Christ, he speaks as it were an Apostle.

[n: (AUG.)] Or, now that I am appointed king, be not sorrowful, ye kings of the earth; for it is expedient for you to be under him, from whom come understanding and learning, that you may not rule rashly, but may serve the Lord of all, and may exult in blessedness.

VERS. 12.-- « Lest at any time he be angry. » [n: (Ibid.)] The doubt is not according to the prophet, but according to those men themselves, who think upon the anger of God with doubting. « From the just way, » that is, being taken away from Christ, or from good working.

[0847C]VERS. 13.-- « When his wrath shall be kindled. » [n: (CASS.)] Now he does not burn when he chastises, as a father, but in the time to come he will burn, because there will be nothing of patience in the time to come. « In a short time his anger. » Because he examines not each of these things separately, but all of them together.

## PSALM III.

VERS. 1.-- « A psalm, » [n: (AUG.)] concerning the passion and resurrection of Christ: the son is Judas, from whom he fled, when, at his departing, he withdrew with the rest into the mountain; or rather when the wisdom of God forsook his mind, so that he was wholly the devil's, which forsaking is called a flight because of its swiftness. Whence: « That which thou dost, do quickly [n: (Joan. XIII)]. » To him Christ showed peace, as David also did to his wicked son, whence he is called the peace of the father: but Judas, like Absalom, perished by hanging. He sets forth two opposed [0847D] parts. The voice of Christ, as it were wondering, when he came to save: « Lord, why, etc. »

VERS. 2.-- « Lord, why. » [n: (CASS.)] The first psalm of those which treat briefly of the passion and the resurrection, against those men, and chiefly the pagans, who do not think that the glory of majesty can descend to lowly things.

VERS. 3.-- « Many say, etc. » [n: (AUG.)] Or this each man says who is assailed by vices and desires.

« They say to my soul: There is no salvation, etc. » By the heaping up of vices there creeps in a despair of health, as though the vices were insulting the soul; or also by devils and his angels, through suggestions.

[0848A]VERS. 4.-- « Thou art my upholder. » [n: (CASS.)] In Christ. The head, that is, Christ, or the mind which serves the law of God. « Thou hast struck all. » It is rightly said in the predestination concerning the devil and his angels. « Upon the people. » This is each one of us. [n: (ID.)] Or the Church and its head speaks this, set amid the storms of persecutions throughout the whole world. Many say, desiring to extinguish the Christian name, « There is no salvation. » Otherwise they would not hope to destroy the Church that was sprouting far and wide. « Upholder. » Because it was taken up in Christ and exalted with him. The head is he himself who first ascended into heaven.

VERS. 5.-- « And he hath heard me from the mount, etc. » That is, by Christ the prayer of the Church is heard, or its righteousness.

VERS. 6.-- « I have slept: » according to that word: [0848B] « Rise from the dead, and Christ shall enlighten thee [n: (Ephes. V)]. » It is no wonder that the Saviour says these things, when he did far greater things for the salvation of souls at that time when he was absent from the body, which things are recounted in the catholic epistle under the name of Saint Peter: « When he preached, having gone forth, to those spirits who were in prison, who had at one time been unbelieving [n: (I Petr. III)]. » [var: Vulg. 1 Pt 3:19 *spiritibus veniens praedicavit*, "coming, he preached to the spirits"; Migne prints *profectas*, an unconstruable feminine accusative for what the sense requires as *profectus*, "having gone forth."]

VERS. 7.-- « I will not fear thousands, » who try to extinguish the name of Christ.-- « Rise up. » O Christ our head, in whose resurrection is salvation.

VERS. 8.-- « For thou hast struck, etc. » Now that the Church reigns, the enemies have been struck with confusion. « The teeth of sinners, » that is, their cursing words; or the princes become teeth unto destruction. Therefore believe, O men, [0848C] that « salvation is the Lord's, » and, O Lord, « let thy blessing be upon thy people. With my voice. » These things are mine by desert, because « with my voice. » He who is good, and asks good things, cries with his own voice; with another's voice, that is, with one not according with his own life. « I have slept. » The end of the matter, whence he sets forth that there is nothing to fear. « I, » with weight; as though: they rage, but I would not sleep, unless I willed it. Or, I slept by consenting; I was made drowsy, by doing evil; I rose up, by forsaking sins. « For. » [n: (AUG.)] He does not weigh back, nor besides does he make safe because he has struck: but from thence the teeth were broken, because he struck; that is, the cursing words were brought to nothing; or, the teeth are those who cut off certain men from the number of the saints, and incorporate them among the evil.



## PSALM IV.

[0848D]

VERS. 1.-- « Unto the end. » [n: (AUG.)] The end is said in two ways, when something fails, or when it is completed. [n: (Ibid.)] A canticle indicates gladness. Psalms are those things which are sung to the psaltery, which the Prophet used in a great mystery. « A psalm, » concerning morals, for the doing of them, that the false gods and the vanities of the world may be forsaken; « Of a canticle, » because mention is made of the eternal delight.

VERS. 2.-- « When I called upon, etc. » [n: (CASS.)] By his own example he rebukes the unbelieving concerning false things. « Thou hast enlarged for me. » [n: (Ibid.)] From the straits of sadness thou hast led me into the breadth of joy. V. « We rejoice in tribulations, knowing that tribulation, etc. » [n: (Rom. V.)] [var: Vulg. Rm 5:3 *gloriamur in tribulationibus*, "we glory in tribulations."] He first showed that he had been heard: now, as though having God in his heart, [0849A] he speaks to him. « Have mercy on me. » He had mercy concerning righteousness and concerning the enlarging. Again, have mercy against the miseries of this life. « My prayer. » Concerning the good things that are to be asked for. And when it is thus concerning me, « Ye sons of men, how long will ye be heavy of heart? »

VERS. 3.-- « Ye love vanity, etc. » [n: (CASS.)] With your desire you embrace, with labour you seek vain idols, and lies, which are not God: or earthly things, which do not do what they promise.

VERS. 4.-- « And know ye, etc. » The new man being set forth, he bids them put off the old. [n: (AUG.)] Note that « And » is placed at beginnings, so that it may join the things said with the vision that is in the heart, so here, as: « Love not vain things. And know ye. » But the diapsalma forbids their being so joined. Diapsalma, according to Jerome, is always, the Hebrews, [0849B] Sela, that is, always, in confirmation of the preceding part. Sympsalma, that is, the joining of a psalm. « The Lord will hear. » Concerning rest it is thus, and so with you: and therefore cry out, being thus worthy to be heard.

VERS. 5.-- « Be ye angry. » If a motion of the soul arises which is not in our power, let not reason consent to it. [n: (CASS.)] A motion of the soul is not in our power, but by the grace of God we are able to temper it: what therefore belongs to custom, he permits; what belongs to fault, he forbids.

[n: (HIER.)] This the Apostle teaches: « Let not the sun go down upon your anger [n: (Ephes. IV)], » so that it is altogether a sin to be angry even lightly: but it is righteousness to soften anger by a swift repentance. « In your hearts. » [n: (AUG.)] Concerning those things which you handle in the heart, which God knows: that is, concerning consent; and concerning those things which you turn over [0849C] in the bed, that is, in the delight of the flesh, in which reason dwells captive, as a wild beast in its lair, « Be ye pricked, » through penance; or the beds are the thoughts of the fierce. [n: (CASS.)] Venial is the anger which is not carried through to effect. Diapsalma. « Sacrifice ye. » [n: (CASS.)] The old man being laid aside, we are bidden to be offered in sacrifice, because we are sure of good things. [n: (AUG.)] « The sacrifice of righteousness, » a contrite spirit, that, punishing itself, it may be slain to God: or, just works after penance, which the diapsalma marks, that from the old life there may be a passing over to the new, and so hope ye for good things. But because there is scarcely one who loves the inner goods, he adds:

VERS. 6.-- « Many say: Who showeth us good things? » Things to come, namely, of which they doubt and despair. Answering to which he shows briefly what [0849D] good things are to be sought.

VERS. 7.-- « The light is signed upon us, etc. » [n: (AUG.)] This light is the whole and the true good of man, wherewith he is signed, as a penny with the image of the king. « Light. » [n: (AUG.)] Light, that is, a shining countenance, and one that enlightens us, an image whereby thou art known. [n: (CASS.)] Or: the cross has been imprinted upon us, for a sign of our king, which is the light of the countenance: because in such men God shines out. The image of creation is reason; of re-creation, grace; of likeness, the whole Trinity. « In the heart. [n: (AUG.)] Not without in vain things, but within, where God dwells, is gladness to be sought.

VERS. 8.-- « From the fruit. » [n: (CAS.)] Because they think that in these things the good is consummated. These three, as being more suited to human [0850A] uses, he sets down for all riches.

VERS. 9.-- « In peace in the selfsame. » [n: (CASS.)] But I will sleep in the peace of the mind, here forgetful of the world, and I will rest in the time to come: because I am already in one hope, not in the manifoldness of the world. « In peace I will sleep and I will rest. » [n: (AUG.)] From all the things of the world, when this mortal shall put on immortality: and this will truly be, because it is already hope: and this is: « For thou, O Lord, » and assuredly that will be which is hoped for.



## PSALM V.

VERS. 1.-- « For her that obtaineth the inheritance. » Because he is honoured here to whom the inheritance is affirmed to belong, from which the rival is shut out. It is the Church, [0850B] which receives the inheritance, that is, eternal life, God himself, which here speaks of the inheritance which it asks for itself and asserts, saying that the rival is shut out. [n: (BEDA.)] That is, for the Church, which, at Christ's rising again, received the gifts of spiritual goods, which is itself also sometimes called the Lord's inheritance, purchased with his precious blood: whence it is said: « Ask of me, and I will give thee the Gentiles, thy inheritance. » The whole psalm therefore is uttered in the person of the catholic Church, which in the first section asks that her prayer be heard, proclaiming that heretics and schismatics are to be shut out from the Lord's gifts. In the second section she prays that by the understanding of the divine Scriptures she may be directed by a straight path to that happy country, asserting that the faithless are thereby made utterly [0850C] strangers to it. At the last, calling to mind the rewards of the blessed, that by one preaching the punishment foretold might convert the wicked also, and the promised rewards rouse the just.

VERS. 2.-- « My words. » [n: (CASS.)] Here the prophet hints that a threefold prayer is to be made. For there are three principal vices in prosperity, three in adversity: against which we ought to make a threefold prayer. In prosperity, against the concupiscence of the flesh, against the concupiscence of the eyes, against the pride of life. In adversity, against the fear of death, of peril, of profit. [n: (Ibid.)] A threefold prayer: words, the psalmody of the mouth, a cry, the affection of the heart, therefore: « Thou shalt hear. » Behold, three times of the same thing. « With thine ears. » The ears of God are power, mercy. Power, whereby he can: mercy, [0850D] whereby he wills to hear; for unless there were mercy, he would hear no one's prayers.

VERS. 3.-- « To the voice. » That prayer has a voice which is worthy to be heard. « King. » [n: (AUG.)] Properly the Son, rightly first king, because through him one goes to the Father, whence he adds: And « my God: » and yet he does not say attend ye, but attend, because God is one.

VERS. 4.-- « For, etc. » [n: (AUG.)] Thus far the prayer, that it may be heard, etc., *as far as* and this in the morning, as soon as it forsakes the darkness.

VERS. 5.-- « In the morning I will stand. » [n: (CASS.)] Or, in the morning when eternity shall begin, and then I shall fully see that iniquity pleases not: because then it shall not dwell, « but the wicked shall be taken away, that he may not see the glory of God *(Isa. [0851A] XXVI)*: » [var: Vulg. Is 26:10 *et non videbit gloriam Domini*, "and he shall not see the glory of the Lord"; the Vulgate has nothing answering to *tolletur impius*] and then they shall not remain, even though they have had his mercy.

VERS. 6.-- « Neither shall the unjust remain. » Who do evil things. For thou hast hated those who work them, not those who have worked them.

VERS. 7.-- « Thou wilt destroy all that speak a lie. » [n: (AUG.)] Because this is contrary to truth. Truth is of that which is. A lie is not a substance or a nature, but is of that which is not, and deservedly is that destroyed which turns aside from that which is to that which is not. [n: (Ibid.)] To lie is to speak against that which is felt in the mind. [n: (Ibid.)] There are certain lies told for safety's sake, etc., *as far as* I did not consider that one raving could demand it back.

VERS. 8.-- « But I. » [n: (AUG.)] Thirdly, he says that he [0851B] will be the house of God, and that now he draws near in fear, before that consummation which casts out fear.

« I will go in. » [n: (CASS.)] By this I shall go in, because I shall adore. [n: (Ibid.)] He asks against those things which the rival brings in: whence she herself shall perish.

VERS. 9.-- « Lord, lead me. » [n: (AUG.)] Fourthly, making progress amid hindrances, he prays to be helped within, lest he be turned aside by deceitful tongues. « In thy justice. » [n: (CASS.)] Because thou receivest the penitent and the suppliant. « In thy sight. » [n: (AUG.)] Where man does not see, who is not to be believed whether he praise or blame: because he does not see into the conscience, in which the way to God is directed. Therefore he adds: « For there is no truth in their mouth. »

VERS. 10.-- « For there is not, etc. » Not only is there no truth in the mouth, but a sepulchre: because they bring forth stinking things [0851C] to corrupt, and if they do not prevail with them, they act deceitfully, that is, they do not only contrive, but accomplish.

VERS. 11, 12.-- « Judge them, O God. Let them fall from their thoughts. » He does not wish it, but foretells what shall be. He falls away who is disappointed of a good hope.

« And let them be glad. » [n: (CASS.)] After the retributions of the wicked he adds the rewards of the good, that as the punishment terrifies, so the rewards may incite.

VERS. 13.-- « As with a shield. [n: (CASS.)] In one word he indicates the free benefits of God. The will which calls is a defence against all things, and a crown, and a shield. Fitted to the head it is a crown, to the heart, a defence.



## PSALM VI.

[0851D]VERS. 1.-- « The octave » is the coming of the Lord, in which the sinner fears to be rebuked, and therefore repents. [n: (AUG.)] Some call the octave the coming of the Lord, etc., *as far as* whence the Church, fearing, here prays.

VERS. 2.-- « O Lord, not in thy fury. » [n: (BASIL.)] It is a deprecatory confession, which avails with God. First there is an exordium, in which he wins goodwill, by the power of the judge, by his own weakness, by the custom of sparing: because he wills to be entreated not by the dead, but by the living. « Rebuke. » [n: (AUG.)] To be rebuked is graver than to be accused, whence damnation is feared. To be chastised is milder, that is, to be amended or instructed. They are rebuked in the judgment, who are without a foundation. They are amended, that is, purged, who have built upon that foundation wood, hay, stubble. [0852A] Here therefore he says: « Heal, » lest there be work there, whence he adds: « Have mercy. »

VERS. 3.-- « Weak » [n: (AUG.)] The soul is made weak by guilt, from which do thou heal her, because the bones, that is, the mind, is troubled at his presence, and the soul, that is, sensuality: therefore how long dost thou put it off, as if he said: What remains but that thou shouldst heal? « How long. » [n: (AUG.)] He hints that he has long wrestled with vices. But God's delay is not cruelty, but is that he may convince the soul into what evils she has flung herself. For she does not yet pray so perfectly that it may be said to her: « While thou art yet speaking I will say, behold I am here [n: (Isa. LXV)]. » [var: Vulg. Is 65:24 *adhuc illis loquentibus, ego audiam*, "while they are yet speaking, I will hear"] That out of the difficulty of the healing she may thereafter be the more on her guard, and that it may be shown what punishment there is for the unconverted, if there is so great a difficulty for the converted, whence: « If the just man shall scarcely be saved, where shall the ungodly appear? » [n: (I Petr. IV.)]

[0852B]VERS. 5.-- « Turn thee. » [n: (CASS.)] Thou who wast turned away, or, from vengeance. « Deliver, » from imminent death. Or, deliver the soul bound by sins. « Save me, etc., » by giving virtues.

VERS. 6.-- « For there is not. » [n: (CASS.)] Therefore I hasten to be released here, because after death and in hell confession is not received. « In hell. [n: (AUG.)] The rich man confessed who saw Lazarus, although out of the deep, and by comparison with him he was compelled to confess his own evils, even so far as to ask that his brothers also be forewarned by these: but after the judgment, in the deeper darkness, they shall see no light of God, to whom they might confess. Or, he calls death the sin in which he is unmindful of God who despises his commandments.

VERS. 7.-- « I have laboured. » [n: (CASS.)] He narrates the hardships [0852C] by which penitents are turned away [sic: *poenitentes avertitur*]; he groans while he sees what he has done, and what punishment is to come. « The bed. » The conscience, which to some is rest, to some torment. The couch, sensuality; I will make fertile what was dry. Or, the bed is the delight of the body, in which a man is dissolved. The couch, the heap of sins as of garments, which being loosed, by the shower of tears, a new harvest of virtues may grow.

VERS. 8.-- « From wrath. » [n: (AUG.)] From anger, either God's, which is in the judgment, etc., *as far as* lest any despair because of the grievousness of his sins, he adds: « Depart, etc. » [n: (CASS.)] A chastisement, that he may be separated from those in whose company he sinned, and may press on in the commandments of God. « Enemies, » [n: (AUG.)] that is, vices, or evil men, who are truly the enemies of those who turn themselves to God, from whom [0852D] every good man is separated, not in place, but in mind. But because so great a labour of penitence is not vain, signifying that he has been heard, he adds: « Depart. »

VERS. 9, 10.-- « The Lord. » Thrice the Lord, because he shows that the Trinity was present to his prayers: he began with tears, being heard he exults. « The Lord hath heard. » [n: (CAS.)] He gives an example that those who pray faithfully are heard.

VERS. 11.-- « Let them be ashamed and be troubled. » [n: (CASS.)] As in conclusion, now free from sins, exulting, he prays for his enemies, that they may be converted, that, like himself, they may have pardon. [n: (AUG.)] « Let them be ashamed. » Not here (where rather they mock those who leave all things, and make the weak ashamed), but in the judgment.



## PSALM VII.

[0853A]

VERS. 1.-- « Unto the end, a psalm. [n: (AUG.)] Chusi, who went over to Absalom's party, etc., *as far as* for it was not to be hidden from those for whom it was done. [n: (AUG.)] This psalm can be understood in the person of Christ; if any things are said humbly, let them be referred to our weakness, which he bore. First he asks to be delivered out of all tribulations.

VERS. 2.-- « O Lord my God. » [n: (CASS.)] This is the first of those psalms in which by the acts of David the future mysteries of the Lord are signified. The mystery of the incarnation was a silence to the Jews; as David from his son, so Christ endured persecution from the people whom he had nourished. « Deliver. » [n: (AUG.)] As it were, when [0853B] every war of the vices has been overcome, now indeed nothing remains but the envious devil.

VERS. 3.-- « Lest he seize, etc. » « Who may redeem, etc. » [n: (CASS.)] Proper words: it belongs to the Lord to deliver, to the lion to seize. The order, first he sets the persecutors, then the lion. For when a man has been cleansed from all spiritual wickednesses he is delivered from the devil.

VERS. 4, 5.-- « O Lord, if I have done this. » [n: (Ibid.)] Literally, of David and Absalom. « If I have done this, » that is, if this evil has befallen me through a past fault. Or, if there be now iniquity in my hands, that is, in my works. Or, « if I have rendered evils to them that repaid me, etc., » (which is called equity among men), « deservedly » (even though it may not seem so to men) « let me fall away from my enemies empty, » from the fruit of meekness.

[0853C] [n: (AUG.)] « If I have done this, » or, the universal sin, which he named without a name, which is pride, which is the root of all evils. Or, that which follows, « if there is iniquity, » which is taken of every sin. « If I have rendered. » [n: (Ibid.)] That is, if I have not imitated thee in a just silence, that is, in the patience which thou didst work for me. « Let me deservedly fall away from my enemies. » [n: (Ibid.)] He does not imprecate this on himself by an oath, but says what befalls those who avenge themselves: he prophesies therefore, he does not swear by an execration; which is the gravest kind of oath. Made empty by a vain and proud gladness, in that he could as it were not be overcome: Solomon, « Better is he that overcometh anger than he that taketh a city » [n: (Prov. XVI)]. [var: Vulg. Prv 16:32 *Melior est patiens viro forti, et qui dominatur animo suo expugnatore urbium*, "Better is the patient man than the valiant, and he that ruleth his spirit than he that taketh cities"]

[0853D]VERS. 6.-- « Let him tread down, etc. » [n: (Ibid.)] The soul of the sinner, of which, « Earth thou art, and into earth thou shalt return [n: (Gen. III)], » [var: Vulg. Gn 3:19 *pulvis es et in pulverem reverteris*, "dust thou art, and into dust thou shalt return"] is the food of the serpent, to whom it was said in the malediction, ( « Earth shalt thou eat, » ) that is, of the devil, because it is earth, that is, earthly. And therefore he here says: « Let him tread down my life in the earth, that is, let him incorporate it into himself when it has been brought down to earth, and let it there be despicable. « My glory. » [n: (Ibid.)] Which ought to be in the conscience, where God sees; and solid in the Lord, as: « He that glorieth, let him glory in the Lord [n: (I Cor. I)]. » « Let him bring down into the dust, » that is, into pride and boasting, namely that I should wish to glory among men. « Let him bring down into the dust. The dust which the wind casteth forth. » [n: (Ibid.)] The boasting of the proud. He wishes therefore that [0854A] his glory be solid within, where God sees: for it is dust, if he glories among men; behold how greatly pride is to be shunned.

VERS. 7, 8.-- « Arise, O Lord. » He asks that the glory of Christ's resurrection may come to his aid. « In thy anger. » For this perfect man does not here pray against men, but against the devil, that he may lose his possession, namely sinners and the ungodly, who are his possession. And because the taking away of a man, because it is a punishment to the devil, is called the anger of God against him. Or, he prays here for Christ's resurrection, as in the preceding for his incarnation: as « O Lord my God, etc. » « In the borders. » The world, or hell, are the borders of the devil.

« And arise, O Lord. » The devil is proud, because [0854B] he is overcome by iniquity, he adds: Or, « Arise, » that is, be made known in the whole world, by rising again. « In the precept, » that thou mayest make the precept of humility to be fulfilled: and this follows thereupon, because the Synagogue, out of divers peoples, or specially of the Jews, « shall compass thee about, » by loving, or by persecuting: « For this, » that is, for her that loves, « return, » that is, make them know thy omnipotence, thou who didst descend that they might see thee; or « for this, » that is, for her that persecutes, in whom thou findest no place, « return on high, » that is, withdraw thyself from those who persecute thee. « Synagogue. » [n: (Ibid.)] The congregation of the people, etc., *as far as* the Lord judges, of the judgment of him that returned, that is, of Christ. « Innocence. » [n: (Ibid.)] Because I have not rendered to them that repaid me. « Upon me. » [n: (Ibid.)] Justice and innocence [0854C] are upon me, because not from me, but from God.

VERS. 10.-- « Searching the hearts. » [n: (Ibid.)] What each one thinks. « The reins. » What delights each one; because the end of care and of thought is delight, to which one strives to attain. Men can see our works in words and in deeds: but with what mind they are done, and whither they desire to attain, God alone sees; who, when he sees the heart to be in heaven, and not to be delighted in the flesh, but in the Lord, that is, when the thoughts are good, directs also their ends, the just man, and not only directs, but helps the just man.

VERS. 11.-- « A just help. » [n: (Ibid.)] There are two offices of medicine, namely to heal disease and to keep health. According to the first he said in another psalm: « I am weak, heal me, O Lord. » According to [0854D] the other this perfect man here says, « my just help is from the Lord. » For both that medicine and this make men whole: but that one carries a man over from sickness to health, this preserves him in health itself. That one is a remedy for escaping disease; this affords a remedy lest he fall back into disease. By that one we are healed when sick, by this we are kept when whole.

VERS. 12.-- « God the judge. » [n: (CASS.)] Here he terrifies by the fear of the judgment to come, and promises hope to those who return. « Is he angry. » [n: (AUG.)] Another reading: « Doth he bring anger every day. » In the Greek more significantly, « he bringeth anger: » because the anger with which he punishes is not in himself, but in the angels of anger, through whom he punishes: who are delighted with human punishment not for the sake of the justice in which they do not rejoice, but for the sake of [0855A] malice: whom he does not gather every day for vengeance, but by patience invites to repentance.

VERS. 13.-- « His sword. » [n: (AUG.)] Another reading: He hath made bright his javelin. [n: (CASS.)] « His sword, » he will exercise manifest vengeance, nor is this said unequally, because he hath bent his bow, » he threatens, lest his patience be thought remiss; « he hath prepared, » that he may be thought about presently to send the arrow. « His bow. » [n: (Ibid.)] From a like case. For as he who has bent a bow threatens, so through sacred Scripture he threatens the wicked, lest the patience of God be thought remiss; and « he hath prepared, » who may be thought about presently to send an arrow: as one prepares a bow, that he may send an arrow. « He hath bent his bow, » [n: (AUG.)] by tempering the Old Testament [0855B] through the New Testament: « He hath prepared, » by explanation. [n: (Ibid.)] Or, « he hath prepared. » God prepares and disposes, etc., *as far as* but for those burning with zeal, arrows.

VERS. 15.-- « Behold. » As it were, these things God hath prepared in the Scriptures. [n: (Ibid.)] Whence it seems to a man to be the cause of damnation. But « behold » that it is not from God, but from man, because « he hath opened a pit, etc. He hath conceived sorrow, » [n: (Ibid.)] that is, by the appetite of temporal things. « He hath brought forth, » he has effected, sent out: and in the iniquity sent out there is a pit, that is, a downfall for others. Whence he adds:

VERS. 16.-- « He hath opened a pit. » And so he is comparable to one opening a pit: for he opens by conceiving [0855C] fraud: he digs out by travailing, by pressing on the work of fraud; he falls in by bringing forth fraud, namely, by perpetrating it.

VERS. 17.-- « His sorrow shall be turned, etc. » The head, the soul, the crown, the reason: over which sins have dominion, and remain over: and they shall come down upon it when they shall thrust it into punishments.

VERS. 18.-- « I will confess to the Lord. » [n: (CASS.)] The conclusion, as it were gathering all things into a sum, he says that he confesses cheerfully.



## PSALM VIII.

VERS. 1.-- « Unto the end, for the winepresses, etc. » For the winepresses, that is, for the Churches, etc., *as far as* souls flow out into rest.

[0855D]VERS. 2.-- « O Lord, our Lord, how admirable is thy name! etc. » [n: (CASS.)] First the praises of God, his majesty, and his exalted working are described. « In the whole. » [n: (Ibid.)] Let not the Jews therefore, or any others, claim for themselves what belongs to all, which is the intent. « Above the heavens. » [n: (AUG.)] Literally, or the Scriptures: because it exceeds the utterances of all the Scriptures; or, « above the heavens. » Continuing with what follows, that is, into the Scriptures, which he bends down to babes, on account of the enemies of the cross: who are destroyed, when the Scriptures are seen, that is, understood, to be led down as far as babes: and when through them they are nourished unto strength, and are raised to the sublimity of understanding.

VERS. 3.-- « Out of the mouth of infants. » [n: (Ibid.)] Of the unwise, to whom the Apostle gave milk, whom those signified [0856A] who praised the Lord, in whom the Lord uses this testimony. Out of these he hath perfected praise, because if only the perfect were in the Church, no counsel would be taken for the human race; as now, when even the least, not yet capable of the knowledge of spiritual things, are nourished by faith in the history of things temporal. « Enemies » are all who speak things contrary to the dispensation made through the Crucified. « Defenders » the heretics seem of the faith, the philosophers of wisdom, although yet the former impugn the faith, and the latter true wisdom. « Sucklings, » [n: (CASS.)] not in age, but as Peter has it, « as newborn babes [n: (I Petr. II)], » who cannot yet take food, but milk; and it is: thou art proclaimed not only by the perfect, but also by the unwise. Or, he calls infants and sucklings the apostles, who were simple and unlearned men; whence: « The foolish things of the world [0856B] « God hath chosen, that he may confound the strong [n: (I Cor. I)]. » [var: Vulg. 1Cor 1:27 *quae stulta sunt mundi elegit Deus, ut confundat sapientes*, "the foolish things of the world God hath chosen, that he may confound the wise"; *fortia* answers to a second clause, *infirma mundi elegit Deus*] The praise is more perfect. Or, out of the mouth of infants and sucklings, that is, out of the words of the Hebrew children thou hast perfected praise, which seemed to be done childishly, on account of the enemies, that it might not seem to be human wisdom, but divinely given. « Thine enemies. » The pagans. « The enemy. » The Jew, who is an enemy of God, while he defends the Father alone: because « he that honoureth not the Son, honoureth not « the Father, » or an enemy in his will.

VERS. 4.-- « For I shall see. » Before the Jew, in spirit. « Thy heavens, » the apostles, I shall see present in their descendants; this well suits those of the first age. « The works of thy fingers. » Because they are perfected by the cooperation of the Trinity, which is signified here in the three fingers, as there: « Who weigheth the bulk of the earth with three fingers. » [n: (Isa. XL.)] And through them, [0856C] « The moon and the stars which thou hast founded. » Not they.

VERS. 5.-- « What is man. » [n: (Ibid.)] Secondly, he says that the nature of man, once corrupted, is exalted in Christ above all things. For before he had shown the omnipotence of the Godhead. By man, sinners; by the son of man, the spiritual. [n: (Ibid.)] Man, any vile person, of whom God is mindful in showing mercy, the Son of man, Christ as man: since through him thou visitest him, when the Word was made flesh, in which the physician came to the sick. « Or the son. » [n: (AUG.)] The disjunction hints that there is a distance between man and the son of man. Every son of man is man, but not conversely, as Adam. Every man therefore, who bears the image of the earthly: the Son of man, who is heavenly, and deservedly a new man, [0856D] son of man: because the new is made out of the old by a certain change. [n: (AUG.)] Why, leaving the higher things, does he speak of these lowest ones? But the sheep, etc., *as far as* who by the seed of the word of God make the hearts of men to bear fruit.

VERS. 8.-- « Moreover the cattle also. » He notes therefore that the wicked also are subject to Christ, which seems less: for these carnal men dwell in the breadth of the plain, that is, in the amplitude of pleasures, not in the mountains of virtues. « The cattle of the field. » [n: (Ibid.)] Who live in the flesh, where they climb nothing steep: but the broad way, to which they cling, is that which leads to destruction: whence also Abel is slain in the field.

VERS. 9.-- « The birds, » the proud: of whom, « They have set their mouth against heaven [n: (Psal. LXXII)]. » Whose head says: « I will ascend into heaven, and I will exalt [0857A] my throne, and I will be like the Most High [n: (Isa. XIV)]. « The fishes of the sea that walk through the paths of the sea, » the curious. These three, the pleasure of the flesh, pride, curiosity, include all vices, which are the concupiscence of the flesh, the ambition of the world, that is, pride, the concupiscence of the eyes, since through the eyes curiosity chiefly prevails.



## PSALM IX.

VERS. 1.-- « Unto the end, for the hidden sons. » [var: Vulg. Ps 9:1 *pro occultis filii*, "for the hidden things of the son", a genitive singular, which is what the gloss's *cujus* presupposes] Nor is it added whose, by excellence he is the only-begotten of God. « Hidden. » Because other things of his are manifest, etc., *as far as* and of the punishment by which he judges, in purging, in converting, in blinding.

VERS. 2.-- « I will confess to thee, O Lord, with my whole « heart, etc. » The prophet sings joyfully, because the worship [0857B] of the devil is destroyed by the coming of Christ: and Christ reigns, judges, helps.

VERS. 4.-- « When my enemy is turned back, etc., » « they shall perish, etc. » [n: (AUG.)] Because when thou hast turned the devil backward, his members are weakened, so that they can do no hurt, and they shall not be, or, they shall be utterly destroyed by thy assault, when thou shalt say, « Go, ye cursed [n: (Matth. XXV)], » [var: Vulg. Mt 25:41 *Discedite a me maledicti*, "Depart from me, ye cursed"] or, they shall perish when God takes away iniquity from them.

VERS. 5.-- « Thou hast made my judgment and my « cause. » [n: (Ibid.)] Because they have served me to my profit, because « thou hast sat. » « Thou judgest. » [n: (HIER.)] The Father, that Christ might die for the race of men. [n: (AUG.)] Or, Christ shall judge at the end, to whom the Father hath given all judgment.

[0857C]VERS. 6.-- « For ever and for ever. » [n: (Ibid.)] The age is this present one which rolls on: the age of the age is eternal, of which that one is the image. Or, it is a determination, lest thou shouldst take this present one.

VERS. 7.-- « The javelins. [n: (CASS.)] The power of the devil shall be destroyed at the end of the world. « Cities. » His peoples. « With a noise, » that is, with the greatest outcry, as happens when prosperous things are shut up with a heavy « end. » But on the contrary the Lord remaineth. « The end, [n: (AUG.)] » is the sword which he shall brandish at the second coming, judging openly, and which now judges secretly, up to which the javelins of the enemy avail somewhat, but then they shall wholly fail. « Of them. » [n: (CASS.)] In the plural, although above he said the enemy in the singular, because although the demons are many, yet because in [0857D] malice they are alike, they are as it were one.

« With a noise. » [n: (AUG.)] That is, both the din and the tumult of impiety hath perished: or, when impiety is overthrown, a din is made, because none comes to the highest peace but he who has fought with the vices with a great din. « His throne. » [n: (CASS.)] The judiciary power, he who was judged in humility shall judge in majesty.

VERS. 10.-- « And he is become. » How great a thing it is, that he who is judge should be a refuge to the poor man, who desires nothing of the world. « In opportunities. » [n: (AUG.)] Man is not more opportunely turned away from the world, unless to these pleasures labours and sorrows be mingled.

VERS. 11.-- « Let them hope. » [n: (AUG.)] When they cease to hope in the things of the world. Those turned away from the world, seeking where to fix their hope, the knowledge of the name receives. The name [0858A] is known, when he whose the name is, is known. His name is Lord, which he knows who willingly serves him, or, who is, and therefore let no one hope in transitory things, which have nothing besides "it was" and "it will be." For what in them is to come, when it has come, straightway becomes past, it is awaited with desire, it is lost with sorrow, it is not held with certainty. « For thou hast not forsaken. » The prophet provokes the faithful to praise, looking upon the benefits of the present time and of the time to come.

VERS. 12.-- « Sion. » [n: (Ibid.)] Contemplation, which is practised now, that we may come to the vision of peace, that is, from Sion into Jerusalem: but any zealous contemplation falls into error, unless the Lord dwell in it. « Pursuits. » [n: (HIER.)] That is, his commandments, upon which zeal is to be spent.

[0858B]VERS. 13.-- « For requiring. » [n: (Ibid.)] But as if they who are sent to evangelize should say, No one believes us, and we are slain, he answered: not without fruit, because « requiring in vengeance their blood, » [var: Vulg. Ps 9:13 *quoniam requirens sanguinem eorum*, without *ultione*] in the judgment, where there shall be glory to the slain, punishment to the slayer: « he hath remembered, etc » [n: (Ibid.)] not after forgetfulness, but after a long time, according to the feeling of the weak, who think him forgetful, because it is not done so quickly as they wish: to whom also add, nay, he is not forgetful, as you think: and if I have said « he hath remembered, » this is against the complainers, who because of the delay think him forgetful.

VERS. 14.-- « Have mercy. » [n: (CASS.)] In his own person he says what each one asks. « See. » [n: (Ibid.)] The look of God is help, and when it comes, the light also puts the darkness to flight: from the pride of the enemy is measured the humility of the sufferer, [0858C] because these are humbled just so much as those are exalted.

VERS. 15.-- « Who liftest me up from the gates of death, etc. » [n: (AUG.)] That is, from all covetous desires, by which one goes to death: death now is that very gladness in the enjoying of things desired; whence the Apostle: « A widow that liveth « in delights is dead [n: (I Tim. V)]. » [var: Vulg. 1Tim 5:6 *Nam quae in deliciis est, vivens mortua est*, "for she that is in delights is dead while she liveth"] « In the gates of the daughter « of Sion. » They are all the best pursuits, etc., *as far as* which neither eye hath seen, nor ear heard. [n: (CASS.)] Gates against gates, because these are of death, those of life. « Sion. » [n: (Ibid.)] She begat all the Churches of the world, because there Christ was born, whence faith began.

VERS. 16.-- « I will exult. » [n: (AUG.)] Thus far the cry, thereafter the Church which is afflicted says, I will exult in Jesus. [n: (CASS.)] For by hope she is saved. « In the snare. » [n: (Ibid.)] [0858D] A hidden snare is a deceitful thought: the foot is the soul's love, which, if it be crooked, is called covetousness, if right, charity. For by it the soul is moved as it were to a place, whither it rejoices to have come by love. « In the snare » therefore, that is, in a fraudulent counsel: the foot, that is, the love which through fraud comes to a vain gladness, is caught, because that delight binds them, so that they dare not break off their love from thence and turn it to useful things. For if they should try, it is grief to leave the things which delight, and that grief does not suffer them to depart.

VERS. 17.-- *He shall be known.* [n: (CASS.)] Or, of the judgment to come, etc., *as far as* as certain men falsely think.

VERS. 18.-- *Let sinners be turned.* [n: (AUG.)] Here is the canticle of the diapsalma, as it were the hidden gladness of the separation: [0859A] which is made here not by place, but by affection, between the good and the evil. [n: (CASS.)] To sinners with Antichrist he foretells the end of evils; as it were this meanwhile, but at last *they shall be turned,* lest they rejoice in their own things. *Into hell,* lest they think themselves to be sent elsewhere.

VERS. 19.-- *Patience.* [n: (AUG.)] Patiently, for this chiefly will God be mindful of the poor, namely because of their patience.

VERS. 20.-- *Arise.* [n: (AUG.)] He prays for the judgment to come. [n: (CASS.)] The prophet, speaking of the end of the world, foresaw the coming of Antichrist; whence as if terrified he cries: *arise.*

VERS. 21.-- *Let them know.* That is, being tormented let them find by experience that they were foolish men.



## PSALM X.-- *According to the Hebrews.*

[0859B]VERS. 1.-- *Why.* [n: (AUG.)] Diapsalma, because it will be permitted to him (to Antichrist) to do such great things that he may be thought God: he adds, as it were, the voice of those who groan and who ask concerning the deferring of judgment.

[n: (CASS., AUG.)] *Why.* He treats at length of the evils of that time, etc., *as far as* but opportunely, namely thus, *While the wicked man is proud.*

VERS. 2.-- *The poor man is set on fire, they are caught in the counsels which they devise.* [n: (AUG.)] Wondrously does the providence of God work out of evils, etc., *as far as* thus the evil are damned, the just are saved.

VERS. 3.-- *For the sinner is praised.* Therefore he is proud, or is caught, because he is praised by his own, by the fulfilling of the desires of his sensuality.

[n: (AUG.)] The tongues of flatterers bind souls fast in sins: [0859C] for it is a delight to do those things in which not only is no reprover feared, but a praiser is even heard.

VERS. 4.-- *Of his wrath.* [n: (CASS.)] Of Antichrist's, because he will do all things turbulently and cruelly; or, of God's: he will not seek God by repenting; or, God will not seek him out, that he should take vengeance here, but is about to punish him more heavily in the time to come.

VERS. 5.-- *Defiled.* [n: (CASS.)] The cause of his wickedness is that he does not hold God before his eyes, and therefore his ways, that is, his thoughts and his works, are foul.

*At all times.* [n: (AUG.)] His own, namely of his life, etc., *as far as* and because *he shall have dominion over all his enemies.*

VERS. 6.-- *He hath said,* etc. I will take possession of all nations, but not *without evil,* that is, without sin, which is proper [0859D] to him. Or: I shall always be without evil; this he promises himself, *whose mouth is full of cursing,* etc.

*I shall not be moved.* [n: (AUG.)] Or, he will think that he will come by evil arts, and not otherwise, from a mortal generation into an eternal one, which Simon Magus also thought.

VERS. 7.-- *Under the tongue.* [n: (CASS.)] That is, his thoughts are of the labour of the saints and of bodily sorrow, which is plain in the martyrs. Or, the labour of the saints and the sorrow which lies in the punishments of the body is *under his tongue,* that is, it is the lighter; his tongue, which harms within: that other, namely, is the punishment without.

Or, *under the tongue,* because words are more feared and will do more harm than stripes, by which the soul is cast headlong and will perish.

[0860A]VERS. 8.-- *In secret places,* etc. [n: (AUG.)], that is, in ambiguous ones, where it is not easily seen what is to be sought and what is not.

VERS. 9.-- *His eyes are upon the poor man,* etc. [n: (Ibid.)] He sets out the ambushes: *the eyes,* as though with mercy and affection; or, he so deceives others that his eyes look cruelly upon the poor in spirit.

*A lion in his den,* etc. Because in him is violence and guile, etc., *as far as* through heretics and false brethren.

VERS. 10.-- *He shall bow himself down.* [n: (CASS.)] When through excessive prosperity he has let his mind slacken into idleness, he shall fall by a sudden destruction: and the Lord shall slay him with the spirit of his mouth.

*Unto the end.* They deny that God sees, etc., *as far as* he rouses the Lord, *Arise, O Lord.*

VERS. 12.-- *Arise, O Lord God, and let [0860B] thy hand be exalted, that thou mayest forget,* etc. [var: Vulg. Ps 9:33 *exaltetur manus tua ; ne obliviscaris pauperum*, "let thy hand be exalted: forget not the poor"] [n: (Ibid.)] He prays for judgment, that these things may be quickly ended here, things so great that no one would wish the time in which they happen to be prolonged.

*Arise.* As it were unlooked for, that they may be crushed, while thine own, exalted with thee, are glorified.

VERS. 13.-- *Wherefore hath the wicked provoked God.* [var: Vulg. Ps 9:34 *Propter quid*, the interrogative "for what reason"] [n: (AUG.)] And so now, both understanding and exulting concerning that judgment, he says, wherefore? that is, what does it profit to do such great evils?

*For God hath said in his heart,* etc. [var: Vulg. Ps 9:34 *dixit enim in corde suo*, with no *Deus*: the speaker there is the wicked man] [n: (Ibid.)] This sense calls for a delivery, etc., *as far as* lest they toil, or grow angry.

[n: (AUG.)] Christ bore with Judas and sent him to preach, and gave him the eucharist, that he might show that the gifts reach those who receive the faith, such as [0860C] Judas was.

VERS. 14.-- *For thou seest that thou considerest labour and sorrow.* This thou hast already foreordained, where thou deliverest them into thy hand.

*To the fatherless,* etc. [n: (AUG.)] To him for whom the world dies as a father, by which he was carnally begotten, so that he may say: *The world is crucified to me, and I to the world.*

VERS. 15.-- *Break thou the arm.* By punishments he terrifies, by good things he soothes.

*It shall be sought.* [n: (AUG.)] While it is still needful for our purging, as above, while the wicked man is proud, etc.; but because it shall not be found, that is, because there is now no one in whom there is room for his sin, smooth iron has no need of a file.

[0860D]VERS. 16.-- *The Lord shall reign for ever, and unto ages of ages.* This kingdom will be the more welcome for the evils that run alongside it, in that it will come when Antichrist has been destroyed; from this kingdom ye shall perish, O nations, but the poor shall have it, because *the desire of the poor,* etc.

VERS. 17.-- *The preparation of the heart.* [n: (CASS.)] Or, concupiscence, which is where a thing is sought more ardently with all the powers of the soul. Or, this, which God himself has prepared in their hearts. Or, that God may hear as soon as they wish to ask.

*The ear,* etc. [n: (AUG.)] When members are named which in us are visible and bodily, in God powers of operation must be understood.

*To judge for the fatherless and the humble,* etc. The second coming [0861A] will be manifest, etc., *as far as* the author of evils will be damned with his own people.

## PSALM X.

VERS. 1.-- *Unto the end.* [n: (AUG.)] A psalm of David the prophet, teaching men to resist heretics.

VERS. 2.-- *In the Lord I put my trust.* [n: (AUG.)] Of the heretics who wish to turn Catholics to their own depravity.

*[n: (Ibid.)] In the Lord I put my trust.* As to the letter it can be read of the passion; and Christ says, according to his manhood, as he is wont, against the terrors of the Jews, *In the Lord I put my trust.*

*In the Lord I put my trust.* When sinners wish to shoot at the upright.

[0861B] *For behold. [n: (Ibid.)]* These are the terrors of those who threaten us with sinners, that we may pass over to them as to just men. For they say: *The sinners have bent their bow,* the Scriptures, whence they may send poisoned arrows, which they prepare in the secret place of the heart.

VERS. 3.-- *In the dark.* [n: (CASS.)] Those who live in the simple sense, or in their own ambiguous opinions; not that Scripture avails for this, but because:

VERS. 4.-- *For the things which thou hast perfected.* He turns his speech away from these to God, as it were: Why do they say, Remove? Why is he terrified by sinners, who bend the bow to shoot at the upright? *because the things which thou hast perfected they have destroyed* in their assemblies, where they slay with poisons the little ones who were to be nourished with milk, out of whose mouth praise is perfected.

[0861C] *But the just, what hath he done?* [n: (AUG.)] According to either part, etc., *as far as* he harms his own soul, not the just man, who believes God.

VERS. 6.-- *The Lord questioneth,* etc. *But the just.* He threatens judgment, by fear of which they may forsake their falsehoods.

*He questioneth. [n: (Ibid.)]* That is, he proves; because the good are not wearied by obscure things, but are exercised in knowledge: they are not puffed up, but confirmed.

*The things which thou hast perfected.* That is, thou hast published them perfectly, or by fulfilling what was foretold. They have destroyed them by expounding them ill.

*[n: (Ibid.)] In the dark.* Or *when the moon is dark.* There are two opinions about the moon, etc., *as far as* he then calls the sacraments holy, when they are given through holy men.

VERS. 7.-- *He shall rain upon.* He shows what he renders to the wicked. --[n: (AUG.)] He rains from the clouds, that is, from false prophets. [0861D] Or from good things, whence snares flow out when they are ill understood: but not save upon those who are sinners. *Fire.* First they are laid waste by the fire of covetous desires: thence by the stench of evil works they are cast out from the saints, thence they are plunged in eternal punishments.

VERS. 8.-- *Justices. [n: (Ibid.)]* In the plural, that thou mayest take it of the just, in whom there are many justices, though God's is one.

*He hath beheld equity.* Equity was seen in his face, that is, in the knowledge of him: for his face is the knowledge of him, that is, the power by which he will become known to the worthy. Or *he hath seen,* because he does not give himself to be known to the evil, but to the good, which is equity.



## PSALM XI.

VERS. 1.-- *For the octave.* [n: (CASS.)] *For the octave,* because [0862A] here the prophet asks that the iniquity of the world be destroyed, so that he may come through to the truth of the promise to come.

[n: (AUG.)] The octave stands for the day of judgment, as was said in the sixth psalm.

VERS. 2.-- *Save me.* And he calls men back from the falsehoods of the world to the words of God, where salvation is; he asks to be saved from the perversity of the world.

*For there hath failed.* [n: (CASS.)] He sets out the many things he feared, gathering them into one, that the force may be the greater.

*The holy one. [n: (Ibid.)]* God, who is everywhere present, has failed so far as concerns men, who do not believe in him.

VERS. 3.-- *His neighbour.* [n: (AUG.)] Every man is a neighbour, since with none is evil to be done: by the condition of the first birth every man is a neighbour; by kinship of the flesh, friends and kinsmen; by the reckoning of mercy, [0862B] any man who does good.

*With a heart,* etc. Germination [cj: *Germinatio*; read *Geminatio*, "the doubling"] signifies a double heart, in which the deceitful man is described; whence: *A man double in mind is inconstant in all his ways.* The good have one heart and one soul.

VERS. 4.-- *May the Lord destroy all lips,* etc. [n: (CASS.)] That is, let them perish on every side who come together in one depravity.

*The tongue that speaketh great things. [n: (Ibid.)]* That is, a proud one: these are hypocrites, having a show in speech to deceive men, and not subject to God. [n: (REMIG.)] Or, philosophers.

VERS. 5.-- *Who have said: our tongue.* [n: (CASS.)] They boast of the power of their lips, as though they had not received it from God.

[0862C]VERS. 6.-- *Of the needy and the groaning of the poor.* AUG., [n: (CASS.)] *The needy,* the Gentiles, who had neither the law nor the prophets; *the poor,* the Jews. *Now.* Which was not so in the former people. *I will arise.* I shall appear in the Son. *I will set.* The consolation of the poor. *With confidence.* Powerfully, in that nothing resists him.

VERS. 7.-- *The words of the Lord. [n: (Ibid.)]* Against the evils of the world Christ was sent by the Father, whose words are true, those of the wicked false. *Chaste.* Against that which is above, *they have spoken vain things.--Tried by fire.* Against, *with deceitful lips, in a heart and a heart have they spoken.*

*Sevenfold.* [n: (AUG.)] Perfectly, etc. *as far as* and it shows what is perfect.

VERS. 8.-- *Thou, O Lord.* [n: (CASS.)] Turning himself to the Lord, he [0862D] shows the usefulness of the divine words.

VERS. 9. *Round about.* A conclusion concerning either part. [n: (AUG.)] *Round about,* that is, in the desire of temporal things, which are rolled round in seven days and do not come to the eighth, that is, to the eternal: whence *a wise king is the winnower of the wicked, and brings in upon them the wheel of evils [n: (Prov. II)]*, [var: Vulg. Prov 20:26 *Dissipat impios rex sapiens, et incurvat super eos fornicem*, "a wise king scattereth the wicked, and bendeth an arch over them"] that is, the circuit of things that roll.

*Thou hast multiplied the children of men. [n: (Ibid.)]* There is also a multiplication in temporal things which turns a man away from the unity of God: but the just are multiplied according to the height, when they go from virtue to virtue.

## PSALM XII.

VERS. 1.-- *Unto the end.* [n: (CASS.)] A psalm concerning the charity [0863A] of Christ, etc., *as far as* according to that word: *I desire to be dissolved and to be with Christ. [n: (Phil. I.)]*

VERS. 2.-- *How long.* [n: (CAS.)] Great is the ardour of him who endures, etc., *as far as* because hope is drawn out.

*Counsels.* [n: (AUG.)] There is no need of counsel, etc., *as far as* grieving and praying for eternal things.

VERS. 3.-- *How long shall he be exalted,* etc. [n: (CASS.)] He says that before the coming of Christ the devil held men captive. Or *over me,* that is, over my cruelty, because he is worshipped in the whole world; and because the world so perishes, he weeps.

VERS. 4.-- *Look thou upon me. [n: (Ibid.)]* After the complaint, a prayer, that he may be enlightened, lest he yield to the frauds of the enemy, he who trusts in mercy. *Look thou upon me.* [n: (AUG.)] Send him whom thou art to send. *Hear me.* [n: (CASS.)] For all, for whom he asks in general. *Enlighten.* If not in body, [0863B] let us see at least in mind. *That I sleep.* When, the light of faith being buried, they are shut up in carnal delight. *In death.* That is, in the sin which is committed against the Holy Spirit, of which John says: *There is a sin unto death, I do not say for it that any man should pray.*

VERS. 6.-- *They shall rejoice.* [n: (AUG.)] Not only for enlightening me, but lest the enemy rejoice. *I have hoped* [n: (CASS.)] And though he makes haste, yet he is patient: because even if it is put off, he is firm in hope, and already seeing inwardly he exults over him whom he longs to see in the flesh: whence he who before complained says that good things have already been given him. *He hath given.* Not, he will give: the great power of faith already has what is to come. *My heart shall rejoice.* If he desires so much, if he so serves him who is to come, what must be done when he has been received?

## PSALM XIII.

[0863C]VERS. 1.-- *The fool hath said.* [n: (CASS.)] A rebuke of the Jews, their malice being described. The first psalm concerning the rebuke of the Jews, etc., *as far as* this man is not God.

VERS. 2.-- *The Lord from heaven. [n: (Ibid.)]* But from thence they are without excuse, because the Lord looked down, that is, he stood forth out of human nature. [n: (AUG.)] From heaven, that is, he looks down through holy souls: for by himself nothing lies hidden from him. Or, from heaven, through holy souls, which are the heaven and the seat of God. Or, the Lord the Father from heaven, that is, from the secret of the divine mind. [n: (CAS.)] Or, from the equality of God the Father. *If there be one that understandeth.* Or, he saw whether there be one that understandeth, and he found that not one understands: because he does not seek, but turns aside, for: *All have gone aside,* etc.

VERS. 3.-- *A sepulchre. [n: (Ibid.)]* Thus made useless to others, [0863D] because their throat is a sepulchre, etc. *A sepulchre.* These three verses, which run as far as the second division, at *Shall they not know,* etc., are said not to be from the Hebrew, etc., *as far as* it does not have them.
