*Destruction* [n: (AUG.)] Thus they have been made useless to themselves, etc., *as far as* and unhappiness in the time to come.

(CASS) The way of the wicked, etc., *as far as: The fear of God before their eyes,* etc.

VERS. 4.-- *Shall they not know.* He threatens judgment to the wicked.

VERS. 5.-- *The Lord they have not called upon,* etc. [n: (AUG.)] For they do not truly call upon him who love the things that displease God.--[n: (CASS.)] Thence destruction is increased, because they must needs do evil, since the proud will not call upon God. As [0864A] it is caution to fear at the fitting time, so it is cowardice to tremble unfittingly, as for nation and place, the fear of God being cast away.

VERS. 6.-- *The counsel of the needy.* [n: (CASS.)] An invective against the Jews, etc., *as far as,* that is, proceeding from the Jews, etc.

VERS. 7.-- *Who shall give out of Sion,* etc. [n: (CASS.)] Last of all the conversion of the Jews is foretold, which will come to pass when the fulness of the Gentiles has come in. *When he shall have turned away.* For the Jews too the Apostle sets down this testimony of Isaiah: *There shall come out of Sion he that turneth away the captivity, or the impiety, from Jacob.*



## PSALM XIV.

VERS. 1.-- *Lord, who?* [n: (CAS.)] This psalm is properly an instructor in morals, a repressing of presumption: where [0864B] it treats of the unity of the present Church and of the quiet to come. First the prophet asks, as a priest standing before the face of the Lord, and seeks answers; wishing to know whom God judges worthy of his Church.

VERS. 2.-- *He that. [n: (Ibid.)]* The answer of the Lord, etc. *as far as* as Christ the lamb, without breaking of members, who properly entered the temple, not that he might be expiated like the rest, but without stain. *He worketh justice.* As when he cast out of the temple those that sold and bought.

VERS. 3.-- *He that speaketh.* Here he follows it out more fully. Truth is when the thing is so as it is said: this Christ did properly. *In his heart,* who at times kept silence toward the unworthy who questioned him fraudulently. *He hath not wrought deceit.* Deceit is in the tongue, when one thing is said and another is in the breast. Or, *he hath not wrought deceit,* because all that he heard from the Father, [0864C] without any addition or suppression, he made known to his neighbours, that is, to the disciples; to the Jews, his neighbours according to the flesh. *Nor hath he done evil to his neighbour.* But he prayed for them; he did not take up reproach against his neighbours, as is plain from this, that he did not tear Judas with a biting rebuke.

VERS. 4.-- *To nothing,* etc. [n: (AUG.)] This is perfection, that the malignant one avail nothing in a man, and that this be in his sight, that is, that he know for certain that no one is malignant save he who turns from the Creator to created things. *The malignant one,* that is, the devil, is despised; as when he said: *Go back, Satan,* etc. On the contrary, *he glorifieth them that fear the Lord.* He swore, when he promised the apostles with most certain truth: *Now I will not call you servants, but friends.*

[0864D]VERS. 5.-- *He that sweareth to his neighbour,* etc. [n: (Ibid.)] These three things are not great, etc., *as far as* since in the conclusion above he set down a past tense, *he is brought to nothing.*

*Money.* Either it is earthly, which is utterly forbidden to be given out at usury, and which the Lord handed over to Judas to be paid out to the poor, not for usury. Or it is spiritual, which is commanded to be given out at usury, that is, preaching for the instructing of men. *And gifts against the innocent.* Christ does indeed receive gifts, as from the Magi, and daily devout offerings; but not against the innocent; rather he gave his soul for them. *He that doeth these things shall not be moved.* Behold the complete answer to the questioner: he does them (he says), that he may invite us to the act, not only to [0865A] the singing of them. *He that doeth these things shall not be moved.* [n: (CASS.)] The conclusion. In like manner he that doeth these things shall rest on the mountain.

## PSALM XV.

VERS. 1.-- *The inscription of a title.* [n: (CASS.)] This title is taken from the Gospel, not from the Old Testament. What the title noted, that is, victory and kingdom, belongs to Christ, and that is what is treated of here.

VERS. 2.-- *Preserve me,* etc. The second psalm, which touches briefly on the passion and the resurrection; first, Christ, showing the one nature of man, asks to be preserved, saying that all that he endured has been brought through to the glory of his inheritance. [n: (AUG.)] Christ speaks here according to his manhood, of whom the royal title stood out in the passion: *Jesus of Nazareth, King of the Jews.--Of my [0865B] goods,* etc. [n: (CASS.)] If God has no need of the goods of Christ the man, but Christ the man has all things from him, the arrogance of men is overthrown, and the heresy of the Pelagians, who think that anything is done by their own good powers.

VERS. 3.-- *To the saints who are in the land. [n: (Ibid.)]* Christ has now set out how he prayed; he now tells what good things God did for him. [n: (AUG.)] *To the saints,* etc., *as far as* and power that I might rise again.

*He hath made wonderful.* [n: (CASS.)] As if to say, these things I said to the Lord: but the Lord made them wonderful, that is, he made my wills wonderful; and they are wonderful in that those who obey them become heavenly out of earthly.

VERS. 4.-- *They are multiplied. [n: (Ibid.)]* Whereby he made them wonderful, [0865C] etc., *as far as* which sound together in one note, by which his wills are shown.

VERS. 5.-- *The portion of my inheritance.* [n: (AUG.)] Let others choose other things, etc., *as far as* but those who choose God as their portion. [n: (CASS.)] Or, *the Lord* is the *portion,* that is, the share and the reward, *of my inheritance,* that is, of the saints. And *the portion of my cup,* that is, the wage of the passion. For the inheritance belongs to the Gentiles, the cup to the passion, which when drunk gives resurrection. But let no one think himself an heir save he that is illustrious.

VERS. 6.-- *The lines,* etc. [n: (Ibid.)] As it were by lot, in which is the divine election, as the lot also fell upon Matthias, and each man takes according to his own measure. *In goodly places.* That is, among the saints.

VERS. 7.-- *I will bless the Lord. [n: (Ibid.)]* He gives [0865D] thanks to the Father for understanding, and for the passion, in which by his favour he conquers, and for the resurrection, and for confession; and these are the things through which the inheritance is restored, so that he is king of the Jews, as the title says. *Understanding,* etc. [n: (AUG.)] Whereby this inheritance can be seen and possessed. *Moreover,* etc. That is, above understanding. *Even till night,* etc. That is, till death, he hath instructed me, that is, my lower part, the assumption of flesh, that he might drive out the darknesses of mortality which that understanding does not have, because for them it could neither be troubled nor fail. *My reins.* From whom according to the flesh I came forth, namely my kindred: they have chided me with injuries, with temptation and with passion.

VERS. 8.-- *I set the Lord before me. [n: (Ibid.)]* From this draw an example, whereby sins may be shunned: for he who [0866A] always beholds God with the keen edge of the mind is not bowed down by sins.



## PSALM XVI.

VERS. 1.-- *The prayer of David.* [n: (CASS.)] The prayer belongs to the person of the Lord, the Church being joined to him, which is his body. *Hear, O Lord. [n: (Ibid.)]* This psalm makes us attend to the true nature of his humanity. The prayer of Christ is an instruction of the faithful: as by weeping over Lazarus he taught us to have charity toward our neighbour; he fled his persecutors, that he might take from us the boldness of ill-considered rashness. First, he asks that it be rendered to him according to justice. *Not in deceitful lips.* As one who is unjustly condemned; or, as one who says: Lord, Lord, and does not do the works. [n: (AUG.)] Or, let my judgment come forth from thy countenance not in deceitful lips, so that in judging I may not put forth [0866B] anything other than I understand.

VERS. 2.-- *From thy countenance.* [n: (CASS.)] That is, according to those things which thou knowest in me, who have no sin. *Judgment.* Whereby he who was judged may be seen to have come forth as his own. *From thy countenance,* not from the disposition of the Jews, which deed is plain in the resurrection. Or, *judgment,* whereby he discerns all things.

VERS. 3.-- *Thou hast proved.* [n: (CASS.)] From the likeness of a furnace, etc., *as far as* thou knowest me to be pure.

VERS. 4.-- *Hard ways. [n: (Ibid.)]* Namely the works of men: which, namely, the works of men deserve. Or, which men ought to imitate, for whom the road of the vices is easy, that of the virtues hard. Or, it was not found, so that he should not speak, that is, that I may not say these things boastfully, or, that I may not sin with my tongue, which is the work of men. [0866C] [n: (AUG.)] Or, the hard ways of the thief, etc., *as far as* after I had gone along the hard ways and completed my steps.

VERS. 5.-- *Perfect thou.* [n: (CASS.)] Even to the end: before that there is nothing but a beginning. Steps, that is, human actions, so that they who wish to imitate may not be moved away from thee, nor their footsteps from them.

VERS. 6.-- *I have cried. [n: (Ibid.)]* Secondly, he prays that his purity be delivered from the ambushes of the Jews. I too have cried, as though thou shouldst say, this belongs to none but the just man, who prays with confidence, because he knows he is heard.

VERS. 7.-- *Make wonderful.* [n: (AUG.)] Wonderful are the things that are new: these are the things which he yielded in the flesh, the Jews marvelling.

VERS. 8.-- *The apple of the eye.* It is very small, and yet through it the keen edge of sight is directed, whereby light and darkness are distinguished, [0866D] just as through the humanity of Christ the godhead is what discerns between the good and the evil.

VERS. 10.-- *Their fat.* [n: (CASS.)] From the likeness of animals, which are puffed up from fat, that is, they have kept pride, not lean, that is, not humble; the fat, that is, they have kept the carnal understanding; or, fattened with crimes they have lost true understanding, whence it remains for them to speak proud things. *Their mouth hath spoken pride.* Not their heart, because often the wicked condemn in their heart what they defend with their mouth.

VERS. 12.-- *They have taken me,* etc. [n: (Ibid.)] Whence had they this opportunity? I answer: they took it from God, whose ordering it is; or, from the betrayer; or, they received it greedily from the judge. *As a lion prepared,* etc. [n: (AUG., CASS.)] [0867A] The princes are to be compared to the lion, the devil, by whose authorship they act: the people is *the young of a lion,* that is, the son of the devil, *dwelling,* remaining in ambushes, because it is the way of evil men to cover their vows, that they may accomplish them secretly.

VERS. 13.-- *Arise, O Lord.* [n: (CASS.)] A prayer concerning retributions. *Prevent,* etc. [n: (Ibid.)] From the likeness of one running: there is no one who does not sin against himself before he sins against another. *Supplant him:* from the likeness of one wrestling, that is, make them ineffectual and cast them down. *Deliver my soul from the wicked one.* [n: (AUG.)] By raising it again from the death brought upon it by the wicked. *The sword.* My soul is the sword, which thy hand, that is, thy power, took up, that through it he might war down the kingdoms of iniquity and divide the just from the wicked. *Divide them,* etc., [n: (CASS.)] that is, the Jews divided, etc., *as far as* misery, as though deservedly: because, of the hidden things. *Of the hidden things* [0867B] *that are thine.* That is, of uncleannesses, etc., *as far as* that is, they shall be sharers of evils even to satiety. *Of the hidden things.* [n: (AUG.)] Not only is this punishment visible to them, but from hidden things, that is, from sins (which are hidden away from the light of thy truth), their memory is full, so that they forget God.



## PSALM XVII.

VERS. 1.-- *I will love thee, O Lord, my strength,* etc. [n: (CASS.)] Christ says this, and so the Church, that is, the whole Christ.

[n: (Ibid.)] First, the prophet, gathering up the sum, gives thanks for the deliverance.

VERS. 3.-- *My refuge. [n: (Ibid.)]* When there was need of counsel, David fled to him and found it. *My deliverer,* as one who, being powerful, sets free a man taken captive by his enemies. *My protector,* [0867C] [n: (AUG.)] because I have not presumed upon myself, as though raising a horn of pride against thee; but I have found thee the height of my salvation, and that I might find it thou hast taken me up.

VERS. 4.-- *Praising I will call upon.* [n: (CASS.)] After all things he does not boast of himself, puffed up, but ascribes all to God: and therefore he is safe; for he who does otherwise, left to himself, perishes, even though he seemed to have conquered.

VERS. 5.-- *They have compassed me about. [n: (Ibid.)]* In the person of the just before the coming of Christ, etc., *as far as* who have been troubled by rushing iniquities.

VERS. 6.-- *The sorrows of hell.* The Gentiles, to be tormented in hell with the sorrow they have deserved. *The snares of death have prevented me,* there follows:

VERS. 8.-- *It was moved.* Behold how, etc., *as far as* and this, which mortality and possibility forestalled.

[0867D] *Out of his temple.* [n: (CASS.)] Either of heaven, or of the body of Christ that was to come.

*It was moved.* [n: (AUG.)] Thus, when Christ was glorified, sinners were moved and trembled. *The foundations,* etc. [n: (CASS.)] The presumptions of the proud, that is, riches and the like on which they leaned: for the hope of the world, which God hates, has passed away.

VERS. 10.-- *The heavens. [n: (Ibid.)]* That is, he bowed the preachers down from the contemplative life to ministry, and to the bearing of troubles. *He came down,* etc. He himself suffered in them, and came into the knowledge of certain men. *Darkness:* the dark, who even persecute, are under his power; or, those who believe simply are subject to him.

[0868A]VERS. 11.-- *And he ascended.* For he makes certain men full of knowledge, in whom he stands out above, whose swiftness of understanding he transcends: but the dark he uses secretly: whence, *Assur is the rod of my wrath, yet he himself hath not known it,* but his own know. *He flew upon the wings of the wind.* [var: Vulg. Ps 17:11 *volavit super pennas ventorum*, "the wings of the winds", plural — the plural the gloss's own *Ventos* takes up] The winds, than which nothing is swifter, his fame outstrips, as when, newly born, he was rather announced by a star.

VERS. 12.-- *Darkness.* [n: (AUG.)] The obscurities of the sacraments; or, in these darknesses, where seeing we walk by faith, and not by sight [n: (Matth. II)].

Not through the heavens. [n: (CASS.)] He came down and ascended [n: (II Cor. V)], but through the ethereal clouds (which is in the Hebrew), that is, through the prophets in whom the teaching is obscure; but through the clouds which (not of the ether, that is, the Apostles) [0868B] are exceedingly bright in the knowledge of him, that is, contemplating more nearly.

VERS. 13.-- *Hail.* [n: (AUG.)] Rebukes, which strike hard hearts; but if the fire of charity dissolves them, in a gentle soul it becomes water that satisfies [ *alias* saliens]. *And coals,* because by that fire dark hearts are kindled and come to life again.

VERS. 14.-- *He thundered.* [n: (CASS.)] To the letter, as when the thunder was seen, God saying: *I have glorified it, and I will glorify it again. He gave his voice:* in that he did not say: This is my beloved Son in whom I am well pleased.

VERS. 15.-- *And he sent. [n: (Ibid.)]* The Lord thundered, and the Lord sent: for the passing over to the Gentiles was not always held to be certain nor reasonable.

VERS. 16.-- *And the fountains appeared.* When Christ came, [0868C] there lay open what the obscurity of the divine word had covered. *At thy rebuke.* A turning to God: as it were with thanksgiving, because through preaching he rebuked sinners, and breathed into them that they should fear the wrath to come.

*He sent. [n: (Ibid.)]* Thus far concerning the coming of the Lord; from here on, concerning Christian times. *He sent,* the Father, Christ. *He took me.* The Church his bride. *Out of the waters. [n: (Ibid.)]* Of baptism. Or *he sent the Spirit,* who is the gift. *From on high,* because he is equal to the Father and to the Son. *He took,* as it were a runaway. *He assumed,* into the height of virtue and of good work.

VERS. 18.-- *From my enemies.* [n: (AUG.)] That is, from vices, which above all enemies do the more harm. *And from them who,* etc. That is, from outward enemies, men, and also demons, as must needs be, because they have been strengthened [0868D] in a certain respect. *Behold, they have prevented,* that is, they have prevailed. By this he delivers the more, because they have been strengthened; for when the Church is afflicted, it grows the more.

VERS. 20.-- *Into a large place. [n: (Ibid.)]* Through the savagery of the wicked, the Church grows in number and in merit. *He hath saved me,* in that it came to the faith. *Because he would have me.* [n: (AUG.)] That is, he chose freely.

VERS. 21.-- *And he will reward according to the justice* of a good will. *And according to the cleanness of the hands [n: (Ibid.)]* the works which come after the first benefits of God, namely faith and charity, which God bestows freely, without preceding merit.

VERS. 22.-- *Because I have kept. [n: (Ibid.)]* Or, joining it thus [0869A] with the preceding verse, etc., *as far as* and as I have begun, *I shall be unspotted,* whence in time to come he will reward me.

VERS. 24.-- *With him,* cleaving to him. *And I will keep myself,* lest I fall again. From iniquity, which is the death of the blessed man.

VERS. 25.-- *And he will reward.* [n: (CASS.)] God rewards in two ways: here in the present, that the just may be justified, and he that is in filth may be filthy still, whence here he says: *With the holy thou wilt be holy,* etc.; and in time to come a reward for the good and a punishment for the evil, whence there: *For thou wilt save the humble people,* etc., as if to say, so here: because in time to come *thou wilt save the humble people.*

[n: (AUG.)] And he will give: now not only for the breadth of faith which works through love, but for long-suffering he will reward me. *In the sight:* [0869B] this belongs to the just, that as they always behold God, so God may continually look upon them.

VERS. 26.-- *With the holy.* [n: (CASS.)] He had said that it was rendered according to justice, etc., *as far as* because *thou, O Lord, wilt save the humble people. And thou wilt bring down the eyes of the proud.*

[n: (AUG.)] There is also a hidden depth, by which thou art understood to be holy with the holy, because it is thou that sanctifiest.

*For thou dost light.* What he declared concerning the elect and the sanctified, he now prays concerning others.

VERS. 29.-- *My lamp. [n: (Ibid.)]* There were preachers, in whom was light, that is, the knowledge of truth, by which they enlightened others; whence: *We have this treasure in earthen vessels. Darkness.* [n: (AUG.)] those who are still in darkness. *Mine.* By predestination.

[0869C]VERS. 30.-- *In my God.* [n: (CASS.)] For my God is the way, undefiled, by which one goes to heaven. Or, the way by which the holy and immaculate Virgin came into the world. And whatever he said has been tried by the Holy Spirit, that is, it is useful and honourable: as a wise man first judges within himself whatever he says. *I shall go over. [n: (Ibid.)]* A man cannot utterly pull it down, but by the help of God he passes over the wall, the obstacle of sins.

VERS. 31.-- *My God. [n: (Ibid.)]* And, joining them, there follows: *Thou wilt deliver me from the contradictions of the people.* The rest he interposes by way of commendation. *The words.* They are proved by the fire of trial. *Of all. [n: (Ibid.)]* No one is excepted, save he who will not hope.

VERS. 32.-- *For who. [n: (Ibid.)]* I say God: for who is another God, save he who also [0869D] is Lord over things? And because he is known in Judea, not the gods of the nations; these things are against the pagans, who fashion many for themselves.

VERS. 33.-- *God who. [n: (Ibid.)]* Still further, beyond what is common, he commends him by what is special, for thirdly Christ is commended for his virtue and his power. *He hath girded. [n: (Ibid.)]* That is, he has armed him thoroughly. Or, from the likeness of one running. Or, it marks dignity and strength, which belongs to him who by his own power will judge the people, or the world. *Unspotted,* without sin. The stag leaps over thorns and gaping places, so Christ over the thorns and pits of sins: and as the stag climbs the heights, so Christ ascends above all creatures.

VERS. 35.-- *Who teacheth:* I make him wary of the ambushes that threaten. *Hands,* works or wills. *To battle,* [0870A] against the devil. *As a bow,* etc., by the likeness of a thing which does not exist, that is, unrelenting strength. *Arms.* The prophets of Christ, and the apostles, through whom he works: who are a brazen bow, because they do not grow soft by preaching, but, always strong, hurl forth the words of salvation.

VERS. 36.-- *The protection of salvation.* Not that I should suffer nothing, which nevertheless is for my profit. Tribulation is correction, and teaching, and enlargement, and confirmation.

VERS. 37.-- *Thou hast enlarged* [n: (AUG.)] Lest fleshly straits hinder, because thou hast made wide the charity that works cheerfully, even out of those things which are under me, namely out of mortal things and members.

VERS. 38.-- *I will pursue.* [n: (CASS.)] By reproving, by wearying them with divers afflictions.

VERS. 43.-- *And I will break them small.* [n: (AUG.)] Behold the same [0870B] operation of the Father and of the Son. Above he says: *Thou hast destroyed, O Father,* and now he says, I will break them small, that is, I will take away their powers, so that afterwards they may yield to every wind of doctrine.

VERS. 44.-- *Thou wilt deliver.* [n: (CASS.)] Upon them, wrath: *But thou wilt deliver me,* the casting down of the Jews, and the exalting of the Gentiles.

VERS. 46.-- *The children.* The Jews whom I nourished, made strangers: *They have lied to me,* as to my honour. *They have grown old and have halted from their paths,* following their own traditions rather than God's.

VERS. 47.-- *He liveth. [n: (Ibid.)]* Fourthly, the Church exulting praises in sum the gifts of God. Or *he liveth,* because he gives life. *Blessed,* because he blesses all things. *And let the God of salvation be exalted.* [n: (AUG.)] I will not think of the God of my salvation after an earthly manner, nor of that salvation itself as earthly, [0870C] but on high I will hope in him.

VERS. 48.-- *Revenges.* [n: (CASS.)] The vengeance is dutiful, when the rebel is subdued. *From the wrathful.* It is a greater glory to deliver from raging evils.

VERS. 51.-- *Magnifying, the salvations.* God who magnifies: Doing, God who does.



## PSALM XVIII.

VERS. 1.-- *Unto the end.* [n: (CASS.)] A psalm of the first coming, whereby the tyrant falls and man is set free; here the prophet looks unto the end, that is, unto Christ, or unto the last times.

VERS. 2.-- *The heavens shew forth. [n: (Ibid.)]* First, speaking of the incarnation, he commends the preaching of the new law. [n: (AUG.)] The heavens, the apostles, concerning the glory of Christ in which [0870D] he is equal to the Father. [n: (CASS.)] Or the heavens, they who sent the star. *The glory. [n: (Ibid.)]*: That God freely forgives sins. *The works.* [n: (AUG.)] Those which are according to man, etc., *as far as* and these same are made night to him, giving to the darkened the knowledge which is of things human. [n: (CASS.)] And this so wonderfully, that it is in all tongues; and so widely, that it is in every part of the compass of the earth.

VERS. 3.-- *Day unto day.* [n: (CASS.)] He treats of it by demonstration. [n: (AUG.)] *Day unto day.* The Spirit brings forth to spiritual men the fullness of wisdom, etc., *as far as* this teaching is continued by days and by nights down to those who come after.

VERS. 4.-- *Speeches.* [n: (CASS.)] Public persuadings. *Words.* Common narrations. *Whose voices are not heard.* He would say more plainly, in whom their voices are not heard, [0871A] or, the voices of all their words are heard: he does the same thing by means of the relative.

[n: (AUG.)] The Spirit made the first men speak with all tongues, he who was to gather all nations into one; then one man was for all, so now one Christ, head and body.

VERS. 6.-- *In the sun* [n: (CASS.)] What he praises others for having done, he himself does: he preaches the incarnation. Or in the sun, in the open sight of the world, or in labour. The sun burns, and shines, and varies the seasons. *His tabernacle.* [n: (AUG.)] As it were a soldier's dwelling, that is, the dispensation of his incarnation, in which he came to storm the errors of the world. Or the tabernacle, the Church in the open, not in secret, so as to lie hid. *A bridegroom.* [n: (CASS.)] So often promised by the prophets, a great likeness of the sacrament: therefore born of [0871B] a virgin, that he might join to himself the Church a virgin. *Out of his bride-chamber.* [n: (AUG.)] That is, the virginal womb, where God was joined to human nature as a bridegroom to a bride. Behold, humble in mercy, but strong in majesty. *As a giant to run.* [n: (CASS.)] Unshaken, not to be called back, overcoming human nature by his power. *From the highest.* Here the Trinity is shewn, etc., *as far as* it marks the unity of the Trinity. *Neither is there any that can hide himself,* etc. [n: (AUG.)] The Word made flesh permitted no man to excuse himself out of the shadow of death: because the heat of the Word pierced even that. Or, the heat is the Spirit, whom he sent when he had returned, who is law, testimony, justice, commandment, fear and judgments, because he does these things.

VERS. 8.-- *The law of the Lord.* [n: (CAS.)] Secondly, he praises the precepts of the New and the Old Testament. [n: (Ibid.)] The law given through [0871C] Moses, correcting all things by the distinction of its words, and sending them on to the grace of Christ. *Converting.* [n: (AUG.)] By liberty, unto the imitating of himself, not pressing down with the yoke of servitude. *Souls.* Wills, not merely hands, as the Old Testament did. *The testimony.* Whatsoever [sic: *Quaecunquae*] testimonies he gave to Israel are full of truth, and have given wisdom to the humble. *Wisdom:* concerning things divine.

VERS. 9.-- *The justices of the Lord. [n: (Ibid.)]* All are right in him, who taught not what he did not do, so that they who imitate them might rejoice in them, seeing they would do them freely with charity, not slavishly with fear. [n: (CASS.)] *Justices,* the law which justifies; whatsoever it commands is just.

VERS. 10.-- *The fear of the Lord. [n: (Ibid.)]* The fear of the world is not holy, because it is not profitable, and it changes with the times. *The fear of the Lord* is without any perturbation, [0871D] because it is a love mingled with dread, because if it fears the judge, it knows him merciful. [n: (AUG.)] Fear, not that penal fear for earthly things, for love of which the soul is afraid, but the chaste fear whereby, the more it loves, the more it is wary of offending the bridegroom, and therefore it is not cast out, but abides. *Judgments.* The judgments of God need not another's authority.

VERS. 11.-- *Desirable things. [n: (Ibid.)]* Or, much to be desired, or much gold and stone; or much that is precious. Desirable above gold, etc., *as far as,* whose will he sets before his own, and they are sweeter than he himself is to himself. *Above gold,* that is, everything that is sought after, whether for riches, or for power, or for pleasure. *Honey.* The food of God is honey; that is, one now loosed from the world; and the honeycomb, that is, because he is still wrapped about, for whom there is need [0872A] of the pressure of God's hand squeezing him out, that he may go into life.

VERS. 13.-- *Who understandeth sins. [n: (Ibid.)]* Thirdly he asks to be purged of vices, that he may be a worthy psalmist, to report these sacraments. To a mind that sees, truth is sweet, and judgments sweet; to one that does not see, what sweetness is there? But why is it not seen? Because of sins: nor is it any wonder: *For who understandeth sins?* [n: (CASS.)] *Sins:* all of them, for certain of them are known. *Mine* defile, *others'* afflict, and now spare, drive back the persuader. *From my secret ones.* He includes all sins under two heads, etc., *as far as* whereby man was seduced; and by consenting he made it his own. [n: (AUG.)] There is no greater sin than to apostatize from God, which is the beginning of pride: but pride is the beginning and the cause of all sin.

## PSALM XIX.

[0872B]

*Unto the end.* [n: (CASS.)] A figure of the working; the Prophet sings the things that are to come.

[n: (Ibid.)] The Prophet out of exceeding charity wishes a prosperous issue for the Church, which he saw would come to pass through Christ.

VERS. 2.-- *May he hear thee:* the things which he wills to be done for her in the time of tribulation. *In the day of tribulation,* wherein we pray to God with the greater desire. *Of the God of Jacob,* that as to him, so to thee he may give the grace of blessing, and set thee before the Jews.

VERS. 3.-- *May he send:* the Father. *Help,* by sending the Son.

VERS. 4.-- *Mindful.* He treats as it were of a priest about to offer sacrifice and of a king about to fight. *Of thy sacrifice. [n: (Ibid.)]* He foresaw the sacrifice [0872C] of the Church, not of cattle, but of the blood and the body of Christ.

VERS. 5.-- *May he give.* Diapsalma, the things which he wishes and affirms for the time of glory. *All thy counsel.* [n: (AUG.)] Not only in that thou hast laid down thy soul for the life of very many; but also in that *blindness in part hath happened in Israel, and so all Israel should be saved.*

VERS. 6.-- *We shall rejoice. [n: (Ibid.)]* Therefore this prayer, because we shall rejoice and shall be magnified; he shews himself to be one of the Church so long a time before.

VERS. 7.-- *In powers.* [n: (AUG.)] Our powers are the salvation of his favour, since he gives help even out of tribulation, because vain is the salvation of men, which is of the left hand, whence also pride is born. *The salvation of his right hand.* [0872D] [n: (Ibid.)], that is, spiritual grace, etc., *as far as* they who presume upon earthly dignity.

## PSALM XX.

VERS. 1.-- *Unto the end.* The third psalm, of the two natures in Christ; it describes the heavenly king, that the world might believe by hearing him whom the Jew, having seen him, despises. He makes a demonstrative oration in praise of God.

VERS. 2.-- *Lord, in thy strength shall he rejoice.* [n: (CASS.)] He sets forth the sum, namely, the merit and the reward.

VERS. 4.-- *For thou hast prevented him.* [n: (CASS.)] Diapsalma; he describes his virtues and his glory, etc., *as far as* as it were for a crown to the head. *A crown. [n: (Ibid.)]* Because the circuit [0873A] of the Apostles hemmed him about as he taught, or the whole circle of the world, that is, the general Church.

VERS. 6.-- *In thy salvation: glory and great beauty,* etc. [n: (CASS.)] By the Word of the Father, whence comes that glory. *Glory* as to the judgment, because he shall judge in his Godhead. *Beauty* in majesty, which shall then appear. *Glory and great beauty,* etc. Glorious, as Peter, by whose shadow the sick were healed; and beauty is *upon him,* beside whom there is no other foundation. Therefore *upon him,* because thou shalt give . . . . .

VERS. 7.-- *In joy with thy countenance. For the king hopeth in the Lord, and in the mercy,* etc. [n: (ID.)]. That is, of this, that his own shall rejoice with thy presence, when thou shalt be all in all.

VERS. 9.-- *Let thy hand be found,* etc. [n: (ID.)] Of [0873B] the punishments of his enemies, as above of the praises of Christ.

VERS. 11.-- *Their fruit shalt thou destroy from the earth, and their seed.* There shall not be for them the fruit of blessedness in the land of the living.

VERS. 13.-- *For thou shalt make.* [n: (CASS.)] Things said of what is to come, etc., *as far as* that when they thought to kill, salvation might come to be.

VERS. 14.-- *Be thou exalted.* [n: (ID.)] The conclusion.



## PSALM XXI.

VERS. 2.-- *God, my God.* [n: (CASS.)] A complaint of being forsaken is set before, and prayer also, as in all the psalms of the passion. [n: (AUG., CASS.)] *God, my God:* he doubles it out of affection, or *God* of all through [0873C] the power of creation, *mine* properly through worship. [n: (AUG.)] When he had said this on the cross, he shewed that this psalm was written of himself. *Look upon me.* [n: (CASS.)] It is not in the Hebrew, but was added by the seventy interpreters. *Why hast thou forsaken me?* [n: (AUG.)] This is a wonder, since God also was with him; but it was for us, who in him are his body. *Far from my salvation,* that is, from that of my old man, because *salvation is far from sinners,* and these are the words not of justice, but of sins. Or, far from salvation are the words of my sins, which are to be taken away.

VERS. 3.-- *I shall cry.* [n: (AUG.)] He speaks of us, of his own body, as when perchance he says: *Let this chalice pass from me,* that is, he is thought to have feared to die; but he said these things for his own weak ones who fear to die, and so this also is the voice [0873D] of the members: *I shall cry and thou wilt not hear. By day, and thou wilt not hear, and by night, and not unto folly.* [var: Vulg. Ps 21:3 cries "by day" once only, without the doubled "and thou wilt not hear," and closes with *mihi*] [n: (CASS.)] Because there are no shadows in him, and yet he is not heard unto bodily salvation.

VERS. 4.-- *Thou dwellest in the holy place.* [n: (AUG.)] And so it is a wonder, because thou hast delivered the fathers; but I am a worm.

[n: (CASS.)] Thou dwellest in the holy place, because God was in Christ reconciling the world to himself. *The praise of Israel,* [n: (AUG.)] that is, of him that sees thee, not of him who wished to hide himself from thy sight.

VERS. 5.-- *In thee have our fathers hoped: they have hoped.* [n: (CASS.)] It is a great beauty, while he repeats *they have hoped.*

[0874A]VERS. 7.-- *But I am,* etc. [n: (AUG.)] Thus far Christ in the person of the old man; here now he speaks properly in his own person. *But I am.* Now properly Christ: I was born without seed, that I might be beyond men in man, in whom I was made a reproach, that at least so the pride of man might imitate the humble. *A worm and no man, a reproach.* [n: (CASS.)] So vilely trodden down, etc., *as far as* a worm, born without coupling, mortal, humble and vile, creeping in silence.

VERS. 9.-- *He hoped in the Lord,* etc. [n: (CASS.)] Saying: *Hope thou in the Lord,* etc. The irony of the Jews; behold, as it were a historical narration, not a prophecy, lest there be any excuse, lest there be any ambiguity.

VERS. 10.-- *From the breasts.* Not from the time when I began to be suckled, etc., *as far as* but thou hast been my God. *From the womb.*

VERS. 11.-- *From the womb.* [n: (AUG.)] Not only of a mother, which is the law of being born for all; but from the darkness of the Synagogue, in which he is unbelieving, [0874B] not yet born into the light of Christ, who trusts in those carnal observances of the sabbath and of circumcision.

VERS. 12.-- *Depart not from me,* thou who hast already begun.

*Thou hast drawn me out:* not a man; lest the virgin's childbearing be doubted, it was done by the power of God. *My hope from the breasts:* true humanity and great perfection; for from the breasts he does what one scarcely come of age can do.

*In thee was I cast,* not into the sins of man. Others are cast into Adam, whence they are guilty. *From my mother's womb, [0874C] my God.* [n: (CASS.)] Neither was the conception without God, nor in sin, as man's is; whence the Angel: *The Holy Ghost shall come upon thee.*

VERS. 13.-- *They have surrounded.* [n: (ID.)] The passion is described, which prayer concludes; to convince the Jews he relates things to come as though they were past.

VERS. 14.-- *They have opened.* [n: (ID.)] As wild beasts wishing to swallow him down, their mouth, not unlocked unto wisdom. *As a lion.* [n: (AUG.)] As a lion seizing they surrounded him, as one roaring they opened their mouth.

VERS. 15.-- *As water.* When water is poured out, etc., *as far as* whence, leaving him, they fled. [n: (CASS.)] As water is the sacrament of the washing away of sins, etc., *as far as* and it was made solid. *Like wax.* Well does he compare the mysteries of the law [0874D] to wax, etc., *as far as* for they have surrounded. *In the midst of my belly.* [n: (AUG.)] The belly signifies the weak in the Church, or the memory of the Church.

VERS. 16.-- *My strength is dried up like a potsherd.* [n: (CASS.)] Not as hay, but as a potsherd, which by fire is made the firmer. *My tongue.* [n: (AUG.)] They kept my precepts with them, through whom I was to speak: and through them I was brought down into the kindred of those who were the dust of death.

VERS. 17.-- *They have dug.* [n: (ID.)] The gospel narration. Although these things are so to the letter, the digging of the hands hints that they did not believe his works; of the feet, that they mangled the Apostles. [n: (CASS.)]: they have dug. [0875A] Dug earth bears fruit, so Christ wounded gave the fruit of life.

VERS. 18.-- *They have numbered all my bones.* [n: (CASS.)] The punishment of the cross is properly described. Thus they numbered the bones, that is, the Apostles; lest they should grow into a greater number, they sought to extinguish them. *They have looked upon me.* [n: (ID.)] With diligence and with delight, whereas the eyes are wont to be turned away from a cruel thing. Behold how obstinate they are: nothing was done by chance, nor was their heart softened by so many miracles.

VERS. 19.-- *They parted my garments among them,* etc. [n: (AUG.)] His own sacraments could be divided by heresies, but not the coat from above, that is, given by God; this is charity, or unity, which no man can divide. [n: (CASS.)] But perhaps it came to certain men even by the gift of God, [0875B] etc., *as far as* as the olive branch brought by the dove to the ark. [n: (AUG.)] Or, through the one soul the head and the body, which being one rejects diversities.

VERS. 22.-- *From the unicorns, my lowness,* etc. [n: (ID.)] The Jews, seeking earthly things only: or, boasting themselves singularly of the worship of God, or being singularly proud, the son also having been slain after the servants.

VERS. 24.-- *Ye that fear the Lord, praise him,* etc. [n: (CASS.)] Human fear brings forth not praise, but blame.

VERS. 25.-- *Let all the seed of Israel fear him, because he hath not despised,* etc. Whom he means by the seed of Jacob he opens, adding: *Of the poor man.* [n: (AUG.)] Not of him who cries out with the words of sins out of a vain life, but [0875C] of him who is not swollen with the pomps of the world.

VERS. 26.-- *With thee is my praise in a great church,* etc. [n: (ID.)] He places his praise with God, who teaches that there be no presuming in man.

VERS. 27.-- *They shall praise the Lord that seek him: they shall live.* [n: (ID.)] The poor are rich in God: therefore poor, because they praise God that they may seek him, and he himself is the riches of the poor. *Their hearts shall remember for ever and ever, and shall be converted to the Lord.* [n: (ID.)] Because he is the food of the heart.

VERS. 30.-- *They have eaten and have adored.* [n: (ID.)] Let not the dispenser forbid to eat, but let him warn the exactor to fear. *In his sight shall they fall.* [n: (CASS.)] The Church receives both, but he himself sees and discerns.

[0875D]VERS. 32.-- *It shall be shewn,* etc. [n: (ID.)] Or to the angels, who offer the prayers of men to God, not for instruction, but for ministry.

[n: (CASS.)] Note that the psalms of the passion end in the great hope of Christians, that it may be known that salvation is from the passion.



## PSALM XXII.

VERS. 1.-- *A psalm of David.* [n: (CASS.)] The Prophet foretold the return of the people from Babylon. *The Lord ruleth me.* A psalm of mercy, enumerating ten graces, that all may hope in the mercy of God. [n: (AUG.)] The Church speaks of Christ.

VERS. 2.-- *In a place of pasture.* [n: (CASS.)] The first grace, a place of pasture, that is, the divine word, which fattens those who meditate upon it. [0876A] *Upon the water.* The second grace, the water of refreshment.

VERS. 3.-- *My soul.* [n: (ID.)] The third grace, etc., *as far as* but first he brought me up upon the water of refreshment, first he converted my soul. *He hath led.* [n: (ID.)] The fourth, in that he led me upon the precepts of charity. *Upon the paths.* [n: (AUG.)] The narrower commandments and the more perfect counsels; as it were upon paths few just men walk, even as paths are narrower than roads.

VERS. 4.-- *For though I shall walk.* [n: (CASS.)] The fifth, that in the shadow of death he fears no evils. *Thou art with me.* [n: (AUG.)] In the heart by faith, that after the shadow of death I may be with thee. *Thy rod.* [n: (CASS.)] The sixth, correction; or the rod is strictness, which converts vices. The staff is governance, which upholds the faithful.

[0876B]VERS. 5.-- *Thou hast prepared in my sight.* [n: (CASS.)] The seventh, the table of the Lord, the blessed banqueting, the fullness of faith, the heavenly food, of which the Apostle: *He that eateth and drinketh unworthily, eateth and drinketh judgment to himself. In my sight a table:* even if not that of the wicked. Of this it is said: *But these at the table of the rich man,* that is, of Christ, *know that thou too must prepare the like,* that is, to suffer as he did. *Thou hast anointed:* the eighth, the anointing of the head, namely of Christ, which was dried up by no barrenness of sin, whence the rest of the members rejoice, because from the head the ointment ran down upon the beard, the beard of Aaron, and unto the skirt of his garment. *And my chalice.* The ninth, the chalice, the blood of Christ, which so inebriates that it makes men sober. *Mine,* because I receive it. Or, thine, because thou givest it. The table and the chalice, flesh and blood, although they are one sacrament, yet under diverse [0876C] species, because both are taken, are here set down separately.

VERS. 6.-- *And mercy.* [n: (CASS.)] The tenth, mercy following after unto keeping, which goes before unto the conferring of grace.

VERS. 7.-- *And that I may dwell.* [n: (HIER.)] This is joined to all the things above. As though: thou hast placed, thou hast brought up, thou hast led, etc., and this to the end that I may dwell.

## PSALM XXIII.

VERS. 1.-- *A psalm of David.* [n: (AUG.)] On the first day of the week the world was founded, on the same day man was restored through the resurrection of Christ: [n: (CASS.)] whence he admonishes us to receive the conqueror with triumph. *The earth is the Lord's.* First he says that the whole world is God's, etc., *as far as* [0876D] but who shall be in his mountain, save the innocent?

VERS. 2.-- *And upon the rivers.* [n: (AUG.)] As rivers glide into the sea, so the covetous glide into the world; and these too the Church overcomes, which, its covetous desires being conquered, is made ready for immortality.

VERS. 3.-- *Who shall ascend?* He determines with what virtues they are endowed in the Church.

VERS. 4.-- *Who shall stand?* [n: (HIER.)] For many ascend who do not abide. [n: (ID.)] *The innocent:* toward his neighbour, in hand and in heart, and he who is innocent toward himself, etc., *as far as* they should desire his presence and his knowledge. *Who hath not taken.* [n: (AUG., HIER.)] That is, who has not reckoned his soul among things that do not abide, but perceiving it immortal shall desire things eternal, and therefore [0877A] has not sworn deceitfully. *In vain.* [n: (HIER.)] That is, who has stained it with no dregs of sins after baptism.

VERS. 7.-- *Lift up.* Diapsalmus. Christian religion having been declared, etc., *as far as* which were set there by the prince the devil. *And be ye lifted up.* Against the gates of death. *Ye everlasting gates:* all the grace which, at Christ's coming, was given.

VERS. 8.-- *Who is?* he asks. The Prophet, to convict the perfidy of the Jews, and that by commending him the more he may invite others.

VERS. 10.-- *The Lord strong* is described by his powers as though by his proper name. *Strong,* [n: (AUG.)] even if ye took him with swords.

*Mighty,* even if ye delivered him to Pilate.

[0877B] *Mighty in battle,* lest ye think ye have overcome him in the fight. *The powers.* [n: (Hier., AUG.)] The ministering powers going before Christ in the ascension cry out to the other heavenly ones, that they may open the way to the Lord as he comes: and these, astonished at the new mystery of one clothed in flesh, ask, who is he?

*Lift up.* [n: (CASS.)] A doubling, etc., *as far as* who gives all good things to all, or of the heavenly powers, that is, of the angels.



## PSALM XXIV.

VERS. 1.-- *Unto the end.* [n: (Hier.)] Here the penitent who has been scourged is set before the rest, lest they murmur in adversities: he who, having had the trial in himself, coaxes through mercy and terrifies through justice.

[0877C] *To thee, O Lord, have I lifted up.* [n: (AUG.)] A psalm against the adversities of the world, etc., *as far as* who not through all, the less perfect.

[n: (CASS.)] First he asks to be taught the ways of the Lord, because he is raised up toward God.

[n: (ID.)] Toward the contemplating of thee; he easily despises earthly things who beholds things divine.

VERS. 2.-- *I trust.* [n: (ID.)] Having had trial of my own weakness in evils, I shall not blush because of my enemies.

*Neither let my enemies laugh at me:* and I am sure that he deceives none that endures patiently.

*In vain.* [n: (ID.)] Because all things are vain and unfruitful which are alien from God.

*Shew me thy ways, O Lord.* [n: (CASS.)] The ways are of the order of life, etc., *as far as: Direct,* belongs to life, [0877D] *teach* to knowledge.

VERS. 5.-- *Teach.* [n: (AUG.)] Truth itself, that I may understand what I believe, because I have endured; and if this suffices not: *Remember.*

*God my saviour.* [n: (CASS.)] There are two things which make men good, that we believe God our saviour, and that we patiently await his retribution.

VERS. 6.-- *Remember.* [n: (ID.)] Of former sins and of those which are daily done, he prays through the mercy shewn to the ancients, and because he fears a true judge.

*Of thy tender mercies.* Compassion is properly in the shewing forth of good things.

*Which are from of old.* [n: (AUG.)] That is, they are from the beginning of the world: therefore let him not fail in me.

[0878A]VERS. 7.-- *Sins.* [n: (CASS.)] They are when the way of equity is forsaken.

*Of my youth.* Not for age, but for the rashness of headlong haste, which is then, when the heat of the mind exceeds moderation.

*Ignorances.* We do those things which we do not know to be evil. Or the sins of ignorance in little ones, who are held at any rate by original sin.

[n: (HIER.)] Of my youth. Before I went out of the house of my wicked father.

*Remember me, thou.* [n: (AUG.)] Whose work it is to have mercy and to forgive sins.

*According to thy mercy.* That is, thy mercy is ample. Mercy is toward the wretched, goodness toward any whatsoever. But God does good both when [0878B] implored by the wretched, and when led by his goodness: none is good but God.

VERS. 8.-- *Sweet.* [n: (ID.)] Because he forgives all former things, where grace is without merits.

*Right.* Because after mercy, etc., *as far as Sweet,* because he gives sweet things; *right,* because he corrects.

*Therefore.* [n: (CASS.)] Because he is sweet and right; not out of cruelty, but out of kindness.

*In the way.* That is, faith, or the precepts of God. Or in the way of this life, where one must live rightly, he will give a law.

VERS. 9.-- *In judgment.* While he punishes, he will direct to better things. But let them be meek who are corrected: for from wild beasts they are tamed, that they may bear it. For if they are mild, that is, of a gentle mind, as though having this by nature: he will teach them the precepts for the neglect of which came [0878C] the scourges.

VERS. 10.-- *Mercy and truth.* [n: (AUG.)] But what ways he will teach, etc., *as far as* and then he guards against the severity of the judge.

*Testament.* [n: (CASS.)] The New, which is excellently called a Testament, where there is a clear and certain promise of an inheritance, which is confirmed by death also.

*And the Testament.* Of the old law, where the Prophets were witnesses of the promises.

VERS. 11.-- *For thy name's sake, O Lord.* [n: (AUG.)] That is, thy glory, etc., *as far as* and they are interpreted *the clean,* who assert themselves clean of all sin.

VERS. 12.-- *Who is the man?* [n: (CASS.)] Here he treats of things eternal; how they are had, labouring and praying, that both [0878D] he himself, and all Israel, may be freed from these evils.

VERS. 13.-- *Soul.* [n: (ID.)] and if not at once, etc., *as far as* whatever others may do, etc.

*Seed.* [n: (Hier.)] It is good, whence fruit is gathered.

*Is a firmament.* [n: (AUG.)] Fear seems to belong to the weak, but the Lord is a firmament to them that fear him, and he brings it about that his Testament be made manifest to them.

*Eyes.* Sal. *The eyes of a wise man are in his head, that is, in his heart.*

VERS. 16.-- *Only.* [n: (ID.)] An only one is the more loved, a poor man the more pitied.

VERS. 17.-- *From necessities.* That is, from adversities, [0879A] which are necessary for all who wish to live godly in Christ.

VERS. 19.-- *Behold.* [n: (AUG.)] The perdition of so many men cannot be without grief, and therefore he prays for them.

VERS. 21.-- *The innocent and the upright have adhered.* [n: (ID.)] The evil were borne with; they did not adhere.

VERS. 22.-- *Israel.* [n: (AUG.)] The people, whom thou hast prepared for the vision of thee.

## PSALM XXV.

VERS. 1.-- *A psalm.* [n: (CASS.)] This psalm admonishes that the fellowships of the wicked be shunned, and that we be joined to the just, because from living together manners are formed.

*Judge.* [n: (AUG.)] Because after mercy, etc., *as far as* they are carried off by the wind of pride.

[0879B] [n: (CASS.)] *Judge:* it is not the presumption of a just man, but the certainty of God's mercy, which he shews there in hoping in the Lord; then, I shall not be weakened.

*In innocence.* [n: (AUG.)] Not that of angels, but such as is of men.

VERS. 2.-- *Prove me.* [n: (CASS.)] That is, first look upon my strength, etc., *as far as* I regard not merits, but the mercy which has made me such.

[n: (CASS.)] *Prove me.* But do thou prove me in temptations, that no sin remain, etc., *as far as* lest any man murmur about them.

*Burn my reins.* [n: (ID.)] With the fire of thy word, the *heart* with the heat of the spirit. *Heart* for thoughts, *reins* for delights.

VERS. 3.-- *Mercy.* [n: (CASS.)] Which suffers not [0879C] that we be tempted above our strength; or that very burning is mercy, not harshness

*I have been well pleased in truth.* [n: (AUG.)] He to whom his own lie is displeasing, and who himself is pleasing along with her and in her.

VERS. 4.-- *I have not sat.* [n: (CASS.)] *I have been well pleased* in truth, because I have not sat. *I have hated,* which is to shun evils. *And I will wash,* which is to do right things.

*The vain.* [n: (CASS.)] *The vain* are they who labour about perishable things; the wicked are heretics, who pervert the Scriptures: those seek superfluous things, these poisonous.

VERS. 5.-- *Of the malignant.* [n: (ID.)] Who do evils to their neighbours.

VERS. 6.-- *I will wash.* [n: (AUG.)] By doing penance. *And the altar,* I will adorn my mind with virtues, or faith, because there is no place of true sacrifice outside the catholic Church.

[0879D] *Among the innocent.* [n: (AUG.)] Conversing; *that I may hear* from them, that is, that I may learn with what voice thou art to be praised; or, *that I may hear:* Come, ye blessed of my Father.

VERS. 7.-- *The voice of praise* of the Holy Ghost, who teaches to praise thee.

VERS. 8.-- *Lord.* [n: (CASS.)] He asks that he be not mingled with the wicked in the judgment, because it is not with them, but with the house of God, that he agrees.

*I have loved the beauty.* [n: (CASS.)] These things therefore will I do, because I have loved to make for thee a comely house, and a place wherein thou mayest dwell in glory, that I may take in something of the glory and the essence of God.

VERS. 9.-- *Destroy not.* [n: (CASS.)] And if for the present thou sufferest us to be together, destroy us not together.

[0880A] [n: (CASS.)] Destroy me not in time to come *with the wicked,* with whom here I have no fellowship.

VERS. 10.-- *Their right hand,* [n: (AUG.)], that is, if they do any good, etc., *as far as* let mercy accompany in all things, which thou owest, because:

VERS. 12.-- *Foot,* that is, affection, which is wont easily to slip, *hath stood* unmoved against all scandals, stretching beyond, and persisting *in the right way.* And because thou so redeemest in the churches, I will bless.

*It hath stood.* [n: (CASS.)] Even though shaken on every side, or confounded by scandals.



## PSALM XXVI.

VERS. 1.-- *Unto the end.* [var: Vulg. Ps 26:1 heads this psalm *Psalmus David, priusquam liniretur*, "before he was anointed"] [n: (AUG., CASS.)] First, David chosen, etc., *as far as* whence groaning and labouring in the sacrament; that he may rejoice in the reality.

[0880B] *The Lord is my light,* etc. [n: (CASS.)] Of the first anointing, through which the second is made sure, he speaks here.

*The Lord is the protector of my life.* [n: (AUG.)] He gives the life of the virtues, which he also protects, without which all his gifts are lost.

*Of whom shall I be afraid?* [n: (ID.)] Whom should the flesh fear, when it shall have put on corruption [cj: *corruptionem*; read *incorruptionem*, "incorruption"].

VERS. 2.-- *The wicked upon me.* [n: (ID.)] That, insulting, they may prefer themselves to me: which God turns to the better, so that with their cursing tooth they consume not me, but my carnal desires. Or *let them eat,* that is, let the cruelty of my enemies take its delight in my death, *the flesh;* the soul they cannot.

*They were weakened.* [n: (ID.)] While Christ took flesh, they were made to waver, and afterwards submitted themselves wholly to faith.

[0880C] [n: (CASS.)] Or, being weakened little by little they came to the ruin of the soul.

VERS. 3.-- *Camps.* [n: (CASS.)] Efforts, battle, conflicts.

*In this will I hope.* [n: (AUG.)] I have hope of victory in the Lord of the eternal reward.

VERS. 4.-- *One thing I have asked.* [n: (AUG.)] This one house is eternal, in which one may abide always, where the days are eternal; why there? That I may contemplate the delight of God, which is the true good.

*All the days of my life.* [n: (AUG.)] That as long as I live, etc., *as far as* I will make manifest the work.

*That I may see.* [n: (ID.)] Another reading: *That I may contemplate the delight of the Lord,* that is, to this end, that at last he may appear, [0880D] etc., *as far as* and therefore not rashly do I ask *one thing,* though a sinner.

VERS. 5.-- *In secret*: [n: (AUG.)] as it were there are many parts, etc., *as far as* him whom presently he calls a rock.

VERS. 6.-- *I have gone round.* [n: (AUG.)] Because he offered himself to me as a leader, etc., *as far as* that I might fulfil all his wills.

[n: (HIER.)] Or I have looked round upon all created things, or the examples of the saints.

[n: (AUG.)] I have gone round in mind all created things, etc., *as far as* the more ardently it desires.

VERS. 7.-- *Hear, O Lord, my voice,* etc. [n: (CASS.)] What labour, what groaning? The prayer before the second [0881A] anointing, that he may be able to have that which he also promises himself by the spirit of prophecy.

VERS. 8.-- *To thee hath said.* [n: (CASS.)] *The heart,* the reason taught by thee, converses with thee of the one thing, that it may handle nothing other than what thou teachest.

VERS. 9.-- *Turn not away.* [n: (AUG.)] Another asks here for temporal goods from God, and rejoices in what is given; this man asks nothing but *one thing.* Another, having them, fears him angered, lest he take them away; this man thinks there is no anger unless he *turn away his countenance,* even if he punish something in him; nothing more divine could he have said.

*Turn not aside.* [n: (AUG.)] He does not turn aside in anger, when he turns his face from sins; or, *turn not aside,* so as to let me slip in anger, that is, in what thou mayest punish; but that our weakness may be made more wary.

[0881B] *My saviour,* etc. [n: (AUG.)] As though: in vain hast thou created and re-created, etc., *as far as* he was chosen by the Lord through Samuel.

VERS. 10.-- *For my father,* etc. He made himself a little one to God, and made him his own *father:* because he commands, calls, founded and rules. *A mother,* because he cherishes, contains, suckles and nourishes; but the world is a father, or the devil, the mother Babylon, that is, the fellowship of the wicked.

VERS. 11.-- *Set me a law,* etc.

VERS. 12.-- *Of them that trouble me.* [n: (CASS.)] As of Saul and his helpers, etc., *as far as* because they are false.

VERS. 13.-- *I believe to see.* [n: (AUG.)] But among all perils, God directing, I believe to see.

*Good things.* [n: (ID.)] Good things which are true, eternal. Those men [0881C] persuade to a blessedness in this earth, which is the earth of the dying.

VERS. 14.-- *Expect.* [n: (CASS.)] As I am sure of it, etc., *as far as* endure what thou sufferest.

## PSALM XXVII.

*A psalm to this one.* [var: Vulg. Ps 27:1 heads it *Psalmus ipsi David*] By David is sometimes understood the head, that is, Christ; sometimes the body, that is, the Church; sometimes both together, that is, the whole Christ is understood, as here.

[n: (CASS.)] The third psalm, which treats briefly of the passion and the resurrection.

[n: (ID.)] Here Christ is set forth (and though he is so worthy) as not trusting in himself, but asking the whole from God; a useful example to the just.

VERS. 1.-- *To thee, O Lord.* [n: (ID.)] A prayer had in the time [0881D] of the passion.

*Be not silent.* Less is in the Hebrew, etc., *as far as* and the raising up from death.

*Be not silent,* etc., *and I shall be likened,* etc. [n: (HIER.)] From this, that the Word is always united to me, I am not such a man as the rest, who are born into the deep misery of the world.

[n: (AUG.)] Where, as it were, if thou be silent, thy word is not known.

VERS. 2.-- *To the temple.* [n: (CASS.)] According to the custom of the Jews, to whom it was commanded that wheresoever they were they should pray toward that quarter where they knew Jerusalem to be: which Jesus also did, fulfilling the law. Or toward heaven, whence we hope for help.

[0882A]VERS. 4.-- *Give to them.* [n: ( Ibid. )] It is to foretell, not to wish, according to what they intended, not according to what came of it: or he wishes the justice of God to be preserved in this.

VERS. 5.-- *Because not,* etc. [n: (ID.)] And if works were wanting, this would suffice for damnation, that they understood not through the law and the Prophets the things which God invisibly works through me, nor even the visible things.

*Thou shalt destroy.* [n: (AUG.)] That nothing hurt me, nor afterwards thy Church, or mine.

*Blessed.* [n: (CASS.)] Having been heard, in the resurrection he gives thanks, adding of the salvation of them that believe.

*The Lord.* [n: (ID.)] The same who is my helper, and my protector, is the strength of the people which trusts in him, and [0882B] a protector that they be not hurt, and thou, O Lord:

VERS. 9.-- *Save.* Behold how great is the hope of them that believe, when he prays for them: he who suffered for them is judge and advocate.

*And rule them,* etc. [n: (ID.)] Lest they turn aside to crooked things.

*Lift up,* etc., from earthly things to the seeking of things eternal, and by the understanding of the Scriptures, and by good work, through this whole world unto the end.

## PSALM XXVIII.

VERS. 1.-- *A psalm of David in the consummation.* [n: (ID.)] The ark brought back from the Philistines, etc., *as far as* that they may offer themselves to such a consummator.

*Bring to the Lord.* [n: (ID.)] First he admonishes that sacrifices be offered.

[0882C] *The sons of rams.* [n: (AUG.)] Because assault is not wanting, etc., *as far as* and the seventy-two disciples sent to preach are signified.

*The voice of the Lord.* [n: (CASS.)] The preaching of Christ terrified minds within, which is, that it gave the spirit of fear to the Jews and to the Gentiles.

[n: (GREG.)] This man ascends from the lowest, that is, from the spirit of fear to the spirit of wisdom, etc., *as far as* that is, spiritual understanding: these the Lord shakes.

[n: (HIER.)] As a husbandman a barren land, etc., *as far as* and he will give strength, and will bless the same in eternal peace.

VERS. 3.-- *The voice of the Lord upon the waters,* etc. [n: (CASS.)] Of the establishing of the Church, where he enumerates the powers of the Holy Ghost [0882D] with great proclamation.

*Of Libanus.* Libanus is interpreted a whitening. *The cedars of Libanus,* that is, high men lifted up in earthly things, whom God by breaking will crush small, that is, will bring them back from pride to himself through his beloved Son: and this he will crush by the spirit of fortitude, as a unicorn taken is crushed.

VERS. 7.-- *The desert.* [n: (CASS., AUG.)] The Jewish people, etc., *as far as* whence flows the living water.

VERS. 10.-- *The Lord.* He shews what is done for the Church now established.

*I have given,* he says, *you power to tread down upon serpents.* [var: Vulg. Lc 10:19 has *calcandi supra serpentes*, "to tread upon serpents"] But as it is unheard of that stags should strike every kind of wild beast with the horn; so that the Apostles, unlettered, should refute all philosophy.

[0883A] *A flood.* [n: (CASS.)] The waters of baptism, which purges the crimes of the world.



## PSALM XXIX.

VERS. 1.-- *A psalm of a canticle, at the dedic.* [n: (AUG.)] Above, concerning the finishing of the tabernacle in a time of war: but this is a psalm of gladness, because the house is dedicated, that is, made firm, which shall abide in eternal peace: the body being made new, not only Christ's but also the whole Church's. The whole Christ speaks in this psalm, because some things belong to the head, some to the body, some to both.

[n: (ID.)] The psalm is briefly of the passion and the resurrection: that the passion may not terrify those whom the hope of the resurrection gladdens.

*The house of David.* [n: (CASS.)] The whole Christ is the bridegroom, etc., [0883B] *as far as* the second state, the state to come, when there is rest in it.

[n: (CASS.)] First he treats of his own dedication: and bids the saints who hope for the same to utter praises.

VERS. 3.-- *O Lord my God, I have cried to thee,* etc. [n: (AUG.)] Christ, etc., *as far as* but not in the dedication.

VERS. 4.-- *Into the pit.* [n: (HIER.)] That is, out of the pit of hell, where the souls of sinners are, lest, like them, I should fall headlong into death.

VERS. 5.-- *Confess ye,* etc. [n: (AUG.)] Because out of his own goodness and holiness, not ours, he was mindful of us.

VERS. 6.-- *For wrath.* [n: (ID.)] That is, vengeance, by which he avenged upon us the first sin, which by death you have [0883C] loosed.

*At evening.* [n: (ID.)] Because wrath, etc., *as far as* that is, in the time of grace after the time before the law and under the law.

[n: (HIER.)] *At evening.* Because, when the Lord had suffered and been buried, the Apostles and the women continued in weeping and groaning. *And in the morning gladness.* Because coming to the sepulchre in the morning they received from the Angels the glory of the resurrection. *Weeping.* [n: (CASS.)] It shall abide for men who look toward the evening, at which Adam turned aside and went out from paradise: but there shall be gladness for those who look toward the morning, at which Christ rose again.

VERS. 7.-- *But I.* How he acted before the dedication and deserved it. [n: (AUG., CASS.)] He acts at evening. But I, while abundance was mine in paradise, [0883D] said: I shall not be moved from this good, believing the devil who said: *Thou shalt not die the death [n: (Gen. III)]* [var: Vulg. Gn 3:4 has the plural and a stronger negative, "ye shall by no means die the death"]. But, O Lord, in *thy will thou hast given strength to my beauty;* which is, I was good and strong not of myself, but of thee. And that thou mightest show it: *Thou hast turned away thy face,* that is, thou hast sent me forth out of paradise: but now, set here, *I will cry to thee,* etc.

VERS. 8.-- *Thou hast turned away.* [n: (CASS.)] God turned away his face from Christ, by exposing him to the passion and to death; and thus Christ was troubled: *My soul is sorrowful even unto death. [n: (Matth. XVI.)] Troubled.* [n: (AUG.)] Calling to mind that time of trouble and of misery, and set in it, I hear the voice of the head who is to die for me, saying: *To thee, O Lord, will I cry.*

[0884A]VERS. 10.-- *What profit is there in my blood.* [n: (ID.)] He shows the necessity by which he does not suffer him to go down into corruption. *Whilst I go down into corruption.* For if I do not rise again at once, and the body is corrupted, I shall announce to no one, I shall gain no one. Confession is twofold, let us never be without it: when it is ill with us in tribulations, let us confess our sins; when it is well, let us confess praise to God in the exultation of justice.

VERS. 11.-- *The Lord hath heard.* [n: (CASS.)] He treats of how he received it, namely the dedication. He said: I will cry, I will beseech, but the Lord heard me beseeching.

VERS. 12.-- *Thou hast cut my sackcloth.* [n: (ID.)] Sackcloth is a rough garment, granted for the use of labour or in a time of [0884B] sadness, as to the Ninivites; that is, human flesh, which was rent by dying: and there came forth the corn of the glorious resurrection, or of the godhead. Or else, the penalty of sin, which being destroyed in the resurrection, the godhead before hidden was brought forth: just as what the sack conceals whole, it opens when rent. [n: (AUG.)] Sackcloth of goats or of kids is mortality, which Christ drew or took from sinners; whence: *Whilst they were troublesome to me, I was clothed with haircloth [n: (Psal. XXXIV)]*, that they might not see what was within.

VERS. 13.-- *I shall not be pricked.* [n: (ID.)] I shall not now die unto the end. *O Lord God, I will confess to thee for ever.* What is: *I will confess to thee for ever?* For ever I will praise thee.



## PSALM XXX.

[0884C]VERS. 1.-- *Unto the end, a psalm.* [n: (AUG.)] Ecstasy is here taken as the dread of a people troubled by the persecution of all the nations and by the failing of faith throughout the world.

[n: (CASS.)] Against dread, hope and prayer are set first, etc., *as far as: They shall be two in one flesh [n: (Gen. II)]*, therefore in one voice also. [n: (AUG.)] The Redeemer speaks first, then the redeemed, giving thanks. Then, at length concerning the disturbance, the voice of the Prophet is put in near the end, and at the end. Here is described the humbling of Christ and of the martyrs: whence follows the exaltation.

VERS. 2.-- *In thee, O Lord, have I hoped,* etc. [n: (ID.)] There is an eternal confusion, from which he asks to be delivered: and there is a temporal one, for an hour, namely the kind that profits.

VERS. 3.-- *Make haste.* [n: (ID.)] In the things to be done, in the [0884D] souls to be loosed and the like, that thou mayest rescue me on the third day and not defer it to the common resurrection; and that this may be, be thou to me a protector as great as it befits God to be. *Be thou to me.* He said: Give justice, etc., *as far as* he affirms the deliverance.

VERS. 5.-- *Thou wilt bring out.* [n: (CASS.)] From death to the things above, so that the counsels of the Jews may be in vain. *Out of the snare which they have hidden.* [n: (AUG.)] The devil tempts by two gates, etc., *as far as* whence: *Thou wilt bring me out of the snare.*

VERS. 6.-- *Into thy hands.* [n: (CASS.)] Note that he speaks here who uttered these same words in the passion. [n: (AUG.)] *Into thy hands.* From these words which he said on the cross, he gives notice that he has spoken in this psalm.

VERS. 8.-- *But I, in the Lord,* etc. [n: (CASS.)] Of [0885A] joy, because he hopes in the Lord, who hates the vain. *I will exult and be glad.* To exult is to rejoice with great eagerness of soul: to be glad, with a tempered affection. *My humility.* He takes it for weakness, and for the very virtue of humility. *From necessities.* [n: (AUG.)] *Necessities* are unbounded, etc., *as far as* and fully in the world to come.

VERS. 9, 10.-- *In a spacious place.* [n: (CASS.)] The spacious place of going freely to the fatherland of the virtues is charity: but because the flesh is weak and evils press: *Have mercy on me, O Lord.* Now the dread is recounted, that is, how many and how great are the things that disturb, against which is the prayer, *I am troubled.* [n: (AUG.)] Since he said before: *Thou hast set my feet in a spacious place,* etc. But for some the place is spacious, out of the thanksgiving of deliverance; for others, out of tribulation, it is a narrow strait. *Troubled.* He is angry [0885B] for the sins of the chaff, etc., *as far as* for the life of the perfect is the advancement of others. *In anger the eye.* In the anger of the persecutors, etc., *as far as* that God may be sought from every degree. [n: (CASS.)] Or the understanding is disturbed and the mind confounded, etc., *as far as* neither in mind nor in body can there be any presumption of man. [n: (AUG.)] Anger through vice blinds the eye of the mind; anger through zeal troubles it: because this too, the mind being shaken, scatters contemplation, which is not except with an unquiet mind; but thereafter, after a little, it profits the more toward seeing.

VERS. 11.-- *In poverty.* [n: (ID.)] Or *in want.* I lack health of body, and the torments are not spared. I lack release, and death is spared; and in this my confidence has been weakened.

[0885C]VERS. 12.-- *Above all my enemies.* [n: (ID.)] They live worse than the pagans and the Jews, etc., *as far as* for whom even their acquaintance are afraid, lest all be such. [n: (CASS.)] Or, among all my enemies, or, held in greater reproach than those who are worthy of it; and *to my neighbours,* to kinsfolk, or to those about to believe; and *to my acquaintance,* to those of the household.

VERS. 13.-- *They that saw me without.* [n: (HIER.)] *Without,* Christ, that is, those who thought him a mere man. *I am forgotten as one dead from the heart.* [n: (CASS.)] Or, I have fallen out of their heart, like a dead man whose memory is buried with his body, of whose restoration there is despair. *A vessel that is destroyed,* Jesus dead is thought fit to be cast away.

VERS. 14.-- *The blame of many.* [n: (AUG.)] [0885D] They are blamed, etc., *as far as* not any man. *Round about. In that while they assembled together.* [n: (CASS.)] Of errors, etc., *as far as* that is, by the appetite of temporal things.

VERS. 16.-- *My lots.* [n: (ID.)] Or, the times of life. [n: (AUG.)] Because we live or die when thou wilt: therefore *deliver.* Although the order of election is with thee, just and hidden, yet I, etc., *as far as* which, without merits, chooses whom it will.

VERS. 17.-- *Illumine.* [n: (CASS.)] Make thy good will manifest upon me, that as they despair of the dying, so they may have confidence in the rising. [n: (AUG.)] *Illumine.* Let it appear who belongs to thee, since now there is confusion; whence elsewhere: *Distinguish my cause [n: (Psal. XLI)]*; yet [0886A] lest he seem to justify himself, he adds: *Save me in mercy,* not in my own justice.

VERS. 18.-- *I have called upon.* [n: (ID.)] Thou callest upon God when thou callest him into thyself, and when thou invitest him into the house of thy heart.

VERS. 19.-- *Let them be made dumb.* [n: (CASS.)] Which here are talkative, while they do not fear the judgment. Dumb in punishments, in hell.

*Which speak.* He fitly sets iniquity over against the just man.

*In pride and in abuse.* [n: (CASS.)] Because the creature is against the Creator, and by a perverse use of the benefits of God, who, besides all else, came for their sake.

VERS. 20.-- *How great.* [n: (CASS.)] Which are the goods of God against the evils aforesaid, and they lighten those very evils. [n: (AUG.)] Seeing these things and marvelling, the Prophet cries out, etc., *as far as* but in the world to come it will lie open.

[0886B]--VERS. 21. *From the disturbance.* [n: (AUG.)] That he may now suffer no human disturbance, even those who are now in tribulation, entering with the mind to the face of God, are not troubled. *Thou shalt protect.* [n: (CASS.)] Lest the promise only of what is to come should trouble them, he adds this: *thou shalt protect,* that they be not harmed.

VERS. 22.-- *Blessed.* [n: (ID.)] As if to say, what more? He concludes the fullness of the benefit with praise, comparing it to his own feebleness. And it is the Prophet that speaks, or the faithful people. *He hath made wonderful.* [n: (AUG.)] The Spirit being sent, he filled the whole world, etc., *as far as* whence: *Thy name is ointment poured out.* [var: Vulg. Ct 1:2 has oil, not ointment, and no verb: "oil poured out is thy name"] [n: (CASS.)] Or else, God the Father made his mercy wonderful, etc., *as far as* according to that saying: *He rested in the heart of the earth.*

VERS. 23.-- *I said in my excess.* [n: (AUG.)] A figure of our fear, etc., *as far as: Therefore thou hast heard the voice of my [0886C] prayer.* [n: (AUG.)] *In the excess of my mind.* Either, his mind being caught up, he saw a height from which he feels himself cast very far off: or, he went out from reason by fearing. *From the face of thy eyes.* If I were in thy face, etc., *as far as* but I accused my own heart.

VERS. 24.-- *O love the Lord, all ye,* etc. [n: (CASS.)] An oratorical or hortatory conclusion, for the obtaining of the things aforesaid. [n: (AUG.)] Having experienced the things aforesaid, he now exhorts us; as if to say: I was in tribulation, I feared, I cried out, I was not confounded: so you also. *His saints,* whose part it is to love God, because they do not love the world, nor the things that are in the world; the friend of this world is set down as the enemy of God. Learn therefore not to love the world, that thou mayest learn to love God: turn away, that thou mayest be converted; pour out, that thou mayest be filled. *The Lord will require,* etc. Even here sometimes, [0886D] if he did this to none, the divine providence would not seem to be watchful, if to all, the divine patience would not be preserved.

*Abundantly to them that do.* Those who do not quickly repent.

*Pride.* For every vice, because it is the root of all.

VERS. 25.-- *Do ye manfully,* etc. The force of the whole psalm, that the frailty of the flesh may not fear so many evils of the world.



## PSALM XXXI.

*The understanding of David.* [var: Vulg. Ps 31:1 heads the psalm with the dative, "to David himself, understanding"] [n: (AUG.)] A psalm concerning the grace by which we are Christians, as the Apostle says [n: (Rom. IV)]. This psalm therefore begins with pardon, treating against the vice of men who do not know their sins or else defend them, and who boast of their merits. [n: (CASS.)] Against the vice of men, then, [0887A] this psalm is set, etc., *as far as* he strengthens those who stand in it.

VERS. 1.-- *Blessed are they whose iniquities are forgiven,* etc. [n: (AUG.)] Not those in whom he finds no sin, but those to whom he forgives it. *Iniquities.* He means the tinder of sin, namely concupiscence, etc., *as far as* but that sin may be no more. [n: (HIERON.)] Iniquities, before faith and baptism: sin, after faith and baptism.

VERS. 2.-- *Blessed is the man.* [n: (HIERON.)] To whom God forgives, for not of himself. He covers it, that it be not revealed in the judgment; and so it will not be imputed unto punishment. There is no guile in him that accuses himself: he holds in the heart what he confesses here with the mouth.

*Blessed:* because formerly, not confessing to God, I came into sin, while I cried out, if I did any good.

VERS. 3.-- *I was silent.* [n: (AUG.)] He who will not confess [0887B] his sins labours without cause in the defence of sins: and since in this he also boasts of his merits, his strength fails the more. [n: (CASS.)] *Blessed is he to whom he hath not imputed.* To some, who have grievous sins, God imputes them; to others he does not impute them, through mercy, even though they have some — namely to those who do not proclaim themselves just when they are evil, but acknowledging themselves make satisfaction. This I now profess, but formerly: *Because I was silent,* etc. [n: (AUG.)] Or, *I was silent,* as to sin with my voice, *while I cried out all the day:* while the penalty of sin, or an inward blinding, showed me a sinner, or while my evil life cried out, though I was silent, whether I had done any good.

*While I cried out all the day,* etc. Although a man be not conscious to himself of anything, yet he is not just: for who understands his offences? Whence: *There is a just man that perisheth in his own justice.* And the Apostle: *I am conscious to myself of nothing,* etc.

[0887C]VERS. 4.-- *The thorn is fastened in.* [n: (CASS.)] *The thorn,* which lifts up the whole body, that is, pride. [n: (AUG.)] Likewise *misery,* etc., *as far as: in the hands of a drunken man.*

VERS. 5.-- *My sin.* Diapsalma. Confession, whence pardon, as above the growing old from governance. *I have made known.* [n: (CASS.)] For *I said* before, etc., *as far as* and God hears.

VERS. 6.-- *For this shall he pray.* [n: (ID.)] Diapsalma. Commending repentance, he says it is common to all the saints; which sundry sects reject, against whom he flees for refuge to God.
