[n: (AUG.)] *For this.* Therefore every saint prays, because thou hast forgiven, otherwise he would not be a saint. *In the time,* etc. It is the time of grace, the law, pressing and convicting the guilty, compelled them to cry out, grace heals. *But yet in the flood,* etc. [n: (AUG.)] Although the saint pray, [0887D] etc., *as far as* the saint will pray, saying: *Thou art a refuge. To him.* That is, to God, for he changes the person, as often. They do not draw near who say that fate makes them sin, or fortune, which rules all things, or the people of darkness, which rebels against God.

VERS. 8.-- *I will give thee understanding.* [n: (ID.)] Diapsalma. It is answered to the penitent: *I will give thee understanding,* etc. Behold a psalm of understanding, lest the suppliant be thought neglected, with God for witness. *Thou, deliver.* [n: (CASS.)] Thou who hast promised, *I will give thee understanding.* Or Christ answers the penitent: A glad end, God gives understanding: whence he exhorts others to the same.

VERS. 9.-- *Become ye not as the horse and the mule,* etc. [n: (AUG.)] Having received understanding, this penitent turns himself [0888A] to the proud who defend their sins: therefore you, whom I invite, etc. *The horse,* the untamed Gentile; *the mule,* the Jew slow to believe. [n: (CASS.)] *The horse,* without discernment of its riders; *the mule,* takes any burdens whatever, etc., *as far as* that is, boasting of one's own strength. *Their jaws.* Or, *the jaws* with which they chew, that is, give victuals more sparingly, that being compelled by necessity they may return. [n: (AUG.)] Or, thou wilt break them here, and in the world to come there will be many scourges. Or: *Become ye not as the horse,* because there are many scourges here and in the world to come. *Who come not near to thee.* [n: (CASS.)] For there are those who, if they sin through the weakness of the flesh, do not withdraw from devout prayers.

VERS. 10.-- *Many are the scourges of the sinner,* etc. [n: (AUG.)] After the bridle, scourges are added for taming, lest he be of those unpunished ones to whom a roving licence [0888B] of their own way is now given: so this man above said that he was tamed.



## PSALM XXXII.

*Unto the end.* [var: Vulg. Ps 32:1 heads this psalm as a psalm of David, with no such title] [n: (CASS.)] Here the prophet enumerates the power and the works of God, and so invites to the praise of God; first he admonishes the just and the upright to praise, because we cannot exult in him save by praising: praise together, he says, becomes the upright.

VERS. 1.-- *Exult ye,* etc. [n: (AUG.)] The crooked man praises God when it is well with him; he blasphemes when it is ill: the upright man always, like Job.

VERS. 2.-- *On the harp.* [n: (ID.)] It sounds from below, and is of the earth, whence we may praise, that is, prosperity and adversity, which are ours from him. *On the psaltery.* When thou attendest to the higher things which he has given thee, the heavenly doctrine. [0888C] By *the harp* the flesh is understood, by *the psaltery* charity: to which the ten precepts of the law belong: three in the first table toward God, seven in the second toward the neighbour.

VERS. 3.-- *Sing ye to him,* etc. As if to say, what more shall I say? Be glad especially over the incarnation. *A new canticle.* [n: (CASS.)] Because it does not suffice, unless your justice abound more than that of the Scribes and Pharisees [n: (Matth. V)].

*Sing ye a new canticle.* Of the incarnation, over which the world exults, of which the Angels sang.

VERS. 4.-- *For it is right.* He has set out the manner of praise: now, why is he to be praised? *And all his works.* [n: (AUG.)] Lest anyone think that he has come to faith by merits, since in faith are all the works which God loves, that is, [0888D] rewards, [n: (ID.)] because he does not work save in the faithful; whence: *Thy faith hath made thee whole [n: (Matth. X)]*. Or, all works are right, etc., *as far as* because he remains wicked, and the more wicked for this, that he sees the just man favour him.

*In faith.* As if in outward things, if a man has been faithful for the sake of justice, He will add inward faith, whom not knowing he feared; whence: who will not give him the things that are his own, who was faithful in another's? [n: (AUG.)] God is faithful in giving to man what he promised: man is faithful in believing him that promises, and by the promise God is our debtor.

VERS. 5.-- *The earth.* [n: (ID.)] The Church everywhere upon the earth.

VERS. 6.-- *By the word.* That is, by the wisdom of God the Father. [0889A] *The heavens.* The Apostles were fully instructed, by conversing with him even bodily: and afterward *all their power,* once had, was strengthened by the Spirit of the Son, through the heavens made firm he gathers and consolidates men into the unity of faith, hope and charity. *The bottle,* the Church, in which is dead flesh. *Even the depths,* that is, those deep in the darkness of vices, he sets among those who are a treasure, whence wisdom enriches others. [n: (AUG.)] *The earth.* The heavens do not need mercy, etc., *as far as* which he works. *Their power.* Since they were among wolves, etc., *as far as* licence.

VERS. 7.-- *As in a bottle.* [n: (CASS.)] According to the letter: as he shuts in the sea by its shores, so he shuts within the Church the wavering peoples; and in the treasures of his wisdom he places the deep heights, that he may prove who searches the Scriptures with a zeal for godliness.

[0889B]VERS. 8.-- *Let all the earth fear.* [n: (ID.)] The Church out of the sea, etc., *as far as* but let the earth fear the Lord. Let it fear — nay rather, let them be moved by the hand of mercy out of the likeness of one asleep. *By him,* because he himself created it: and therefore he governs it profitably, because something is moved by the devil also, but what is moved by God is moved profitably, therefore *by him. Let them be moved.* [n: (AUG.)] Not by the terrors of men, etc., *as far as* power not save from God.

VERS. 9.-- *He spoke and they were made.* He notes creation and formation together. He commanded the re-creation.

VERS. 10.-- *He scatters the counsels.* [n: (CASS.)] That the counsels against himself, as those of the Jews, and against his own, may have no effect. *Of the Gentiles.* When he destroyed idolatry. *Of the peoples.* Of the Jews, whose will, though fulfilled, was yet reproved by the resurrection. *Of the princes.* Of tyrants [0889C] or of demons.

VERS. 11.-- *But the counsel of the Lord,* etc. [n: (ID.)] The secret of the incarnation, in which he took counsel for man.

*Standeth for ever.* Because it is unto the obtaining of eternal salvation for us.

*The thoughts.* Predestination, in which all things are laid up, which also so abide as they were foreseen.

VERS. 12.-- *Blessed is the nation.* [n: (CASS.)] He calls him blessed who has come to the worship of God: which happens in Christian times, in which the Gentiles are converted, these are the just and the upright whom praise becomes. [n: (AUG.)] All blessedness is in the mind, etc., *as far as* blessed is the people that has God for its possessor.

VERS. 13.-- *From heaven.* [n: (AUG.)] From the man Christ. He looked forth in the coming of the Son and saw mercifully, [0889D] not the sins, [n: (CASS.)] from which he turns away his face; but he saw all the children of men, that is, all who wish to be born again unto a new life. [n: (AUG.)] How did he choose? etc., *as far as* he inspired every good will.

VERS. 15.-- *He hath fashioned.* [n: (AUG.)] He formed them with the hand of grace. *Severally,* giving to single ones certain single things. [n: (HIER.)] Whence nevertheless the unity of the body, that is, of the Church, may come to be. *Severally,* that is, not out of Adam, as some say that a soul comes from a soul, and he knows them all at once, and all their acts.

VERS. 16.-- *The king is not saved.* Thus are the just made, but neither, once made, are they saved by their own strength. Not only spiritual things are from God, but outward things also. [n: (HIER.)] No one has anything except from him, because neither the powerful, like a king, nor the strong, [0890A] etc., *as far as* even if he be in great abundance of strength.

VERS. 17.-- *The horse is deceitful.* [n: (AUG.)] An animal in whose swiftness or strength trust is put, figuratively, any greatness of the world whereof men are proud: but the higher thou art lifted up upon it, the heavier thou fallest. [n: (CASS.)] Or, *the horse* is the body of a man, however comely and well formed, yet it is deceitful for bodily health, and for spiritual. [n: (AUG.)] Or, *the horse* is worldly happiness, which carries men, and while it hurries too much, the horse falls and destroys its rider. *The horse,* human wisdom. *A giant,* someone proud, lifting himself up against God.

VERS. 18.-- *Behold the eyes.* In his own [d: in] strength no one is saved, but: Behold whence salvation is, *the eyes,* etc. *The eyes,* [0890B] the regard of the godhead, which will protect them. *Them that fear him.* [n: (CASS.)] To fear the Lord and always to hope are joined together.

VERS. 19.-- *That he may deliver,* etc. [n: (ID.)] These are the two desires of the faithful man: that in the judgment he may be snatched from death, and that here he may be fed with spiritual things, where there is want of them.

VERS. 20.-- *Our soul waiteth for the Lord.* What he thinks of the things aforesaid, as it were a conclusion.

VERS. 21.-- *For in him.* [n: (AUG.)] Because to those who were absent he sent his name through faith.

*We have hoped.* That we shall come to God, not by our own merits. [n: (CASS.)] Because in him — lest anyone murmur about patience — he adds the gift, namely that he is glad here in the Lord. And we hope in the name of Christ, whom the Prophet, glad and patient, awaited as one to come: *We have hoped.* [0890C] It notes a continuous time, because it is not lawful to leave off, whence the weary soul can always be refreshed.

*Let thy mercy, O Lord, be upon us,* etc. He desires that the incarnation be accomplished, that he may be able to be perfect. *In thee.* He says this to the end that every superstition and depravity be shut out.



## PSALM XXXIII.

VERS. 1.-- *A psalm of David.* [var: Vulg. Ps 33:1 heads the psalm with the dative alone, "to David," and names no psalm] [n: (AUG.)] Christ speaks, and in him the Christian: he teaches humility, while he commends the body and the blood. *When he changed the countenance* — Christ, that is, the sacrifice — *before* the Jews, who are the kingdom of his own father David, according to that saying: *And the Lord shall give unto him the seat of David,* the Church is the kingdom of the Father.

[0890D]VERS. 2.-- *I will bless.* [n: (CASS.)] The second psalm through an imperfect alphabet, etc., *as far as* he is glorified together with him.

VERS. 3.-- *In the Lord shall my soul be praised.* [n: (ID.)] For his own praise is the preaching of his Lord, as a good servant glories in the good repute of his lord. *The meek.* Not the learned, not those who fast, because they are proud: but the meek, because they have temperance in all things. The meek, not being proud of their own understanding; not bearing the yoke of God after the manner of the untamed.

VERS. 4-6.-- *O magnify the Lord.* Spiritual grace does not wish to do alone what profits many; whence it calls others: *Magnify,* that it might draw those invited, [0891A] it tells what goods came to it thereby: *I sought the Lord. Out of all.* It cannot be told piecemeal how great things he obtained who sought nothing other than God. *Come ye.* [n: (CASS.)] Now piecemeal he admonishes the saints concerning the new countenance. As if to say, *Magnify:* and if among the first, come ye into what is pleasant. Or, *Magnify,* and in what order? behold: *Come ye* by faith. [n: (AUG.)] Come ye by cleaving with faith, and by running with the two feet of a twin charity. [n: (CASS.)] The praises being set first, he exhorts to communion. *Let them not be confounded.* [n: (AUG.)] Whatever happens here to the just man, he does not blush, because he is always the higher with God: but the proud man blushes in adversities, because he has not drawn near to God.

VERS. 7.-- *The poor man.* [n: (CASS.)] *This one* is here the spiritual man, because he draws near and is enlightened, and shall not blush; because, [0891B] when he cries out, he is freed from all the straits of the world at his death, when he now passes over to God.

VERS. 8.-- *He shall send in an Angel. Come ye,* [var: Vulg. Ps 33:8 has the nominative and no such object: "the angel of the Lord shall encamp round about them that fear him"] and as this poor man cried out and was heard, in the same way *he shall send in an Angel,* etc. [n: (AUG.)] *He shall send in an Angel.* Other interpreters have rendered it thus: *The Angel of the Lord surroundeth in a ring them that fear him.* An Angel from heaven, or a man, who is a minister of the will of God. Faulty codices have it. *The Lord shall send in an Angel.*

VERS. 9.-- *Taste and see.* Here openly of the sacrament which he bore in his hands. *Taste* the flesh and blood of Christ. *The Lord is sweet.* [n: (ID.)] And delightful: because he is life to him that takes him, driving away all troubles in the world to come, that is: Hope through this for eternal life. [n: (CASS.)] And because God is life, the flesh which he [0891C] united to himself is life-giving.

VERS. 10.-- *All ye his saints.* [n: (CASS.)] No one, however well deserving, shall leave off from this, let him not presume upon security. *For.* [n: (AUG.)] The Lord was feeding him that despised him, and he will forsake him that fears him. But he promises not superfluous things, but necessary ones. *There is no want.* Because even in the present they have as much as nature requires.

VERS. 11.-- *The rich have wanted and have suffered hunger.* [n: (CASS.)] Because they have not faith. *They have suffered hunger,* because they are not filled with the body of the Lord. For what have they who have not God? Or, *the rich have wanted,* that is, they shall lack the highest good, although they hunger, that is, desire. But those who seek with their whole zeal shall have without diminution the good which is every good.

[0891D]VERS. 12.-- *Come, children, hear me.* Diapsalmus. The sacraments of the Church having been treated, he admonishes those who are outside, who take the first rudiments of faith, lest the name of fear be shuddered at, he adds: *I will teach you,* the sons, the fear of the Lord, as a useful thing.

[n: (CASS.)] Now he warns beginners also from what things they should abstain.

VERS. 13.-- *To see good days.* Because all confess that they wish it.

VERS. 14.-- *Keep.* [n: (ID.)] Note that he says the tongue is to be restrained first, since, when we wish to speak, we first move the lips, which the motions of the tongue soon follow. *Keep* thou who desirest life. Behold how they ought to fear.

[0892A]VERS. 15.-- *Turn away from evil.* [n: (CASS.)] This avoids fault, etc., *as far as* otherwise he will not be received in the world to come.

VERS. 16.-- *The eyes of the Lord,* etc. [n: (ID.)] Of the retributions of the good and of the evil, lest in dangers the just man grow soft with any doubt.

*The eyes of the Lord.* Nor shouldst thou distrust out of thy weakness, because *the eyes.*

*Unto the prayers.* [n: (ID.)] The swiftness of hearing, since the hearer dwells in them.

VERS. 17.-- *The memory.* [n: (AUG.)] That not even the mention, etc., *as far as* which they have committed.

VERS. 19.-- *The Lord is nigh.* He delivered: for he is inclined to this, because *he is nigh;* or, there is an allusion to the crying out.

VERS. 20.-- *Many are the tribulations of the just.* As [0892B] also in the New Testament, *he is nigh, and he will save,* which is necessary, because *many are the tribulations.* [n: (CASS.)] *Many;* from the devil, from envious men, and from oneself. *He will deliver.* [n: (AUG.)] Not bodily, but by giving them patience: or, he will deliver spiritually.

VERS. 21.-- *The bones.* [n: (ID.)] The virtues. *And one,* etc., that not even patience may perish.

VERS. 22.-- *Most evil.* [n: (ID.)] Not merely evil, although it end amid riches and perfumes and the lamentations of men, yet it is most evil, because in sins it drags down to hell.

*That hate.* Whose? Not of all, because they are sinners; but of those who hate the just one, who justifies, that is, Christ, through whom he that is not reconciled perishes: but *he will redeem the souls,* in respect of which the death is [0892C] most evil.



## PSALM XXXIV.

VERS. 1.-- *Judge, O Lord.* [n: (CASS.)] He begins from a prayer for both sides of the battle, with a recalling of the fault, and there was for each one what he deserves, showing how much he bore from his adversaries, and the punishments that are repaid to them for their iniquity.

VERS. 2.-- *Arms and shield* [n: (HIER.)] With arms he strikes the enemies, with the shield he protects us. The shield he calls elsewhere: *of good will;* the arms are we ourselves, because as we are armed by him, so he himself is armed from us. [n: (AUG.)] Or, the soul of Christ, which vanquished the enemies, which does nothing unless God fight on its behalf.

*Take hold of arms.* Make me to have arms, teaching, miracles. *Shield.* Steadfastness.

[0892D] *Arise.* Make me to use the things received.

VERS. 3.-- *Pour out.* Me, who am now in obscurity, make clear so evidently that thou mayest shut them in and convict them, that they may not have what to bark at: and speak to my soul in such wise that all may know that salvation is hers; against this, which they say: *There is no salvation for him in his God [n: (Psal.III.)] The sword.* [n: (AUG.)] The sword is the soul of the just man. When the just abound, the sword is poured out, while life flashes forth: whence the enemy is shut in, having nothing to speak ill of us. [n: (ID.)] It is a prophecy, not a wish, or this concerns those who are to be converted.

VERS 5.-- *Let them be.* [n: (ID.)] A prophecy: or in that manner in which God does it, by just judgment, not with a bitter mind. *As dust before the face of the wind.* [n: (CASS.)] Loosed [0893A] by God, so that they do not even cleave to one another, they whom divine grace does not moisten.

VERS. 8.-- *Let the snare which he knoweth not come upon him.* [n: (CASS.)] Or in a good sense, the *snare* of truth, which the sinner knows not. *And the net which he hath hidden.* That is, the hidden death, namely of Christ. *Let it lay hold on them.* [var: Vulg. *apprehendat eum*, "lay hold on him," singular where Migne prints the plural (Ps 34:8)] Namely, that they be not left to their own crimes. *And let him fall into the snare.* A good one, by which they may be restrained from evils. *Into that very one.* That is, out of that very death. *Into that very one.* [n: (AUG.)] Not into another, but into the one which they stretched out. For there is no evil man who does not first hurt himself.

VERS. 9.-- *But my soul shall rejoice.* [n: (CAS.)] Having set forth the hope of good and the joy, he relates how malignantly it acts toward the kind one. *In the Lord.* [n: (AUG.)] Than whom nothing is sweeter: nor has he failed, who made all things: seek him therefore, [0893B] and in him thou shalt have all things.

VERS. 10.-- *All my bones shall say.* Carnal men have not the word of praise and confession, because things weak before thee are utterly silent; but strong things praise thee as great and tremble. [n: (ID.)] *All the bones.* All the strength of the mind, because another cannot say this, namely: O Lord, who? etc., as if none, because he alone delivered: *The helpless out of the hand.* [n: (CASS., AUG.)] The human race is *helpless,* because mortal; *needy,* because it seeks bread in sweat; *poor,* because, cast down from pure wisdom, it has a thin and shadowy reason.

VERS. 11.-- *Unjust witnesses rising up.* [n: (CASS.)] The calamities of man having been told, lest Christ himself should seem a stranger to them, he sets forth his passion. *Which I knew not.* [n: (AUG.)] They are sins, etc., *as far as* but not [0893C] by committing them.

VERS. 12.-- *They repaid.* [n: (ID.)] When I had brought fruitfulness, they repaid barrenness: I life, they repaid death; I honors, they insults; I medicine, they wounds. *Barrenness.* In the fig tree the Lord cursed, where, when he sought fruit, he found none, but leaves: so in the Synagogue, words and not deeds; whence: *Thou that preachest that men should not steal, thou stealest.*

VERS. 13.-- *With haircloth.* [n: (ID.)] The flesh, a haircloth, on account of the likeness of the flesh of sin. *My prayer.* [n: (CASS.)] As he said: *Your peace shall return to you [n: (Matth. X)]*; so his prayer, not accepted by the Jews, returned to him. *In my bosom.* [n: (AUG.)] The bosom is a secret place, in which we are commanded to pray. [0893D] But the secret place of Christ is his divinity, to which his humanity prays.

VERS. 14.-- *As a neighbor and as a brother,* etc. Case for case. As it were: as concerning our neighbor and brother, so I was well pleased, that is, I rejoiced in the kinship of those who were persecuting me. Behold grace for hatred, a benefit for injury. [n: (AUG.)] *As a neighbor.* It befits one drawing near to God to say this, and one rejoicing, who pleases God, as to a brother and a neighbor.

*As one mourning.* [n: (CASS.)] Other translators, *as a mother mourning her son, I was bowed down over them. As one mourning.* Though I did all these things, yet they would not be converted; and yet, though they would not be converted, so was I humbled, that is, I grieved, as a father mourning a son.

[0894A]VERS. 15.-- *And against,* etc. When I am piously saddened, the Jews impiously rejoice.

VERS. 16.-- *They were scattered.* [n: (CASS.)] Loosed, when the miracles had been seen at the passion, and yet *not,* for their hardness, *pricked to the heart.*

Or, *scattered* through the whole world: *nor* yet were they *pricked to the heart,* that is, did not repent. Or, *scattered,* that is, divided: some say that he is good, others not.

*They tempted me.* [n: (AUG.)] The things done to the head on the cross are done everywhere to the body. *They gnashed.* It is the way of savagery, when reason fails, to gnash with the teeth: and what it wills, it shows in silence. In prayer he ends these things:

VERS. 17.-- *Lord, when wilt thou look.* [n: (ID.)] When shall we see vengeance upon those who insult us. *My only one.* [n: (CASS.)] The only one is the soul of Christ, [0894B] which alone is free from sin.

VERS. 18.-- *I will confess to thee.* [n: (AUG.)] Now from the fruit he sends before the confession of the whole Church: thence he prays, with a recalling of the fault, for the confounding of the persecutors and the exultation of the faithful.

VERS. 19.-- *Let them not rejoice over me.* [n: (ID.)] He repeats concerning the passion, that he may add concerning their punishment. *They wink.* [n: (CASS.)] Because by turns they warn one another with silent nods, to deceive and to tempt.

VERS. 20.-- *Speaking.* [n: (CASS.)] That is, while they were speaking peaceably. *In wrath.* That is, destructively. For a man plots guile who does not wish to kill: but these were plotting thus, because they wished to slay.

VERS. 21.-- *And they have opened wide.* [n: (ID.)] First winking, then plotting guiles, they opened wide their mouth, crying out death with free [0894C] and open voice: Crucify, crucify.

VERS. 22.-- *Thou hast seen, O Lord.* [n: (ID.)] This is said when we wish to call something back to memory: thou hast seen that they are cruel, be not silent, delay not to give sentence.

VERS. 23.-- *Attend.* [n: (ID.)] By restoring the name above every name, that I, undeservedly stretched out. *In my cause. [n: (Ibid.)]* The punishment of a like crime, but not the cause: for it is not the punishment that makes a martyr, but the cause.

VERS. 24.-- *According to thy justice, O Lord God.* [n: (ID.)] Which is in himself, or which he gave to him. *Let them not rejoice over me.* [sic: *super gaudeant*] [n: (CASS.)] Let them rather weep than rejoice at having fulfilled evils.

[0894D]VERS. 25.-- *We devour.* [n: (AUG.)] Those who are incorporated are devoured.

VERS. 26.-- *Let them blush and stand in awe together.* [n: (CASS.)] Let them rather blush at the glorification of the resurrection, and stand in awe, that is, fear the punishments. *Let them blush.* He who blushes condemns his own acts by his own estimation: but he can blush who has not reverence; therefore he adds: *reverence,* that he may mark conversion. Reverence is the fear of the Lord with love. *Let them be clothed with confusion and reverence.* With shame, as with the haircloth of penitence and a mourning garment. *Shame,* against the boldness which they had in their raging; *reverence,* against the shamelessness of falsehood, that by these they may cure those.

VERS. 27.-- *Let them rejoice and be glad.* [n: (ID.)] In great hope is the end of the faithful, as others are concerning the passion. *Let them rejoice and be glad.* Those say, Well done, to their own soul, from which [0895A] gladness flees, these not to their own soul, but they turn their vows to God, and place their gladness in his praises, not in themselves, which gladness is perpetual.

VERS. 28.-- *And my tongue shall meditate thy justice.* [n: (AUG.)] Let this be done, and my preachers out of zeal will announce thee just, who hast fulfilled thy promises, and all things universally in which thou art to be praised. [n: (CASS.)] *My tongue.* His tongue meditated justice, when it preached the New Testament to the peoples.



## PSALM XXXV.

VERS. 1.-- *Unto the end, a word.* [var: Vulg. Ps 35:1 *In finem. Servo Domini ipsi David.*, "to the servant of the Lord, to David himself"] [n: (CASS.)] He treats here of the error of certain men, etc., *as far as* which nevertheless hangs over all.

There is no fear before the eyes: because there is guile, that is, because [0895B] he obeys the flesh and not God, though reason suggests otherwise, namely that God sees even if no other does: or, that the unjust man himself, what he sees, knows from God, so that it may be found by that same one that his own iniquity is to be held in hatred with God and with all men.

[n: (ID.)] First, he asserts that evils are from man.

VERS. 2.-- *The unjust hath said,* etc. [n: (CASS.)] There are two kinds of sinners: because either through the weakness of the flesh they cannot follow the commands of the law; or they are blasphemers who sin freely, as though God cared not for this; whence it is: *He hath said,* etc. [n: (AUG.)] A description of the wicked man. *There is no fear of God.* [n: (CAS.)] Because guile was unwilling to study the law, by which it might be excused through ignorance.

VERS. 3.-- *For he hath done deceitfully.* [n: (AUG.)] Many search out their iniquities, etc., *as far as* nor are they excused for [0895C] ignorance.

VERS. 4.-- *The words.* (ID). He shows the iniquity, etc., *as far as* that he might act well.

VERS. 5.-- *On his bed.* That is, in the heart, in which evil works please them; and this is, *he hath set himself in every way,* with zeal and with lingering: he found indeed, and knew them to be evil, but nevertheless he did not hate them. *He was unwilling.* [n: (AUG.)] Not that he could not; he acts more perniciously against himself who does not will what he can. For there are certain things which men cannot understand even if they will, as: *In the beginning was the Word,* and yet, that they might act well, they can understand. *On his own bed.* [n: (ID.)] That is, in the heart, where the Lord commanded us to pray with the door shut, etc., *as far as* wisdom, namely of the flesh, which is an enemy to God.

VERS. 6.-- *O Lord, in heaven.* [n: (Cass.)] Good things are from God [0895D] through mercy. As if he should say: Evils are from man, but goods from mercy. O Lord, in those who are *heaven,* that is, who know hidden things, is thy mercy: and that they themselves are *clouds,* is from thee. [n: (AUG.)] Or *in heaven,* that is, mercy, etc., *as far as* but announces by sending forth his truth.

*Even to the clouds.* That is, the preachers, through whom God thunders in announcing, flashes in miracles, rains in teachings. Who are also mountains, whom the sun first clothes with light, which afterward, from them and yet not by them, descends to the lowest of the valleys: for they are ministers of teaching, but God enlightens.

VERS. 7.-- *Thy judgments.* [n: (AUG.)] That thou hast mercy on these, and blindest those; they are utterly searchable to none: [0896A] yet thou savest both, these bodily only, those spiritually as well, wherein is great mercy.

VERS. 8.-- *Men,* etc. He shows that temporal things also are from God.

VERS. 9.-- *They shall be inebriated.* [n: (ID.)] The thing cannot be expressed, but by this likeness he intimates that, being full, they lose their mind, so that they no longer recognize their own fathers, sons, and other friends. Or, then they are drunk in reality, who now hope. *With the torrent.* As if: There shall be a rush of grace and mercy to inebriate them. [n: (ID.)] *With the torrent.* Not that the beatitude should be passing, but plentiful. Or, thy will breaks all obstacles in man. Or, the wisdom of Christ is a torrent, because it is sudden and swift.

VERS. 10.-- *For with thee is the fountain of life.* [n: (CAS.)] *With thee,* O Christ, *is the fountain,* that is, the beginning of every good; [0896B] and *in thy light,* because through his preaching we see the light of the Father and of the Holy Spirit, and therefore *hold forth,* that in this world one may go more safely.



## PSALM XXXVI.

[n: (CASS.)] The third psalm, an imperfection of the alphabet: wholly concerning the correction of morals.

VERS. 1.-- *A psalm of David.* [var: Vulg. Ps 36:1 *Psalmus ipsi David.*, "a psalm for David himself"] This psalm is as it were the garment of Christ, which covers his whole body with precepts, and protects it from adversities, and cherishes it as in the bosom of counsel. It is a draught against those who murmur at the flowering of the wicked and the toil of the good, as though all things were done by chance, so that, spurning God, they cleave to the world. The Church therefore admonishes that no one imitate the malignant, but that whatever good is hoped for be sought from the Lord, who gives things profitable and [0896C] eternal.

VERS. 2.-- *The herbs of the grasses.* [n: (AUG.)] It is now winter, etc., *as far as* because it hath not yet appeared what we shall be.

[n: (AUG., CASS.)] Herbs not of gardens, but of grasses, that is, the meanest, which spring up of themselves through the fields. *Hay,* the noble, who prosper easily; herbs, the middling: but both those quickly wither and these fall away.

VERS. 8.-- *Cease.* [n: (AUG.)] Anger is sudden, fury lasting; but both are to be forsaken, through which men sin. [n: (CASS.)] This is said to him who is still weak, etc., *as far as* by doing it he flourishes for a time.

VERS. 10.-- *And yet a little while,* etc. [n: (AUG.)] After this short life, etc., *as far as* nor did he feel the worm of a gnawing conscience.

[0896D]VERS. 12.-- *He shall watch,* etc. [n: (CASS.)] It having been said that the good ought not to emulate the wicked, etc., *as far as* he strives to slay.

VERS. 13.-- *But the Lord,* etc. [n: (ID.)] So let us also, seeing that they are to perish, laugh even at those who are present, it is a great consolation.

VERS. 14.-- *The sword,* etc. [n: (ID.)] If he prevail neither by flatteries nor by threats, he proceeds to punishment. If he does not prosper so either, he lies in wait with guiles, when by speaking well he intends evil.

VERS. 16.-- *Better is,* etc. [n: (ID.)] A little before, concerning malice and endurance: here, concerning riches and poverty.

VERS. 17.-- *But the Lord confirmeth,* etc. [n: (AUG.)] Chiefly by the example of his cross. For the cross of Christ was ended [0897A] in punishment, and abides in glory: for from the places of tortures it has passed to the brows of emperors.

VERS. 18.-- *In the days of famine.* [n: (CASS.)] That is, in this life, etc., *as far as* by how much the more they are lifted up, by so much the more quickly shall they fail like smoke.

VERS. 21.-- *He shall borrow.* [n: (ID.)] He hears the word of God, he does not repay it in works. *And shall not pay.* That is, he does not repay in works what he received by hearing. *He sheweth mercy and shall repay,* etc. Concerning those things which he receives, etc., *as far as* bounty follows.

VERS. 23.-- *The steps.* [n: (ID.)] Which of themselves are crooked, because we are born in sins. *His way.* [n: (AUG.)] The way of the earth is not hard, but is full of robbers: or, God shall will the way of man.

VERS. 25.-- *I have been young,* etc. [n: (ID.)] He treats here [0897B] that the just man is never forsaken, mingling the rewards of the good and the punishments of the wicked with exhortations set between. [n: (AUG.)] The Church speaks as one man, which in the first times was younger, at the end of the world grew old, *in a plentiful old age,* because she was spread through all nations. [n: (ID.)] *I have been young.* A boy in Abel; in the prophets and patriarchs a young man; in the time of the martyrs as it were an old man; at the end of the world as it were in decrepit age. [n: (CASS.)] *Young,* when I received the beginnings of the law in Adam; *I grew old,* when in an honorable age I received Christ at the last.

VERS. 26.-- *All the day he sheweth mercy.* Or, he speaks of almsgiving, which begins from mercy, and afterward opens the hand: or, of spiritual things.

VERS. 28.-- *He loveth judgment and will not forsake [0897C] his saints.* [n: (CASS.)] Therefore: do thou also love, that thou mayest rejoice in thy good things.

VERS. 31.-- *They shall be supplanted.* [n: (ID.)] To supplant is to spread pits of ambush before the soles of the feet.

VERS. 33.-- *But the Lord* [n: (ID.)] The consolation of the just man. *In his hands.* [n: (AUG.)] Job's earth was delivered into the hands of the impious, that is, his flesh: but God brings forth his soul unconquered, that it may not yield.

*When he shall be judged.* [n: (CASS.)] Or, he does not permit the just man to be condemned when he is judged by the impious; or, for that impious man, namely to his own harm.

VERS. 34.-- *And he shall exalt.* [n: (ID.)] Because none but one exalted can dwell in heaven. *Thou shalt see* [n: (ID.)] What thou now believest; in two ways he marks the joys of the good; [0897D] first, because then they shall feel in how great delights they are. Thence, because they shall see the punishments of the wicked, whence the beatitude of those set free from such evil is the more welcome.

VERS. 35.-- *I have seen the wicked highly exalted.* [n: (ID.)] Against this, etc., *as far as* from whom, being bound, the place of harming has been taken away.

VERS. 36.-- *I passed by.* The world, striving toward God by a better manner of life: and I saw that it could do nothing, since all those things are nothing in which man glories. *I sought.* I called back to memory: because the good then grieve the more over the impious, that they are separated from them, when they are changed for the better. *The place.* Their world is what shall also be dissolved. *I passed by.* [n: (AUG.)] He passes by who attends not to the present, [0898A] but to the last things, etc., *as far as* he who has this virtue is set in the place of the blessed.

VERS. 37.-- *The remnants.* The good things which shall be left after this life, or a good memory after death.

VERS. 39.-- *But the salvation of the just is from the Lord.* All these things are for the just, yet the whole is from God, and he gathers it up summarily.



## PSALM XXXVII.

VERS. 1.-- *A psalm of David, in the remembrance of the sabbath.* [n: (AUG., CASS.)] That we may always have in memory the sin which we are utterly unable to flee, so that we may at least restrain ourselves from it, he here brings in the torment of penitence in many ways, so that he seems to set forth Job himself as an example to recruits, that they may embrace torments, less indeed than their deserts, by which we are freed from [0898B] eternal punishment. [n: (AUG.)] The sabbath is the rest of eternity, which he who is conscious of sin dreads to lose, and he who is grievously afflicted desires to have.

VERS. 2.-- *Lord, rebuke me not in thy indignation.* First, he seeks mercy through punishments summarily. *Rebuke me.* [n: (CASS.)] That the punishment may be from mercy unto correction, not from anger unto vengeance.

VERS. 3.-- *The arrows.* [n: (AUG.)] The vengeances which thou wast threatening to Adam, saying: *In whatsoever day ye shall eat, ye shall die the death.* [var: Vulg. Gn 2:17 *in quocumque enim die comederis ex eo, morte morieris*, "in what day soever thou shalt eat of it, thou shalt die the death," singular where Migne prints the plural] Because he ate, they are fixed in us also; and not for an hour, but *thou hast confirmed* them, that it may be even to the end of the world. [n: (AUG., CASS.)] *For the arrows.* He numbers the things he suffers, etc., *as far as* that which can lightly be hurt.

VERS. 4.-- *There is no health,* etc. [n: (AUG., CASS.)] From [0898C] the pressing of the vengeance put forth upon Adam. Or, he is terrified by the fear of the wrath to come, so that there is no health in his flesh: after the manner of a modest man, who is already afflicted by the scourges to come. Or, from *the face of wrath,* because I have foreseen the face of wrath, that is, what thou threatenest, that I may not see the wrath. *There is no peace.* A grievous sorrow reaches to the inward parts from the face of sins, because the holy man is afflicted with sins beyond the punishments of the body, thinking that he deserves heavier things. *There is no health.* [n: (CASS.)] Here he seeks mercy from the power of the judge, and from the weakness of the person. *There is no peace in the bones.* He takes the bones for the flesh. And he expounds what he had said, adding another thing: namely, from *the face of sins.*

VERS. 5.-- *They are gone over.* [n: (AUG.)] Or, they have lifted up, that is, because iniquity lifted up the head of the proud man against God, God weighed him down, as with a bundle, and pressed him [0898D] that he should not now be raised up.

VERS. 6.-- *They are putrefied.* [n: (CASS.)] A narration, in which the miseries are enumerated. *My scars.* [n: (AUG.)] Or *my bruises;* another reading, that is, my sins. He is not sound who has scars, which have putrefied and stink: that, turned away from them, we may adore the sabbath, and groan: *Running with the bridegroom after the odor of the ointments.*

VERS. 7.-- *I am become miserable.* [n: (ID.)] It having been said in what manner [sic: *qualiliter*] the iniquities are gone over, he says how they are made heavy.

VERS. 8.-- *For my loins.* [n: (CASS., AUG.)] Not only do I sin by my own weakness, but also the devil, who afflicts the body and wearies the soul with empty imaginings; and through this there is again no health, as also [0899A] above through the arrows. [n: (AUG.)] *The loins,* or, the soul is filled, etc., *as far as* when we shall see the truth as it is.

VERS. 9.-- *I roared from the groaning.* [n: (CASS., AUG.)] The *groaning* is of the heart, the *roaring* of the voice: from a likeness of the beasts, to which he compares himself: that a stronger groaning may be shown: the virtue of patience, which breaks forth into a groan, not into words.

VERS. 10.-- *Before thee is all.* [n: (CASS.)] Such is it as deserves to be before thee, because he asks for sins. [n: (AUG.)] and therefore *it is not hidden,* but the groaning is heard, because it is godly, for the liberation of the soul: others God cares not for. *My desire.* [n: (ID.)] It is prayer, nor does he ever cease to pray who ceases not to love: charity is a cry.

[0899B]VERS. 11.-- *My heart is troubled.* [n: (CASS.)] He expounds the sorrows of the mind, because the virtue of patience has left him. *Troubled,* because the mind has succumbed to its own suffering. *The light,* that is, reason lately disturbed, which he has not with him because of the huge troubles.

VERS. 12.-- *My friends and my neighbors.* [n: (ID.)] And in these two comforts human nature rejoices, but to this man they are a heaping up of suffering. [n: (AUG.)] *My friends.* Thus far he has confessed the things he suffers from himself within, etc., *as far as* and these things also befit anyone living among men.

VERS. 14.-- *As one deaf they heard not* [var: Vulg. Ps 37:14 *audiebam*, "I heard not"]*, and as one dumb not opening his mouth.* [n: (CASS.)] Not opening it to the iniquity of those who counselled him, etc., *as far as* against [0899C] the sorrows of the body he roared.

VERS. 16.-- *For in thee, O Lord, have I hoped,* etc. [n: (ID.)] Because amid so many evils he hoped in the Lord, he affirms that he is to be heard in the recalling of his amendment. *I have hoped.* A saving medicine in evils, to hope, not to fail. *In thee, O Lord,* who changest sad things into glad.

VERS. 17.-- *For I said: Lest at any time my enemies should rejoice over me.* [n: (AUG.)] Likewise the head concerning the weak feet, though it be in heaven. It does not forsake that which it has on earth. *While my feet are moved.* [n: (CASS.)] If they are at any time moved by human weakness, the impious laugh at that whereof the godly grieve.

VERS. 18.-- *I am ready.* [n: (ID.)] Knowing that I have offended. [0899D] *Sorrow,* because he has offended: the impious man defends himself; the just man is angry against himself.

VERS. 19.-- *For my iniquity.* [n: (ID.)] The two modes of perfect penitence, confession and amendment, through weeping, alms, and the like.

VERS. 20.-- *They live.* [n: (AUG.)] They enjoy the world, being established in worldly things, and daily they become more.

VERS. 22.-- *Forsake me not.* [n: (ID.)] Absolved from things past, professing God with thanksgiving to be the author of salvation, he joyfully cries out that he may not now be left. [n: (CASS.)] Because the offense is heavier after pardon. [n: (AUG.)] An exultant conclusion, that there may be a sure hope for those who imitate.

*Depart not from me.* How then is it said above: *Why hast thou forsaken me?* It is the voice of the old man who was crucified [0900A] with him, but here he speaks in his own person.



## PSALM XXXVIII.

VERS. 1.-- *Unto the end.* [n: (AUG.)] Because here it is concerning eternal blessedness, for which it is delightful to leap across, even if it be hard to the flesh. And thence he teaches to pass over from the world to God. But the leaper always draws upon himself, from his good repute, the envy of the wicked and their slander: whence he resolves to keep silence among the wicked, lest he say anything amiss. *Idithun.* [n: (ID.)] Leaping over them, that is, those who remain in the world. [n: (CASS.)] First, concerning the restraint of the tongue among blasphemers and slanderers: whence he also desires to know the end, where these things are not.

VERS. 2.-- *I said: I will take heed to my ways,* etc. [n: (AUG., CASS.)] This psalm admonishes, among blasphemers, [0900B] the wicked, and slanderers, restraint of the tongue, and the repressing of contentions and brawls: for this silence is very useful. [n: (CASS.)] I said: I deliberated in my heart: where wise men deliberate before they speak. *Ways* [n: (HIER.)] That is, actions and words. That I may not offend by foolish or idle things.

*In my tongue.* [n: (AUG.)] Not without cause is the tongue in the word: because it easily slips. *I have set to my mouth.* After I passed over earthly delights, etc., *as far as* teaching us to beware not to imitate this. [n: (CASS.)] *I have set* even to the point that I was dumb, etc., *as far as* who seeks an occasion of contention.

VERS. 3.-- *From good things, and my sorrow,* etc. [n: (ID.)] To be announced, etc., *as far as* that is, of those who leap over, I have spoken. [n: (AUG.)] Which is, unwillingly I kept silence from the wicked, [0900C] etc., *as far as* and if the acts of one leaping over are anything.

VERS. 4.-- *My heart grew hot within me.* [n: (AUG.)] If the slothful man shall be condemned, etc., *as far as* he becomes a greedy steward.

VERS 5.-- *I have spoken.* [n: (ID.)] Not to a hearer who is to be instructed, but to God who hears, by whom I am heard within. *And the number of my days.* [n: (HIER.)] The number of the days which now are, is not truly for him who leaps over; but to him who clings it seems to be.

VERS. 6.-- *Behold, measurable.* [n: (CASS.)] As it were, the end being known, and the days which are, he attends to those things which he has left, and compares them one with another. *And the substance,* etc. [n: (AUG.)] He who sees that this substance is nothing, etc., *as far as* which shall be before thee.

[0900D]VERS. 7.-- *But yet.* [n: (CASS.)] Seeing the coming of Christ, he returns to the weakness which he has among the virtues. [n: (AUG.)] Great are the things we have said and believe; but nevertheless that is not eternal, but all is vanity. And, desiring the eternal in place of mutability, he reproaches human life, to which he is subject, in what he says. Lest anyone think the saints strangers to temptations. Or, though the days be measurable, though there be substance, it is nothing: yet all is vanity in the rational creature, which according to divers men craves the whole creation. *But yet.* Although I gape after eternal things, etc., *as far as* whose conversation is in heaven. *Every man.* [n: (AUG.)] If he is evil, he is vain in worldly things; if he is good, since he wills to be with Christ, his life is vain.

[0901A]-- *But yet.* [n: (AUG.)] Diapsalmus. How all are under vanity and chance; and thence he sets over against it that he waits for the Lord, and asks to be delivered. *He passeth in an image.* [n: (ID.)] Although he thus pursues vanity, etc., *as far as* because with misery and toil he craves earthly things. *He layeth up treasure.* [n: (ID.)] Out of the other vices the Prophet chose avarice, to show what he intends: for it is itself the root of all evils.

VERS. 8.-- *Expectation.* [n: (ID.)] Of the first coming, or of the second, etc., *as far as* not in our purses.

VERS. 9.-- *Deliver me from all my iniquities.* [n: (CASS.)] Not only from these, lest he roll back into those things which I leapt over, but from all altogether. For man is imperfect: because he has not received what he desires; perfect, who knows this very thing that is wanting to him.

[0901B]VERS. 10.-- *I was dumb.* [n: (ID.)] Held in reproach, etc., *as far as* and it is necessary, because I have failed.

*Remove thy scourges from me.* [n: (AUG.)] Because *thou hast made* me, do not destroy me; only yield that I may be brought forth; not that I may be broken small

VERS. 11.-- *With rebukes for iniquity.* With which thou hast rebuked the human race: which nevertheless thou hast rebuked out of our own desert, on account of our iniquities, which rebukes are unto correction; for otherwise iniquity would remain uncorrected. *Thou hast corrected man.* [n: (AUG.)] There is a formless sorrow which does not amend: a formed one, which by its instructions supplies a form. And such a sorrow is a precept, that is, it holds the place and the image of a precept.

VERS. 12.-- *And thou hast made the soul to waste away like a spider.* [n: (ID.)] Dried up from the love of the virtues and of knowledge. [0901C] As a spider weaves an empty web in the gathering of temporal things. Or, while it draws forth iniquity from itself like a spider, it dies by the voiding of its bowels. [n: (CASS.)] Or, the spider has a slender body, and stands not on the ground but on high, and weaves its webs by the voiding of its bowels: so the soul of one converted and afflicted forsakes earthly things, and, wasted away by the fear of God, accomplishes subtle works. *But yet.* [n: (AUG., CASS.)] Although he be rebuked, although they waste away, yet he is confounded by human weakness in various things, save he who contemplates God with a pure mind.

VERS. 14.-- *Hear my prayer.* [n: (CASS.)] Diapsalmus. A journey. He asks for himself the evils of the world to be at hand, and his sins to be forgiven, that he may go forth with a prosperous end. [n: (ID.)] But because I do not ask these things, etc., *as far as* mercy to be moved.

[0901D] *Be not silent, for I am a stranger.* [n: (AUG.)] Already with thee, but still a lodger: because I am to pass hence to eternal things, where I shall not be a lodger. *A stranger with thee.* [n: (CASS.)] Not received in the city of the devil, but in thine, translated from Babylon to Jerusalem. *As all my fathers.* Lest anyone be thought excepted from this condition. *A stranger.* I forsake my own kingdom for thine. *A pilgrim.* Not yet choosing a seat for myself here.

*Forgive me.* [n: (ID.)] Here he asks that it be forgiven him, so that being sure he may come to the judgment.



## PSALM XXXIX.

VERS. 1.-- *Unto the end, a psalm.* [n: (CASS.)] First he brings in the Church as having awaited the Lord, and as having received [0902A] him, and from him the New Testament, which many also are to follow: whence he strikes at all superstitions and invites to the wonders of God.

[n: (ID.)] Here Christ, out of his members.

VERS. 2.-- *Waiting I have waited.* [n: (ID.)] He waits, and is ungrateful: but waiting he waits. [n: (AUG.)] Authority from the ancients, in Abraham and the rest, etc., *as far as* who is ours by worship, not of others.

*The Lord.* [n: (ID.)] Not a man, who can deceive and be deceived, and who is in need together with me. *He gave heed.* [n: (ID.)] To me, that he might profit me: not me, as the wicked also.

VERS. 3.-- *The pit.* [n: (ID.)] The darkness of iniquities, that is, of unbelief and of ignorance, etc., *as far as: to a rock* against mire. [n: (HIER.)] But in *the pit* and in *the mire,* the Gentiles worshipping idols: or, all who love earthly things.

[0902B]VERS. 4.-- *A song.* [n: (HIER.)] Not a shameful one, not a theatrical one, but through the New Testament. *They shall see.* [n: (CASS.)] The wonders wrought in the time of the incarnation. [n: (AUG.)] They shall see: this which he first said of those before, many later peoples shall see.

VERS. 5.-- *A name.* [n: (ID.)] Which is Saviour. As if: *Blessed,* etc., who hopes not for temporal things from him, but for salvation, which his name indicates. In the Hebrew genitives are often doubled in this manner: or, *blessed* is he to whom the name belongs, that is, by whom is the praise of God, that from him alone he may believe salvation to be, whence also is his hope. *Madnesses.* [n: (CASS.)] Idols, which deceive away from the truth: or, madnesses, in deceitful spectacles. Therefore in the plural, that it may be taken of them all. These things are false, but thy wonderful works, heaven, earth, dashes superstitions to pieces.

[0902C]VERS. 6.-- *Thy wonders.* [n: (AUG.)] Peter walking upon the waters in a sign, not in the world, that is, in the fruits of the world. [n: (AUG.)] Salvation is truthful, madness lying; it is not what it is thought to be. [n: (HIER.)] Earthly things are vain, madnesses, angers and brawls, etc.

*There is none like unto thee.* [n: (AUG.)] No authors of the arts. *I have declared.* Here Christ in the head and in the members. These are the wonders of God, these the thoughts to which no one is like. [n: (CASS.)] Behold the wonders: the incarnation, the justice of preaching, the deliverance of men from iniquities, the confounding of enemies, the gladness of those who hope. First, the rude and unformed were prepared, that they might willingly receive the words of Christ. [n: (AUG.)] See the wonders of God. I declared beforehand through the Prophets, and afterward I myself spoke. *They are multiplied in number.* [n: (HIER., AUG.)] Above the number [0902D] of those to be saved: or, above what anyone could count: or, while I speak, more have believed than of old.

VERS. 7.-- *Sacrifice and oblation thou didst not desire.* [n: (AUG.)] When the thing came, he did not want, as it were, the words that promise, etc., *as far as* but he who poured it out, being deaf, hears not. *But ears thou hast perfected for me.* That is, thou hast made me perfectly understand what thou wouldst. *Or, a body thou hast fitted to me,* as the Apostle says to the Hebrews [n: (Heb. X)], that is, thou hast given me a body fit for offering.

VERS. 8.-- *A holocaust.* Holocausts also thou didst not require for sin. *For sin.* Which stands above *oblation,* as concerning the goat ἀποπομπάιῳ, that is, the emissary, which was sent into the desert with the sins of the people: or, for sin [0903A] may be two words. *Behold I come* [n: (CASS.)] Since the sacrifices cease, it is established that Christ comes.

VERS. 9.-- *In the head of the book it is written of me.* Behold, I come at once to do thy will, because in the head of this book. Or, it is thus predetermined with God the Father, who is my head, I who am the book, that is, the form of justice for men.

VERS. 10.-- *A great church.* [n: (AUG.)] In the whole round of the lands, not a small one as before: and if indeed fear shall rise up, it will not forbid speaking. *Lord, thou hast known.* [n: (ID.)] Lips sound to men, etc., *as far as* that I be not slothful unto this.

VERS. 11.-- *I have not hidden.* [n: (CASS.)] (When I could profit) the truth, the promise, and the salvation.

[0903B]VERS. 12. *Thy mercy.* [n: (AUG.)] Love and fear lead me through unto thee.

VERS. 13.-- *Hairs.* [n: (AUG.)] Rightly are hairs brought in by way of comparison with offences. For therefore in the Old Testament the priests were shaven, that so they might be seen to have laid aside the vices of the flesh.

VERS. 14.-- *May it be pleasing.* [n: (CASS.)] *May it be pleasing,* marks the Trinity of persons; *to thee,* in the singular, marks the unity of essence. [n: (AUG.)] *May it be pleasing,* he says, not distrusting the power of God, if the will be present. As if: If thou wilt, thou canst, Lord. *Look.* [n: (CASS.)] His regard is our help.

VERS. 16.-- *Who say to me: Well done, well done.* [n: (AUG.)] Both he who speaks good things with an evil mind, and he who evil things by praising them deceitfully, each is evil and to be guarded against. Of the first sort [0903C] he said: *Who wish me evils;* of the second: *Who say to me: Well done, well done.*

VERS. 17.-- *And let them say.* [n: (CASS.)] This is their exultation: for this profession makes Christians rejoicing unto eternity.

VERS. 18.-- *But I.* [n: (CASS.)] This Christ, out of the form of a servant: lest any should arrogate to himself the glory of the aforesaid gladness. A beggar, who does not suffice for himself. *Delay not.* [n: (AUG.)] *Unless the days had been shortened,* etc., *as far as* who now nourish the things they have sought.



## PSALM XL

VERS. 1.-- *Unto the end.* [n: (HIER.)] *An understanding for the sons of Core,* that although poor and suffering, etc., *as far as* the Apostle saying: *But to me,* etc.--[n: (AUG.)] It is as it were [0903D] the voice of one man, of the head and of the body.

VERS. 2.-- *Blessed.* He is mocked who reverences a dead poor man; but *blessed.* [n: (AUG.)] The Prophet celebrates him that understands concerning the needy and the poor. [n: (HIER.)] First the Prophet from himself, afterward he will bring in Christ. *Who understandeth.* [n: (AUG.)] Note *who understandeth,* that it may be given even to those who do not ask: he who gives to one asking does well; he who understands one that is silent is without doubt blessed.

VERS. 3.-- *May the Lord preserve.* [n: (ID.)] Well does the Prophet wish it for him that understands concerning the needy and the poor, which is a sure promise. *And may he quicken.* [n: (ID.)] In the life to come, or, here through justification.

VERS. 4.-- *The Lord bring help.* [n: (CASS.)] Do not think that the blessed man has rest here: he mitigates his sorrows, and prays [0904A] that the infirmity of man be not overcome by hard things; as it were, these temporal goods are a bed of rest: as it were, he makes them full of sorrows, that hope be not had in them. *The bed.* [n: (AUG.)] For *the whole* indeed *couch,* that is, worldly happiness, God providently turns into straits. [n: (CASS.)] Here he himself, trembling, asks for mercy: because he who knew that he had sinned feared to be handed over to these things by right: but lest the servants of God, afflicted by these things, should seem deserted by God, since God himself bore such things; whence he says: *My enemies have spoken.* Or, *a bed of sorrow.* [n: (AUG.)] The flesh, in which is sin, etc., *as far as* when Adam was weakened by transgressing. *Help* [n: (AUG., HIER.)] In the infirmity of the flesh, which weighs down the soul. *The couch.* [n: (AUG.)] There is some earthly thing which the sick man, etc., *as far as* for the house of eternal habitation.

[0904B]VERS. 6.-- *My enemies.* [n: (CASS.)] The Lord concerning his passion, which was after the manner of the needy one. [n: (AUG.)] *My enemies.* These things are said specially of Christ, but they can be said also of his members: and today tribulation is made, that the name of Christ may be extinguished: but through his own death and that of his own the Church has grown the more.

VERS. 7.-- *And if he came in.* [n: (CASS.)] Note that the wicked are to be borne with.

[n: (ID.)] Not only open enemies, but also those who feigned themselves friends.

VERS. 8.-- *He went forth abroad.* From the bosom of truth; an evil entrance, an evil going out. *Unto the selfsame.* [n: (AUG.)] That is, with one counsel they all whispered against me. *They whispered.* [n: (CASS.)] They do not dare to uncover their counsel, because in the crime all are *partners:* whereas few [0904C] are wont to be the *authors* of crimes.

VERS. 9.-- *Shall he.* [n: (AUG.)] He who has the power of laying down his soul, has he not also the power of taking it up again?

VERS. 10.-- *I have hoped.* [n: (AUG.)] In the members, because other brethren hoped somewhat in him, because he seemed such as the rest. So too he says that he hungers in the poor.

VERS. 11.-- *But thou, O Lord.* [n: (AUG.)] Of the resurrection, which is the glory of the needy one, for the confirming of the hope of the faithful. *And I will requite them.* [n: (AUG.)]. For because they are scattered through the world, it is for admonition, not yet for damnation.

VERS. 13.-- *But me.* [n: (ID.)] He affirms what he prayed for.

VERS. 14.-- *Blessed.* [n: (CASS.)] A concluding praise, the passion and resurrection having been set forth. *The God of Israel.* [0904D] [n: (AUG.)] The Jews are our book-carriers, who carry the codices for us: we are Israel. Otherwise the pagans would have thought the things said of Christ and the Church to be feigned, but they are overcome by the testimony of enemies. *From the age,* etc. [n: (CASS.)] From which all things began to be administered, unto the age to come, where all things are eternal.

*Let it be done.* [n: (HIER.)] The doubling shows that this is to be done by all. In place of *let it be done,* in the Hebrew is written *amen, amen,* which Aquila translated *truly, truly,* or *faithfully.* And here ends the first book of the Psalter according to Jerome.



## PSALM XLI.

VERS. 1.-- *At the end, an understanding.* [var: Vulg. Ps 41:1 reads "In finem" in the accusative and adds "filiis Core": "unto the end; an understanding for the sons of Core"] [n: (CASS.)] *Core* is interpreted *Calvary.* Therefore the sons, etc., *as far as,* whom [0905A] the insult of the wicked meets. [n: (HIER.)] *Sons of Core,* that is, *sons of the bald man,* etc., *as far as* the word of the boys mocking Elisha.

VERS. 2.-- *As the hart desireth,* etc. [n: (AUG.)] This voice suits catechumens, and also all who are still journeying abroad.

*The hart.* The hart, burdened with age, its hairs and horns growing out, draws in a serpent by its nostrils: whence, burning with the venom, it most ardently desires a fountain from which to drink; and when it has drunk, it lays aside its horns and its hairs.

VERS. 3.-- *It hath thirsted.* [n: (AUG.)] He determines his desire, because the hart could be understood to desire a fountain for the sake of drinking, or of washing; here all desire is shown to be toward God. *When shall I come.* What is quicker to God is slow to the desire of man. [0905B] Such a hart, while it runs, suffers those who insult; whence: *My tears have been my bread.* In prosperity also, which he despises, as in adversity. *Bread.* [n: (CASS.)] Not bitterness, or destruction, but *bread.*

VERS. 5.-- *These things I remembered.* [n: (AUG.)] While they ask, Where is thy God? I also have meditated through all these visible things, and through my own soul, that through those outward things I may find the invisible things of God. Behold the understanding of the title. *I poured out. [n: (Ibid.)]* For understanding and attaining I have spread it out above itself. *I poured out.* [n: (CASS.)] Or, I have rejoiced. My soul is melted within me; not outside, but pricked with compunction it returns into itself. Although I do not see God visibly, yet I consider that through the Church, in which the saints are wonderful, I shall come to the heavenly Jerusalem, [0905C] taken captive by the sweetness of the angelic praise, which is received by the mind. *Into the place of the tabernacle.* [n: (AUG.)] The tabernacle of God on earth, etc., *as far as,* that is, of those rejoicing in the eternal refreshment. *In the voice of exultation. [n: (Ibid.)]* Of the eternal festival there sounds something sweet to the heart: whence the hart is snatched away to the fountains of waters, and is soothed. But because the world makes a din, and the flesh weighs down the soul, comparing this sorrow with those joyful things, he says:

VERS. 6.-- *Why art thou sad?* etc. [n: (CASS.)] Of the disturbances against which reason strengthens the sensual soul with hope of God. *Why dost thou trouble?* [n: (AUG.)] Who, if thou searchest, there is nothing in the world for which thou shouldst owe this: hope rather for the saving remedy still, and if not now, fully in time to come. *Hope.* [n: (CASS.)] Or he can hope, because *still* in the present *I will confess,* that is, I will do penance [0905D] for my sins, that I may not withstand thee.

VERS. 7.-- *To myself.* Turned to God he tells the troublings from himself, the end of them from God: whence in him is his hope. And this is *to myself,* or from myself. [n: (CASS.)] Behold, he who above was correcting the soul to the condition of man, having turned back says here that the soul is troubled by itself, which, were there not the vices of the flesh, would remain tranquil. Behold out of what diversity man is composed. As if: therefore, that is, on account of the troublings of the soul, *I will be mindful.* [n: (AUG.)] That is, I hasten to thee, because thou shuttest out all evils. Behold the remedy by which he may escape the troublings: whence he subjoins for what things he is mindful: *Of the land of Jordan,* where the Lord consecrated baptism, in which the humbled are washed, and [0906A] become fruitful earth. [n: (CASS.)] He who is mindful of these things is not troubled by evils. *Therefore mindful.* [n: (AUG.)] Whereby the soul is troubled toward me, therefore *mindful,* etc. No one runs to remission, save he who is displeasing to himself, save he who confesses himself a sinner: which *Hermoniim* denotes; no one confesses unless he humbles himself, which is denoted in *the little one. Jordan. [n: (Ibid.)]* A going down. Descend therefore that thou mayest be lifted up. [n: (CASS.)] In the Jordan the Lord consecrated baptism, in which the humbled are washed, and become fruitful earth, which yields fruit thirtyfold, sixtyfold and a hundredfold [n: (Matth. XIII)]. *Hermoniim.* [n: (AUG.)] A small mountain beside the Jordan, etc., *as far as* because it is not among the proud, but among the humble, that one is mindful of God.

VERS. 8.-- *Deep unto deep. [n: (Ibid.)]* It is fitting that [0906B] I be mindful, etc., *as far as* and on account of all these things I pray in adversities, and this is: *With me a prayer. [n: (Ibid.)]* Or *deep calleth unto deep,* that is, the judgment which God brought upon the sinner, etc., *as far as* because he fears the judgments. *Of the cataracts.* Cataracts, according to the letter, are the mouths of rivers.

VERS. 9.-- *In the day. [n: (Ibid.)]* That is, in prosperity, etc., *as far as* whence we may live in adversity. *With me a prayer. [n: (Ibid.)]* Prayer in troublings is mine, and this is with me.

VERS. 10.-- *Why hast thou forgotten.* [n: (CASS.)] Why dost thou now suffer me to be vexed with these miseries? He saw what sweetness of the fatherland there was, and shuddered at the perils of the world.

VERS. 11.-- *While my bones are broken.* [n: (AUG.)] As it were bones are broken, while the strength of patience is afflicted, [0906C] at which the wicked laugh. This is that reproach: *While it is said to me: Where is he?* either there is none, or he cares not for thee.

## PSALM XLII.

*Unto the end, a psalm of David.* [n: (AUG.)] Of the body of Christ, which groans here among the tares, that is, among the wicked, having many things in common with them: to be born, to die, prosperity and adversity: with whom (because it has not a common cause of merits) it cries out: *Judge me,* etc. [n: (Ibid.)] This psalm is against pressures, lest they too draw us back from God. [n: (CASS.)] He complains of the mingling of the wicked, and prays for separation.

VERS. 1.-- *Judge me. [n: (Ibid.)]* He does not ask that his own sins be examined, which is dangerous, but that his cause be made ready apart from the impious, that he may be with the elect: whence he adds: *And [0906D] distinguish.* The nation that is not holy is the unjust and deceitful man, from whom he is separated in the judgment: but here too lest he be corrupted by them.

VERS. 2.-- *Why do I go sorrowful?* [n: (AUG.)] He seeks the cause of sadness, which is sin, as justice is the cause of gladness. Isaias on account of sin: *For a little while indeed I made him sorrowful,* for so God punishes.

VERS. 3.-- *Send forth thy light.* [n: (CASS.)] Because, when he comes, all the sadness of confusion will be taken away. [n: (AUG.)] Because we now have a sure pledge, since those very things, that is, light and truth, have led the present Church away from her sins, which is the tabernacle.

VERS. 4.-- *And I will go in.* As the high priest to the Holy of holies. [n: (CASS.)] *And I will go in.* Of the final gladness, [0907A] for which he gives praise in tribulation, he who above was sad, whence also he strengthens himself.

VERS. 5.-- *I will confess to thee on the harp.* [n: (AUG.)] He who does the precepts of God, and does not suffer, confesses on the psaltery: but he who does and suffers, which is from the lower nature, *on the harp,* which sounds from below, as the psaltery from above. *On the harp.* [n: (CASS.)] Behold, he who above was sad about the evils of the world, makes joy for himself out of good understanding. *Why dost thou trouble me? [n: (Ibid.)]* Frequently and vehemently he urges that the sadness of the world must be fled, which extinguishes patience, charity and hope, and confounds the whole life. *The salvation of my countenance.* [n: (AUG.)] And elsewhere: *Have mercy on me,* etc., *as far as* almsgiving, and prayer which flies to God on those two wings.



## PSALM XLIII.

[0907B]

VERS. 1.-- *Unto understanding.* [n: (AUG.)] Let them not so seek these things to be done for themselves, etc., *as far as* and let not these things be loved. [n: (CASS.)] He distinguishes the benefits of God, which were given to beginners in the world, to the perfect in heaven.

That it may be the more wonderful that present things are left behind, he recalls things past.

VERS. 2.-- *God, with our ears.* [n: (AUG., CASS.)] First he says: *God,* which one is wont to do when glad or heavily afflicted.

*We have heard.* That there may be greater faith in the matter, lest it be thought doubtful.

*Fathers.* Not young men, that they may be seen as our true ones, who would tell sure things to their own.

[0907C] *The work which,* etc. [n: (Ibid.)] And although he always works, yet he is said to work when he shows some sign of newness.

VERS. 4.-- *But thy right hand.* [n: (AUG.)] That is, the good success which thou gavest them. *And thy arm,* etc. [n: (Ibid.)] That is, strength; and *illumination,* that is, wise counsel: all which thou hast done out of thy good will.

VERS. 5.-- *Thou art thyself my king and my God.* [n: (AUG.)] The times are changed; the author is not changed.

VERS. 7.-- *For I will not hope in my bow.* [n: (CASS.)] The lesser strength of man, because it avails less in war.

*A sword.* The greater strength, which in war is believed to avail more. [n: (AUG.)] Or, *my sword shall not save me,* but thou, because in this world also thou hast saved us, lest they should drag us [0907D] after them, and thou hast made them ashamed: and therefore then *we shall be praised.*

VERS. 8.-- *Thou hast saved,* etc. [n: (AUG.)] Note also that things said in the past are to be taken for things to come.

VERS. 10.-- *But now thou hast cast us off.* [n: (CASS.)] Diapsalmus. He enumerates the evils which the good did here, yet not having forgotten God. [n: (CASS.)] He exaggerates the evils, that he may obtain the good will of the judge. *Thou hast confounded,* while here they are held in reproach by the wicked. [n: (AUG.)] These things are truly to come, etc., *as far as* this is a fast not of abstinence, but of luxury.

VERS. 11.-- *Thou hast turned away.* [n: (CASS.)] For those who fail. Or, to be turned away is to flee the enemy; whence: *If they shall have persecuted you in one city, flee into another.* [var: Vulg. Mt 10:23 reads "Cum autem persequentur vos in civitate ista, fugite in aliam": "and when they shall persecute you in this city, flee into another"]

[0908A] *They plundered.* This happens to those put to flight, that they fall as prey, who cannot resist their enemies.

VERS. 12.-- *Thou hast given us like sheep for food.* [n: (CASS.)] Those persisting in faith. Here diverse martyrs, that is, diverse kinds of martyrdom: some food for beasts, others delivered to the Gentiles into servitude; whence he says: *Like sheep for food,* where the slaughter of certain ones is noted.

VERS. 13.-- *Thou hast sold thy people without a price.* [n: (AUG.)] While they are given to foreigners and no one gains: which is done for proving or for correcting; or no one is gained for them, or if any, yet few.

VERS. 14.-- *A reproach to our neighbours.* [n: (CASS.)] A grievous affliction is heaped on by neighbours, because that is not uttered in passing which one inflicts from among those standing round. *A derision to those who are round about.* [n: (CASS.)] Derision is a confused voice of gladness, [0908B] announcing insult with immoderate hilarity. Contempt is abject vileness.

VERS. 15.-- *Into the likeness of the Gentiles. [n: (Ibid.)]* [var: Vulg. Ps 43:15 has the dative, "in similitudinem gentibus": "for a likeness to the Gentiles"] In the likeness of Christ they are set beside the Gentiles, when they afflicted them with equal punishments.

VERS. 17.-- *Of him that reproacheth. [n: (Ibid.)]* To reproach is to curse to the face; to speak against is to detract from one absent, and to bite deceitfully.

VERS. 18.-- *They have come upon us. [n: (Ibid.)]* Because they were not in us through fault.

VERS. 19.-- *It hath not gone back. [n: (Ibid.)]* So that it should go backward, although sometimes it descends from the contemplation of the deity. *Thou hast turned aside.* [n: (AUG.)] Our paths were in the pleasures and prosperities of the world: but thou hast taken them away from their own way, and hast shown the narrow way which leads to [0908C] life. [n: (HIER.)] Or *and thou hast turned aside,* that is, thou hast made our works to hang upon thy law. Or, *thou hast not turned aside* (another reading), that is, thou hast not permitted our paths to turn aside and go astray. *Paths,* that is, works; *from thy way,* that is, from the law; therefore because *thou hast humbled us.*

VERS. 20.-- *The shadow of death.* [n: (AUG.)] Or, this mortality is the shadow of death: but true death is damnation with the devil.

VERS. 21.-- *If we have forgotten the name of God. [n: (Ibid.)]* This is the understanding of the sons of Core, who on account of all these evils do not forget God, knowing that God will require these things.

VERS. 22.-- *He hath known. [n: (Ibid.)]* God is said to harm [sic: *Nocere*] both what he knows and what he makes thee know. *He hath known,* because he will require on account of thee, that thou mayest find thyself, and give [0908D] thanks to him who made thee, as was done in Peter, who proved what he could do. *For thy sake we are put to death.* [n: (CASS.)] Not for our crimes, but that thy faith may be increased among the Gentiles.

VERS. 23.-- *Arise. [n: (Ibid.)]* He prays for help, that he may succour those afflicted here at the time of the resurrection. [n: (AUG.)] Thus far not forgetting; and that we may persevere: *Arise, for on thy account we are put to death.* [n: (AUG., HIER.)] And although they know that Christ does not sleep, and that he has risen for himself, yet they pray him to rise through faith, not for himself, but for the Gentiles, who think him dead. For if they too believed that he had risen, believing in him they would not persecute, but they too would believe. [n: (AUG.)] Or *arise,* strengthen.

[0909A] *Thou sleepest.* Thou seemest to neglect. Wherefore, even if we know not, thou knowest why: either for fault, or for benefit. But if for fault, would that it were absent; if for benefit, would that it were present. Some thou dost repel, because thou withdrawest thy face, that is, thy knowledge, from them: thou sleepest to certain ones who groan over evils.

VERS. 24.-- *Why dost thou forget?* that is, thou givest not the spirit of fortitude, without which we are helpless. *And of tribulation.* [n: (CASS.)] Because thou dost not mitigate it, etc., *as far as* who easily yield to unlawful motions, and cleave to earthly things.

VERS. 26.-- *Arise, O Lord.* Here *arise* is set without sleeping, which pertains to the resurrection, in which is the hope of all the faithful; in which human captivity is destroyed; through which we are helped [0909B] and set free. *In the earth our belly.* [n: (AUG.)] He who fixes his knees on the earth has whereby he may be more humbled: but he who is more humbled, so that his belly also cleaves to the earth, has nothing further whereby he may be more humbled. He expresses therefore an exceeding humiliation, so that, since it has come to the highest point, the mercy of God may now be present. Or he laments those who are the belly, that is, the carnal, and *they have cleaved,* that is, consented to the wicked.



## PSALM XLIV.

VERS. 1.-- *Unto the end,* etc. [n: (AUG.)] An epithalamium, that is, the praise of the bridal chamber. A canticle for those who are to be changed and for the beloved, that is, for the bride and the bridegroom, is written for the sons of Core, that they may venerate. For here is described through whom they shall be changed, because the praise of the bridegroom and of the bride is here sung. [n: (CASS.)] The Prophet promises that he will utter forth [0909C] the heraldings of God.

VERS. 2.-- *It hath uttered forth.* [n: (AUG., HIER.)] For the Prophet speaks within, etc., *as far as* before that is which he is about to do. But the counsel of the Father is the Word.

*Good.* By which all good things were made. *I speak.* That is, I bring forth the Word: the same here as before; but he says that in that very word are all the works of God; whence: *What was made in him was life,* so that nothing may be in the work which was not first in the counsel. Of this it is said: *God hath spoken once.*

*A pen.* He compares the Word not to sound, which passes away, but to writing, which remains. Or, *swiftly,* because not one thing after another, but all things together in one eternal Word.

*Swiftly.* The power of prophecy, which does not under [0909D] human torment devise what it divulges to others out of the secret of the Godhead.

VERS. 3.-- *Beautiful in form.* [n: (CASS.)] He begins the praise of the bridegroom first from his form. *He hath blessed.* The former things the Prophet said in the person of God, these from his own; or all from himself. Or, *he uttereth forth the word,* who says a hymn to God.

*I speak my works.* [n: (AUG.)] to the praise of him who is from him. *Tongue, a pen.* Because what I say, I also write. Or, *swiftly:* because the things which I write are quickly to come.

[n: (CASS.)] *In the lips.* Because, when he spoke, the world received the grace of reconciliation.

VERS. 4.-- *Gird thyself. [n: (Ibid.)]* Here he praises him from his power; and do thou, being such, *gird thyself.*

VERS. 5.-- *With thy comeliness and beauty. Comeliness* to [0910A] the humanity, in which he appeared to the world; *beauty* to the Deity, by which he adorns all things.

*Attend.* Pitying, look upon man as he perishes.

*Prosperously.* Setting men free.

*Proceed.* From the womb of the Virgin, as a bridegroom: or, from these conquered to others to be conquered.

*Reign.* Show thy power in the faith of men, thou who hast always reigned. And through this, because both through thyself and through thine own thou teachest the truth, by which thou destroyest our falsehood. And because meek in bearing with the wicked, as when he prayed for his enemies, so also his own: And because he showed the justice of life. And because it shall lead thee on without stumbling through the course of the whole life, or from people to people

*Wonderfully.* Because all shall wonder, or, because he will do miracles.

[0910B]VERS. 6.-- *The peoples shall fall under thee.* [n: (AUG.)] That is, many of his enemies shall put themselves under him, and this by reason or by will. This is what the title said: *For those who shall be changed out of the sons of Core.* [var: Vulg. Ps 44:1 has no preposition and divides the clause: "pro iis qui commutabuntur. Filiis Core": "for those who shall be changed; for the sons of Core"] -- *Into the hearts.* [n: (CASS.)] Of thy enemies, that they may be friends, as Saul was.

VERS. 7.-- *A throne. [n: (Ibid.)]* Here from judgment: *A throne* pertains to judgment. *A rod. [n: (Ibid.)]* The rule of divine equity, which is truly straight and is never bent: this governs the just, strikes the unjust. [n: (AUG.)] Let no one presume much to himself concerning mercy, or flatter himself: because the rod of direction, if it governs some, crushes others: it governs the spiritual, it crushes the carnal.

VERS. 8.-- *Thou hast loved. [n: (Ibid.)]* Or, because *thou hast loved justice,* etc., these rewards have been rendered, he made thee king, [0910C] giving *the name which is above every name.*

*And thou hast hated.* If a man hates iniquity in himself, he acts with God: God hates iniquity, do thou also hate it.

*O God.* [n: (AUG.)] It is set twice openly in the Greek, the one a vocative, the other a nominative. This one is anointed, that one is the stone which Jacob anointed.
