VERS. 9.-- *Myrrh,* etc. [n: (CASS.)] Here the Prophet praises the bridegroom from his wife and from his adornment; first from the adornment. [n: (AUG.)] *Myrrh and stacte.* Perfumes are fitly applied to the bridal chamber, etc., *as far as* that is, redemption, through the water of baptism. *Of ivory.* [n: (HIER.)] In ivory, chastity, which, being dark before, is made bright by saws and files; so the saints, filed down by the cuttings and rubbings of divers punishments, are made bright by the variety of virtues. *The daughters of kings.* [n: (AUG.)] Of earthly ones, not caring for the honour [0910D] of their fathers, etc., *as far as* and they took them for the raising up of seed.

VERS. 10.-- *The queen stood,* etc. [n: (CASS.)] Here he commends the bridegroom from his wife. *With variety. [n: (Ibid.)]* Although there be unity in charity, yet she is adorned with gold, that is, the doctrine of the Apostles, with the scarlet of the martyrs, with the gems of virgins, with the purple of the penitent.

VERS. 11.-- *Hear, O daughter.* [n: (CASS.)] The praise of the bride is lower, but fourfold: from her form, from her glory, from the maidens, from her offspring. [n: (AUG.)] *Hear, O daughter.* The Prophet addresses the queen, as one of the fathers. The Jews saw and slew; do thou hear the Gospel, and by believing thou shalt see God: here through an image, in time to come [0911A] through sight. Or, Hear the prophecies which foretold the incarnation, and see them fulfilled.

VERS. 12.-- *And the king shall desire.* [n: (CASS.)] Here he praises her from her form.

VERS. 13.-- *Of Tyre.* [n: (CASS.)] By Tyre, the neighbour of Judaea, the Gentiles are designated; whence the Canaanite woman is a figure of the Church. [n: (CASS.)] *Of Tyre,* of the Gentiles, the whole from a part. *With gifts.* [n: (AUG.)] That they may offer themselves, etc., *as far as* the Gentiles, namely, who are in the straits of their sins.

VERS. 14.-- *All the glory.* [n: (CASS.)] From the glory of the bride, as above from the power of the bridegroom. *From within.* [n: (AUG.)] Or inwardly. Let almsgiving therefore be in secret, where God sees. Or not in a circumcision made by hand, as in the old law, but in spiritual things. *In fringes.* [n: (CASS.)] The end of the garment signifies the end of the world or of man. [0911B] *Golden.* Not gilded, as above, but golden, because in the end is the whole perfection, where charity is found full.

VERS. 15.-- *They shall be brought to the king. [n: (Ibid.)]* Here the bride is praised from her friends, as above from judgment. *[n: (Ibid.)] They shall be brought,* by opportune preaching. *After her. [n: (Ibid.)]* First unity is spoken of, afterward the enumeration of the parts is distinct, when it is said: *Virgins and her neighbours.* [var: Vulg. Ps 44:15 has no connective: "virgines post eam ; proximae ejus", "virgins after her; her neighbours"]

VERS. 17.-- *Instead of thy fathers sons are born to thee. [n: (Ibid.)]* Or in place of the ancient fathers, worshippers of idols, the Apostles were born, princes of preaching. [n: (AUG.)] Or after the prophets the Apostles, after the Apostles others.

This psalm is sung on the feast of blessed Mary the Virgin, because the things which are here said generally of the Church can be referred specially to Mary. It is sung also [0911C] for virgins, because it is there said: *Virgins shall be brought to the king.* And for the Apostles, because where it is said: *Sons are born to thee.* Likewise of the nativity of the Lord, because there it treats of the praise of the bridegroom and of the bride and of the nuptials of Christ and the Church, whose espousal was made in the incarnation of the Word.



## PSALM XLV.

VERS. 1.-- *Unto the end.* [n: (CASS.)] A psalm of David, of some faithful man of Judea, that the testimony may be the more certain.

*For the hidden things.* For the manifestation of the hidden things, that God is man, that salvation is promised to both peoples, that blindness has befallen Israel, that the Apostles passed over to the Gentiles, that peace is on earth; whence: *They shall turn their swords into ploughshares and their spears into sickles* [0911D] [n: (Isa. II)].

*For the sons of Core.* [n: (CASS.)] The sons of the cross. *Of the hidden things,* that is, he speaks of the coming of Christ.

VERS. 2.-- *Our God.* [n: (CASS., AUG.)] In the coming of the Lord all hope is shown to be had, even in the passing over of the Apostles to the Gentiles. *They have found us exceedingly.* [n: (AUG.)] For we were fleeing them. Or, *they have found us* exposed through Adam. *Exceedingly,* by so much the more powerful is he who delivers.

VERS. 3.-- *The earth shall be troubled, and the mountains shall be removed,* etc. [n: (CASS.)] Judea, as it were dry land: without idolatry, hemmed about by the bitterness of the Gentiles. Or, *the sea,* [n: (AUG.)] the world.

*The heart of the sea.* The savagery of the Gentiles

[0912A]VERS. 4.-- *They have sounded, and are troubled,* etc. Those who contradict, the Gentiles: or the Apostles by preaching.

*And their waters were troubled.* The reference is made to the sense, not to the word: that is, the waters of the sea, namely the peoples, or their doctrines, which have been made foolish, the doctrine of the Saviour flashing forth; whence: *They were swallowed up,* etc. And this is the rod of Moses, which devoured the rods of the Magi.

VERS. 5.-- *The stream of the river.* [n: (CASS.)] Diapsalmus. Because the Church is founded in Christ, to whom the nations and kingdoms are made subject. [n: (AUG.)] As if he should say: Thus the sea rages; but amid these waves and noises they fear not, who have fled to that refuge.

VERS. 6.-- *He shall help her.* [n: (CASS.)] Or, *with his countenance,* that is, by the showing forth of his help, when by helping he [0912B] declares himself. *In the morning.* In the very beginning of a good work; and this, *at daybreak,* that is, through the illumination of the Holy Spirit, who is light as it were of day.

VERS. 7.-- *He gave.* As it were a great reward. *His voice.* Preaching, at which the earthly man trembled. He shall tremble for fear of the judge.

VERS. 8.-- *The Lord of hosts. [n: (Ibid.)]* Briefly he defines what Christ is. *With us.* As it were: He who sent the angels, now he himself has come, and is with us, because with us as our upholder; therefore: *Come,* as it were I invite you now secure. *Ours.* Not save of the sons of the cross. *The God of Jacob.* Who gave us as great things as he gave Jacob: for he is not the God of him alone.

VERS. 9.-- *Come and see. [n: (Ibid.)]* Diapsalmus. The mighty works of the incarnation. *He hath set.* As it were signs, for the calling of [0912C] the people. *Prodigies.* As concerning the birth from the Virgin, and the star, and the rest. *Making wars to cease.* [n: (AUG.)] Because through his coming the idolatry and superstition of the whole world perished, or, at that time for his presence the world was at peace.

VERS. 11.-- *Be still. [n: (Ibid.)]* If the arms are burnt up, *be still,* not having wherewith you may fight.

## PSALM XLVI.

VERS. 2.-- *All ye Gentiles.* [n: (AUG.)] As if he should say: they insult, the boys, saying: *Bald head, bald head,* you, all the Gentiles to whom grace has passed over: *Clap your hands,* etc. The Gentiles to whom grace has passed over, and whom in the former psalm he called to faith, he invites to praise.

VERS. 3.-- *High.* [n: (HIER.)] Powerful in what he wills. [0912D] *Terrible.* [n: (REMIG.)] Because he is to judge in power.

VERS. 4.-- *He hath subdued the peoples unto us.* [n: (CASS.)] This befits faithful men, to whom all outsiders are made subject, or, he has subjected to himself those who are to be associated with us. *The peoples,* the Jews. *Unto us. [n: (Ibid.)]* Namely to the universal Church; this it befits all the faithful to say, to whom outsiders are made subject. *Under the feet. [n: (Ibid.)]* By *feet,* understand preachers, to whom the peoples are made subject. [n: (AUG.)] *Under the feet.* So is the Church exalted, that it has those not yet believing under its feet.

VERS. 5.-- *He hath chosen for us,* etc. Or in the person of the Apostles, he chose to give us his inheritance, of many peoples, that we may cultivate it, striking their hearts with the sword of preaching, and destroying their malice. [0913A] *The inheritance.* [n: (AUG.)] The heavenly inheritance is the beauty of the Christian people, in which he shall be comely, shining as the sun, who now is abject. *The beauty of Jacob which,* etc. That is, those like to Jacob or signed by Jacob, who give earthly things for heavenly, as Jacob did, which was the grace of God, because *he loved him, Esau being reprobate.*

VERS. 6.-- *He is ascended.* [n: (CASS.)] Diapsalma, the ascension and the kingdom of God is described, whence he urgently admonishes to sing psalms. *In jubilation.* [n: (AUG.)] The joy of the wondering Apostles, etc., *as far as* what can neither be kept silent nor be uttered.

VERS. 7.-- *Sing ye psalms.* [n: (CASS.)] Praise by works: very salutary is that which is so often repeated, because it is God who creates, and a king governing the things created.

VERS. 8.-- *Of all the earth. [n: (Ibid.)]* Not as Jupiter of Crete, [0913B] Mars of Thrace. *Wisely.* He teaches how one ought to sing psalms. Of old they sang psalms without understanding, while they worshipped stones. [n: (AUG.)] No one does wisely what he does not understand.

VERS. 9.-- *He shall reign. [n: (Ibid.)]* Now is this prophecy fulfilled: *God sitteth upon the holy seat. The holy seat.* [n: (AUG.)] Thrones, Virtues, and the saints.

VERS. 10.-- *The princes of the peoples.* [n: (CASS.)] The first-fruits of all the Gentiles came together by believing in Christ, because, the Jews being cast out, the Gentiles succeed into the sons of Abraham, having the promise, because the Jews were lifted up in pride. [n: (HIER., CASS.)] Or *the strong gods,* namely the Apostles, whom neither death nor life shall separate from the charity of Christ. *Lifted up,* by miracles and doctrine.



## PSALM XLVII.

[0913C]

VERS. 1.-- *A psalm, or the praise of a canticle, for the sons of Core, the second,* etc. [n: (AUG.)] On the first day, God made light, etc., *as far as* of which it is here sung to the sons of Core. [n: (CASS.)] Concerning the founding of the city, which he describes commendingly. [n: (AUG.)] He commends the city, etc., *as far as* from its eternal stability.

VERS. 2.-- *In the holy mount,* etc. [n: (Ibid.)] This is that mountain, etc., *as far as* upon which the Church is founded.

VERS. 3.-- *It is founded with the joy.* [n: (HIER.)] As if he should say, she who before was subject to curses, etc., *as far as* he himself is Christ, who through the universal Church spreads joys abroad. *Mount Sion, the sides.* [n: (AUG.)] Whosoever [0913D] cleave to anyone are called his sides, etc., *as far as* likewise *from the north shall every evil be spread abroad,* etc.

VERS. 4.-- *In the houses.* Or, in the degrees, that is, according to the degrees of merits, because in each one severally he will give the knowledge of himself. *When he shall receive her.* To be guarded, and what need is there? *For the kings against.* But seeing by faith, namely thus, that this city is so worthy, they wondered, etc.

VERS. 5.-- *The kings.* [n: (AUG., CASS.)] The princes of the Jews. *Being gathered together.* By Herod inquiring the place of the nativity. *They came together.* Saying with one accord: *In Bethlehem of Juda according to the Scriptures.*

VERS. 6.-- *Seeing.* The things which they had read in the prophets. *They wondered.* At so great glory, not lightly or idly. [0914A] *But they were troubled.* For sin, and *They were moved.* To faith, and so great was the dread, that the body trembled.

VERS. 7.-- *There pains as of a woman in labour,* Of penance, seeing thus; but:

VERS. 8.-- *With a vehement spirit,* Of Herod. *Thou shalt break the ships of Tharsis.* Because, in Tharsus, the ships of Cilicia which were believed to have carried the Magi over into their own country, were broken by Herod. *Tharsis.* [n: (AUG.)] Cilicia is a region named from Tharsus, etc., *as far as* having run through all he concludes. Therefore:

VERS. 9.-- *As we have heard. In the city of the Lord of hosts,* because there he appoints angels as guards. He repeats. *In the city of the Lord of hosts, in the city of our God.* That is, the Catholic Church, lest the assembly of heretics usurp this name to itself. But lest it be thought temporal, [0914B] he adds: *God hath founded it for ever.* Not for an hour, as before the tabernacle of Moses

VERS. 10.-- *We have received, O God, thy mercy.* [n: (CASS.)] Giving thanks for the coming of Christ, he admonishes concerning the degrees of the Church that are to be distributed, through which the Saviour may be known. *In the midst of the temple.* Jerusalem: they are the more grievously accused, who despise him who was beheld in the midst of them. By this the incarnation of Christ is noted, who according as he is man was presented in the temple.

VERS. 11.-- *The right hand.* [n: (CASS.)] On this side the just are placed.

[n: (AUG.)] *We have received, O God.* In what order she was founded, or what we have heard and seen. *Of thy temple. [n: (Ibid.)]* That is, of a people not receiving [sic: *sucipientis*] mercy, who to many: [0914C] so that no grains seem to be there, but nevertheless some, because according to the name. *According to the name.* [n: (CASS.)] Which is everywhere spread abroad, because there is none, even if he know not the worship, who does not submit himself to this name. *Or according to the name.* This is, as the reverence of thy name, etc., *as far as* thy propitiation hath justified many, who all praise thee.

VERS. 12.-- *Let it be glad.* [n: (AUG.)] Although the just labour among thorns, etc., *as far as* it is theirs now to gather, God's to separate. *Mount Sion, and let them rejoice.* [n: (CASS.)] The Church, which is in the beholding of the people. *The daughters of Juda, because of thy judgments, O Lord.* Holy women. By Juda their nation is shown, which is on account of Christ, who is of Juda, and let these rejoice, that in both sexes the Church may be. [n: (AUG.)] The cause of gladness is the judgments [0914D] to come, wherein they know that they shall come to blessedness. Let those who are within be glad; you who are without, encompass.

VERS. 13.-- *Encompass.* By showing honours. *Embrace her.* With charity. *Tell in her towers. Set your hearts.* [n: (CASS.)] Preach to others in the concord of the Apostles, etc., *as far as* lest, being remiss through gladness, they grow slack.

VERS. 14.-- *Her strength.* [n: (AUG.)] The strength of the city is charity, which nothing overcomes; whence Solomon: *Love is strong as death.* [n: (CASS.)] He admonishes to set the hearts on the strength of the Church. *Distribute her houses, that you may relate it in another generation.* That is, the offices distinguished, etc., *as far as* whom the Lord first chose.

VERS. 15.-- *For this is,* etc. This is what [0915A] must be related, a brief sentence, but one which concludes all things. *For ever,* against those who made temporal men gods.



## PSALM XLVIII.

*Unto the end, a psalm,* etc. [n: (CASS.)] The Prophet treats of the lovers of the world, and of Christ the redeemer, not of those, but of the despisers of the world, that he who is cast down in the world may not set a price upon the exalted. [n: (AUG.)] They who worship God, etc., *as far as* he makes them attentive to new things and to mighty things.

VERS. 2.-- *Hate these things, all ye nations.* [var: Vulg. Ps 48:2 *Audite haec, omnes gentes*, "Hear these things, all ye nations"] [n: (CASS.)] I except none, etc., *as far as* when, namely, the cockle shall be separated from the wheat, the sheep from the goats.

VERS. 3.-- *And ye that are earthborn.* [n: ((AUG.)] I withhold my speech from none, let the heir of the earth hear, because of the judgment: [0915B] the just man, because of the kingdom.

VERS. 4.-- *My mouth shall speak.* [n: (CASS.)] He commends the things to be said. *Wisdom* for divine things, *Prudence* for instructing morals; in these two the whole divine discourse is signified.

VERS. 5.-- *I will incline.* [n: (HIER.)] I will humble myself to the truth speaking within, which teaches to speak in parables, lest it lie open to all.

VERS. 6.-- *Why shall I fear.* [n: (CASS.)] He begins to narrate the things he promised, saying that Christ will not redeem the ungodly; here the prophet, in his own person pleading a general cause, seeks the cause of the damnation and the fear of the wicked, which is iniquity. [n: (AUG.)] Why shall I fear? as though there is not what I should fear, since to avoid iniquity is in my own power; for that alone shall there be fear in the [0915C] last day, which shall be evil to the damned, not to the just. *Of the heel. [n: (Ibid.)]* He alludes to the words, etc., *as far as* that is, it shall oppress and shall make to fear.

VERS. 7.-- *They that trust.* These, namely, who trust, etc., *as far as* in which they are so foolish.

VERS. 8.-- *A brother doth not redeem.* If a brother, that is Christ, does not redeem, etc., *as far as* because such a one gives not to God a propitiation.

VERS. 9.-- *And shall live still. [n: (Ibid.)]* After the end of life, he shall live according to the soul, because the soul shall not perish with the body, but shall be saved unto grief, until, the body being received, he be tormented on both sides.

VERS. 11.-- *He shall not see. [n: (Ibid.)]* That is, he does not understand, etc., *as far as* when the wise man passes through life. Or, *the unwise. [n: (Ibid.)]* Who attends not. *The fool.* Who, if he attends, does not [0915D] beware. *Riches* [n: (CASS.)] For the sake of which they did evil. *His own:* which are not according to God.

VERS. 12.-- *They have called their names.* [n: (AUG.)] The heirs, who at the sepulchres of the dead make themselves drunk in memory of them, but neither does a drop reach the tongue of him that burns in hell.

VERS. 13.-- *And man when.* And both in this, and in the rest, man does not understand; or, therefore God is not to be accused, he understood not his own dignity, he acted not understandingly, but was compared, reason being lost, to beasts, not in another thing, but in wisdom.

VERS. 14.-- *This way.* [n: (CASS.)] Here now he gathers into one all that has been said. *Way,* that is, life, because it leads to death. *A stumbling block.* A goad and a grief, etc., *as far [0916A] as: This people honoureth me with their lips, but their heart is far from me. In their own mouth they shall be well pleased.* [n: (AUG.)] Since they are such, etc., *as far as* and they are worse than open evils.

VERS. 15.-- *Like sheep.* [n: (CASS.)] Diapsalmus. What straitness is to be rendered to the ungodly. They boast themselves, but to me it is already clear that they are placed in hell. Sheep, their wool being lost, remain, so in these, without the consuming of their substance, punishment finds what it may torment. *Death.* [n: (AUG.)] As Christ is life, so the devil is death; who is a shepherd to them, as to sheep, whom he draws to the pastures of death. *He shall feed upon.* [n: (CASS.)] From the likeness of beasts, etc., *as far as* he shall lead them from punishment to punishment. *In the morning.* [n: (AUG.)] Because now it is night, while the merits of the just do not appear, and the felicity of the ungodly is as it were named. *He shall grow old in [0916B] hell from their glory,* etc. [n: (AUG.)] How great glory he seemed to have, etc., *as far as: From his own glory* they were cast out.

VERS. 17.-- *Fear not.* [n: (CASS.)] Diapsalmus. An admonition, that the powerful be not feared, who leave all things behind with the light. For why these things are permitted to sinners, is a common complaint. *He is made rich.* For these things the abject also have. *Glory.* In the honours of the world. *House.* Because not he only, but all who pertain to him.

VERS. 19.-- *He shall be blessed. [n: (Ibid.)]* Not for a good act, but for delights shall he be blessed, by the favour of the luxurious, who amid banquets wish well to those who feed them. *He will praise thee when thou shalt do well to him.* [n: (AUG.)] Despised is the confession which prosperity makes; but that is of [0916C] great merit, which the force of grief takes not away.

VERS. 20.-- *Into the generations of his fathers. [n: (Ibid.)]* They who follow the worst fathers are to be damned by their fellowship.

*And man.* [n: (CASS.)] He repeats the deformity of man, that the evil man may rest from evil.



## PSALM XLIX.

VERS. 1.-- *A psalm of Asaph.* [n: (HIER.)] *Asaph,* in Latin a congregation; in Greek, a synagogue, which here speaks as faithful, which awaits Christ, through which the unbelieving Jews are the more confuted. [n: (CASS.)] *Asaph,* about to bring in the voice of God concerning the change of the sacrifices, first treats of both comings, by the first inviting, by the second terrifying. *God of gods.* [n: (CASS.)] Lest anyone think meanly [0916D] of the incarnation, etc., *as far as* by way of appellation, as the false gods, Jupiter, Venus. *He hath spoken, and hath called the earth.* [n: (AUG.)] By himself, by heralds. *From the rising of the sun unto the going down.* [n: (CASS.)] As if he should say, etc., *as far as: the law shall come forth from Sion, and the Word of the Lord from Jerusalem.*

VERS. 2.-- *Out of Sion, the loveliness of his beauty,* etc. [n: (AUG.)] The comeliness of the Gospel began, etc., *as far as* that they might be witnesses in Jerusalem and in the whole earth.

VERS. 3, 4.-- *Fire shall blaze in his sight and round about him. [n: (Ibid.)]* It shall go before him; let punishment therefore terrify those whom rewards do not invite, etc., *as far as* it shall accompany the wicked to hell. *A mighty tempest. He shall call heaven from above, and the earth.* [sic: *Tempesta*] [n: (CASS.)] Not a wind, etc., *as far as* first he called all in common, [0917A] then he shall call that he may separate. *To judge his people.* [n: (AUG.)] First he called the mingled from the rising of the sun unto the going down, but then that he may separate.

VERS. 5.-- *Gather ye together his saints unto him. [n: (Ibid.)]* The Prophet seeing things to come, etc., *as far as* it is said of the wicked, *of whom he hath not filled his hand. Who set his covenant above sacrifices. [n: (Ibid.)]*. The New Testament above the Old, etc., *as far as* who hold the works of charity of more worth than the sacrifices of cattle.

VERS. 6.-- *And the heavens shall declare his justice,* etc. [n: (Ibid.)] Already the heavens have declared to us this justice of God, etc., *as far as* and if now things are mingled, because *God is a just judge.*

VERS. 7.-- *Hear, O my people, etc.* [n: (CASS.)] The voice of Christ, that, the sacrifices of cattle being left, there may be made a sacrifice of praise. [n: (AUG.)] Or, he repeats under another word, Hear [0917B] as Israel, that is, as one seeing God, and *I will testify,* these things, namely, *I am thy God.*

*Thy God.* [n: (AUG.)] First he says simply: *I am God.* And afterwards he says: *I am thy God.* As if he should say: Even if I am not thy God, yet I am God. For to my own good I am God, but to thy evil I am not thy God. *I am.* [n: (CASS.)] To be is proper to the Deity. *Hear, O Israel. [n: (Ibid.)]* As if he should say: If thou art Israel, that is, if thou seest me, thou dost not neglect to hear. That God is general, thy God properly.

VERS. 8.-- *But the holocausts.* [n: (AUG.)] If the soul is inflamed, and seizes the members into its own use, that they may not serve concupiscence: whence it is commanded in Leviticus, that they offer the fat with the little kidneys, by which the inward [0917C] charity is signified.

VERS. 9.-- *I will not take,* etc. [n: (CASS.)] Even if thou shalt so do: *I will not take calves out of thy house,* as though I took the things signified, not those out of the house. *For mine are:* as if he should say, because they are not thine, but mine. *For mine are,* etc. [n: (AUG.)] Mine are the things which thou dost not possess, the untamed, namely: *The beasts of the woods, the cattle, the oxen,* etc. [n: (CASS.)] By these likenesses he figures the Church, etc., *as far as: The flesh of bullocks* he wills not in sacrifices, but in the poor.

VERS, 11.-- *I know all the fowls.* [n: (AUG.)] With him are all things, by a certain knowledge of ineffable wisdom.

VERS. 12.-- *If I should be hungry, I would not. [n: (Ibid.)]* If you give these things, perhaps you think that I hunger, which is madness?

[0917D]VERS. 13.-- *Shall I eat the flesh of bullocks?* Of old these things were sacrificed, not for this reason that God was delighted therein, but lest they should be offered to demons, and they were sacrificed to God only, in which there was some signification.

*Sacrifice to God.* [n: (CASS.)] He says not "to me," but of himself, as of another, as also afterwards: *But to the sinner God hath said, not I.*

VERS. 15.-- *Of tribulation.* [n: (AUG.)] Our tribulation is properly by the fear of salvation, not of carnal things.

VERS. 16.-- *But to the sinner. [n: (Ibid.)]* From whom he would have sacrifice, where to the sinner his own wickednesses are imputed

[0918A] [n: (CASS.)] These things he said to his own people, etc., *as far as* For reading is not denied to those who are to be corrected.

VERS. 17.-- *But thou hast hated. [n: (Ibid.)]* But thou sayest with the mouth, and deniest discipline in deed: if I correct, thou murmurest.

*The thief, thou didst run. [n: (Ibid.)]* In these he thunders against all vices; the whole from a part, or the thief of the sound understanding of Scripture. *With adulterers.* Who set down the false, by taking away the true.

VERS. 19.-- *Thy mouth hath abounded,* etc. [n: (Ibid.)] He accuses the conscience, and the tongue, etc., *as far as* he detracts from another not by chance, but of set purpose.

VERS. 20.-- *Sitting. [n: (Ibid.)]* What he does passing by or standing, he does not do with the will. *Brother. [n: (Ibid.)]* Every man. *And against thy mother's son,* of the Church, to whom we are joined by charity. *Thou didst lay a scandal,* heresies or other things by which the innocent may be caught.

[0918B] *Thou didst speak against.* [n: (AUG.)] While he detracts who seems learned, etc., *as far as* with the food of stronger doctrine.

*Like unto thee.* [n: (CASS.)] By consenting to the wicked.

*I will set against thy face.* [n: (AUG.)] What thou hast put behind thy back, I will set before thy face.

VERS. 22.-- *Let him snatch away.* [n: (CASS.)] God unto punishment, and *there be none to deliver.* When the devil snatches, God delivers; but when God snatches, none avails to set free.

VERS. 23.-- *The sacrifice of praise.* [n: (CASS.)] Not that which the wicked sing, *the sacrifice of praise,* not of animals. And this is the way to God, the ladder of Jacob, by which *the angels,* that is, the faithful, *ascend* to the praise of the divinity, and *descend* to the praise of the humanity. *There is the way by which,* etc. [n: (Ibid.)] In the sacrifice of praise. *The salvation of God.* [n: (AUG.)] Christ, who came with grace, whom he knows, who attributes anything [0918C] to another than to the grace of God.



## PSALM L.

VERS. 1.-- *Unto the end, a psalm.* [n: (CASS.)] When Nathan rebuked him, the king did not blush to confess his sins publicly, etc., *as far as* what avails with God for the penitent. *Of David.* [n: (AUG.)] He is set forth as an example, not of falling, but of rising again if thou hast fallen, that thou despair not. [n: (CASS.)] The first thing is entreaty, etc., *as far as* full remission of sins is granted.

VERS. 3.-- *Have mercy on me, O God. [n: (Ibid.)]* This is a penitential psalm, etc., *as far as* because he foresaw that through this psalm many would perish. *According to thy great mercy.* Of a grave sin one would have to despair, [0918D] were there not a great mercy [sic: *misericodia*]. A great one cannot be defined, how great it is. *Great mercy. [n: (Ibid.)]* A great mercy brought God down from heaven, etc., *as far as* when God is asked to pardon according to himself? *Of thy tender mercies,* etc. [n: (CASS.)] He asks through mercy and through tender mercy. And mercy is in the nature, tender mercy in the effect.

VERS. 4.-- *Wash me yet more.* [n: (CASS.)] What manner of blotting out he would have, he adds: *Cleanse me,* etc. [n: (HIER.)] So wash that thou make clean; for certain things are washed which nevertheless are not pure.

VERS. 5.-- *For iniquity.* [n: (AUG., CASS.)] Whose mercy he asks that he may pardon, because he knows him just, that he punishes sins, he adds: Punish thou not, because [0919A] I punish. *For my iniquity,* etc. *Against me.* [n: (AUG.)] He had his sin behind his back, when the king gave sentence against the rich man who had seized the poor man's ewe, forgetful of himself.

VERS. 6.-- *To thee only have I sinned.* [n: (HIER.)] For he alone punishes justly who has not in himself anything that may be reproved; and among men this is Christ alone. *To thee only.* [n: (CASS.)] Because a king is superior to all, he is to be punished by God alone. *Before thee.* Because he knows that he is everywhere; whence he accuses himself, because in his presence he did not blush to sin. *That thou mayest be justified,* etc. Whose words do not pass away, but are fulfilled. *And mayest overcome when thou art judged.* God is of such great justice [sic: *t antae*], etc., *as far as* that he may overcome as between himself and him, if they be judged.

*To thee only.* [n: (AUG.)] To Christ, who had no sin, [0919B] etc., *as far as* in that he had so grievously offended God. *And mayest overcome.* [n: (AUG.)] Or, thou art so just that thou overcomest the devil, and death, and the Jews, when thou art judged: and in the judgment wherein thou wast judged.

VERS. 7.-- *In iniquities.* [n: (CASS.)] The odium of the sin is lessened, etc., *as far as* he is accused of one and confesses all.

VERS. 8.-- *For behold,* etc. [n: (AUG.)] He so gives mercy that he keeps truth, so that not even the sins of him whom he pardons are unpunished. For he pardons the man who punishes himself. Mercy therefore it is that a man is set free; truth, that the sin is punished. *Truth,* etc. [n: (CASS.)] Because in confessing he spoke the truth, which God seeks above sacrifices, he entreats that help be brought him. *The uncertain and hidden things.* [n: (AUG.)] There are hidden things also, in that [0919C] to such men, etc., *as far as* whence he had the more to beware of sin. *The hidden things.* [n: (CASS.)] From the authority of him who makes them manifest, the confidence of washing and of being made white.

VERS. 9.-- *Thou shalt sprinkle me.* [n: (CASS.)] The second thing is confidence in the mercy of God, which is very profitable: lest despair creep in, which is graver than all sins. *With hyssop. [n: (Ibid.)]* Dipped in the blood of the victim, it was sprinkled over the body of the leper, signifying that the stains of sins are to be washed away by the blood of Christ. [n: (AUG.)] Hyssop is a lowly herb, etc., *as far as* in which there is a swelling, so humility, pride. *Above snow. [n: (Ibid.)]* Incomparably: whence also on the mountain the garment of Christ shone as snow.

VERS. 10.-- *To my hearing.* [n: (AUG.)] Mark the humble man. Let me hear thee, etc., *as far as* and because he is now humble, now [0919D] *the bones that were humbled rejoice.* [var: Vulg. Ps 50:10 *exsultabunt*, "shall rejoice"]

VERS. 11.-- *Turn away.* [n: (CASS.)] Made secure by that confidence, he asks the blotting out of all sins and a full restoration.

VERS. 12.-- *In my bowels. [n: (Ibid.)]* Whence the adultery went forth. In both parts, in soul and in body, he asks the remedy of healing, because in them he sinned. He so desires to be cleansed that he may be seen to wish to commit no such thing further.

VERS. 13.-- *Cast me not away from thy face.* [n: (CASS.)] Turn thy face from my sins, but not me from thy face, whence light and health may come.

VERS. 14.-- *With a principal spirit.* [n: (AUG.)] Some here note the Trinity in God, who is spirit, since [0920A] not body: by *a right spirit,* the Son; *the Holy Spirit,* by his own name; by *a principal spirit,* the Father. [n: (CASS.)] The spirit of prophecy, which is more precious to him than all things, he asks that its strength alone be not taken away, saying nothing of the rest.

VERS. 15.-- *I will teach. [n: (Ibid.)]* What he pays when restored: before, weeping; now he becomes a teacher.

VERS. 16.-- *From blood. [n: (Ibid.)]* Because carnal sins are many, etc., *as far as* which come from the corruption of flesh and blood. *Thy justice. [n: (Ibid.)]* He praises justice, when he ought to give thanks to mercy; but it belonged to justice also to receive one confessing; and these two things are always joined together in the judgment of the Lord.

VERS. 17.-- *Thy praise.* [n: (AUG.)] That thou didst create me; [0920B] that thou didst not forsake me when I sinned; that thou didst admonish me to confess; that thou didst cleanse me.

VERS. 19.-- *A sacrifice to God.* [n: (CASS.)] Which at that time was offered for expiation. *Afflicted [n: (Ibid.)]* A glad and wanton spirit binds a man to sin. There follows the promise of this sacrifice: *A contrite heart.*

VERS. 20.-- *Deal favourably. [n: (Ibid.)]* Now rejoicing, he prophesies of the building of Jerusalem, where is the sacrifice of justice. *The walls.* [n: (Au.)] Let the defences of our immortality be built up in faith, hope, and charity.

VERS. 21.-- *Then shalt thou accept a sacrifice. [n: (Ibid.)]* Now the sacrifice is for iniquity, etc., *as far as* then justice shall be the sacrifice, *oblations* the lesser saints, *holocausts,* the perfect.

[0920C] *Then shall they lay.* [n: (CASS.)] The priests; *calves,* the innocent, etc., *as far as* the faithful who suffer for thy name shall be acceptable to thee. *The altar.* [n: (AUG.)] The heavenly Jerusalem.

## PSALM LI

*Unto the end, for,* etc. [n: (HIER., AUG.)] Doeg betrayed David, etc., *as far as* This is not here a prayer for the wicked, but a prophecy of what shall come upon them.

VERS. 2.-- *Doeg.* [n: (CASS.)] The adversary of David signifies Antichrist, who shall stir up the whole world; who shall come by signs and wonders before the coming of the judgment, and seeking help from Saul, that is, from the devil, shall announce that David, that is, Christ, has come into the house of Achimelech, that is, to the Church [sic: *Eccesiam*], which is the kingdom [0920D] of the Father.

*Abimelech.* [n: (HIER.)] He says, in the Hebrew Achimelech, etc., *as far as* therefore Abimelech is the kingdom of my father, and it signifies the Jews. [var: Vulg. Ps 51:2 *Achimelech*, the form the gloss itself contrasts]

VERS. 3.-- *Why dost thou glory in malice,* etc. [n: (CASS.)] The first is an invective against Doeg, that he may strengthen the faithful against Antichrist and his accomplices.

VERS. 4.-- *It hath devised. [n: (Ibid.)]* A light mind, a headlong utterance: it does not think before it speaks, whence: *In the mouth of fools is their heart.* [sic: *stulorsorum*]

*As a razor,* etc. [n: (Ibid.)] Which, while it promises renewal, cuts: so does that deceitful one, namely Antichrist, cut blessedness, and slay.

VERS. 5.-- *Thou hast loved malice,* etc. [n: (CASS.)] He does not forsake [0921A] evil who loves it above kindness; and if kindness touches his mind, he flees.

VERS. 6.-- *A deceitful tongue. [n: (Ibid.)]* That is, deceitful words, the ministers of falsehood, of men who bear one thing in the heart and bring forth another in the mouth.

VERS. 7.-- *Therefore God.* [n: (CASS.)] Of the swift end of the wicked man. *And thy root out of the land of the living.* [n: (AUG.)] Our root is charity, etc., *as far as* for here is the dust which the wind drives from the face of the earth. [n: (Ibid.)] The root of evil is covetousness, which does not bring forth fruit in the land of the living. Or, he speaks of Antichrist, whose root is his ministers, who with him shall have nothing with the saints.

VERS. 8.-- *The just shall see. [n: (Ibid.)]* Derision for his casting down. *[n: (Ibid.)] The just shall see.* What shall befall the wicked, etc., *as far as* because more than by all other evils he shall deceive the faithful [0921B].

VERS. 10.-- *But I,* etc. [n: (Ibid.)] How the good man bears himself against him. [n: (CASS.)] Thus far the censure of that man; here the praise of the contrary part: behold a perfect demonstration. *A fruitful olive tree.* [n: (AUG.)] The people of God is an olive tree, from which the proud branches were broken off, that is, the Jews, and the wild olive of the Gentiles was grafted in. [n: (CASS.)] A fruitful olive tree, from whose shoot the Lord Christ came into flower, who was anointed with spiritual oil above his fellows.

*For ever and for ever and ever.* [n: (AUG.)] What he said *for ever,* he repeats *for ever and ever:* that by repeating he may confirm how firmly he is founded in the love of the kingdom of heaven.

[0921C]VERS. 11.-- *Thou hast done, and I will wait on thy name. [n: (Ibid.)]* The things foretold, namely, that I am an olive tree, that I have hoped in mercy for ever.



## PSALM LII.

VERS. 1.-- *Unto the end, for,* etc. [n: (CASS.)] The understanding of David the prophet, etc., *as far as* here of the coming of the judgment. [n: (AUG.)] Or, for mahalath, etc., *as far as* consolation at the end of the psalm.

*The fool hath said. [n: (Ibid.)]* A rebuke of the wicked.

*[n: (Ibid.)] The fool,* every one who lives ill, etc., *as far as* he says, there is no God.

VERS. 3.-- *God from.* [n: (CASS.)] Or from heaven, that is, through holy souls he gave warning, that he might see, etc. *If there be,* etc. [n: (Ibid.)] Or, if he does not understand, if at least he is one seeking [0921D] to understand God.

VERS. 4.-- *They are all gone aside. [n: (Ibid.)]* A fitting order. First they go aside; then they are become unprofitable; thence there is none that doth good.

VERS. 5.-- *Shall they not know. [n: (Ibid.)]* A threatening of the wicked by the end. [n: (AUG.)] He threatens them, etc., *as far as.* But deservedly this befalls them, because:

VERS. 6.-- *They have not called upon God. [n: (Ibid.)]* He consoles those who groan, lest they imitate the prosperous, because *they have not called upon God,* whence they shall quickly perish: a consolation of the faithful. *The bones of them that please men.* [sic: *qni*] [n: (CASS.)] The good displease themselves while they chastise the flesh: the wicked please.

*They have been confounded. [n: (Ibid.)]* That is, they shall be confounded, they shall be separated, [0922A] so that it may be read generally of all the wicked; but Israel shall be saved.

VERS. 7.-- *Who shall give out of,* etc. [n: (AUG.)] But salvation is to the Church in her travail.

## PSALM LIII.

VERS. 2.-- *The Ziphites.* [n: (HIER., AUG.)] They are interpreted the flourishing ones [sic: *Florente*], etc., *as far as* he admonishes us to praise God in adversities.

VERS. 3.-- *O God, in thy name.* [n: (AUG.)] He prays among the Ziphites. [n: (AUG., HIER.)] Here, save me in thy name; afterwards, I fear not to be judged in thy strength. [n: (AUG.)] O God, in thy name: the words of the Church hiding among the Ziphites, whose good is within and whose reward is hidden; others ask to be delivered by other means: I, in thy name.

VERS. 4.-- *O God, hear.* [n: (CASS.)] Again God, a name [0922B] of power; he repeats it that the help may not be slow. *Give ear,* [n: (AUG.)] The Ziphites, even if they hear these things, yet do not understand, who know nothing except things temporal.

VERS. 5.-- *Strangers.* [n: (AUG.)] Ziph was a hamlet which belonged to the tribe of Juda, whence the Ziphites.

VERS. 6.-- *For behold.* [n: (CASS.)] Diapsalmus. What God does for him and for the Ziphites, whence he pays his praises. *He helpeth.* [n: (AUG.)] Which they themselves know not, among whom I lie hid; but this, that he helps me, they would see, if they set God before their eyes.

VERS. 7.-- *Turn back the evils upon mine enemies. [n: (Ibid.)]* Or, turn back: he prays for such as are to be converted; and this is the victim which he pays for his deliverance. *In thy truth.* [n: (AUG.)] Or according to another reading: *In thy strength,* destroy those whom [0922C] in thy weakness thou hast borne with. *I will freely sacrifice. [n: (Ibid.)]* Because for his own sake, not for anything else that I might wish from him. He who praises for the sake of something else praises of necessity; if he had that thing, he would not praise. *But to me,* and the things he gave are pleasing for the giver's sake.



## PSALM LIV.

VERS. 1.-- *Unto the end, in songs.* [n: (AUG.)] *Understanding.* The Church ought with great care to understand, etc., *as far as* who for the troublesomeness of the citizens fled into the wilderness.

VERS. 2.-- *Hear, O God, my prayer.* [n: (CASS.)] A prayer sent before against evils, or against the waves of the world. *Despise not my supplication. [n: (Ibid.)]* For if God gives temporal things to one who asks, his prayer is nevertheless not acceptable to him, because it lacks its due end.

[0922D]VERS. 3, 4.-- *In my exercise,* etc. [n: (AUG.)] Every wicked man lives either that he may be corrected, or that through him a good man may be exercised. *I was troubled at the voice of the enemy.* [n: (AUG.)] In the very battle, while he wishes to love his enemies too, and, as the warfare grows, begins to hate them, and so begins to be sunk in the waves. [n: (HIER.)] Troubled. Or, I was troubled at the voice of the enemy, who called me a Samaritan and one having a devil.

VERS. 5.-- *My heart is troubled.* [n: (CASS.)] By the law of the flesh the mind is disturbed within me, it does not go forth from me.

VERS. 6.-- *Fear and trembling. [n: (Ibid.)]* It is the property of death that we first fear it, whence we tremble. *Darkness hath covered me.* [n: (HIER.)] *The darkness* is wicked men, who persecute the Church, [0923A] and who cover it over: he that hates his brother is in darkness.

VERS. 7.-- *Who will give me wings as?* [n: (AUG.)] But in this weakness what does he say? *Who will give me wings?* He wishes to be parted for a time from so many scandals, lest, being weak, he should increase his sins by remaining among them. *As of a dove,* etc. [n: (Ibid.)] Not as to a raven, etc., *as far as but it is necessary to remain for your sakes,* and so the merit is from the desire.

[n: (CASS.)] The dove. Or, the dove that was sent out, finding muddy places, returned to the ark; so Christ, finding the muddy hearts of the Jews, flew away to the Gentiles.

VERS. 8.-- *Lo, I have gone far off.* [n: (CAS.)] He touches on this, that Christ avoided his persecutors, to take away the occasion of their anger, and remained praying in the desert places: wherein [0923B] he taught us to pray and be still in the secret of the conscience, when we are pressed by the wicked. *And I remained in the wilderness,* of the Gentiles; whence: *Let the desert [sic: *disertum*] rejoice, and flourish as the lily.* [var: Vulg. Isa 35:1 *Laetabitur deserta et invia, et exsultabit solitudo, et florebit quasi lilium*, "the desert shall rejoice"] And elsewhere: *Rejoice, thou barren, that bearest not [n: (Isai. XXXV. Ibid. XXXIV)]*.

VERS. 9.-- *I waited for him.* Diapsalmus. Now safe in the wilderness he hopes for the help of God, and calls down curses on his fellow citizens. [n: (CASS.)] I waited (that it may be read in the person of Christ) in the wilderness, not secure of myself, the protection of the Father *I looked for, who saved me from faintness of spirit,* that I might not fall nor yield to the wicked in the passion, *from the tempest* lest I should be angry.

VERS. 10.-- *Cast down.* [n: (CASS.)] Lest they fortify their thoughts by contriving. *Divide. [n: (Ibid.)]* Lest their unity be proud, lest their malice have its effect. *In the city.* This [0923C] is the city of which the Lord says by the prophet: *I am God and not man, and I do not enter the city.* [var: Vulg. Os 11:9 *Deus ego, et non homo; in medio tui sanctus, et non ingrediar civitatem*, "I will not enter the city"] Yet there is another, which God inhabits, of which it is said: *A city set upon a mountain cannot be hid.*

VERS. 11.-- *And labour in the midst thereof,* etc. [n: (AUG.)] Now labour is in the midst, because iniquity is there: but rest is in humility.

VERS. 12.-- *Usury,* etc. [n: (Ibid.)] Because he exacts more, to another's hurt. Or, whatever thing he has received, he does not let it go, that it may be let go to him.

VERS. 13.-- *For if mine enemy.* [n: (CASS.)] A reproach of Judas, while it is read in the person of Christ; or, thus far the Church has dealt with open enemies, now with hidden ones. *I would perchance have hidden myself from him,* etc. [n: (Ibid.)] Great patience would so give place [0923D] to words, that it would hide itself, that it might not see the adversary.

VERS. 14.-- *But thou, a man.* [n: (AUG.)] Once, because at some time thou gavest good counsel. *Of one mind.* [n: (CASS.)] Because among the Apostles he was a leader, sent before to the strongholds and villages. *Familiar,* because he knew the traitor beforehand, so that Christ speaks thus especially against Judas.

[n: (HIER.)] Or thus, generally against any Jewish man, etc., *as far as* the ordinances of the law, or the morsel. *Of one mind.* [n: (AUG.)] Thou who once didst admonish well; *Leader:* I made thee leader over my people. *Familiar,* of the same nation.

*My familiar. Who together. [n: (Ibid.)]* Thou who wast with me in the same Church.

[0924A]VERS.15.-- *Meats.* [n: (CAS.)] Bodily ones, which could be sweet to the wicked alone. The man of iniquity is wont to be softened by banquetings, whence his guilt is the heavier.

VERS. 16.-- *Let come. [n: (Ibid.)]* As above against the open enemies, so here he imprecates against the hidden. And lest the one man he was dealing with be thought to be meant, he makes the punishments common to the Jews, that is, he imprecates against the Jews in common with others.

[n: (AUG., HIER.)] *Let death come.* Here he touches the beginning of the schism, etc., *as far as* and Christ and the Church speak in one voice.

[n: (AUG.)] *Let come:* As upon those who were the beginning of the schism and in their pride separated themselves, a new death came; for the earth opened, and swallowed them alive. *Let them go down alive into hell. [n: (Ibid.)]* As the earth swallowed the leaders, etc., *as far as* so these, sinning knowingly, [0924B] go down. *Alive. [n: (Ibid.)]* Knowing that they perish, and yet, as they perish, being swallowed up by the gulf of earthly desire. *In their dwellings.* They are alien, not natural, but received as guests, as it were newcomers; or, first *in their dwellings,* that is, in secret, afterwards *in the midst,* that is, in the open.

*But I,* etc. [n: (CASS.)] He affirms what shall be done to him and what to them, proceeding by turns.

VERS. 18.-- *Evening and morning.* [n: (HIER.)] Because he saved, therefore evening, when he was taken; *morning,* when he was judged; *noonday,* when he was crucified. *I will tell,* the works that are past. *I will declare,* the powers and works which he shall do: and therefore he will hear, etc.

[n: (AUG.)] *Evening.* Of things past *I will tell,* etc., *as far as* against those who love not peace, because I have loved [0924C] peace.

[n: (CASS.)] To tell belongs to the evening, when he said all that Judas was contriving. *To declare* belongs to the morning, when he said to Pilate: *For this was I born, that I should give testimony to the truth.*

VERS. 19.-- *They draw near to me.* In name, not in the divine power; in body, not in mind; in number, not in merit. *Because they were with me among many.* [n: (HIER., AUG.)] *Because in many things, with me,* that is, in the baptism of the Gospel, in reading, in feast days and the like, but not in the few things, with me. In all the sacraments with me, but not in faith, hope and charity, without which all things are nothing.

[n: (AUG.)] Or, *in many things,* that is, among the chaff, etc., *as far as* so are they also with me in many things.

VERS. 20.-- *God shall hear,* etc. [n: (CASS.)] *At noonday,* [0924D] when he sent forth the Spirit. Behold how great mysteries in few words: or, *thou shalt save at morning, and at evening, and at noonday:* whence, being not ungrateful, *tell,* and *declare,* and *he himself will hear,* etc. *He shall humble them, he that is before the ages. [n: (Ibid.)]* From their pride, that they may believe. Or *he shall humble* them so as to take away their place and their nation. *For there is no change with them, and they have not feared God: he hath stretched out his hand in repaying,* etc. [n: (AUG.)] Because the mind of the faithless is unchangeable.

VERS. 21.-- *They have defiled:* [n: (CASS.)] because they defiled and were divided: the just are the more armed, the weak are set free, who have their care upon God.

VERS. 22.-- *Wrath. [n: (Ibid.)]* The vengeance of the judgment, etc., *as far as* he appropriates to his own. *The heart.* [n: (AUG.)] The secret will [0925A] of God, which is contained in the Scriptures. *His words are softened.* [n: (CASS.)] Or, this befalls them because they softened their words by flattery, which words are *darts* when they say: *He is guilty of death.* [n: (AUG.)] But if to thee, a child who art nourished with the milk of a lesser teaching, they are still hard, be not angry with the breasts of the Church; but cast [thy care] upon the Lord, not another — he it is who is a harbour; the other is a rock to a ship, not a harbour, to one tossed about in the world. *The same words,* etc. [n: (CASS.)] Because, being softened, they do not lose their power, but by them the evangelists are armed, and they strike the hearts of men to the love of peace, and set the weak free from the entangled questions of heretics.

VERS. 23.-- *Thy care. [n: (Ibid.)]* The care of the world, etc., *as far as: so the sadness of a man harms his heart [sic: *tristia*]. Wavering.* And if here, the words of wavering were: [0925B] *I was made sorrowful in my exercise,* etc.

VERS. 24.-- *But thou,* etc. [n: (AUG.)] These then shall not waver, etc., *as far as* Woe! to them that sin and do not repent. *Their days, but I,* etc. [n: (Ibid.)] Days of their own choosing, not appointed by God.



## PSALM LV.

VERS. 1.-- *Unto the end,* etc. [n: (AUG.)] *The people that is made far from the holy ones, in the inscription of a title:* [var: Vulg. Ps 55:1 *pro populo qui a sanctis longe factus est*, the perfect where Migne prints the present] whom the Jew contradicts, and every one who will not have Christ for king; and those also who afflict the Church: against whom this psalm strengthens the Church. *In the inscription of a title, to David himself, when they held him.* The inscription of the title, etc., *as far as* the persecutors who tread the Church as grapes. *The Allophyli.* The same as [0925C] *the people that was made far from the holy ones. Geth,* that is, in the winepress. We read of David, etc., *as far as* but, when it is pressed, it is not barren.

VERS. 2.-- *Have mercy on me, O God.* [n: (CASS.)] The Church prays, touching summarily on her tribulations, in which through hope in God she *ends* by declaring that she fears not, that is, at the end of the first part she says that she fears not. *Fighting against me he hath afflicted me,* etc. [n: (AUG.)] Man visibly, the devil invisibly, whence the tribulation is the more dangerous.

VERS. 4.-- *From the height.* [n: (CASS.)] *The height of the day,* the world, where is the swelling of pride: that the tribulation may be the graver, she says that she is trodden down by the proud. [n: (AUG.)] *From the height.* Another reading, etc., *as far as* he does not say: I will not fear, but, *I will hope* in thee. *Of the day. The Day,* is Christ, whose twelve hours are the twelve Apostles: whence he himself says: [0925D] *Are there not twelve hours in the day?* The *height of this day* his enemies cannot reach, whence in the passion of Christ: *There was darkness.*

VERS. 6.-- *All the day,* etc. [n: (CASS.)] She enumerates the evils, by the enduring of which the conversion of her enemies also comes about, whence again through hope in God she says that she fears not, as she had done before.

VERS. 7.-- *They shall dwell.* [n: (AUG.)] Or, some of those who are far from the holy ones, etc., *as far as* for if they say false things of thee, they do not take away heaven, nor do these men hurt thee.

VERS. 8.-- *For nothing. [n: (Ibid.)]* Because thou hast no labour from it, as though it were nothing to thee, because, even though they are past hope, yet thou healest them by a word; or, for no merits of theirs, but freely, out of thy mercy. *In thy wrath thou shalt break the peoples in pieces,* [0926A] while by the scourges of God they come to their senses, or while they foresee the wrath of God. *In thy wrath thou shalt break the peoples in pieces.* Because thou terrifiest by tribulations, etc., *as far as* and the wrath of consuming, whereby the wicked are punished unto destruction.

VERS. 9.-- *O God, I have declared my life to thee.* The life of man is threefold: of nature, whereby we live and breathe; of grace, whereby we live in Christ; of sin, which is passed in vices. Two are from God, because good, namely the life of nature and the life of grace: the third is from man, because evil. All these we ought to declare to God. [sic: *Ha*] [n: (CASS.)] *O God, my life. Thou shalt break them in pieces,* and I, for their instruction, declare my life beforehand, which was always evil. He declares his life to God who confesses his sin: not teaching God, but condemning himself, that he may be loosed. *Thou hast set my tears,* etc. [n: (AUG.)] Devout tears, which [0926B] plead for enemies: and therefore acceptable to God, as he himself promised.

VERS. 10.-- *In whatsoever day,* etc. [n: (CASS.)] Whether in boyhood, or in middle age, or at the end of life. Wonderful clemency, which waits for whatever time of our conversion, and whensoever it is asked, forgives! *Thou art my God, thou.* [n: (AUG.)] Though thou art the God of all, mine properly, who succourest me, whom I worship and love. Others are strangers.

VERS. 11.-- *In God I will praise the word. [n: (Ibid.)]* The word, that is, teaching and discourse; whatever I speak, I will praise in the Lord, that is, and I will attribute [cj: *et*; read *ei*, "to him"]: and if anything presses me, I have hoped, and therefore I shall not fear. [n: (AUG., HIER.)] Or, in God the Father the Word, that is the Son, because whatever is said of the Father and of the Son according to substance, is said of the Holy Spirit also. This is said of the power [0926C] of the Word, whereby it is eternal and consubstantial with the Father [sic: *consubtantiale*]; and afterwards, of the humanity, he adds: *In the Lord,* that is, in Christ, who is the Word made flesh. *I will praise the discourse,* etc., that is, the preachings by which he gave to the world a rule of living and salvation. These two things this man preaches, the power of the word and the humanity of Christ. *In God I will praise.* Or thus, that the Trinity of the nature stands personally in the three words which he sets down. *In God* the Father *I will praise the Word,* that is, the Son; *In the Lord,* that is, in the Holy Spirit, *I will praise the discourse,* that is, the Son.

VERS. 12.-- *They are in me.* Within, things which never depart from us; *Vows of praise* [var: Vulg. Ps 55:12 *vota tua, quae reddam, laudationes tibi*, "thy vows, which I will render, praises to thee"] which shall be rendered in eternity. [n: (CASS.)] *They are in me.* The Church, now free from the evils of the world, says that in the beatitude to come, or even here, [0926D] she will sing the praise of God.

VERS. 13.-- *Because thou hast delivered.* [n: (CASS.)] Or it may be taken thus of the future, etc., *as far as* where also she pleases God perfectly, whence he adds: *That I may please,* etc.

## PSALM LVI.

VERS. 1.-- *Unto the end.* [n: (AUG.)] A psalm of the resurrection and the passion, in brief. This the psalm does, *lest* thou, Pilate, or thou whosoever thou art, *shouldst corrupt David,* [var: Vulg. Ps 56:1 *ne disperdas*, "destroy not"] etc., by denying him king, because, being covered with flesh, he escaped the knowledge of the Jews [n: (Ibid.)] No title was inscribed to David, etc., *as far as* where Christ lay hid, unknown to the Jews.

VERS. 2.-- *Have mercy on me.* [n: (CASS.)] The prophet in the person of the Lord, because in him the Lord, as it were amid [0927A] evils, cries out and foretells deliverance. [n: (CASS.)] Christ prays according to that which is man, so humble concerning the passion that he might be thought to be one of the faithful crying out. *And in the shadow.* The voice of the body is joined to the head: and speaking in the person of the members and in his own, he will say how he can be delivered, *he sent his Word.*

VERS. 4.-- *He sent his Word from heaven.* [n: (AUG., HIER.)] Out of his own hidden essence, etc., *as far as* whom they thought they had put out. [var: Vulg. Ps 56:4 *Misit de caelo, et liberavit me*, without *Verbum*] *He sent from heaven.* [n: (HIER.)] Diapsalmus. Now he declares what he sent and from what he rescued them, and he speaks in the person of the members. *He hath delivered. [n: (Ibid.)]* Because *He hath sent his Word* is now certain, it is as though he already were who *hath delivered,* that is, hath delivered [cj: *liberavit*; read *liberabit*, "will deliver"]: and *he hath given,* that is, will give *for a reproach them that trod upon me. God hath sent.* [n: (AUG.)] His Word, in whom he dealt mercifully, in whom also he fulfilled the promises [0927B]. *Mercy,* because out of mercy he died for us. *Truth,* because, as he had foretold, he rose again, to justify us. *His mercy,* etc. [n: (HIER., AUG.)] For both are contained in him. For if there had been mercy alone, there would have been licence for us to sin; if truth alone, man could not have borne it.

VERS. 5.-- *I slept.* [n: (AUG.)] Easy to be roused out of a deep rest: *Troubled* by the passion, *I slept.* David did not sleep in the cave, but Christ in death. [n: (CASS.)] Troubled, according to the flesh, by the memory and the anguish of the passion, whence; *My soul is sorrowful,* etc. *And Jesus began to be weary and to fear. The sons of men.* The right order is this: the sons of men prepared snares; but he inserts in what manner they troubled him. *The tongue.* Those cries [0927C] Crucify, crucify, and the like, wrought death also, as a sharp sword [n: (Matth. XXVI; Marc. XIV)].

VERS. 6.-- *Be thou exalted. [n: (Ibid.)]* In the person of the prophet, the injuries being seen, and it being seen that in the resurrection the Jews were made empty, the man on the cross cries out rejoicing: *Be thou exalted, O God, above the heavens,* that he may judge the Jews on the earth. *Be thou exalted.* The Lord [speaks] the things above; the prophet indeed, but in the person of the Lord, because the Lord speaks in the prophet. Even when the prophet speaks out of his own person, the Lord speaks through him, who dictates [sic: *dicta*] to him. [n: (AUG.)] *Be thou exalted.* The words of Christ. But, O Father, etc., *as far as* because the whole world is made subject to him.

VERS. 7.-- *They prepared a snare.* The exaltation being interposed, he pursues the narration he had begun. *For the feet* [0927D] [n: (CASS.)] of the Lord are the rebukes of the wicked and the promises to the faithful, for which *they prepared deceits,* as in the matter of the adulteress. *They bowed down,* while he, having compassion on them, was made barren by them. *A pit before my face,* etc. When they said to his face: He is guilty of death.

VERS. 8.-- *Prepared. [n: (Ibid.)]* Diapsalmus. After the resurrection he promises praises. Or, prepared to give thanks (which he there shows) *I will sing.*

VERS. 9.-- *Arise, my glory. [n: (Ibid.)]* And therefore, O flesh, that art the cause of my glory, *Arise,* mightily, as it were a rejoicing together at a miracle to be wrought, being certain: whence, affirming the same thing, he repeats it. *Arise,* I say, because I am certain also of the hour, because *I will arise early. Arise, psaltery and harp.* [n: (AUG.)] The one [0928A] flesh of Christ rose again, etc., *as far as* the *psaltery and harp,* therefore, rose again, and give praise to the Lord.

VERS. 11.-- *To the heavens. [n: (Ibid.)]* Which are above the clouds, where is the seat of the angels, etc., *as far as* mercy ascends to the heavens.



## PSALM LVII.

VERS. 1.-- *Unto the end, destroy not, of David, in the inscription of a title,* etc. [n: (CASS.)] He warns that no one contradict the kingdom of Christ. The first thing is a reproach of the Jews, that we may not do the like.

VERS. 2.-- *If in very deed you speak justice.* [n: (AUG.)] It is easy for all to speak justice, etc., *as far as* because I am your king, as the title says: *Rightly.* The Jews so judged of Christ that they are proved to have spoken of him feignedly.

VERS. 3.-- *Your hands.* The hands follow the heart: [0928B] whatever thou willest and canst not do, God accounts as done. *They frame together.* Or they knit together, that is, joining one thing to another, etc., *as far as* whence with a scourge of little cords the Lord beat out those buying and selling in the temple.

VERS. 4.-- *Sinners are alienated from the womb.* Or, they are alienated from the womb of the Church, etc., *as far as* they are cast out as untimely births.

VERS. 5.-- *They have fury. [n: (Ibid.)]* The thing itself is not praised by Scripture, from whatever quarter a likeness is taken, as in the case of the unjust judge, who scarcely heard the widow that asked him. *Of the deaf one, and that stoppeth.* [n: (CASS.)] Not only do they not do, but they will not even hear. [n: (AUG.)] *The earth,* present things, in which man delights; *the tail,* things past. [sic: *canda*]

VERS. 6.-- *Of the sorcerer that charmeth. [n: (Ibid.)]* Or according to another reading: *Of the medicine compounded by a [0928C] wise man.* The medicine compounded, etc., *as far as* this therefore is the wise man by whom the medicine is compounded.

VERS. 7.-- *God shall break.* [n: (CASS.)] What their recompense is. [n: (AUG.)] Asps, the Jews, while they were attempting treacherous things; lions, while they clamoured for his death; of these are the *Grinders broken,* when Christ rose again. *Teeth.* Deceitful words. *In the mouth.* By their own words he shall take them. *The Lord shall break the grinders of the lions. They shall come to nothing:* The clamourings for death. [sic: *Iuclamationes*] *He shall break them,* lest they come to effect; which he expounds, *To nothing,* saying.

VERS. 8.-- *As water. [n: (Ibid.)]* They are compared also to torrents, because, sounding against the rocks of pride, they are sudden and headlong, but quickly pass away. To so many evils are they compared. *He hath bent his bow.* Vengeance, until [0928D] they be weakened within and without, and be taken away from the sight of God, sent into hell, and here in the present, from their own land.

VERS. 10.-- *Before your thorns. [n: (Ibid.)]* That is, before they understand their sins. *The bramble,* that is, sharp damnation: at the last the fire of vices swallows them up [sic: *ignia*], that is, devours them. *The bramble.* A kind of herb, soft at first, but which afterwards turns into thorns: so also sins delight for a while, but afterwards prick. *As living, so,* etc. Because the life of the ungodly is not true but false: they seem to live, and they do not live. *So in his wrath he swallows them up,* because God judges in tranquillity, or because it is not wrath in comparison with that which is to come.

VERS. 11.-- *The just shall rejoice when he shall see the revenge* [n: (CASS.)] The correcting of the just by the punishment of the wicked.

[0929A]VERS. 12.-- *And man shall say.* [n: (AUG.)] As it is done by the just judgment of God, etc., *as far as* and because fire swallows up the wicked, therefore there is *a God that judgeth them* on the earth.

## PSALM LVIII.

VERS. 1.-- *Unto the end, destroy not.* [n: (CASS.)] In vain does any man attempt to destroy what the divine authority so often forbids. *Destroy not.* [n: (AUG.)] When Pilate said: *What I have written, I have written,* etc., *as far as* and to have renown among all nations. [n: (CASS.)] He prays first to be delivered, ascending from the lowest things to the highest: he prays also for the salvation of the Gentiles. [n: (AUG.)] Here the whole Christ speaks, namely the head and the body.

VERS. 2.-- *Deliver me. [n: (Ibid.)]* While he who is without stain asks this, he shows what we must needs ask. *From my enemies.* [n: (CASS.)] There are four kinds of adversaries, [0929B] etc., *as far as* of these therefore he says: *Deliver me,* etc.

VERS. 4.-- *The mighty.* [n: (AUG.)] There are also those who presume upon their own justice, etc., *as far as* but *hoping in riches they have prevailed in their vanity.*

VERS. 5.-- *Iniquity.* [n: (CASS.)] To the ill-willing [cj: *Malivolenti*; read *malivolentiae*, "of ill-will"]: *sin,* of the deed.

VERS. 6.-- *Arise.* [n: (AUG.)] Because thou seemest to sleep, *To meet me.* As though I run to thee by deserving: and do thou run to meet me, that is, render according to my desert. *See. [n: (Ibid.)]* That is, cause it to be seen how I run to thee, etc., *as far as* after thou hast seen me die, thou believest me equal to the Father.

VERS. 7.-- *They shall be converted.* [n: (CASS.)] Diapsalmus. The salvation of the Gentiles that was prayed for is affirmed, and, for him who is yet to pray, the salvation of the Jews. *At evening,* that is, later, after the ascension and the sending of [0929C] the Spirit, etc., *as far as* I fast twice in the week. *They shall go round about.* [n: (AUG.)] They shall fortify the Churches against the assaults of heretics. And, behold, they shall speak openly.

VERS. 8.-- *And a sword.* That they may slay and eat. *For who hath heard?* As though none had: so shall all wonder, etc., *as far as* thou shalt bring to nothing, or convert. *A sword. [n: (Ibid.)]* This is the sword twice sharpened, cutting with either Testament, wherewith Peter slays the creeping things shown to him in the dish, when it was said to him: *Arise, Peter, slay and eat.* [var: Vulg. Act 10:13 *occide, et manduca*, "kill, and eat"]

VERS. 9.-- *Thou shalt laugh them to scorn unto nothing. [n: (Ibid.)]* Those who say, *Who hath heard?* Why? Because he will count it as nothing to convert all the Gentiles, because all the Gentiles are to be Christian; and you say, who hath heard?

[0929D]VERS. 10.-- *I will keep my strength to thee, for God is my protector, O God. [n: (Ibid.)]* Even though I could destroy the Jews — why have the mighty rushed in upon me? — [cj: *quid*; read *qui*, "who"], yet I permit them to live, keeping my strength for thee, that is, for thy disposing.

VERS. 12.-- *He hath shown. [n: (Ibid.)]* that is, how great a mercy he has toward me he has shown me in those toward whom he has none; whence the Apostle: *He endured the vessels of wrath, fitted, and unto destruction, with much patience, that he might make known his riches upon the vessels of mercy [n: (Rom. IX)]*. [var: Vulg. Rom 9:22–23 *sustinuit in multa patientia vasa irae, apta in interitum, ut ostenderet divitias gloriae suae*, "the riches of his glory"] *Slay them not.* It may be taken of the Jews, etc., *as far as* it is shown what mercy belongs to *those grafted in.* [n: (CASS.)] To one considering what is to be done concerning them, if they be not witnesses of the law and of the death of Christ. *Lest at any time they forget.* [0930A] [n: (AUG.)] Even though they have not just now forgotten, since they are not witnesses of the law and of the death of Christ. *In thy strength.* Because thou art strong, not they. *Bring them down,* there was a reproach there to all. Or, bring them down in that wherein they are evil, that the good may rise: as in Paul, because Paul would not rise, unless Saul first fell [n: (Act. IX)].

VERS. 13, 14.-- *The sin of their mouth. [n: (Ibid.)]* It is not them, but *the sin,* that thou art to slay, so that they may fear him whom they wished to destroy, now raised up again. [n: (CASS.)] *And let them be taken* when converted; because their deed, etc., *as far as* but pardon blotted out the crime. *In their pride.* [n: (AUG.)] Pride is the companion of envy: nor can it come about that a proud man should not envy: whence the devil also envies man. *And of cursing.* Or of the curse, which you thought a curse, etc., *as far as* because in these things they were caught. [0930B] *They shall be declared in consummation.* Or, consummations shall be declared, that is, it shall be preached by the Apostles: Where the sin of exercise and of lying abounded, the grace of perfection did more abound; and it belonged to the medicine of man's humility and weakness. *In the wrath of consummation.* For there is a wrath, that is, a vengeance of God, *of consummation,* because God sometimes takes vengeance in order to perfect; and there is one *of consuming,* as when he shall say: *Go, ye cursed, into everlasting fire. And they shall know.* [var: Vulg. Mt 25:41 *Discedite a me maledicti in ignem aeternum*, "Depart from me, ye cursed"] For before this the Jews seemed just to themselves: but it is shown to them that this is not so without Christ, who rules over Jacob: and, their pride laid aside, they know themselves equal to the Gentiles, because all have sinned.

VERS. 15.-- *They shall be converted at evening.* [n: (CASS.)] Diapsalmus. The salvation of the Jews is affirmed, and, both [0930C] walls being gathered in, thanks are given.

VERS. 16.-- *They shall murmur. [n: (Ibid.)]* Or, *they shall murmur,* that is, they shall grieve that even now they are so far scattered; or, *they shall murmur* against the slothful, that is, they shall rebuke others who are unwilling to believe.

VERS. 17.-- *But I. [n: (Ibid.)]* Here this psalm is concluded, where, both walls being joined in the corner stone, the whole Church now rejoices. *And I will exalt.* When the times are past, when the night of the world shall have gone by, as Christ also rose in the morning: whence *In the evening weeping shall have place, and in the morning gladness.* The Apostles wept in the evening for him who was dead; in the morning they rejoiced at him rising again. *Mercy.* Because thou givest all things freely: though he has said many things, yet not all, in this one thing he has comprehended all.



## PSALM LIX.

[0930D]VERS. 1.-- *Unto the end, for these.* [n: (AUG.)] It is not read in the histories, etc., *as far as* a man unto the faith; earthly men, that they may live as heavenly.

[n: (BASIL. et THEOD.)] The history of the kings describes these things. But he foresees that the people will transgress, that they are to be led away captive, and that, repenting, they are to be brought back. Therefore he inscribed it: *For those who shall be changed. Doctrine.* [var: Vulg. Ps 59:1 *pro his qui immutabuntur … in doctrinam*, "for them that shall be changed … unto doctrine"] [n: (AUG.)] *They are changed* into doctrine, etc., *as far as Edom*, the earthly man, his life into a heavenly one. *And he smote.* When he smote Edom with the sword of the spirit, etc., *as far as* he teaches that blessed is the contrition through which he proves those who are to be changed for things eternal.

[0931A]VERS. 3.-- *O God, thou hast cast us off.* [n: (CASS.)] First are recounted the strokes inflicted for the avoiding of eternal punishments: *Thou hast cast off.* In Adam. *And thou hast destroyed* from immortality. Because *thou art angry* for our sins, and yet *hast had mercy,* through the Son, in such wise that thou hast moved us out of error: *And thou hast troubled,* [var: Vulg. Ps 59:4 *conturbasti*, "hast disturbed"] penitence: as therefore thou hast begun, complete it, heal.

VERS. 5.-- *Thou hast shewn.* [n: (AUG.)] For when a man has been changed for the better, etc., *as far as* to bear adversities even unto the end. *Hard things.* [n: (CASS.)] That is, the miseries of time, which are delightful to us through charity, and grievous through the infirmity of the flesh. *With wine,* that is, with tribulation, by which the sufferer is purged, as a wound is by wine: *with wine,* spiritual [sic: *Viuo*], whence not vices, but compunction.

VERS. 6.-- *A bow.* [n: (AUG.)] The further back it is drawn, [0931B] the more violently is the arrow carried forward, that is, the longer judgment is deferred, the more heavily will it come. *And that they may be delivered.* [var: Vulg. Ps 59:6 *ut liberentur*, a purpose clause, "that they may be delivered"] [n: (CASS.)] Diapsalmus. Concerning the deliverance which he prays for and which is affirmed, for the sake of which the strokes are welcome.

VERS. 7.-- *Thy right hand.* [n: (AUG.)] That we may stand at the right hand, etc., *as far as* concerning earthly things not save profitably.

VERS. 8.-- *God hath spoken. [n: (Ibid.)]* With this confidence I pray, which God promised in Christ, etc., *as far as* it is the voice of the Church: *I will rejoice. Sichem.* It is interpreted *shoulders,* that is, the burden of Christ, etc., *as far as* others may bear the yoke of Christ. *The vale of tabernacles:* because of the sheep of Jacob it signifies the Jews, who also are divided; because some believe, others do not, that is, of the Jews [0931C] I shall have not a few.

VERS. 9.-- *Manasses. [n: (Ibid.)]* Or, Manasses is every man who, having forgotten his former life, etc., *as far as: and thou shalt not remember the reproach of thy widowhood. Ephraim.* Fruit-bearing: and this is the *strength of the head,* that is, of Christ: for the dying grain has power in itself, that it might bring forth fruit.

VERS. 10.-- *Moab is the pot of my hope. [n: (Ibid.)]* Daughters ill-using their father, etc., *as far as* when charity burns against the pot of iniquity. [n: (CASS.)] *Moab is the pot of my hope.* Or by *Moab,* etc., *as far as* whence there is hope of life for me. [n: (HIER.)] *A pot,* whence: *I see a boiling pot,* etc., *as far as:* From him, therefore, the pot of tribulations boils up in the Church.

VERS. 11.-- *A fortified city.* [n: (CASS.)] The [0931D] Jerusalem that is to come. *Into Idumaea,* that is, even to earthly men, that, the number being filled, it might come together unto blessedness.

VERS. 12.-- *Thou wilt not go forth.* [n: (AUG.)] But thou wilt work within, and so he will lead thee better, because thus he is seen to fight not for the temporal life, but for the life to come.

## PSALM LX.

VERS. 3.-- *I cried while my heart was in anguish.* [n: (CASS.)] In anguish, etc., *as far as* there follows, of the cry heard, on the rock Christ. *Thou hast exalted me.* [n: (AUG.)] Out of the depth of vices. [n: (CASS.)] And thou hast led me exalted. [n: (AUG.)] *On a rock,* and therefore I am not overcome; *Thou hast exalted:* by the example of this man a great hope of being heard is afforded to others. *Thou hast led [n: (Ibid.)] me,* through thee *the way,* unto thee *the truth and the life.*

[0932A]VERS. 5.-- *I will dwell.* [n: (CASS.)] He trusts in the benefits of God, that against the perils of the world he may persevere safe in the tabernacle of God. *For ever.* Until the ages be accomplished; for although many are against him: *I shall be protected under the covering. I shall be protected under the covering of the wings.* Which neither burden, and turn away every hurt. For ever, not one man for ever, but the Church, which until the end of the world dwells on the earth, and is never rooted up by adversities.

VERS. 6.-- *For thou, my God, hast heard my prayer,* etc. [n: (Ibid.)] Diapsalma. He now affirms what has been heard concerning the inheritance possessed or to be possessed, whence he promises praises. *Thou hast given.* Nor will I say, thou wilt give: so certain am I, as though I were already in it.

VERS. 7.-- *Day upon day.* [n: (AUG.)] Whatever thou wilt, [0932B] thou sayest of eternity, etc., *as far as* but let the saints reign with him for ever. Or *thou wilt give an inheritance.* [n: (CASS.)] This one, namely, because it is upon the days of Christ, etc., *as far as* because that king abides for ever, so also do they. *Mercy and truth.* [n: (AUG.)] It is *mercy,* because he forgives sins not by merits but by goodness, and promises life. *Truth,* because he does not deceive, but renders what he promised. *Mercy* we ought to follow toward the weak and toward enemies. *Truth,* by not sinning, that he may be just. [n: (CASS.)] *Mercy and truth.* Here is the conclusion of the psalm, etc., *as far as: who shall seek them for him?* [var: Vulg. Ps 60:8 *misericordiam et veritatem ejus quis requiret*, "who shall seek his mercy and truth"] [n: (AUG.)] That no one may seek to learn these things in books, and afterwards live to himself, not to him.



## PSALM LXI.

[0932C]VERS. 1.-- *Unto the end, for Idithun.* [n: (AUG.)] *Leaping over,* seeing so many things that meet him, he submits himself to God, who is higher than he; and to those beneath him he answers as from on high, by rebuking. *Leaping over,* he answers the envious, who wish to deceive.

VERS. 2.-- *Shall not. [n: (Ibid.)]* That is, *let not the foot of pride come to me. I shall not be moved any more,* that is, *and let not the hand of the sinner move me. Subject to God* always, desiring nothing besides him. It shall be indeed, because *from him is my salvation,* from the Father the Son, the author of salvation, because he is God also, lest he be thought less.

VERS. 4.-- *How long do ye rush in. [n: (Ibid.)]* Or, according to another reading: *How long do ye lay on,* and the sense is the same. For the wicked lay *upon a man,* that is, upon the Church, the burdens of sufferings: so that unto the end [0932D] every faithful man may fill up in his own body what is wanting of the sufferings of Christ. *All ye together.* [n: (CASS.)] Or, according to another reading: *All* of you, as though ye all kill that man, so far as concerns the man, not *all* one man, but the Church; but because God is my upholder I shall not be moved [cj: *quis*; read *quia*, "because"]. [n: (AUG.)] *How long,* from the blood of Abel, etc., *as far as* not, namely, that which is *the most high founder. Upon a man.* [n: (CASS.)] Power has been given you over the body, not over the soul. *Ye rush in.* Not lightly, but ye kill the innocent; ye think that he is without a defender, and that he easily falls, like a leaning wall, which would fall even if it were not pushed. *And as a fence,* which is a construction of stones without the binding of mortar; and lest by its very newness thou [0933A] shouldst think it firm, he adds *pushed,* [var: Vulg. Ps 61:4 *depulsae*, "thrust down"] or, ye must not rush in, because ye kill your own selves, all of you, even those who consent.

VERS. 5.-- *The price.* [n: (CASS.)] Christ, that he should not be worshipped, or, to take away blessedness. *They have thought.* [n: (AUG.)] Note the diverse kinds of enemies, etc., *as far as* of her the prophet says: *Glorious things are said of thee, O city.
