I ran in thirst.* For although he always drinks them, yet he always thirsts beyond. *With their mouth.* Because they are not able by open evils, they attempt by soft ones.

VERS. 6.-- *But yet to God,* etc. [n: (CASS.)] Diapsalma. Again he exhorts himself, that is, his own soul, to be made subject, and admonishes the rest. *My soul is subject.* [var: Vulg. Ps 61:6 *Verumtamen Deo subjecta esto, anima mea*, an imperative, "be thou subject to God, O my soul"] This petition fixes strength in our mind.

VERS. 8.-- *In God is my salvation. [n: (Ibid.)]* Then [0933B] safe and glorious in him: but now what? He himself is the God of my help.

VERS. 9.-- *Hope ye. [n: (Ibid.)]* His own faith being published abroad, he admonishes all who labour under superstitions to hope in the Lord. *Pour out.* [n: (AUG.)] Through tears the heart is poured out, etc., *as far as:* Whence, *Prick the eye, and it produces a tear; prick the heart, and it produces knowledge [n: (Eccl. XXII)]*. [var: Vulg. Sir 22:24 *Pungens oculum deducit lacrimas, et qui pungit cor profert sensum*, "he who pricks the eye draws out tears, and he who pricks the heart brings forth understanding"] *Hearts,* etc., evil thoughts.

VERS. 10.-- *But yet vain. [n: (HIER.)]* Or, join it thus, God is our helper; and although he so works with us, yet the sons of Adam are vain and lying in the balance, perverting the Scriptures, by which they may prove false things. [n: (CASS.)] Diapsalmus. He reproaches those who go astray, and admonishes them to trust in God, not in things that perish, which is what the psalm intends. *Lying in the balances.* Who with unjust weights [0933C] deceive buyers, or wish to seem just, though they are deceitful. *In the balances.* In the scale of justice, whereby they deceive the more easily. *Unto the same thing,* whereby they may be believed fair.

VERS. 11.-- *Hope ye not in.* [n: (AUG.)] As if he should say: Neither hope ye, nor desire ye, the things which, when gathered, are snatched away by others. *Do not,* thirsting he turns himself to them. *Do not. Rapines.* The prey which thou wilt seize is in a trap, thou holdest and art held; thou holdest, another's goods that is, and art held by the devil. O ruinous gains! thou findest money, thou losest justice. *Let them abound. Set not your heart.* [n: (CASS.)] He does not say: Do not possess, for he does not condemn riches, whereby we purchase heaven; but the heart set upon them, which does not spend but hoards.

VERS. 12.-- *He hath spoken once.* [n: (AUG.)] *Set not your heart upon them.* Why? Because the *power* of judging belongs to the Son of God, [0933D] etc., *as far as* nor let mercy be despaired of on account of that power. *Power belongeth to God:* there is no power to any man save from God who is just, although his equity lie hidden from thee.

## PSALM LXII.

*A psalm of David.* [n: (CASS.)] He speaks of the Church, which in the drought of the world thirsts, and seeks after mercy, until she lay hold of things eternal, teaching men to converse well in the midst of a depraved nation. [n: (AUG.)] A psalm in the person of the head and of the members; his voice is ours, and ours is his.

VERS. 2.-- *O God, my God, to thee from the light,* etc. [n: (Ibid.)] How she fares in the desert, namely the bride, she desires to see God, whom she also praises, and she wishes to do this [0934A] the more. *God. [n: (Ibid.)]* Common, *my God* in a somewhat more particular way; see the affection of devout entreaty through this doubling. *From the light.* Or at daybreak, when Christ rose again, that the Church may praise him then, when by the example of his own resurrection he quickened her to rise. *I watch,* even while I sleep, not loving the things that are of the world. [n: (CASS.)] And I thirst in mind and in body. *From the light.* [n: (AUG.)] For thou wouldst not watch; unless the light arose, which should rouse thee. *My soul hath thirsted for thee:* and here the flesh for God, while it seeks from him the things needful here.

VERS. 3.-- *Desert. [n: (Ibid.)]* Where none of the saints dwells. *Trackless,* because there is no way by which thou mayest go out thence. *Waterless,* because neither, while thou tarriest where thou art, is there water there. But God here made a way, Christ, and sent the water [0934B] of his Word, filling the preachers with the Holy Ghost: *That there might be made in them a fountain of water springing up into life everlasting [n: (Joan. IV)]*. [var: Vulg. Jo 4:14 *fiet in eo fons aquae salientis in vitam aeternam*, "shall become in him a fountain", indicative and singular] *So in the sanctuary have I appeared to thee, that I might see,* etc. [n: (AUG.)] He to whom God does not appear, let him be in holy desire, etc., *as far as* For the sun by its own light makes us to see, and so also does God.

VERS. 4.-- *Above lives. [n: (Ibid.)]* Of human life, of which there are many, as that of the soldier, of the husbandman and of the rest: better is what thou givest to the corrected than what the perverse choose. *My lips.* After the benefits of God: he promises praise. *Shall praise,* toward my neighbour, for whom also I am solicitous, because unless thy mercy went before me, I could not praise thee, and as with the lips, so let them bless in life. Or thus, *Because above lives.*

VERS. 5.-- *Thus will I bless. [n: (Ibid.)]* As thou hast granted, through [0934C] mercy, that I should praise thee, *so will I bless,* that I may ascribe to mercy the life in which I praise. *In thy name I will lift up my hands. [n: (Ibid.)]* He lifts up his hands who, persevering in good works, with hands stretched out in the figure of the cross, adores God [d: unde]; whence the Apostle: Lifting up pure hands in prayer, without anger and contention.

VERS. 6.-- *With fatness. [n: (Ibid.)]* In the inward parts. Fatness is in the flesh, that is, wisdom, which is in secret, and the strength of the spirit.

*I will lift up my hands. [n: (Ibid.)]* In Exodus. Moses, while he lifted up his hands to the Lord, etc., *as far as* he works for earthly things, he is overcome by Amalec.

VERS. 7.-- *If I have remembered.* [n: (CASS.)] He sets forth the good things and the evil of either party, etc., *as far as* save he who works in [0934D] Christ.

[n: (CASS.)] *In the mornings,* that is, in the light of Christ, as above, *from the light I watch unto thee,* that the son of light, that is, of Christ, may watch unto the author of light, unto God.

VERS. 8.-- *A helper,* etc. [n: (AUG.)] In the perils of Idumaea, etc., [sic: *atc.*] *as far as* because *it hath upheld me* from perils, *thy right hand,* that is, propitiation.

VERS. 10.-- *But they have sought my soul in vain.* But the king. In these two verses he compares the good things and the evil of the different parties.

*Into the lower parts of the earth.* [n: (CASS.)] Because they were not carried up to the heavens. Evil thoughts are swallowed up by the earth, because, born of the earth, they sink into the depths of the earth, as into mire.

VERS. 11.-- *They shall be the portions of foxes,* etc. [n: (CASS.)] [0935A] The beginning of the world, etc., *as far as* while by a perverse will they join themselves to the deceitful.

VERS. 12.-- *In God.* [n: (CASS.)] The Father, whence: *I am in the Father, and the Father in me. They swear,* not by him, lest he should command an oath, but in him, by promising him obedience.



## PSALM LXIII.

[n: (CASS.)] This psalm treats briefly [cj: *isto*; read *iste*], etc., *as far as* that in sufferings we may hope in the Lord. [n: (AUG.)] As our head did, so let us pray in tribulations.

VERS. 2.-- *Hear, O God, my prayer.* That thou mayest exalt, *and deliver,* because I entreat. *From the fear of the enemy.* [n: (CASS.)] But not from the fear of death.

VERS. 3.-- *Thou hast protected me.* [n: (AUG.)] This was done in the head, [0935B] which the members also experience: by this example the rest are made firm.

VERS. 4.-- *For they have whetted their tongues like a sword.* Or, those namely who are malicious and who work iniquity. *A bow.* Hidden snares, as through a disciple, which is a bitter thing, that a guest at table should be a betrayer. *Bitter.*

VERS. 6.-- *They will shoot suddenly. [n: (Ibid.)]* Treacherously, as at one who does not know, so it seems to them who believe that they deceive God. *They will not fear. [n: (Ibid.)]* O hard heart! to kill a man who raised the dead, and thereafter not to fear. *They have made firm.* [n: (CASS.)] By obstinacy, not by truth, their word. *He is guilty of death:* or, *It is expedient that one should die. For themselves. [n: (Ibid.)]* Not for the Lord, but for themselves, when they say: *His blood be upon us and upon our [0935C] children. That they might hide.* The way of the wicked; he who prepares a deceit thinks that he deceives.

VERS. 7.-- *They have searched out.* [n: (CASS.)] How they might accuse the just man, or how they might destroy him, lest the world should go after him. [n: (AUG.)] Searchings, that is, bitter and hidden counsels; but the more keenly they think, the more do they fall away from truth and equity, and are plunged into darkness: for because they do not see God, they think that they are not seen by God. *A man shall come.* [n: (CASS.)] For he who searches out unjust things, etc., *as far as* but it grows great in the heart of man. [n: (AUG.)] They searched out, but in vain, because of his own accord the man shall come to the passion. *To a deep heart. [n: (Ibid.)]* He had one, because he did this according to a deep counsel.

*The arrows of little ones are made.* [n: (CASS.)] He derides the enumerated [0935D] wickedness of the Jews, who were the more troubled when the resurrection was announced, while the just rejoiced.

VERS. 9.-- *Troubled. [n: (Ibid.)]* They were, who, seeking counsel for their own salvation, said: What *shall we do, men and brethren?*

VERS. 10.-- *And every man feared.* [n: (AUG.)] If a man fears, let not a man terrify him from proclaiming, as the Apostles did, whom the chief priests threatened, saying that they should not preach in the name of Jesus.

*And they understood his doings. [n: (Ibid.)]* Why did he suffer? why did he not come down from the cross? because he had to die and to rise again: which then was not understood.

VERS. 11.-- *The just shall rejoice in the Lord.* [n: (CASS.)] A conclusion from the things aforesaid, and the promise of the just is shown. [0936A] *All. [n: (Ibid.)]* Do not think that it is said of the apostolic men alone.



## PSALM LXIV.

*Unto the end.* [n: (AUG.)] Jeremias prophesied *(XXV, XXIX)* that after seventy years the people would return. Among the captives was Ezechiel and other prophets. When the seventy years were completed the temple was restored. It is a figure of us, who sigh from Babylon toward Jerusalem, from the world and from the body of sin toward heaven.

VERS. 1.-- *Of Jeremias.* [n: (AUG., HIER.)] Exalted; and it signifies those who say: *Our conversation is in heaven. Aggoeus,* festal. It signifies those who are rejoicing in hope, who in the remnants of their thoughts keep a feast day to the Lord; *Ezechiel,* the Lord strengthened me, and [0936B] it signifies those who are strengthened in the Lord; these prophesied the return to Jerusalem.

*Of the people of the transmigration.* [n: (AUG.)] That is, which passed over from heaven to earth, from perfection to misery and confusion. *When he began. [n: (Ibid.)]* [var: Vulg. Ps 64:1 *cum inciperent exire*, plural, "when they began to go out"] He begins to go out who begins to love; charity makes a citizen of Jerusalem, cupidity a citizen of Babylon: and so therefore, mingled in body, they are separated in desire. [n: (CASS.)] A consolation in exile; lest any despair: because it makes the return certain, and by whom it is, and what riches there are for those who return. He affirms the return, and prays.

VERS. 2.-- *It becometh thee.* Not the gods of the nations, and not elsewhere, save in contemplation, that is, in faith, and there out of good works *the vow shall be paid,* in the heavenly Jerusalem, the vow which those men used to pay to Mars after battles, and to the other [0936C] gods in this fashion. *Sion.* Sion is the heavenly country, the same is Jerusalem.

VERS. 3.-- *Hear my prayer.* [n: (CASS.)] He first does what he says is to be done by the rest, namely, *hear. All flesh.* [n: (AUG.)] That is, man, that is, out of every kind: poor, rich, men, women, learned, unlearned, boys, old men.

VERS. 4.-- *The words of the wicked.* [n: (CASS.)] In what hope dost thou pray this? Because *to thee all flesh shall come,* not the soul only, but the evil man also. It is necessary that thou hear. For the words of the wicked are the words of the devil and of our first parents Adam and Eve. *They have prevailed.* That is, they cast us into exile, or the pagans persuaded their own rites to their sons, but thou wilt be merciful in the sacrifice of Christ. [n: (AUG.)] *The words of the wicked,* that is, we have followed the evil rites and manners of our parents [0936D] (who, as in age, so in authority, surpass boys). *Thou wilt be merciful.* [n: (AUG.)] Propitiation is made through the sacrifice which the priest offered, Christ. The priest and the victim are therefore the same.

VERS. 5.-- *Blessed is he whom thou hast chosen,* etc. [n: (CASS.)] And blessed is every man whom thou hast chosen out of the mass of the lost. *You have not chosen me, but I you [n: (Joan. XV)]*, and thou hast taken him into thy lot, into eternal rest. [n: (AUG.)] Or thus, thou wilt be merciful through the blessed man: etc., *as far as* things which cannot be told. *In the good things.* [n: (CASS.)] Which things are good, holiness in oneself, equity toward one's neighbour. *The temple.* The Church, or the flesh of Christ.

VERS. 6.-- *Wonderful.* [n: (AUG.)] Not in outward things, like a house of earth, but in justice, which is fairer to the inward [0937A] eye and more lovely, and which we ought now to hunger for, that then we may be filled with it. *In equity.* [n: (CAS.)] When he shall render to each what is worthy, to whom [each] shall also say: *Hear us, thou who art the Saviour. The hope of all.* [var: Vulg. Ps 64:6 *Exaudi nos, Deus, salutaris noster*, "Hear us, O God our saviour"] He describes the praises of God, and his powers. *And in the sea afar off.* [n: (AUG.)] Therefore afar off, because in the sea. The sea is spoken of as a figure of the world, bitter with the falsehood of iniquities, and stormy with the tempests of temptations, where the fishes devour one another.

VERS. 7.-- *Preparing the mountains.* [n: (AUG.)] For fitting uses. *In thy strength.* Because through them he wrought miracles. How did he prepare them? Girded with power, that is, with Deity, because the humanity of the Word is girded about with majesty, so that what the man had not, the Deity might give. *Girded. [n: (Ibid.)]* Christ in the midst, girded on every side, because we all alike have him who [0937B] believe in him, but because faith is not from us, but from him, he is said to be girded with his own might.

VERS. 8.-- *Who troublest the depth of the sea.* Behold, he who through those prepared ones wrought it, troubling them unto penitence. The depth of the sea, not the surface, but hearts more deeply sunk, or the hearts of princes. These were troubled, when they saw both their gods and their hopes come to nothing. *The noise.* [n: (AUG.)] Sounding with contradiction, tossing with persecution, etc., *as far as* while they perceive that these prevail more than their own gods.

VERS. 9.-- *The morning and the evening.* [n: (AUG.)] The morning is prosperity, because the morning is glad after the sadness of the night. The evening is adversity; in these a man is tempted, but it is granted him to go out thence, lest he be caught by gains, he to whom riches are God: lest he be terrified by adversities, he for whom the Lord [0937C] suffered. *Thou wilt delight.* Thou wilt make them delightful even in this life. For if the going out itself does not delight a man, he does not labour to go out thence, but is caught by gains, he to whom the promise of Christ is not pleasing. And he is terrified by adversities, whom he does not delight who was willing to suffer for him, that he might make a going out for him.

VERS. 10.-- *Thou hast visited the earth and hast inebriated it.* Through preachers wilt thou do this, for within thou teachest those to whom they preach.

*A river.* Of the deep, for the receiving, it is filled with waters, with the gifts of the Holy Ghost, for thou hast prepared food. *The river of God.* [n: (CAS.)] The mercy of God is compared to a river in full flood, etc., *as far as* as it is disposed by him, without whom nothing is done there.

VERS. 11, 12.-- *Its streams.* The Apostles and others, who [0937D] drank of the river. *Inebriate them,* that they may suffice for themselves and for others. *Multiply,* that through successions preaching may not fail. *The crown of the year,* the whole world, where the Church is. *Its streams. [n: (Ibid.)] Thou hast prepared,* etc. And, O Lord, etc., *as far as* whereby the Word of God grows. *The crown of the year. [n: (Ibid.)]* Because the glory of victory belongs to him who conquers the devil. *Of thy bounty,* etc. [n: (AUG.)] Let no one glory in his own merits.

*Thy fields shall be filled.* [n: (CASS., AUG.)] Men, because of their evenness, that is, the just. *The hills,* because God raises up the humble *The desert:* all the Gentiles, to whom no prophet was sent.

VERS. 13.-- *The beautiful places of the wilderness shall grow fat.* When the Apostles came to them, through whom they were made comely.

[0938A]VERS. 14.-- *Valleys. [n: (Ibid.)]* Lowly peoples shall bring forth much fruit. *They shall cry out. [n: (Ibid.)]* [sic: *Clamabuut*] Not in blasphemy, but in a hymn, thence they abound with corn.

This psalm is sung at the funerals of the faithful, because it treats of the return from the Babylon of this world to the heavenly country, to which the saints, led forth from the flesh at their death, happily pass over, receiving the single robe, until in the resurrection they be glorified with the robe of a twofold beatitude.



## PSALM LXV.

*Unto the end.* [n: (AUG.)] The Jews from the justice of the law hoped for themselves temporal goods, and the resurrection, etc., *as far as* that for the faith of the Gentiles he would call them to the same hope of resurrection.

[0938B]VERS. 1.-- *Shout with joy to God, all the earth,* etc. [n: (CAS.)] First he admonishes all to rejoice over the resurrection of Christ, which gives life to all.

VERS. 3.-- *Give ye glory.* [n: (AUG.)] Not as the Jews, who give justice to their own merits, and envy the Gentiles their grace. *How terrible. [n: (Ibid.)]* If thou rejoicest, fear also, lest what has been given to the humble be taken away from the proud: as was done to the Jews. Do not therefore insult over the broken branches, but fear, lest perchance he spare not thee either. *Thy works, O Lord.* [n: (CAS.)] Briefly he calls the mysteries of the incarnation works, that God is man, that he suffered, died, and the like. *In the multitude of thy strength.* [n: (AUG.)] Here he hints at the resurrection, to which, the lying Jews being shut out, the Gentiles are admitted.

[n: (BEDA.)] The Lord rose again; many believed in him, etc., *as far as* they drank through grace. *They shall lie.* [0938C] They are works of the earth, which the enemies belie, against those who lie, *let all the earth adore,* etc. [n: (AUG.)] *They shall lie.* Enemies are wont to lie, etc., *as far as* let all the earth adore.

VERS. 5.-- *Come ye.* [n: (CAS.)] Diapsalma. He invites beginners to consider the works of God, that one faith may join those whom one reward awaits: where, setting forth those works, he plainly says that the resurrection pertains to the Gentiles [sic: *ressurrectionem*], the Jews being repressed.

VERS. 6.-- *Who turneth. [n: (Ibid.)]* The works in which he is terrible, and what they are, he subjoins: *Who turneth the sea.* In the river Jordan according to the letter, mystically in the passing world. *In the river. [n: (Ibid.)]* Although sins are forgiven, yet in the course of mortality they are still there, for immortality is not at once [sic: *immortalitai*].

[0938D] *In the river.* [n: (AUG.)] The river is the mortality of the world, where some come and others depart: but he who believes in Christ passes over on foot, humble in himself, not on horseback and proud. *There we shall rejoice. [n: (Ibid.)]* Or thus: The works are terrible, etc., *as far as* and in these Christians rejoice. *In him. [n: (Ibid.)]* When he shall be all in all, or in him who rules, in his own strength.

VERS. 7.-- *Let them not be exalted. [n: (Ibid.)]* On account of the law, as though more were owed to them. *For all have sinned,* etc. [n: (Rom. III.)]

VERS. 8.-- *Bless ye.* [n: (CAS.)] Diapsalma. He admonishes the Gentiles to bless God, who, although he tries them by tribulations, yet leads them to the kingdom; for these things seem to stand in the way of the promised resurrection. *Make ye heard.* [0939A] [n: (AUG.)] Openly, so that he who loves God loves his neighbour also. *The voice of his praise.* Which the Father speaks of the Son: *This is my Son.*

VERS. 9.-- *Who hath set my soul. [n: (Ibid.)]* Here he enumerates for what causes he ought to be preached.

VERS. -- *For thou hast proved us.* [n: (AUG.)] Thou hast not given us over unto commotion. Because thou hast proved us, thou hast not cast us headlong, thou hast proved us by adversities, by the fire of charity, which does not suffer us to feel torments, or, thou hast tried us by the fire of tribulation, etc.

VERS. 12.-- *A snare.* [n: (AUG.)] Not one that deceives, but one that proves. *We have passed over.* [n: (CAS.; AUG.)] Thou all these things, and we, etc., *as far as* this happens to us in temptations.

VERS. 13.-- *I will go in.* [n: (CAS.)] Behold what good things against [0939B] the evils that went before, that he may strengthen us. *In holocausts.* That is, that I may offer myself to thy sight, etc., *as far as* but he withdrew that which is dearer, himself.

VERS. 14.-- *They have distinguished.* [n: (AUG.)] He distinguishes who says that he himself is nothing, and God all things; and that he himself has need of God, not God of him. *And my mouth hath spoken.* Whom before all men I have declared to prevail above all other vows in my tribulation, not overcome by tribulation.

VERS. 15.-- *Marrowy holocausts.* Myself, with the marrow of inmost love. *With the burnt offering of rams. [n: (Ibid.)]* Or, with *the burnt offering,* which is prayer; and with *rams,* because the leaders of the flock pray most of all for the flock. *With the burnt offering.* [n: (HIER., AUG.)] With the prayer of the Apostles, because that pleases God which is done by their tradition. Or with the burning and destruction of brutishness and of resistance. [0939C] Oxen; stiffness of neck; *with he-goats,* wantonness; these vices being slain in me. *I will offer thee oxen.* [n: (AUG.)] If those who thresh are offered, yet they do not remain sinners: but healed through penance they are offered; and they not by themselves, but rather joined to the oxen, because they have made themselves friends of the mammon of iniquity.

VERS. 16.-- *Come ye, hear.* Diapsalma. All things now being set forth, he again admonishes not so much the untaught, but the devout, that by the example of his own deliverance they may trust the more in the Lord: blessing him for the hearing of his prayer. So do I, and O ye Gentiles, *come ye.*

VERS. 17.-- *To him I have cried with my mouth.* [n: (CAS.)] He sets forth how great things he did for his own soul, subjoining: *To him,* etc. This he did for my soul, that I should cry to him, while the Gentiles cried to idols: and what I speak without [0939D] I have within, and not iniquity, because thus is the purity of prayer, it is heard. *I have cried and exalted in my heart.* [var: Vulg. Ps 65:17 *et exaltavi sub lingua mea*, "and I extolled under my tongue"] [n: (AUG.)] That is, I have preached him publicly, and confessed him in secret. What I have in the mouth, this I have in the heart also: yet *I will offer marrowy holocausts.* By these two the Christian is made perfect.

VERS. 18.-- *Mine.* That is a man's own which is not perverse; he sets it forth with reason, that they may learn to labour first before they seek the reward.

VERS. 20.-- *My prayer,* etc. These two are joined together; if prayer is not suspended, neither is mercy deferred.

## PSALM LXVI.

*Unto the end.* [n: (CAS.)] After the canticle of the resurrection, the Prophet subjoins his own [0940A] entreaty, that the blessed may be led to the knowledge of God. He prophesies the blessing of all through the coming of Christ. [n: (AUG.)] Because in the preceding psalm he had admonished us to bless God, which we cannot do unless he first bless us; therefore he prays for this, to be blessed, saying: *May God have mercy on us and bless us,* etc.

VERS. 2.-- *May God have mercy. [n: (Ibid.)]* The blessing of God is first, which is as it were a rain watering the earth of our mind: thence we bless him, which is as it were the fruit; and in both it is we that grow, not he. Let him therefore who desires the rain of the Lord's blessing say: May God have mercy on us, etc. *May God have mercy on us and bless us.* [n: (CAS.)] But many who wish, etc., *as far as* and because Christ is known among the nations: [0940B] *Let them confess,* etc. *May he bless us. [n: (Ibid.)]* The Creator blesses the creature, by multiplying it, in number and in merit. The creature blesses the Creator, by praising him with heart, voice and work, and by ascribing to him every good thing that it has.

VERS. 4.-- *Let the peoples confess to thee, O God. [n: (Ibid.)]* After knowledge he exhorts them to confess, for direction and for judgment.

VERS. 5.-- *Let them be glad and rejoice.* [n: (AUG.)] Because, having now confessed, they do not fear the judgments to come, to whom Christ is an advocate, not a judge, *let them be glad.*

VERS. 6.-- *Let the peoples confess to thee, O God.* [n: (CAS.)] The same admonition, tending to another thing, because the earth has given her fruit, and although the beginning of the sense is the same, yet it tends to different things.

[0940C]VERS. 7.-- *The earth hath given her fruit.* [n: (HIER.)] To Mary, Christ, etc., *as far as:* But not she alone owes this, nay rather, *let him bless* also *us. God, our God. [n: (Ibid.)]* Three times, the Trinity, *him,* the unity. *And let them fear him.* [n: (CAS.)] Fear is asked for in the midst of benefits, that what is entrusted may be warily kept: the fear of God begins and completes; he who has learned to fear does not cease to love.



## PSALM LXVII.

VERS. 1.-- *Unto the end.* [n: (CAS.)] A psalm of David the prophet, concerning the exultation [sic: *ne*] of Christ and of the Church here and in time to come, that all may sing to God. When Christ was rising, as though the ark were lifted up, etc., *as far as* Moses is said to have used words like these.

VERS. 2.-- *Let God arise, and let them be scattered. [n: (Ibid.)]* [0940D] He confidently wishes for what he knows will be. *And let them that hate him flee.* [n: (HIER.)] Or it can be read of the penitent, etc., *as far as* that, when the fire of the Holy Spirit is applied by the tears of penitence, their hardness may be dissolved. *That hate him.* [n: (AUG.)] Let the obstinate be taken away from the presence of him who is everywhere present; a grave curse, that they should will and strive after what can in no wise come to pass.

VERS. 3.-- *As smoke faileth.* [n: (CAS.)] Smoke from a flame vanishes in its rising; so sinners bring forth smoky deeds out of the flame of wickedness, and by that very lifting up they fail. *As it melteth.* [n: (AUG.)] It can be said of the penitent; or, in the judgment the wicked will be taken away from the face of God: who will be a fire for the punishment of the ungodly, and a light for the just.

[0941A]VERS. 4.-- *Let them feast.* [n: (CASS., AUG.)] In the delights which are in the sight of God. *And let them be delighted,* this can delight them, that they are glad [sic: *qui*] because their gladness is not to be ended.

VERS. 5.-- *Sing ye to God.* [n: (AUG.)] He turns himself to those to whom he has given so great a hope, and exhorts them. *Who ascendeth.* The new life will not receive him unless the old has died; or, by rising again he overcame the fall of the body. *Upon the going down.* [n: (CAS.)] The going down of death befalls him upon whom the sun of justice rises. And lest thou doubt who this may be, because *the Lord is his name,* a name essential to him, whom the Jews slew as a common man. *In the sight.* The consideration of the reward to come mitigates hardships.

VERS. 6.-- *Of orphans.* [n: (AUG.)] Whose father, the world and the devil, is dead, and their mother, concupiscence. [0941B] *Widows,* for whom their husband, error, dies; in whom God dwells, whom he gathers, whom he draws, because out of widows and orphans he makes himself a temple, in which he is.

VERS. 7.-- *God who maketh to dwell.* [n: (HIER.)] In the Hebrew it stands: *But the Lord maketh monks to dwell in a house,* in whom sin does not dwell together. *Of one manner.* [n: (AUG.)] Of one mind, thinking one thing, etc., *as far as* not by merits, whence he adds: *Who bringeth out them that were bound. He bringeth out them that were bound.* [n: (CAS.)] Out of hell, the devil being bound. *Who provoke.* Blasphemers against God. Or, by persecuting, buried in tombs, in stinking carnal things, so that they neither will nor can walk. *They provoke.* [n: (AUG.)] By resisting justice, since they are altogether dead in dead works; but the bound do will to walk, and cannot; these are loosed by grace, those are raised up.

[0941C]VERS. 8.-- *O God, when thou didst go forth.* [n: (CAS.)] A Diapsalmus concerning the word of the Gospel, by the going forth of Christ. *O God, when thou didst go forth.* Truly thou bringest out, because his going forth was prefigured before Israel, when he appeared in the pillar of cloud and of fire in the desert, and filled them with manna. [n: (AUG.)] *A going forth,* when he appeared to men through his humanity, or when *he bringeth out them that were bound,* that is, raises them up. *He passed into the desert,* [var: Vulg. Ps 67:8 *cum pertransires in deserto*, "when thou didst pass over in the desert"] when he came to the Gentiles. [n: (BASIL.)] *Thou didst go forth.* As if he should say: when thou wast about to pass over that solitude which had not yet received the splendour of light, the earth indeed thou didst shake and stir [cj: *commovistis*; read *commovisti*, 2nd sg.], but from heaven thou didst distil the drops of thy grace.

VERS. 9.-- *The earth was moved.* Because then earthly men were moved at seeing such great wonders. *The heavens dropped down [0941D] at the face of God:* manna, when in the manner of a dripping manna came down from the face of God, whose presence was known by the pillar. [n: (AUG.)] Mount Sinai smoked at the face of God (according to another reading) when Moses received the law there.

VERS. 10.-- *A rain.* This is what the heavens dropped down, manna, *a voluntary rain* which would satisfy the will of that nation, greedy to eat. *Thou shalt set apart, O God, for thine inheritance.* Because he did not give to other nations what he then gave to the Jews. *It was weakened,* etc. For a time, while it did not believe in Christ. [n: (CAS.)] *He hath made it perfect.* [var: Vulg. Ps 67:10 *tu vero perfecisti eam*, "but thou hast made it perfect"] God made his inheritance perfect, when he admitted the Gentiles to fill it up. He adds whereby he made it perfect.

VERS. 11.-- *Living creatures.* That is, thy nations, when they [0942A] believe, dwell in the inheritance, that is, in the Church. He sets forth what the living creatures are. *Thou hast prepared:* he means the predestined, because from the origin of the world they were made ready. [n: (AUG.)] Or, *mount at the face of the God of Israel,* supply "dropped down", etc., *as far as* in whom grace superabounded. [n: (CAS.)] These things mystically, but according to the figure, etc., *as far as* he sends therefore to the mystery. [n: (AUG.)] Certain books do not have *mount,* but *at the face of the God of Sinai, of Israel;* that is, he who gave the law to the people of Israel: because he who gave the law through Moses, whereby he might terrify, gave also the blessing through the Apostles, whereby he might set free. *In sweetness* [n: (CAS.)] *Sweetness* is that whereby good is done, not from fear of punishment, but from delight in justice.

VERS. 12.-- *The Lord shall give the word.* Many powers to them that preach the word.

[0942B]VERS. 13.-- *The king of hosts.* [n: (HIER.)] Of the Apostles, or of the beloved. [n: (CAS.)] The Father is the king of the angels, over whom the Beloved himself also commands. [n: (AUG.)] *The king of hosts of the beloved.* Of the Son, that is, the Father; or, after the manner of the old Scripture, Christ is *the king of hosts;* of Christ, that is, of his own, as Moses did, "as the Lord commanded Moses." *Of the beloved, of the beloved.* [n: (CAS.)] The repetition of *of the beloved* is for commendation; but this repetition is not in all the books, yet whether it be there or not, this is the sense: *of the beloved* not only for other things, but also for dividing the spoils, by dividing which he made the Church beautiful.

VERS. 14.-- *If you sleep.* [n: (AUG.)] *To sleep among the midst of the lots* is to rest in the authority of the two Testaments and peaceably to acquiesce. *The wings of a dove covered with silver,* the preachers, by whose preaching [0942C] the glory of the Church is borne up to heaven. *Of a dove* [n: (AUG., CAS.)] The dove is the Church, etc., *as far as* by preaching it is borne up to heaven. *And the hinder parts.* [n: (AUG.)] Where the roots of the wings, etc., *as far as* the vigour of wisdom is charity. *Among the midst of the lots.* [n: (AUG.)] It would be of the Latin tongue "among the lots", etc., *as far as* "there shall be wings for you" is ambiguous.

VERS. 15.--[n: (CAS.)] A Diapsalmus, *while he distinguisheth,* concerning the gifts of the ascending Christ, who alone can do it, and no other. [n: (AUG.)] While by the grace of the Holy Spirit kings are distinguished, etc., *as far as* and those who believe in him are protected.

VERS. 17.-- *The mountain of God, a fat mountain.* [n: (AUG., CAS.)] He adds a commendation of the mountain, etc., *as far as* For this is the mountain which in Ezechiel wounds the prince [0942D] of Tyre. *It is well pleasing to God,* etc. [n: (AUG.)] *For the Lord shall inhabit it unto the end,* [var: Vulg. Ps 67:17 *etenim Dominus habitabit in finem*, without *eum*; the gloss construes the verb transitively] that is, even to the term of his majesty, which is an end without end, and the fullness of all things. *For the Lord hath dwelt.* None is such as he, because this mountain, namely the Lord, will inhabit those mountains until he lead them *unto the end,* that is, into himself, God to be contemplated. But the other mountains are the chariots by which God is carried into the whole world. *Chariots,* because they are of one mind, coupled together by charity, whom God guides to the ministry of his will, *by ten thousands,* by countless peoples; a multitude of saints.

*Unto the end.* Because he is personally united to him in whom dwelt *all the fullness of the Godhead bodily.*

[0943A]VERS. 18.-- *Thousands of them that rejoice.* [n: (AUG., CAS.)] It signifies a great multitude of saints, etc., *as far as* and therefore they rejoice. *Sinai in the holy place* [n: (CAS.)] It is the mountain on which the law was given. It is called a commandment in which the Lord rests, because in his holy commandments his presence is wont to be beheld. *In Sinai.* [n: (HIERON.)] Or, in Sinai the holy, that is, he who once gave the law on mount Sinai is himself the same in the holy one, that is, in Christ, who is the Holy of holies.

VERS. 19.-- *Thou hast ascended.* [n: (AUG.)] He who came down that we might be instructed. *On high.* Above all the heavens. *Thou hast led captivity captive.* [n: (CAS.)] Those formerly held captive among the dead: these are the spoils, the likeness of a triumph; *thou hast received gifts in men* from the Father, that is, men. Or *thou hast given,* according as thou art God, *gifts to men,* [0943B] which also is done in triumphs.

*Thou hast led captivity captive.* [n: (AUG.)] Whence they are called captives, because they were caught in Peter's nets and put under a light yoke: whence in this chariot there are thousands of them that rejoice, not of them that weep.

*Thou hast received.* Paul by apostolic authority, etc., *as far as: What you have done to one of the least, you have done to me.*

VERS. 20.-- *Daily. [n: (Ibid.)]* Because daily he takes captives captive until the end of the world. And because he leads that chariot *unto the end,* he adds *a prosperous journey. Journey.* The course of life, which is truly prosperous, since the leader is one who brings salvation.

VERS. 21.-- *Our God.* [n: (CAS.)] Diapsalma. He sets forth the workings which come about from the things said above: the shattering of enemies, the conversions of sinners; [0943C] martyrdoms from either sex. *Of saving.* [n: (AUG.)] He commends grace, because none is saved but through him; but lest thou say, Why then are we to die? bear it patiently, and be not indignant at death, because even of the Lord, who was without sin, the issue was none other than of death, yet by his own will, lest man should fear death. *The issue from death.* [n: (CAS.)] That is, the resurrection; do not complain of death, because Christ went out thence, and granted a going out thence. Lest thou believe the evils of the obstinate unpunished, he adds, "but yet the heads": pride, or the authors of the Jewish division, or of sedition, or of heresy.

VERS. 22.-- *The crown of the hair. [n: (Ibid.)]* The subtleties of empty questions, which seek such enmities of calumnies that they seem to scrutinize hairs.

VERS. 23.-- *From Basan.* [n: (AUG.)] That is, from confusion, [0943D] etc., *as far as* to those who were most desperate.

VERS. 24.-- *Thy foot. [n: (Ibid.)]* That is, that they who before were enemies may become *thy foot,* preaching even unto blood, that is, even unto death: and *dogs,* barking for God, *which eat of the crumbs,* etc. *Thy foot.* Those alone are to be taken up to the office of rule who preach to others what they perceive, who carry out with the hands of good works what they preach or teach with the mouth; whence in Leviticus: The mouth of the turtledove is twisted back to its armpits. [var: Vulg. Lev. 5:8 *retorquebit caput ejus ad pennulas*, "shall turn back its head to the little wings"]

*In blood.* [n: (BASIL.)] Since he shows God, etc., *as far as: and when I overcame them, my garments were sprinkled. Of the dogs.* [n: (AUG.)] In a great sacrament Gedeon was commanded to lead those who lapped water like dogs, [0944A] three hundred only and no more, in the sign of the cross, because of *thau,* which signifies three hundred. *From him.* As if thou shouldst ask whence so great a good, that they should be *dogs* of him they were enemies? [cj: *sint erant*; read *sint cujus erant*, "should be of him whose they were"] he says: *From him,* that is, by his grace, not from themselves.

VERS. 25.-- *They have seen thy goings, O God. [n: (Ibid.)]* [sic: *in gressus*] Or, thy steps have been seen, O God, by which thou camest into the world. This grace lay hidden in the Old Testament: now it is manifest, when the Son, being sent, is announced among the Gentiles. *Who.* God. *Is in the holy place,* in the body, though the faithless see it not, according to that in which he suffered. In this also, by suffering or by believing, the princes *went before* them, or they went before unto praise of thee.

VERS. 26.-- *Princes.* [n: (BASIL., EUTHYM.)] He calls the Apostles *Princes,* etc., *as far as* and by preaching [0944B] they followed after them. *In the midst.* The ministers set over the new churches of them that praise God, with the flesh tamed and the hide dried from the moisture of sin, and stretched out from the wrinkle of duplicity.



## PSALM LXVII. (cont.)

VERS. 27.-- *In the churches bless ye.* [n: (CAS.)] Diapsalma. He prays and affirms that this beginning may be enlarged and confirmed, and for its sake that the heretics also may be destroyed. [n: (AUG.)] *In the churches.* These are the young maidens playing on timbrels, lest anyone should think carnally of the dances of luxury. For the churches are young girls adorned with new grace. *In the churches,* not elsewhere, not in other conventicles. *From the fountains of Israel.* [n: (CAS.)] That you may preach unto their imitation, as is fitting: because they are the princes of faith, of hope and of charity, and through this Benjamin, that is, the son of the right hand.

[0944C]VERS. 28.-- *There Benjamin,* etc. *Princes,* etc. [n: (Ibid.)] By the names of the tribes he points to Christ and the Apostles. *There,* namely in the temple of Jerusalem, or in the Church. The interpretation of the names also suits the Church. [n: (CAS.)] Perhaps the Apostles were of these tribes [sic: *Apo-toli*], etc., *as far as: confession* in faith, *fortitude* in hope, *breadth* in charity. *In the height of mind,* [var: Vulg. Ps 67:28 *in mentis excessu*, "in ecstasy of mind"] or, as the Greek word properly stands, etc., *as far as* as a faithful and prudent steward, he divided.

VERS. 29.-- *Give command, O God, to thy strength. [n: (Ibid.)]* O God, make the Son known; as it were: what I announce as to come, do thou perfect. Or, *to thy strength,* that is, enjoin it upon thy strong ones, that they may announce thee. Or, O Father, commit thy work *to thy strength,* to the Son, or to the Holy Spirit. [n: (AUG.)] *For God commendeth his charity toward us, since, [0944D] when we were sinners, Christ died for us:* which he prays well and confidently. *Confirm this, O God, which thou hast wrought.* By making the beginning.

VERS. 30.-- *From thy temple in Jerusalem.* Which is free, our mother, which is the temple, from which is every best gift; from that temple, therefore. *The kings of the earth.* Or, particular ones shall offer thee gifts, that is, sacrifices of praise. But because the heretics clamour against this praise.

VERS. 31.-- *Rebuke the wild beasts of the reeds.* [n: (AUG.)] That is, those who are moved by every wind of doctrine. *That they may shut out them that are approved.* This is what the Apostle says: *That they who are approved may be made manifest [n: (I Cor. XI)]*; or, let the approved shut them out from among the cows. For the stable, the strong, and the grave have not wherewith to seduce: *In the mighty people I will praise [0945A] thee. Scatter thou.* Rebuke the wild beasts, and also scatter those that dissent. *The nations that will wars.* Heretics and others consenting to them, whom he calls nations because of the kinds of the diverse sects, lest a series of successions there confirm the error.

VERS. 32.-- *Out of Egypt, Ethiopia.* [sic: *Aegppto*] [n: (AUG.)] By the name of Egypt or of Ethiopia he signifies the faith of all nations, the whole from a part. He prophesies therefore that the heralds to come are not from the Jews alone, but from the Gentiles. [n: (HIER.)] Ethiopia, the Gentile world black with sins, hastens to give her hands to God. A likeness taken from the conquered, who, their arms laid down, give them to the victors, lest they perish. *His hand shall prevent. [n: (Ibid.)]* That is, his vengeance, through confession, lest, remaining in their sins, they be punished.

VERS. 33.-- *To God, kingdoms.* That *to God* may stand at the beginning [0945B] of the following verse. Or rather it stands at the end of the preceding verse, *to God.* And faith is commended (as if to say, a man is not justified by preceding works) which begins to work through love. Ethiopia therefore will believe God, and so *shall prevent* his works, that is, Ethiopia's, which in the Greek is not ambiguous — that is, by believing in God she shall prevent her own works. Or *his,* Egypt's, to God, that is, unto God. *Ye kingdoms of the earth, sing to God.* [n: (AUG.)] Having run through all the things which we now behold fulfilled, he exhorts to the praise of Christ, and foretells his coming that is to be.

*Sing ye psalms.* [n: (CASS.)] Diapsalma. *Sing ye psalms:* he will come who ascended, where he will make the saints perfect.

VERS. 34.-- *He ascendeth. [n: (Ibid.)]* according as he is man. For according as he is God, equal to the Father, [0945C] above the heaven of heaven, above the loftier creatures, so that he had nothing above himself. [n: (HIER., CAS.)] *The East.* The Word begotten of the Father. *And he ascended from the east* [var: Vulg. Ps 67:34 *ad orientem*, "to the east"; the gloss takes up that reading next] by the power of the Word.

*To the east.* He has expressed the place where he rose again and whence he ascended.

[n: (AUG.)] *To the east,* of our illumination. Or because Jerusalem is in the East. *To his voice. [n: (Ibid.)]* He who was lowly in the passion will give the voice of power in the common resurrection. *The voice of power.* [n: (CAS.)] The efficacy of raising up all the dead.

VERS. 35.-- *His magnificence and power.* [n: (AUG.)] Because then there will no longer be a mingling of the wicked, thence his magnificence over the true Israel will be evident. *In the clouds.* Because he will not come alone to the judgment, but [0945D] with the elders of the people, who are the clouds: and lest thou take them otherwise, he adds: *Wonderful in his saints.*

[n: (EUTHYM.)] By *clouds.* Here he understands the Apostles themselves [sic: *Aposlos*], as existing above, and standing high away from earthly affairs, and as it were full of spiritual water, which they drew from the depths of the Holy Spirit, that they might water the fields and plains of souls, that is, the believers themselves.

VERS. 36.-- *He himself will give power and strength to his people; blessed be God.* (CAS). He has promised great things, lest anyone, because of human infirmity, should despair of coming to such great rewards.



## PSALM LXVIII.

VERS. 1.-- *Unto the end.* [n: (AUG.)] Adam was changed from [0946A] the form of God for the worse, etc., *as far as* "to suffer", according to the Greeks. [n: (CAS.)] The fourth psalm, which treats more at large of the passion and the resurrection. The cause of the *change* is handled [cj: *commutationibus*; read *commutationis*], that is, the passion of Christ. Here Christ speaks, head and body. *Save me.* First he prays to be made safe, because he suffers many things — as touching himself, without cause — but what another had seized, he himself pays. This is the voice of the mustard seed despised, while it is buried in the garden, which grows into a great herb: this seed dark and altogether contemptible. So also Christ, of whom: *We saw him having no beauty nor comeliness,* but nevertheless he took up the face of the earth, being made a great mountain.

*Waters even unto my soul.* [n: (AUG.)] That is, the crowds, etc., *as far as* beyond which they have not who may do anything to them.

[0946B]VERS. 3.-- *In the mire.* [n: (AUG.)] Man was made of the slime of the earth, etc., *as far as* that is, the power of the flesh to escape the passion. *Substance.* Riches are so called, etc., *as far as* that is, I have not found in them what he made. *I am come.* Because willingly. *Into the deep.* The depth of a mad people.

VERS. 4.-- *I have laboured.* [n: (AUG.)] Long he cried: *Woe to you! Scribes and Pharisees,* so as to be hoarse as touching them, because he was not understood by them. *Crying out,* etc. [n: (CAS.)] He labours whose desire is not fulfilled. *My eyes.* Like the eyes of the two who did not know him on the way, in whom that which was within came to be also without, that is, the blindness which was made in the heart was also in the eyes. For they said: *We hoped that he would have redeemed Israel [n: (Luc. XXIV.)]* [AUG]. But he restored hope to them, etc., *as far [0946C] as* because they would have been lost. *While I hope.* [n: (CAS.)] The reason is added: I wait for mercy, I do not entreat power.

VERS. 5.-- *They are multiplied.* [n: (AUG.)] What before he said figuratively, now he says openly in the fact. *Above the hairs.* For an unlimited number; or, they are more than those which are for adornment. *I have seized.* They willed to seize the Godhead, and lost happiness: Adam seized, presuming, as the devil did, upon Godhead.

VERS. 6.-- *O God, thou knowest.* [n: (CAS.)] Because he pays for others, he prays that *they that wait,* the ancients, and *they that seek,* the others, namely the moderns, be not disappointed in him, since he bears such great things so evenly.

*Foolishness.* [n: (AUG.)] From the members. Or, *that which is foolish [0946D] of God is wiser than men.* What they mocked in me, who seemed to themselves wise, thou hast known why the mockery came to be, namely, such mockery and death too. [n: (CAS.)] *Foolishness.* That they who are ignorant of me who am God, these are they who are changed into wise and just men.

VERS. 7.-- *Let them not be ashamed. [n: (Ibid.)]* He sets forth what things he has paid; here he shows what he had said above concerning the members. [n: (AUG.)] *Let them not be ashamed,* that is, let it not be said to them: Where is your Christ? or, let the Church not be pressed so that her sons are ashamed. *That wait.* [n: (CAS.)] What the prophets and the just could not see with their eyes. Lest therefore it turn out otherwise than the Holy Spirit foretold through them, there follows: *the God of hosts,* [var: Vulg. Ps 68:7 *Domine virtutum*, "O Lord of hosts"] to whom nothing is impossible. *The God of Israel.* [n: (AUG.)] That they who are able to behold his Godhead [0947A] may not be led astray by his carnal infirmity.

VERS. 8.-- *Because for thy sake I have borne reproaches.* [n: (CAS.)] [var: Vulg. Ps 68:8 *opprobrium*, singular; the gloss argues from the many things gathered into one] He gathers into one the many things he endured, to stir up odium against the Jews; *reproach:* he casts out demons in Beelzebub, a wine-bibber, a glutton. [n: (Matth. XI.)]

*It hath covered.* This is wont to befall those torn in a good cause, who are wont to be made so much the sadder for a time as they know that wicked things are being fabricated against them.

VERS. 9.-- *A stranger.* Or a guest, who is received into a house for a time; not as a most dear brother, which I was.

VERS. 10.-- *The zeal of thy house hath eaten me up.* When with a cord he cast out them that sold. What follows upon this zeal he [0947B] subjoins: *And the reproaches of them that reproached thee are fallen upon me.* Because the Jews, being rebuked, cast their insults upon him like darts, as: *Is not this the son of Joseph? [n: (Luc. IV.)] Of them that reproach thee.* When they said: *Can God furnish a table in the wilderness? [n: (Psal. LXXVII.)] These are thy gods, O Israel [n: (Exod. XXXII)]*. Whence he now says that the same insults fall upon himself which they formerly cast as reproaches, that thou mayest see that they did this out of impious custom and not newly.

*They are fallen.* [n: (CAS.)]. Evils fall upon us when we cannot avoid them: *they are fallen,* therefore, that is, they took effect.

VERS. 11.-- *And I covered it in fasting,* etc. [n: (AUG.)] I chose rather to fast than to take gall and vinegar; [0947C] that is, the old and the bitter, who do not enter into his body. [n: (CAS.)] *And I covered.* I did not exercise power by avenging. *And it became,* which appeared in the spittings, *a reproach.* [n: (AUG.)] Of detractors and mockers.

VERS. 12.-- *And I put on.* [n: (AUG.)] I set flesh before them, against which they might rage, and I hid my Godhead. *Haircloth.* [n: (AUG., HIER.)] Mortal flesh, that of sin he might condemn sin.

*Haircloth,* mournful flesh, as when he wept over Jerusalem [n: (Luc. XIX)], and for Lazarus [n: (Joan. XI)].

*Into a parable.* [n: (AUG., HIER.)] A mocking one, so that, in cursing anyone, they would say: So may it befall as it did the crucified. Or, because I was teaching them in parables; whence: *To you it is given to know the mystery of the Word of God, but to the rest in [0947D] parables.* [var: Vulg. Lc 8:10 *mysterium regni Dei*, "the mystery of the kingdom of God"] And then they spoke against me.

VERS. 13.-- *They that sat.* [n: (CAS.)] They sit in the gate who mingle in the assemblies of men with busy curiosity. He says therefore that his passion was the talk of the Jews, so that none was excepted who was a stranger to speech of this kind; whence Cleophas says: *Art thou only a stranger in Jerusalem, and knowest not,* etc. [n: (Luc. LXXXIV)].

*In the gate.* Of the city: where judges are wont to sit to judge causes, that is, in public judgment.

[n: (HIERON.)] As the Scribes and Pharisees having the key of knowledge: but neither entering themselves, nor suffering others to enter.

*And they sang against me.* [n: (CAS.)] They spoke of me in public [0948A] assemblies, and at banquets they sang: my mockery was their psaltery. *Who drank wine.* [n: (AUG.)] Who are made drunk with temporal pleasure. Or, [n: (CAS.)] let them sing to God with approved work who drink spiritual wine.

VERS. 14.-- *But as for me.* [n: (CAS.)] Against the aforesaid evils prayer is set on his own part, that we may learn to oppose to wranglings not brawls but prayers. *A time of good pleasure.* [n: (AUG.)] Therefore now is *the time of good pleasure* and of reconciliation, through the incarnation, through the blood, through the death; then he sets forth how this time came about: *In the multitude of mercy. In truth.* He set down first concerning *mercy,* now he subjoins concerning *truth,* because *all ways are mercy and truth* [var: Vulg. Ps 24:10 *universae viae Domini*, "all the ways of the Lord"]: mercy, in forgiving sins; truth, in rendering what was promised.

[0948B]VERS. 15.-- *That I may not stick fast.* [n: (AUG.)] In mind; above he said he was fixed fast in body; and this is said from the infirmity of the members. *Out of the mire,* that is, out of miry desires. *Out of the deep waters.* [n: (CAS.)] The Jews are deep in the malignity of their counsel, turbulent with seditions.

VERS. 16.-- *The deep.* [n: (CAS.)] Of sins, which swallows down souls; whence: *The sinner, when he is come into the depth of sins, contemneth [n: (Prov. XVIII)]*. These dangers hang over his members. *Nor let it shut her mouth upon me,* etc. [n: (AUG.)] Even if thou hast fallen, the pit does not shut its mouth upon thee if thou dost not shut thy mouth; but confess and say: *Out of the depths he hath cried to thee, O Lord,* [var: Vulg. Ps 129:1 *clamavi*, "I have cried"] and thou shalt escape. It shuts upon him who contemns in the deep, from whom, being dead, as though he were not, confession perishes.

[0948C]VERS. 17.-- *Hear me, O Lord,* etc. [n: (CAS.)] The dangers of men being set forth, he prays with their voice: *Hear me, O Lord, for mercy is kind,* not because I have deserved it. *Kind.* [n: (AUG.)] Mercy is sweet to the afflicted, as bread to the hungry. Tribulation comes; God defers to succour, that he may stir up desire, and that the help may be sweet; and when this is done, there is now nothing for which he should defer.

VERS. 18.-- *From the child,* etc. [n: (AUG.)] any holy one, little, not proud; from whom the discipline of tribulation takes away pride.

VERS. 19.-- *Because of my enemies,* etc. [n: (AUG.)] That they may be confounded by my deliverance, etc., *as far as* whence Nabuchodonosor himself, being converted, proclaims God. *Because of my enemies.* [n: (ID.)] This unto the open deliverance, that the flesh may rise again, and, [0948D] ascending, may send the Spirit; whence enemies were converted, to whom it will profit nothing if thou deliver the soul alone.

VERS. 20.-- *Thou knowest my reproach.* [n: (ID.)] Because why these things are mine, thou knowest; they know not. And because they themselves are in thy sight, not knowing these things they will not be able to be confounded or corrected, unless thou plainly rescue me. And therefore he says: *Because of my enemies.* [n: (ID.)] *Reproach,* or shame, etc., *as far as* but by helping may perfect. *Reverence.* [n: (ID.)] Others [read] ignominy, as the buffets, the scourges, and that he was handed over to Pilate as a guilty man: these things he bore not for his own guilt, but for us, that in such things he might instruct us.

VERS. 21.-- *In thy sight.* [n: (CAS.)] He stirs up odium [0949A] against them, that they committed so great a crime with God present.

*My heart.* Because he endured of his own will, according to that: *With desire I have desired to eat this pasch with you.*

VERS. 21.-- *One that would grieve together with me, and there was none; and one that would comfort me, and I found none.* [n: (AUG.)] The disciples were grieved, and the women who wept over him, but not together with him. For they were grieved carnally over the mortal life which was to be changed by death, whereas they ought to have been grieved with him over the rest, who were slaying the physician — which no one then did.

VERS.--22.-- *And they gave me gall for my food, and in my thirst they gave me vinegar to drink.* [n: (AUG.)] That I found none at once grieving and comforting, this was to me [0949B] bitter gall by reason of sorrow.

*Vinegar,* etc. That is, sour, because of their oldness. It is read in the Gospel that gall was offered him, but in drink, not in food. Mystically therefore it is said, that is, upon the sweet food which he had taken with his disciples, which is the food of the unity of Christ, they cast gall: which is still done to this day, and the despiser of him that sits in heaven sins more grievously than he who crucified him that walked upon the earth. They give a bitter draught to drink who by living ill make scandal for the Church, as heretics do.

The Lord tasted, teaching us also to taste, that is, to bear such men: but he did not drink, because such men cannot be received into his body. Mystically therefore is this foretold, as it was also mystically done in the passion.

[0949C] [n: (CAS.)] *And they gave.* This is the consummation of the passion; when he had said: *I thirst,* they offered gall with vinegar, showing by this deed their most bitter manners.

VERS. 23.-- *Let their table become.* [n: (CAS.)] A prophecy against the adversaries. [n: (AUG.)] *Let their table become.* The table is Scripture: this the Jews had, while they cherished the commandment of God; after they were fasting from the law, this table was given to the Gentiles before their very eyes, that they might grieve the more bitterly.

*A snare.* That they who were binding others by the letter of the law might fall bound in it. *A recompense.* Because it was taken from them and given to the Gentiles. It was a recompense of evils, since, being unwilling to feast at it, they were shut out from it. *A stumbling block.* Because, forsaking the author of peace, they stir up for themselves brawls and depraved works.



## PSALM LXVIII. (cont.)

[0949D]VERS. 24.-- *Eyes.* [n: (AUG.)] The teachers who go before. *Let them be darkened,* that is, let them lose the light of knowledge. *And their back,* etc. That is, the lesser ones. For when they who go before lose the light of knowledge, those who follow are bowed down to bear the burdens of sins and to seek after earthly things; or, *the back,* the mind of the same. Always this concerns those who do not repent.

VERS. 25.-- *Pour out,* etc. [n: (CASS.)] That it may be abundant after the manner of a river.

*Let it take hold.* That it may not be permitted to escape.

VERS. 26.-- *Let their habitation become.* [n: (AUG.)] This now in the open, etc., *as far as* in which they grew proud against God. *In their tabernacles.* [n: (CAS.)] The temple and the royal palace. To this he applies *to dwell within,* which is done by the affection of a devout [0950A] mind; to houses, *to dwell,* which is done by bodily abiding.

VERS. 27.-- *Thou hast smitten.* [n: (CAS.)] Not by his own fault: thou hast smitten him by the passion, unto the salvation of the Gentiles; this they fulfilled with a cruel purpose.

*And upon the sorrow.* Because it was for their perdition.

*They have added.* To inflict death. Or, upon the sorrow of my wounds, that is, of those which the lance and the nails had fixed in me, they added the guards of the sepulchre.

VERS. 28.-- *Add thou iniquity upon iniquity,* etc.) [n: (AUG.)] Their iniquity was that they should kill a man, and upon this God added that they should kill his Son, so that, as the servants sent before, so also the heir himself.

[0950B]VERS. 29.-- *Let them be blotted out,* etc. [n: (ID.)] Because they think themselves written down, etc., *as far as* and that they shall stand at the right hand. *Out of the book of the living,* etc. [n: (CAS.)] The knowledge of God, which predestined and life, those whom he foreknew to be made conformable to the image of his Son. Yet it is not so to be taken, as though God writes anyone in this book whom he afterwards blots out: but according to the hope of those who think themselves written down.

VERS. 30.-- *I am poor,* etc. [n: (CAS.)] Here he sets forth the dispensation of the incarnation and the holiness of his purpose, saying: What is the end of the passion? *Sorrowful.* [n: (AUG.)] This according to the head: he sorrows for all: whence: *He himself hath borne our sorrows [n: (Isa. LIII)]*, which no other did. Therefore he says:

VERS. 32.-- *And it shall please.* [n: (CAS.)] The praise of a pure heart [0950C] pleases more than the slaying of cattle. *A young calf.* [n: (AUG.)] For sins a young calf was offered, which signified the newness that was to follow; which has its defence in its horns, the guard of its feet in its hoofs (by which it may tread down the rough things of the world); *bringing forth,* because the faithful man is daily increased by the power of God. [n: (AUG.)] The calf stirs up the earth with its hoofs; so he who does not contradict, but nevertheless has a base earthly relish, is to be stirred up with hoofs: and upon this calf my praise will be pleasing among the angels, where neither will there be an adversary to be winnowed, nor a sluggard to be stirred up out of the earth.

VERS. 33.-- *Let them see.* [n: (CAS.)] That the offering is now in the mind, not in things, where the poor man is made equal to the rich. And, O ye poor, do this nevertheless with your gaze upon God: *seek ye the Lord.* [var: Vulg. Ps 68:33 *quaerite Deum*, "seek ye God"] Or, that you may see, seek God, [0950D] the food of the mind. *The bound.* [n: (AUG.)] He who seeks beyond what is needful loads on fetters; but sufficient for the day is its own evil. *The bound.* [n: (ID.)] Bound by mortality, wherewith the Lord, being offended, put fetters on his servants: but from thence he hears them crying out.

VERS. 35.-- *Heaven, earth, sea.* [n: (CAS.)] According to the letter, that is, the heavenly things, the earthly, and the swimming things: these praise him, while from them the creator is praised. [n: (ID.)] It is the riches of a poor man to see created things, and thence to praise the creator. *Let them praise him,* etc. [n: (ID.)] What before he urged all together, now he urges one by one; or, let the heavenly things, the earthly, the swimming things praise him. And, that he may perfectly include all things, he adds: *All things that are in them:* [var: Vulg. Ps 68:35 *et omnia reptilia in eis*, "and all the creeping things in them"] thus every creature is invited to praise. [n: (HIER., CAS.)] As if he should say: *Let every [0951A] knee bow, of those in heaven, on earth, and under the earth [n: (Phil. II)]*. *And all the creeping things in them.* [n: (CAS.)] All monstrous kinds, sticking fast in earthly sins.

VERS. 36.-- *For God will save Sion,* etc. [n: (AUG.)] In the hope of the faithful people the building of the Church is brought to its end: whence every creature is urged to praise.

VERS. 37.-- *And they that love his name,* etc. [n: (CAS.)] Why go through them one by one? all who love thy name shall dwell there. [n: (AUG.)] These are the seed of the servants. For this verse expounds the preceding one.

## PSALM LXIX.

VERS. 1.-- *In remembrance.* [n: (CAS.)] Memory in two ways, etc., *as far as* and they take up the hope of the resurrection. [n: (AUG.)] The voice of those in tribulation for Christ, etc., [0951B] *as far as* first asking that they be helped, and that the wicked be put to shame.

VERS. 2.-- *O God, unto my help.* He urges that we glory in God, not in ourselves. [n: (CAS.)] Here every petition is an affirmation, whence tribulations are not to be feared. This little verse is said beforehand at the beginning of all the hours, because, when we are wont to pray, the devil strives most of all to tempt us, so that dying flies may spoil the sweetness of the ointment.

VERS. 3.-- *Let them be confounded.* [n: (CAS.)] To be confounded is to be troubled over one's perversity. *Let them be ashamed,* lest punishment, an offspring from justice, follow them. *That seek,* etc. [n: (AUG.)] The times in the Church are diverse: first there was the onset of persecutors, whence, *that seek;* now there remains the ill will of those who plot, whence, *that will.* [n: (ID.)]

[0951C]VERS. 4.-- *Let them be turned backward.* [n: (ID.)] There are two kinds of persecutors, etc., *as far as* he comes forth whole who is caught by neither.

VERS. 5.-- *Let all rejoice and be glad in thee.* [n: (CAS.)] He urges the good to be glad, whom, being needy, God helps and delivers. *Always.* Because he ought not ever to cease from his praise. *Let God be magnified.* [n: (CAS.)] [var: Vulg. Ps 69:5 *Magnificetur Dominus*, "Let the Lord be magnified"] Said after our manner, where one grows by praises; for it is not God, but we, who by praising make progress.

VERS. 6.-- *But I am needy.* [n: (CAS.)] What I exhort, I do, etc., *as far as* where he is rich, he ever goes about, desires and receives. *Delay not.* [n: (ID.)] That seems slow which is sought with great desire. [n: (AUG.)] Lest what thou art to come should seem to me too slow — which comes about through patience; and he who lacks it, to him it seems slow, and [0951D] he is turned aside from it: like Lot's wife, who became a pillar of salt, that she might season thee: for she is an example to thee, that thou look not back.



## PSALM LXX.

VERS. 1.-- *Unto the end.* [var: Vulg. Ps 70:1 has no *In finem*; the title begins *Psalmus David, filiorum Jonadab, et priorum captivorum*] [n: (AUG.)] Concerning the commendation of grace, etc., *as far as* grace is commended in almost every single syllable.

*Of the sons of Jonadab.* [n: (ID.)] To sons that are willing, etc., *as far as* who of themselves are evil.

[n: (CAS.)] Or thus: we are admonished in the title to have the devotion of the sons of Jonadab, etc., *as far as* but they, by their accustomed malice, remained in obstinacy.

VERS. 2.-- *In thee, O Lord, have I hoped.* The just man in general [0952A] speaks, from the beginning of the world unto its end: placing all his hope in God, he commends his grace, lest we presume upon false things in ourselves.

[n: (CAS.)] Or, the former captives are in the world, from which one can escape: the second in hell. Or, *of the former,* that is, of those who were taken by vices before they became sons of Christ. This whole psalm commends with the utmost earnestness the grace of God, which is given freely.

VERS. 3.-- *Be thou unto me a God, a protector, and a place of strength,* etc. Two things he promised, two others he renders.

VERS. 4.-- *Of the sinner.* [n: (AUG.)] There are two kinds of sinners: some received the law, as the Jews and the Christians; others did not, as the pagans.

VERS. 5.-- *From my youth.* [n: (CAS.)] That is, from my conversion, or from the time when I began to fight against the devil. [0952B] [n: (AUG.)] *From my youth,* from the time when I began to hope in thee, etc., *as far as* a young man fights, but falls, unless God be his hope.

VERS. 7.-- *In thee is my song.* [n: (CAS.)] It is a delight to me to be in thee. *Always.* That the benefits may be unbroken. *Always.* [n: (AUG.)] Both in the present, etc., *as far as* against which nothing stands.

VERS. 8.-- *Let my mouth be filled.* [n: (CAS.)] He has told the benefits; now he asks that he may be able to give thanks. For if God does not fill the mouth, desire does not burn. *All the day thy greatness.* [n: (AUG.)] That is, without intermission, etc., *as far as* under the name of greatness therefore he includes all things.

VERS. 9.-- *Cast me not off.* [n: (CAS.)] Concerning the grace to be given in the time of old age and of decrepitude. *Of old age.* [n: (AUG.)] [0952C] Old age was in Christ, when he was made a wonder, when he was made weak upon the cross and mocked; and it is called old age because he took it on from oldness, whence: *Our old man is crucified with him [n: (Rom. VI)]*.

VERS. 10.-- *For my enemies have said.* [n: (AUG.)] This is said of Christ, who among the hands of his enemies was made weak, and, once seized, as though he could do nothing. But here this is said of his members, because he had such things also from our person.

VERS. 11.-- *God hath forsaken him.* [n: (AUG.)] Whence elsewhere: *Why hast thou forsaken me?* that is, why do they think me forsaken, to their own hurt. *For if they had known it, they would never have crucified the Lord of glory.*

VERS. 12.-- *Depart not from me.* [n: (CAS.)] They think him far off while he delays; *depart not,* that he may expose [them] to graver things [0952D].

VERS. 13.-- *Let them be confounded,* etc. [n: (CAS.)] Because the things ordained of God are not changed. *That detract my soul.* Otherwise, that set my soul at strife. [n: (AUG.)] That is, provoking us to resist God, which is to wage war against God; as those do to whom God is not pleasing in their adversities, which is to hold him unjust. The soul is also set at strife if it hates those from whom it suffers. *That seek.* [n: (ID.)] As Job's wife did, who said: *Say somewhat against God, and die [n: (Job. VII)]*. [var: Vulg. Job 2:9 *Benedic Deo, et morere*, "Bless God, and die"] As also Tobias's wife says: *Where are thy justices? [n: (Tob. IV, 2.)]* [var: Vulg. Tob 2:22 *Manifeste vana facta est spes tua*, "Manifestly thy hope is become vain"] She said this that God might be displeasing to him.

VERS. 14.-- *And I will add.* [n: (AUG.)] The addition of praise is, after the creation of all things, the praise concerning the incarnation [0953A] and the resurrection, which the lines below teach. *All.* [n: (AUG.)] Because, though he be praised in all other things, not yet in this, that he raised up our flesh unto the eternal way [cj: *viam*; read *vitam*, "life"], which is the praise added for the resurrection of Christ. Or, all praise would be there, and nothing would be lacking to praise, if he condemned sinners, because it would be justice; but because thou deliverest even these: *I will add above all thy praise.*

VERS. 15.-- *My mouth shall show forth justice.* Christ; but lest thou take it otherwise, he adds: *Salvation.* Why? because it is not approved that any man is justified by the works of the law. And this is: *Because I have not known learning,* that is, the figures of the law, through which I might be made mighty. *All the day.* [n: (AUG.)] Or thus, etc., *as far as* let him serve justice. *Because I have not known learning.* [n: (ID.)] I will preach thy salvation all the day, etc., *as far as* because [0953B] in habit he was found as a man [n: (Philip. II)], and the dead man rises.

VERS. 17, 18.-- *Thou hast taught me.* [n: (CAS.)] Through books, or by inspiring even until now: through the spaces of the times doctrine has grown. *Wondrous things.* That out of an unjust man a just man is made. *Wondrous things.* [n: (AUG.)] What is more wonderful than to raise the dead? etc., *as far as* this is "from my youth" and "now". *Youth.* [n: (ID.)] Some mark four ages of the Church, others that only three are distinguished, signifying the same by *now* and *old age.* Whence the youth of the Church was when the courage of the martyrs did battle. [n: (CAS.)] But the time going forward is "now", when faith advances through the churches. [n: (AUG.)] Old age, that is, the heavy time of life, when the charity of many shall begin to grow cold, as the times of the world diminish. *Decrepitude.* [n: (CAS.)] The declining time, etc., [0953C] *as far as* against whom the Lord says: *I am with you even unto the consummation of the world,* etc. [n: (Matth. XXI)].-- *Until I show forth thy arm.* [n: (CAS.)] While through the incarnation he sets the captives free. And the justice of judgment, that is, of punishing, whereby by a just judgment he condemns the sinning man.

VERS. 19, 20.-- *Even unto the highest.* And lest thou think that man alone stands in need of grace, etc., *as far as* he holds them together in that which they are.

*Even unto the highest.* Because the man who is God sits at the right hand of the Father, reigning with him. *The great things thou hast done.* Both are wonderful: that man is redeemed through grace, who through justice had been condemned.

*O God, who is like to thee? How great troubles hast thou shown me, many and grievous, and turning thou hast brought me to life again, [0953D] and out of the depths of the earth,* etc. To what person what grace belongs — namely to the captive person: the twofold resurrection of mind and of body; whence he promises thanksgiving.

VERS. 22.-- *In the vessels of psalm.* [n: (CAS.)] In the quickening of the spirit, or in the contemplative life.

VERS. 23.-- *My soul which thou hast redeemed.* [n: (AUG.)] With those inward lips with which it silently cries out to God.

VERS. 24.-- *When they shall be confounded and put to shame.* Then is there place for true and secure exaltation, when it is not permitted to the ungodly to bear rule.



## PSALM LXXI.

VERS. 1.-- *Unto Solomon,* etc. [n: (AUG.)] The true peaceable one, etc., *as far as* he who is here commended according to [0954A] each nature. [n: (CAS.)] The fourth psalm concerning the two natures of Christ admonishes that we should believe Christ, true God and true man, to be king, and it is a prophecy about Christ.

VERS. 2.-- *O God, judgment.* [n: (CAS.)] Concerning the judgment, in wishing that it be given to the Son. *Thy justice,* etc. [n: (AUG.)] Note that repetitions of the same thing, or in other words, greatly commend the divine utterances, which are chiefly in the psalms: and in that kind of speech by which the affection of the mind is to be moved.

VERS. 3.-- *Mountains.* [n: (AUG.)] The Prophets, the Apostles, who by their height come near to heaven; *peace* to be announced to the faithful people, Christ. *And the hills.* Let them receive *justice,* that is, Christ, or *the mountains peace for the people:* and *hills* he says of both sorts of preachers, those going before and [0954B] those following. Or *let the mountains receive,* that is, the greater ones.

*Peace.* The word of reconciliation, namely the word of the Gospel, to this end, that they may announce it to the people.

*And the hills.* That is, the lesser ones; justice, that is, obedience, that they may obey the teaching of truth.

By saying *thine* and *thy own,* he shows that all things which are the Father's are the Son's also, and conversely.

VERS. 4.-- *He shall judge the poor of the people.* [n: (CAS.)] He treats the same matter concerning the same judgment, by affirming it. After the wish, by the power of prophecy, he says what things are to be furnished at the Lord's coming. [n: (AUG.)] Or according to another reading: *Justice shall judge the poor man.* But those codices which have [it] in the letter are the more approved. *The poor of the people.* That is, those who are poor among the people, in which the good and the bad are [0954C] mingled together. *He shall humble the calumniator.* For while he helps by his grace, etc., *as far as* he who strives to drag the innocent into guilt. [n: (HIERON., AUG.)] Or, *he shall humble,* and, the devil being conquered, *there shall remain with the sun* a brightness; that is, the Son with the Father, sitting at the Father's right hand for ever, that is *before the moon,* unto generation and generation.

VERS. 7.-- *He shall come down like rain,* etc. [n: (CAS.)] Behold who and of what sort is that king who is to judge. [n: (AUG.)] He alludes to the deed of Gedeon, etc., *as far as* and afterwards the Gentile world was rained upon, the fleece of the Jews being dry. [n: (CAS.)] Christ shall come down, etc., *as far as* who then, after such open likenesses, doubts of the Virgin's childbearing? *Into the fleece, and like drops.* [n: (AUG.)] *The fleece,* Judaea, which was to be despoiled of the authority of doctrine, as a sheep [0954D] of its fleece: or, because it withheld the rain of doctrine, which it was unwilling to preach to the Gentiles.

VERS. 8.-- *And he shall rule from sea to sea.* [n: (CAS.)] He shows what the breadth of the kingdom is. *From the river.* [n: (AUG.)] From the Jordan, where the voice was:. *This is my beloved Son.* Thence therefore his teaching began: thence the authority of the master is spread as far as the ends of the earth, the Gospel of the kingdom being preached. [n: (CAS.)] Thence the rule of baptism flowed out through the whole world. In few words he teaches great sacraments.

VERS. 10.-- *The kings of Tharsis and the islands shall offer presents.* [n: (CAS.)] The obstinate are called enemies, etc., *as far as* or, it can be taken historically of the kings who came to adore Christ.

[0955A]VERS. 12.-- *For he shall deliver the poor man from the mighty,* etc. [n: (CASS.)] He treats of his works, etc., *as far as* in the participation of justice.

VERS. 14.-- *From usuries.* [n: (AUG.)] *Usuries,* sins, etc., *as far as* by helping through grace to the doing of justice. Or, *from usuries,* concerning what is to come, that is, eternal punishments; and *iniquity,* that is, sin itself, for which the punishment was owed. *Name.* The Christian name. *Before him.* Because in his kingdom they shall live before him.

VERS. 15.-- *And he shall live, and to him shall be given of the gold,* etc. [n: (CAS.)] Concerning the honour paid to the king. *Of Arabia.* [n: (AUG.)] By Arabia, the Gentiles; by gold, wisdom, which excels among the teachings. *Of him always.* [n: (ID.)] By multiplying, or by obtaining, or concerning his institution, because he himself taught us to pray.

[0955B]VERS. 16.-- *A firmament.* [n: (AUG.)] It belongs to the Prophets, because what they foretold he fulfilled. *On the tops of the mountains.* [n: (ID.)] The authors of the Scriptures, the Prophets and Apostles; whom he strengthens, because all things foretold by them have been fulfilled in him.

*In the earth.* Because for the sake of those who are on the earth all things were written. Whence he himself also came into the earth, that he might fulfil them.
