*Above Libanus.* [n: (AUG.)] As Libanus brings forth tall cedars, so Christ brings forth saints to heaven. *Like hay.* [n: (CAS.)] It is not age that is compared to the old man, but greenness to the saints. The good, departing from this city of the present Church, shall flourish in that blessedness. [n: (AUG.)] In Genesis under the name of *hay* grain also is understood, etc., *as far as* and whatever [0955C] is held for great in this world shall be compared to the hay of the earth [sic: *corparabitur*].

VERS. 17.-- *Let his name be blessed,* etc. [n: (CAS.)] He admonishes all to speak praises to him, because all shall be blessed in him. *Before the sun.* [n: (ID.)] That is, before every creature, lest thou think the Son of God temporal. *All nations.* [n: (AUG.)] Lest thou take it of the twelve tribes, he adds *all nations.*

VERS. 18.-- *Blessed be the Lord God of Israel,* etc. [n: (AUG.)] He praises from his own part. Note the exclamatory blessing, with admonition and prophecy. *So be it, so be it,* etc. [n: (CAS.)] It is a magnificent thing, that he concludes thus: it belongs to one who wishes, to say it once; to one who desires exceedingly, to repeat it. As if he said: What thou hast commanded, Lord, so let it be done, let it be done, until what began from the river may reach as far as the ends of the earth. [n: (HIER.)] It is to be known that the seventy-[0955D]first psalm is the end of the second book: whence also at the end it has: *So be it, so be it,* for which in the Hebrew it is said: *Amen, amen.*

VERS. 20.-- *The praises have failed.* [n: (ID.)] This also is to be observed the more diligently, that that saying: *The hymns of David the son of Jesse have failed,* [var: Vulg. Ps 71:20 *laudes*, "praises"] belongs to the end of the seventy-first psalm. But that which follows: *A psalm of Asaph,* is the beginning of the next one.

A hymn has three things: the praise of God with a song: for unless it be sung joyfully, it is not a hymn: and he does not sing who does not love. If there be praise, and it is not of God, it is not a hymn: and if it be of God, and not with a song, it is not a hymn. Beyond his custom he adds: *the son of Jesse;* for David, a man, a certain son of Jesse, reigned in [0956A] Israel in the time of the Old Testament, in which the New was, but hidden: just as Christ also was in David and in Abraham, but hidden, as the fruit is in the root, yet does not appear. Now few of the Prophets knew both Christ and the New Testament, namely in secret, and they declared that it was to be revealed. In that time, therefore, for that kingdom in which the man, the son of Jesse, reigned, for carnal things of this sort a carnal people praised God. But that kingdom and the rest of this sort failing, the praise fails, not of the Son of God, but of *the son of Jesse.* And these things failing which God withdrew, that those things might be sought which lay hidden in them, *Asaph* speaks, that is, the Synagogue, but a faithful one, which piously worshipped God, although for the sake of earthly things, which it sought from God, not from demons, as the Gentiles did. But observing [0956B] blasphemers to abound in these things, for the sake of which it served, it began to waver, not knowing what lay hidden in these figures, as though there were not anything better which God might give: but admonished by these things withdrawn from it to seek those things which it does not have in common with the wicked, chastising itself with wonder, it says: *How good is Israel!* But to whom? *To the upright in heart.* [var: Vulg. Ps 72:1 *his qui recto sunt corde*, "to them that are of a right heart"] First therefore, describing the wickedness of the wicked, it says that it was jealous of their prosperity; yet corrected from that, and being corrected it corrects others.

[n: (CAS.)] Here it is treated, that *the praises have failed:* because God now does not give pleasant things, as to boys who are to be enticed to serve; and this the Synagogue says, which now bears witness against itself against that which it did before, that it may be the more credible.



## PSALM LXXII.

[0956C]VERS. 1.-- *How good.* [n: (CAS.)] He admonishes that we should praise God not for temporal things, but for eternal ones.

VERS. 2.-- *Feet.* [n: (CAS.)] For the judgment of the mind, which was at variance with the truth. *Steps,* headlong acts; *almost,* because he quickly returned.

VERS. 3, 4.-- *The peace of sinners.* [n: (AUG.)] Temporal, fleeting, perishable. *Seeing.* Not understanding. Because this peace is seen, it is not understood, it eludes the eyes, for it is fleeting and perishable. [n: (CAS.)] *The peace of sinners, for there is no turning aside to their death.* [var: Vulg. Ps 72:4 *quia non est respectus morti eorum*, "no regard for their death"] That is, they do not die quickly, but live long; although *a stroke* is sometimes not firm, as the scourge of correction lasts. *And a firmament in their stroke.* God does not have mercy by consolidating their strokes: or, by a likeness, that of an incurable man, [0956D] to whom nothing is forbidden.

VERS. 6.-- *They are covered with their iniquity and impiety.* [n: (CAS.)] Lest they come to their senses; not merely wrapped about, so as at least to have the head free, but wholly covered.

VERS. 7.-- *Their iniquity hath come forth as it were,* etc. [n: (AUG.)] There are some who are evil out of leanness, that is, out of need: others out of abundance, as the poor man steals out of want, the rich out of covetousness. *They have passed over.* [n: (CAS.)] As it were from the straight paths.

VERS. 8.-- *On high.* [n: (AUG.)] Openly, in the open: or, blasphemers against God.

VERS. 9.-- *Into heaven.* [n: (ID.)] As if they were gods; or, against God, whom they blaspheme, saying that God either does not know their own evils, or does not punish them. *The tongue hath passed over the earth,* etc. [var: Vulg. Ps 72:9 *lingua eorum transivit in terra*, "their tongue hath passed on the earth"] [n: (ID.)] Which speaks beyond the measure of humanity, [0957A] etc., *as far as* but their tongue passes beyond human frailty.

VERS. 10.-- *Therefore shall [my people] be converted.* [n: (CAS.)] Expounding Asaph, etc., *as far as* he shall return to it. *Here.* [n: (AUG.)] In this opinion in which I now am. *Full days shall be found in them.* [n: (HIERON.)] They are found perfect, although it does not now appear. [n: (CAS.)] Or, *the days are full,* when Christ came: these therefore are among the Jews, who have known Christ.

VERS. 11.-- *And they said.* [n: (AUG.)] Whence shall they be converted? etc., *as far as* this is why it seemed to them that he did not know this. *How doth he know.* [n: (ID.)] He does not say, He knows not: but he still hesitates and is near to health: and from so grave an infirmity health is the nearer, just as the sick are more feverish when health is at hand.

[0957B]VERS. 13.-- *And I said: Then have I in vain justified my heart,* etc. [n: (CAS.)] These things they said, for the confuting of whom I say these: or still in the person of one doubting. He says that he was deluded by foolish thoughts, so that, when he shall have repudiated them, he may the better provide for us.

VERS. 14.-- *And chastisement.* Otherwise also *my discloser in the morning.* [n: (CAS.)] Behold now the remedy, Christ the discloser, who disclosed the way of truth, and, rising in the morning, transferred our hope from the world to the heavens.

VERS. 15.-- *If I said.* [n: (ID.)] Behold, now he restrains himself by the authority of the saints, and now, with the truth near at hand, he deliberates thus: *If I said. Behold the nation.* [n: (AUG.)] He begins to resolve another incongruity. *I have reproved;* I judged that which is not. *I thought,* by the things aforesaid I wish to prove God not just; but in this manner I think, I do not [0957C] absolve. *Before me,* that is, as regards present things; or, greater than I, surpassing me, unless God open it. Or, behold the nation of thy sons, to which I have appointed, and with which I have sung together, understand it, meets and is over against me [cj: *obvia*; read *obviat*, "meets"], that is, my former preaching meets me, which I made to the Israelites, that they should worship God, who created all things, and who renders to all according to their works: the teaching comes against me, to which I agreed and to which I was fitted. But lest I should reprove the nation of the former fathers, or contradict my former teaching: *I thou[ght],* etc.

*Of thy sons.* For all, Abraham and the rest, teach otherwise, whom I ought to believe: for I am not wiser than all. But lest I should reprove: *I thought,* or, I undertook to know; he is now called back from his fall, who does not presume himself to be knowing against the saints. That I might know [0957D] the truth of this matter, which I did not before, while he was saying that God did not care for these things. I was thinking that I might know this: the first degree of knowledge, when a man knows that he does not know what he seemed before to know: and he repudiates the former sense, while he seeks another.

VERS. 16.-- *Labour is before me.* [n: (AUG.)] As it were a wall stands in my way, etc., *as far as* he sees them condemned in the judgment.

VERS. 17.-- *The sanctuary.* [n: (AUG.)] Christ, who as God discerns the merits of each. Or *the sanctuary,* the Holy of holies, that is, the secret of the divine disposition.

VERS. 17.-- *But yet.* [n: (AUG., CASS.)] Behold what this man learned in the sanctuary: that the wicked are deceived here, and afterwards [0958A] at the end are to be forsaken by all; from which evil he himself was freed, who also sought earthly things, but from God; here he says that in this world the wicked are not free and unpunished; because although they are happy, yet they bear guilt and frequently run into ruins. *But yet,* that is, but: and here thou hast set for them this flower and abundance of temporal things, *because of deceits,* etc.

*Because of deceits.* [n: (AUG.)] Or, beside a slippery place. As if: In a deceitful manner thou hast dealt with them, giving evils under the appearance of good. Or, beside a slippery place, such as temporal things are: where they fall who are near by love; for while a deceitful man wishes to cast down another, he sets him beside a slippery place.

VERS. 20.-- *As a dream.* [n: (ID.)] An open comparison. With our eyes closed we see goods which presently, when they are opened, we do not find [sic: *mo*]; in life a dream: in death they awake, [0958B] knowing what it is. *In the city.* In the sight of thy people, or, while thy city is being established. [n: (HIER.)] Or in the heavenly Jerusalem their image, the likenesses of the deformities which they make for themselves: one a lion by ferocity, another a boar by the filth of lust. [n: (CAS.)] Or the glory of time, which is not true, but a likeness of glory: because when truth shall be wanting to them, the image shall perish; nor shall they retain the likeness, who shall have nothing of blessedness, therefore thou wilt destroy them, because through them,

VERS. 21.-- *My heart hath been inflamed.* That is, I envied the happy. *And my reins have been changed.* By which the standing, that is, the constancy of the mind, was dissolved, so that I loved prosperity; it is a grave fault to afford to others an occasion of sinning. *I am brought to nothing.* Clinging to vain things, made injurious to God. *I knew not.* It is a great and deep [0958C] ignorance not to know that thou sinnest. [n: (CAS.)] *In thy city the image.* Or this can be taken of the heretics, etc., *as far as* or in the supercelestial Church. *For it hath been inflamed.* [n: (AUG.)] Behold the conversion of this Asaph, etc., *as far as* they have been changed into spiritual ones

VERS. 24.-- *With,* etc. [n: (ID.)] Because before I was brought to nothing, that is, I went down to those things which are nothing: because I knew not that it was nothing, and afterwards I was made gentle as a beast of burden for the bearing of a load, and I shall always be with thee.

*A beast of burden.* [n: (CAS.)] With God they are in the place of cattle, who think against God: and yet, *erring* in these things, not knowing the judgments of God, I was always with thee in a manner, a stranger to idols, and believing thee, and thou didst hold me.

*Thou hast held my hand.* [n: (AUG.)] Because, although a beast, he did not depart [0958D] from God. *Thou hast held my hand,* that is, the power of the right hand, that is, thou hast covered him, in that he is always with thee, in that he is a son. *He gave,* indeed, *power to be made the sons of God to them that believe in his name. On the left,* a beast, loving these temporal things. Or the *right* hand.

[n: (CAS.)] Not the left. God holds the right hand, while he directs to good works, and delivers us lest we be drowned, as he did Peter. [n: (ID.)] Having seen the evil of the impious, and his own good, he accuses himself of his former state, because he sought earthly things from God, and he invites to the contrary.

[n: (AUG.)] He compares the heavenly reward with his own earthly pleasure, and accuses himself.

*For ever,* etc. From him I shall never be separated any more.

*Who go a-fornicating from thee,* etc. Contrary to this fornication [0959A] is chaste fear, which loves him freely, in whom it has all things.

VERS. 28.-- *In the gates of the daughter of Sion.* [n: (AUG.)] In public, or in the concord of the Apostles, who are the gates of the Church. Because preaching which is outside the unity of the Church is empty. Or, by virtues the Church is the daughter of Sion, that is, of contemplation.



## PSALM LXXIII.

VERS. 1.-- *The understanding of Asaph.* [n: (AUG.)] Of the Synagogue, etc., *as far as* that the hardness of the Jews might either be terrified by calamities, or softened. [n: (CAS.)] He admonishes, the figures being left behind, to pass over to the truth.

*Why, O God.* [n: (ID.)] Describing the irreparable overthrow of Jerusalem, he asks the cause, wherefore, wondering and complaining; and he prays for the people cast off, and for vengeance [0959B] upon the destroyers, which indeed is useful to them, and has already been done. *Thou hast cast us off.* [var: Vulg. Ps 73:1 *Ut quid, Deus, repulisti in finem*, without *nos*] [n: (ID.)] As it were strangers to thy protection. *Unto the end.* Not even the temple is spared. He signifies the destruction and the captivity, which came from Titus and Vespasian. He names sheep, that he may move the mercy of the loving shepherd. *Thine.* We take vengeance more temperately upon those whom we remember to have been ours at some time.

VERS. 2.-- *Thou hast possessed.* [n: (CASS.)] Or, thou hast created, although he creates and disposes all, yet the Jews especially, to whom he gave the law and the Prophets. *Thou hast redeemed.* [n: (HIER.)] Thee for a price, that is, through thee a price. *Mount.* [n: (AUG.)] An eminence; Sion is contemplation, in which, in which thing thou hast dwelt. Or it is redundant in it. [n: (HIER.)] The mount, or of mount Sion, or in it. Thus far the deeds of the true Testament; what follows is the truth promised. By Sion he signifies Jerusalem, [0959C] in which the presence of Christ dwelt, and therefore he entreats that he give it not over to devastation. *The rod of inheritance.* [n: (AUG.)] That congregation itself is called the rod of inheritance: wishing to possess which, he turned the rod of Moses into a serpent, but when the tail was seized it returned into a rod. [n: (HIER.)] Because death entered into the world by the persuasion of the serpent, the rod is in the serpent, Christ in death. Whence he also who looked upon the serpent, that is, believes Christ dead, is healed from serpents, that is, from eternal death. Moses was terrified and fled, so the Apostles when Christ was dying. The tail being held, that is, the later things, it becomes a rod, because first he was slain, afterwards he rose. The tail also is the end of the world, in which we return to the hand of God, and are made stable, we who through death, as through a serpent, have flowed away. [n: (AUG.)] But because these things are also of the Gentiles, he expresses of whom he [0959D] speaks, *Mount Sion.* That is, not of the Gentiles. Or, this mount Sion, which thou hast inhabited in it: *Lift up thy hand against their pride,* [var: Vulg. Ps 73:3 *Leva manus tuas*, plural] by whom Jerusalem was overthrown, that is, the kings of the Gentiles. *Unto the end,* because they too have now known Christ.

VERS. 3.-- *Lift up.* [n: (CAS.)] Thy power, that thou mayest turn their pride to humility, leading them unto the end, that is, Christ, which also has been fulfilled, because at Rome the Christian religion flourished. *Lift up,* because from being exceedingly superstitious she has been made most holy. *How greatly he hath done wickedly,* etc. [n: (AUG.)] The priests and the ministries of the temple were plunder. The whole nation was either slain or led captive. *The enemy,* the Roman army; and to this end he set *their* beforehand by prolepsis.

[0960A]VERS. 4.-- *They have gloried.* [n: (AUG., CASS.)] Of their own victory. Behold that they did it not with a good zeal. Having gloried: whereas repentance is wont to follow a crime, these turn it to glory; *who hate thee,* the slaves of demons, who are by no means to be tolerated. *In the midst of thy solemnity.* Not in the outermost place, but in the middle court. Another reading: they committed sacrileges. *In the holy place.* [n: (CAS.)] In the sanctuary, through contempt of the holy temple, where the Godhead seems to be despised, it stirs up ill-will against the presumptuous. *Signs,* etc. [n: (AUG.)] Eagles, etc., *as far as* that is, through the high places.

VERS. 5.-- *In the going out.* [n: (CAS.)] Or as in a going forth from above, that is, they did not know that this licence went forth from God. *As in a wood,* etc. [n: (CASS.)] Before it was built, he prophesies the overthrow of the temple.

*With axe and hatchet they have defiled the tabernacle of thy name,* [0960B] etc. [var: Vulg. Ps 73:6 *in securi et ascia dejecerunt eam*; Ps 73:7 *in terra polluerunt tabernaculum nominis tui*] [n: (AUG.)] *The axe* cuts beams: *the hatchet* follows up more minutely. By this therefore he says that both great things and the least were cast down, so that the city was utterly laid waste, first cut down, afterwards the fire devoured all.

VERS. 7.-- *On the ground,* etc. Down to the ground they cast the roofs raised for the praise of God. He magnifies the crime, that it may be more atrociously avenged. [n: (CAS.)] Or the whole can be read in the person of the despairing Jews, etc., *as far as* who amid these things do not repent. [n: (AUG.)] These things they said, but how long dost thou endure the reproaches of the Jews: He did not say this in order that he might wish them to perish, but to be changed. *Unto the end* of the world, namely the evening, when the Jews shall be converted.

VERS. 9.-- *We have not seen our signs.* [n: (AUG., CASS.)] [0960C] As if they should say: Deservedly have these evils befallen us, Because we have not seen the signs; as if he should say: We have seen, but we have not understood [sic: *dica*]: and truly we have seen, because this is the sign, that there is no Prophet, nor priest, nor unction; whence: *When the Holy of holies shall have come, there shall cease the unction [n: (Dan. IX)]* of yours. [var: Vulg. Dn 9:24 *et impleatur visio et prophetia, et ungatur Sanctus sanctorum*] *And he shall no more know us,* some Prophet, or God through a Prophet; that is, he shall not announce Christ as about to come, because he has already come. *Any more.* [n: (ID.)] Or he shall not know as yet, until, namely, the Jew acknowledge him.

VERS. 10.-- *How long?* etc. [n: (CAS.)] And as if he had suddenly come to know. *How long,* he subjoined, *doth the adversary provoke?* as if he should say: So long shall he reproach, as long as the adversary, that is, the Jew, provokes thy name. *Why dost thou turn away.* [n: (AUG.)] After the sign of the rod there was added [0960D] the sign of the hand of Moses; this is the people of God: which, sent forth outside from him, is unclean, but being called back returns into its own colour. He says therefore: How long dost thou not call back thy hand, that it may acknowledge the Saviour. *Thy hand and thy right hand,* that is, the Jews.

VERS. 12.-- *But God is our king.* [n: (CAS.)] The signs being destroyed, he shows the thing itself, namely, that in the coming of Christ salvation was made everywhere. [n: (AUG.)] *But God.* Asaph, understanding, says, etc., *as far as* [sic: *uque ad*] if therefore he did not go before, let him at least follow.

*In the midst of the earth.* [n: (CAS.)] Made man; Jerusalem is said to be in the midst.

VERS. 13.-- *Thou hast confirmed.* [n: (AUG.)] He made firm the fallings of the Red Sea, that there might be dry ground between. Whose [0961A] mystery he subjoins, that in the waters of baptism he annihilated the heads of the unclean spirits.

VERS. 14.-- *Meat.* [n: (AUG.)] Christ is meat, etc., *as far as* or they bite him by reproving, by accusing. [n: (CAS.)] Or while he tempts, he is meat, because thereby profit is made. Or, to the peoples of the Ethiopians. [n: (HIERON.)] To those unwilling to be converted thou hast given him for meat, in whom they may take delight.

VERS. 15.-- *Fountains and torrents.* [n: (AUG.)] In others the word is made, etc., *as far as* and he notes the abundance of preaching. [n: (CAS.)] Or the whole of the wicked. *Fountains,* whose evils flowed in continually: *Torrents,* who sin suddenly and rapidly: these God breaks up and dries. *The rivers of Ethan.* The floods and counsels of the devil.

*Thine is the day and thine is the night.* [n: (AUG.)] He illumines the day [0961B] but of unchangeable wisdom. The moon consoles the night, that is, the manifestation of the flesh: *The Word was made flesh and dwelt among us [n: (Joan. I)]*. And likewise: *Night to night sheweth knowledge [n: (Psal. XXVIII)]*. *The dawn and the sun.* [n: (AUG.)] Or *the moon,* that is, the carnal man, who like the moon is changed, who nevertheless is not to be forsaken. Or *the dawn and the sun,* that is, the beginning and the advance of wisdom, the unwise and the perfect.

VERS. 17.-- *Thou hast made all.* [n: (CAS.)] Or all things historically from this place: *Thine is the day. The bounds of the earth,* etc. [n: (CAS.)] The Apostles and Prophets: as boundary-marks distinguish the limits, so these guard the laws of the faith: or all things historically.

VERS. 18.-- *Be mindful.* [n: (CAS.)] Now what is done everywhere: he prays for the Jews, and by the benefits reckoned up he provokes the merciful judge [0961C]. As if he should say: Be mindful of this thy creature. *Of this.* [n: (AUG.)] Of thy creature; of what creature of thine? *The enemy hath reproached the Lord, and a foolish people hath provoked thy name,* etc. [n: (AUG.)] Asaph with understanding grieves over the former blindness, and so prays for the Jews, that they may confess their errors. This he prays, which he knows will come to pass; for there were among the Jews vessels of mercy.

VERS. 20.-- *Have regard to thy testament.* [n: (ID.)] Let the remnant be saved, because many who tend toward the earth have been darkened and filled with earth. [n: (CAS.)] *Have regard.* Fulfil what thou hast promised; that thou mayest establish the New Testament, which is concerning eternal things. He calls the promise to mind, because merits are wanting. Likewise for this reason, because they have been filled with iniquities, whence darkness follows, [0961D] because they lose the light of wisdom, being filled with iniquities, because the houses of the earth have been darkened by an earthly habitation, that is, blind through love of earthliness.

VERS. 21.-- *Let not the humble be turned away, made confounded.* [n: (CAS.)] The acceptable is set for the hateful, that the love of the devout may temper the hatred of the obstinate. *Confounded.* [n: (ID.)] To be confounded belongs not to the humble, but to the proud, who attributes not to God but to himself.

VERS. 22.-- *Arise, O God.* [n: (CASS.)] Now Asaph (understanding) shakes off the earthly promises of the Old Testament. *Thy cause.* For they say: Where is thy God? He was despised by the Jews as he walked on the earth, and by false Christians as he sits in heaven. Judge therefore; let not my hope be deceived, who have believed what I have not seen. *Thy [0962A] cause.* After all things, he makes it the judge's cause, to whom the character of his own business is made known. *Thy cause,* against those who murmur, that with the truth made manifest they may preach what they foolishly denied. *All the day.* [n: (AUG.)] He says this for this reason, that the very continuance might rouse the mildness of the patient judge.

VERS. 23.-- *The pride of them that hate thee.* [n: (CAS.)] At the end he sets the execrable evil, which is pride.



## PSALM LXXIV.

VERS. 1.-- *Unto the end, destroy not,* etc. [n: (AUG.)] This psalm admonishes, etc., *as far as* that the Jews may believe, and not corrupt the faith.

VERS. 2.-- *We will praise thee, O God.* [n: (AUG.)] Certain therefore of what has gone before, he begins: *We will praise.* Repetition [0962B] is affirmation, lest it repent thee of having confessed: whence the Lord too doubles it. *Amen, amen.* So be it, so be it. And Joseph concerning the dream of Pharao, who saw seven ears of corn, and as many barren ones. And seven fat oxen and as many lean. [n: (CAS.)] Repetition is confirmation for the signifying of truth. One would suffice for the signifying of firmness; a second was added. To confess sins even once to an earthly judge brings in death, but frequent confession to God, life? Thence invocation, afterwards narration; whence follows: *We will narrate. We will call upon thy name.* [n: (ID.)] After confession God can be called upon, into a man thus cleansed: and as by confession he is emptied of evils, so by invocation he is filled with good things.

*We will narrate [sic: *Nafrubimus*] thy wonders,* etc. [n: (CAS.)] The proud man is terrified [0962C] by the judgment, etc., *as far as* in whose person it is subjoined:

VERS. 3.-- *When I shall take a time,* he receives after the manner of man what God himself gives, that is, the power of executing judgment. *Justices.* Therefore let those who are to be judged pray beforehand to be absolved.

VERS. 4.-- *The earth is melted.* [n: (CAS.)] He shows all to be inexcusable in the judgment, through this, that he sent before the Apostles and Prophets. *The earth and all.* [n: (AUG.)] Firm before, it flows away into divers concupiscences. Or, the earth, bound before by vices, when the Holy Spirit blew, was melted, that it might send forth the buds of virtues.

VERS. 5.-- *To do wickedly.* [n: (AUG.)] It is evil to sin; it is worse to persevere; pernicious, by defending [0962D] oneself, to pour back the fault upon God. *Lift not up your horn.* [n: (CAS.)] The vice of excusing is the greatest; he as it were raises a horn who strives to excuse his own sins through others, as through the persuasions of devils, or through the necessity of the stars.

VERS. 6.-- *To speak.* As those who say that they have sinned not by their own fault, but by the necessity of the stars, that it may be imputed to God, not to themselves. Or, those who know their own sins, but promise themselves impunity.

VERS. 7.-- *For neither from the east.* [n: (CAS.)] Why these things? because the judge is such that he cannot be avoided: because he is everywhere; for he is God. [n: (AUG.)] He judges from no place separately, who is everywhere present as God. [n: (CAS.)] Mystically. *The east,* those for whom the sun of justice [0963A] rises, etc., *as far as* because God is present and is a knower.

VERS. 9.-- *For a cup.* [n: (AUG.)] The cup, the law and all Scripture, etc., *as far as* what went before, *and he exalteth this one.* [n: (CAS.)] *A cup,* the law; *pure wine,* the heavenly mystery, etc., *as far as* not only the Jews.

VERS. 10.-- *But I will declare for ever: I will sing to the God of Jacob.* [n: (CAS.)] All shall drink, but severally: I, that is Christ, with the body, *will declare for ever,* as long as the world shall be.

VERS. 1.-- *The horns of the just,* that is, the gifts of Christ, which are the loftiness of the just.

## PSALM LXXV.

VERS. 1.-- *A canticle.* Because it treats of the good things of Christ, [0963B] namely that he is known, that he *dwelleth,* that he *enlighteneth,* that he *saveth.* It breaks the glory of the Jews. *To the Assyrians.* [n: (CAS.)] Against the Jews, who are not directing themselves, but erring. Or, because they raise themselves up, since without grace they seek salvation and persecute the Church, as of old the Assyrians persecuted the Jews. And these are here confuted, the names of glory being taken away, and are proved to be able to do nothing without grace.

VERS. 2.-- *Known in Judea.* [n: (AUG.)] The Jew glories that God is known to the Jew, and it is true; but to the Jew within, not in the flesh. [n: (AUG.)] The Prophet: if ye are Jews, then God is known among you: but if he is not, then ye are not Jews. *In Judea.* [n: (ID.)] The two sons of Joseph being taken up, with the remaining eleven sons of Jacob there are twelve tribes, await [cj: *exspecta*; read *excepta*, "the tribe of Levi being excepted"] the tribe of Levi, which was priestly, and lived on the tithes. Among twelve tribes the land [0963C] was divided. Among these one is Juda, whence the kings, and from the kingdom all are called Jews. They confessed Caesar, not Christ, and being of Juda they are now not Jews.

VERS. 3.-- *In Sion.* [n: (AUG.)] Mark the fitting order of the words: Judea is confession; after that, Israel, that is, seeing God, and thus there is a place in one at peace with God: whence follows: *In peace,* after *in Sion,* that is in contemplation (when he shall see God face to face), there shall be a habitation.

VERS. 4.-- *There he broke the powers.* [n: (CAS.)] In Judea, in Israel, in Salem, in Sion, where God the author of peace came, all these things (which are also the names of their glory) flee away.

VERS. 5.-- *Thou enlightening wonderfully from the everlasting hills, they were troubled,* etc. Diapsalma. Christ enlightens, [0963D] the Jew sleeps, Christ judges, that man perishes. [n: (AUG.)] Thou *enlightenest,* but through *the mountains,* who first receive the light, namely through the Prophets and the Apostles. But from the enlightening *all the foolish of heart were troubled.* He sets down thence why he is terrible in the time to come, etc. *They have slept.* [n: (CASS.)] Growing cold from eternal goods, they acquiesced in the flesh, not seeing the things that would profit them. *Their sleep,* which differs from the rest of the good: because this one is deceitful; seeing in a dream that they have treasures, when they awake they find nothing, whence he says: *And all the men of riches have found nothing.* The definition of the covetous. *They have found nothing.* Because they placed nothing in the hands of Christ, who says: *I was hungry,* etc. [n: (REM.)] For he hungered in the poor, who in heaven is rich. *Men of riches.* [0964A] Who place their only hope in the uncertainty of riches. Whence he does not say, having riches: for many have who do not love. *In their hands,* etc. [n: (CAS.)] As though they still sought in their hands, when they now know that they have held nothing.

VERS. 7.-- *At the rebuke,* etc. [n: (AUG.)] The noise of a rebuke is wont to arouse, but so great is the weight of the God who rebukes, that they slumber, that is, they have a hardened heart. *Who have mounted.* [n: (ID.)] Whose pride grew, not humbled by the humble Christ. *Horses.* [n: (CAS.)] Any secular exaltations whatsoever: whence it had been commanded in the law to the children of Israel, that they should not multiply horses to themselves. Solomon too had no horses from Judea, but used to buy them from Egypt.

VERS. 9.-- *Hearing from heaven.* [n: (AUG.)] Whence the Apostle: [0964B] *In the revelation of our Lord Jesus from heaven they shall come with angels to execute judgment [n: (I Thes. I)]*. [var: Vulg. 2Thes 1:7 *in revelatione Domini Jesu de caelo cum angelis virtutis ejus*, "with the angels of his power"]

VERS. 10.-- *When he should rise up,* that is, when he shall appear as mighty, who here was humble. Or, *when he should rise up* here from death, afterwards to come to judgment. [n: (AUG.)] When, coming from heaven, thou shalt make a hearing, that is, [make it] known to all. The power of the judgment is set forth, whence earthly men are terrified, and, their power lost, are still; it was still, when he should rise up. *The meek of the earth.* [n: (ID.)] Who have peace of mind, not refusing the yoke of God.

VERS. 11.-- *For the thought.* [n: (CAS.)] Diapsalma. He now admonishes the devout to offer vows. As if: deservedly he saves the meek. *For the thought,* that is, they shall praise thee in thought and in work, that is, *the remainders of the thought,* [0964C] etc. *The thought of man.* [n: (ID.)] The conception of a vow. *The remainders,* the rendering of the vow, the working, the things remaining after the thought. *The remainders of the thought.* [n: (AUG.)] Since the first thought, etc., *as far as* which is to celebrate solemnities. [n: (CAS.)] Because the Lord alone is he to whom festivity is owed: they shall render vows to God. The first thought condemns evils. *A remainder.* Because afterwards a man ought always to remember past evils, that he may mourn: although the clean man here keeps a feast-day, when he feels himself free from sin, as after amendment the Jews are commanded to offer a festal gift to God.

VERS. 12.-- *Vow ye and pay.* [n: (AUG.)] Some vows are common, concerning those things without which there is no salvation; others proper, as to vow chastity, and things of this kind. [n: (CAS.)] *Vow ye.* There are certain things which, not even promising them, [0964D] we ought to fulfil: as, Thou shalt not kill, and things of this kind, etc., *as far as* who are round about, that as servants they shall offer, or let them offer, etc. [n: (AUG.)] There are, as it were, middling ones, etc., *as far as* having the spirit of God, not of princes, that is, a proud spirit. *Gifts.* [n: (ID.)] Which are offered upon the altars. *To him that is terrible and to him that taketh away.* Whom the good fear, but the wicked do not fear. *The spirit of princes.* [n: (CAS.)] He takes away the spirit of pride or of fear, and makes them reins of his own, who govern their own body.



## PSALM LXXVI.

VERS. 1.-- *Unto the end, for Idithum.* [n: (AUG.)] The congregation speaks, passing over, that it may arrive at the end, who is Christ. It teaches therefore to trample upon the desires and enticements of the world, to set nothing before itself, save him from whom [0965A] all things are. [n: (CAS.)] Transcending all things, it seeks God and finds him.

VERS. 2.-- *I cried to the Lord with my voice.* [n: (AUG.)] With the intention of devotion toward the Lord, seeking nothing else from him, because he is Lord, and because he is God. *My voice unto him.* [var: Vulg. Ps 76:2 *voce mea ad Deum*, "with my voice unto God"] The voice is for this, for the sake of which it is uttered; this he adds, lest he be thought to cry to God for anything else than for himself, above whom there is nothing. [n: (CAS.)] He does not say what he asks, because there is no need that it be said to him who knows what is expedient. *He hath attended to me.* [n: (CAS.)] *For God to attend* is to give the whole of what is asked, and more than is asked.

VERS. 3.-- *In the day of my trouble I sought.* [n: (AUG.)] How didst thou seek? etc., *as far as* he who does not do justice before men, that he may be seen. *He refused to be comforted.* [n: (ID.)] He expounds the tribulation, etc., *as far as* [0965B] he who in all men either finds or fears a scandal, is secure in none.

VERS. 4.-- *And I was exercised.* [n: (AUG.)] Or, *I chattered,* that is, I exulted, speaking as a chatterer, who, for gladness, neither would nor could be silent: and again in chattering the spirit failed, which before had wasted away with weariness of evils.

VERS. 5.-- *They prevented the watches.* [n: (CAS.)] Diapsalma. God being found, who alone is good, he deliberates: will he bring him back? [n: (AUG.)] They prevented the watches of others. *My eyes,* like that man who rises before the time to his business. Or, *my eyes,* my affections. *They prevented,* the diligence of others. Or, by *watches,* he signifies the night offices which we make, and which we usually so call among the praises of God, and therefore: *Troubled.* [n: (CAS.)] [0965C] Because I could not obtain what I seek. *I spoke not.* I did not murmur against God. [sic: *Dum*] Or, *I was troubled,* because while by night he searches out the secrets of the mind, he sees the human race rushing into sins: and he is tormented with a loving grief for others, who has already passed beyond vices. *He is silent,* because by night he has not the solace of human conversation, at which time he thinks more warily; therefore he subjoins: *I thought upon the days,* etc. *My eyes.* Otherwise *my enemies prevented the watches.* [n: (AUG.)] That is, they watched over me, etc., *as far as* yet he does not cease, he wishes to transcend this also, whence he subjoins:

VERS. 6.-- *I thought upon the days of old,* etc. Therefore troubled: because *I thought upon the ancient days of Adam,* in which man fell. But over against *the ancient days, the years* he sets *eternal:* therefore *troubled,* because man desires these, and the eternal [0965D] he neglects. *I thought upon the days of old, and I had in my mind the eternal years.* [n: (CAS.)] What befell Adam for his disobedience? From him I have known this defect of man to be. And the eternal years, which by persisting he would have had, I had in my mind, which the foolish do not. *And moreover I meditated in the night,* that is, in this defect. *With my heart,* that is, with reason, whether in any way I might return to the eternal years. He speaks silently with reason, who is quietly exercised in it.

VERS. 7.-- *And I was exercised.* In that very meditation: *I swept my spirit;* I purged the mind. Others [read] *I winnowed my spirit,* thus dealing with myself:

VERS. 8.-- *Will he cast off for ever,* the human race? *that he may be more favourable.* As one who has already been [0966A] favourable by his grace in the Old Testament: will he not add that he may be more so in the New through the coming of the Son? this he thinks, who treats of God's goodness. *I was exercised and I swept.* [n: (AUG.)] Or, *I chattered,* not now outwardly, etc., *as far as* where he finds this, that *God will not cast off for ever. That he may be more favourable,* namely that he himself, who was angry by casting off, by bringing in evils, may be gentler by delivering, by calling back.

VERS. 10.-- *Or will God forget to shew mercy.* [n: (AUG.)] In another manner, there is no mercy unless God bestow it: and he himself will be without it. The stream runs, the fountain is dried up.

VERS. 11.-- *And I said: Now have I begun.* [n: (CASS.)] What he found by deliberating, namely that Christ changes men, and redeems them. [n: (ID.)] *And I said.* To this meditation, [0966B] etc., *as far as* when, passing beyond myself also, I came to God, here there is no danger. *I have begun.* [n: (AUG.)] Something, where I am secure, where enemies do not prevent me, now the Most High has begun to change me. *The change of the right hand,* etc. [n: (CAS.)] From wrath to mercy, in that of servants there are made sons. [n: (ID.)] Of this change, etc., *as far as* these are the things that soothe a holy man.

VERS. 13.-- *In thy inventions.* [n: (AUG.)] That is, in the precepts which thou hast invented, or by which thou art found. [n: (CAS.)] Or *in thy observances,* that is, in the precepts which are to be observed, I shall be exercised: because it is not enough to read the Scriptures, but it behoves to show the fruit of works: instructed by these he breaks forth into praises. [n: (AUG.)] *In thy inventions* or *thy affections,* not in mine; or there is affection in earthly things: so in the works and in the meditation [0966C] of God there is a certain inward affection, when the world is beheld, and in creatures the creator is sought, and is found nowhere displeasing, but everywhere pleasing.

VERS. 14.-- *O God, in the holy place.* [n: (AUG.)] By the things aforesaid I am certain that I shall come, and with him for guide. [n: (ID.)] *O God, in the holy place,* that is, in Christ, the way of returning to thee. And since this is so, *who is a great God?* etc. *Thy way.* [n: (ID.)] Of worshipping thee and of coming to thee, whence he joyfully prefers the power of God to the images, which were falsely worshipped.

VERS. 15.-- *Wonders.* [n: (AUG.)] The deaf, the blind, the feeble, the dead, the paralytic were healed. These things were done in bodies. In the soul certain men are sober, who a little before were drunkards, unbelievers, adorers of images. *To the peoples thy power.* [n: (ID.)] *To the unfaithful, that* they may know [0966D] Christ only in body, *to the faithful,* in reality and in Godhead; he subjoins: What thou hast bestowed, *thou hast redeemed.*

VERS. 16.-- *The sons of Joseph.* [var: Vulg. Ps 76:16 *filios Jacob et Joseph*] [n: (AUG.)] That is, the Gentile people. Or, by *Joseph* Christ is understood: because, as Joseph, sold into Egypt, reigned, so Christ reigned among the Gentiles, having first been humbled in the martyrs. [n: (ID.)] *Joseph,* who came to the Gentiles, is interpreted *increase:* and he signifies the Gentiles, from whom the people of God has been increased. Or, *Joseph,* Christ sold by the Jews, and driven to the Gentiles, *his sons,* whom he chose out of the Gentiles.

VERS. 17.-- *They saw.* [n: (CAS.)] Diapsalma. He expounds what things Christ works in the world. [n: (AUG.)] As if he should say: Since thou hast made thy power known, either through him [0967A] *they saw thee, O God the Father,* or, *thee, O Christ,* who art the power and the arm, etc.

VERS. 18.-- *A multitude.* [n: (CAS.)] Not a few, but *a multitude. The noise,* in the groaning for faults, in the giving of thanks: for the divers vows of the peoples sound as it were a broken sea. *The noise of waters.* [n: (AUG.)] Whence are these noises of waters, this troubling of the deeps? because *the clouds have uttered a voice,* of which: *I will command my clouds that they rain no rain upon it.* [var: Vulg. Is 5:6 *et nubibus mandabo ne pluant super eam imbrem*]

VERS. 19.-- *The voice of thunder.* [n: (CAS.)] The voice of thunder is rolled about, so that a wrinkled and winding sound is perceived. An allegory: *in the wheel* of the whole world. *A wheel.* [n: (HIER., AUG.)] It is that which he expounds as the circle of the earth, which the clouds went about, thundering with precepts, flashing with miracles. *The earth trembled.* [n: (CAS.)] They who dwell in the earth. That very [0967B] earth is called a sea by a likeness, because human life is bitter, subject to tempests, where the greater fish devours the lesser.

VERS. 20.-- *In the sea.* [n: (AUG.)] To the letter, when Christ walked upon the waters. Or, in the hearts of men, whom he subjects to himself. Or, by *the land,* the Jews; by *the sea,* the Gentiles are understood. *And thy footsteps shall not be known.* A silent rebuke of the Jews; as if he should say: Although he came openly, and showed himself by miracles, he was not known by the unbelieving Jews whom he led forth in the hand of Moses and Aaron, that is, by the working of the miracles which they did. *Thy footsteps,* etc. He touched some, that is, the Jews, who say, Christ has not yet come; they do not see him walking upon the sea.

VERS. 21.-- *As sheep.* Because they are not truly sheep, but [0967C] goats. *Of Moses and Aaron.* [n: (CAS.)] Moses is interpreted [sic: *intrepretatur*] *taken up from the river;* Aaron, *a mountain of strength:* by these names the powers which they wrought are expressed. Moses among other things made a way in the sea, signifying that the people is delivered through baptism. Aaron held the image of the Church, which is compared to a most strong mountain.



## PSALM LXXVII.

VERS. 1.-- *Understanding.* [n: (AUG.)] Because these things narrated, etc., *as far as* the things that seem open are parables and riddles. *Attend.* [n: (CAS.)] First the Lord himself speaks, that reverence may grow. *My people,* who obey me. The mouth of God is Asaph, who spoke these things below: *How great things we have heard from the beginning.* Of the Old Testament: or, the faithful Synagogue reproaches the wicked.

[0967D]VERS. 2.-- *In parables.* [n: (AUG.)] Parables are likenesses of things. *Propositions.* Which in Greek are *problemata,* questions having something to be resolved.

VERS. 3.-- *How great things.* [n: (CAS.)] He tells summarily what God did for the Jews: yet for men ungrateful to him, and thus, if not to them, it profits the sons nevertheless, lest they become as their fathers. [n: (AUG.)] First God spoke in a man, now the man himself: *How great things we have heard.* That foretold word God spoke from himself, lest his speech be despised.

VERS. 5.-- *And he raised up,* etc. [n: (AUG.)] This testimony. *The beginning,* whence above: *I will utter propositions from the beginning.* The beginning is the Old Testament, the end the New. There is fear, which is the beginning: but here is Christ, *the end of the law unto righteousness to every one that believeth [n: (Rom. X)]*, who gives the Spirit, [0968A] whence: *Charity, which drives out fear [n: (I Joan. IV)]* [var: Vulg. 1 Jo 4:18 *perfecta caritas foras mittit timorem*, "perfect charity casteth out fear"]. The old law is called the testimony of this New Testament, because it has testimony from the law and the Prophets: whence that was called the tabernacle of testimony which prefigured so many mystical things: *Testimony* therefore the Old Testament is called in so far as it proves and testifies, law in so far as it commands. [n: (CASS., REM.)] *He raised up* the testimony as though dead in Adam, that is, the law; or, while it is written down it is raised up, having been lulled asleep before by ignorance. *A testimony in Jacob,* this perhaps is that Jacob, having wrestled with the angel, went halt when his thigh was touched: which signifies that the Jews partly stand fast in faith, partly depart from salvation: and so what befell Jacob was a testimony of this thing. Behold a parable. *To their sons.* And these same sons are another generation, namely the people of grace.

[0968B]VERS. 6.-- *Another generation.* Through the generations the commandments of God were handed on, lest any think them given to one only.

VERS. 7.-- *That they may set.* This is the profit of the fathers' preaching. *Let them seek out.* [n: (AUG.)] Those who were said above to have known, now *let them seek out,* that hoping in God they may be helped by him to fulfil it.

VERS. 8.-- *Froward.* [n: (CAS.)] Which does not receive the truth, and is therefore twisted, *provoking* beyond all bitternesses, so that they were armed against the Saviour. Whence by Ezechiel the Lord says: *Go and speak to the provoking house [n: (Ezech. II)]*. *It set not its heart aright.* [n: (ID.)] He has a twisted heart who does not correct what he has ill understood, and does not perform what nature teaches: *His spirit was not trusted with God [n: (Joan. I)]*, that is, and if reason persuaded him well, he yielded to it. [0968C] *It was not trusted.* [n: (AUG.)] When the spirit of man works together with the Spirit of God working, that is done which God commands: and this is not save by believing in him who justifies the ungodly, which faith the froward and embittering generation has not.

[n: (ID.)] He speaks much more expressly, etc., *as far as* but that generation, although it believed God, yet not in God.

VERS. 9.-- *Shooting the bow.* [n: (AUG.)] He speaks figuratively, etc., *as far as* David to his brethren, so the Christian people to the people of the Jews. Or, *The sons of Ephrem.* [n: (ID.)] *Bending and shooting the bow,* that is, shooting something with a bow. Or, *bending the bow,* and shooting arrows, that is, promising to keep the law, saying openly: *All things whatsoever the Lord shall say to us, we will do [0968D] and we will hear [n: (Exod. XXIV)]* [var: Vulg. Ex 24:7 *Omnia quae locutus est Dominus, faciemus, et erimus obedientes*, "all things that the Lord hath spoken we will do, and we will be obedient"]. *They were turned* and failed *in the day of battle,* that is, of temptation; whence Osee the prophet says: *Ephraim is become [sic: facti] a dove [sic: *fuctus*] without understanding, not having a heart [n: (Ose. VII)]* [var: Vulg. Os 7:11 *factus est Ephraim quasi columba seducta non habens cor*, "as a dove that is decoyed"], because not hearing, but temptation proves the promise of obedience.

*Of his benefits.* [var: Vulg. Ps 77:11 *benefactorum*, "his good deeds"] [n: (EUTHYM.)] Of the benefits which he conferred upon the people of Israel, as, that he led them through the Red Sea unwetted. *Of his wonders* however he says, because he had turned the rivers of the Egyptians into blood. But as to his saying that God showed these things to them, he does not say it as though they themselves had seen them with their own eyes, but because they received them so from their elders.

VERS. 12.-- *Before their fathers,* etc. [n: (CAS.)] He begins now to tell what he did for them from the leading out of Egypt: [0969A] and yet they did not cease from murmuring and from temptation. [n: (AUG.)] *Before the fathers.* Who the fathers are, etc., *as far as* the wood of the cross coming to it as the rod of Moses. *In the land of Egypt.* [n: (ID.)] That he may take away all doubt, he names both the province and the place of the deeds. So Christ, set upon the earth, knew before the fathers, and taught the humble commandment: *Learn of me, for I am meek and humble of heart. [sic: *Discit*] [n: (Joan. VII.)]*

VERS. 14.-- *In a cloud.* [n: (HIER. et CASS.)] This they judged to be future. *The day* is Christ, yet he is hidden from the faithless by the *cloud* of the flesh; and as for them the *cloud* against the heat of the sun, so for us Christ through the incarnation gives refreshment against the heat of vices. *All the night,* all the time of life. *In the light of fire.* The fire of the Holy [0969B] Spirit keeps Christians in this world by the brightness of his name.

VERS. 15.-- *The rock.* [n: (CASS.)] *In the wilderness.* Before, the water of the sea was as a rock, now the rock is a fountain. *In the wilderness,* where the gift must needs be the more welcome. *He gave them drink.* As cattle, not as men, because they gave no thanks.

VERS. 16.-- *As.* [n: (CASS.)] The water which comes forth from Christ fails by no diminishing, but becomes a fountain of water springing up unto life everlasting.

VERS. 17.-- *To sin yet more.* [n: (CASS.)] They added this, that amid so many benefits they did not believe: and that this might be understood as the greatest sin, when the Lord had said in the Gospel: *The Spirit shall convince the world of sin,* he added by way of exposition: *Of sin indeed, because they believe not in me [n: (Joann. IV, XVI)]* [var: Vulg. Jo 16:9 *quia non crediderunt in me*, "because they have not believed in me"]. As therefore he there calls sin *not to believe,* so here by *to sin* he means, not to believe. [0969C] *Unto wrath.* [n: (ID.)] Not believing that God could do hard things for them, which is most grievous in the sight of God; so we too, washed by baptism, provoke God to wrath. *In the waterless place.* [n: (AUG.)] Either in another wilderness according to the letter, or rather in themselves, because although the skins were full of water from the rock, their minds nevertheless were dry.

VERS. 19.-- *A table.* [n: (CASS.)] Mystically, the refreshment of wisdom, or the body of Christ.

VERS. 21.-- *Therefore he heard.* [n: (CASS.)] In that, vengeance being deferred, he gave first what they were asking, lest he should seem not to be able. *He deferred.* [n: (AUG.)] The punishment; giving first what it was asking, though by way of tempting, lest he should seem not to be able: and after he had given, fire, that is wrath, went up upon Israel. And this is what he said before: *A fire was kindled in Jacob.*

[0969D]VERS. 23.-- *And he commanded.* Both were set down briefly, namely that he heard, and that wrath went up; here he begins to tell it openly. And first he follows out how he heard, afterwards in the following part how he took vengeance. *And the gates of heaven.* [n: (CASS.)] The gates are the preachers themselves, by whom entry is made into heaven; or the Scriptures. *He opened.* Because he gave his Spirit to his own, that they might understand the Scriptures.

VERS. 24.-- *Manna to eat.* [n: (AUG.)] It signifies the bread which came down from heaven, which is the food of angels: which, that man might eat it, *the Word was made flesh [n: (Joan. I)]*, who is rained upon the whole world through the evangelical clouds, and, their hearts being opened as heavenly gates, is preached to the believing Church, [0970A] not to the tempting Synagogue. [n: (CASS.)] *Manna, what is this?* because this food is sought after with wonder, and is known to be the body of Christ. *The bread of heaven.* [n: (ID.)] No other than Christ, by whom the heavenly ones, that is the angels, are refreshed in the contemplation of him; and this bread, that is the Word, on which great food the angels are fed, made milk through the flesh, man eats.

VERS. 26.-- *The south-west wind.* Because the quails are there.

VERS. 27.-- *And he rained upon them,* etc. [n: (AUG.)] So also a little faith, etc., *as far as* whence: *If I have told you earthly things, and you believe me not, how, if I tell you heavenly things?* etc. [n: (Joan. III.)] [var: Vulg. Jo 3:12 *Si terrena dixi vobis, et non creditis : quomodo, si dixero vobis caelestia, credetis*, "how, if I shall speak to you heavenly things, will you believe"]

*As dust.* [n: (CASS.)] *Dust* here signifies the subtlety of understanding, which climbs to the things above. *Sand,* the countless abundance of prudence seasoned [0970B] with the savour of salt. *Feathered fowls.* Heavenly desires, on which the soul is fed and grows fat: these we can receive within the enclosures of the Church, whence he adds:

VERS. 28.-- *And they fell in the midst of their camp.* [n: (AUG.)] Signifying by this that the winds come of their own accord to men, and carry to them the words divinely sent, that they may hear them in their own seats, and about their tents: because their sound went out into all the earth.

VERS. 29.-- *And they did eat.* They were convicted, who had thought this impossible to God.

VERS. 30.-- *As yet their meats,* etc., *and the wrath of God,* etc. [n: (CASS.)] Of the punishments, and of their feigned returnings, through fear of penalties: and yet the sentence of God is tempered by mercy. *Their meats.* [n: (ID.)] The manna, the quails. *He hindered the chosen of Israel.* [n: (AUG.)] That [0970C] is, he hindered them from profiting those whom they desired to counsel with a father's affection. Or rather, he fettered them, that the chosen might bear troubles together with them for an example, not of righteousness only, but of patience also. *He hindered.* [n: (ID.)] By their murmuring, as Moses, who said: *Can we bring forth water for you out of the rock? [n: (Num. XX.)]*

VERS. 32.-- *In all these things they sinned still, and they believed not in his wondrous works.* Corrected after all these things, the sins are the graver, which ever increase.

VERS. 33.-- *And they failed,* etc. [n: (AUG.)] Whereas they might, by believing, have had their days in truth without failing. *Their days in vanity and their years.* [n: (CASS.)] From the likeness of him who dies at his rising: he was born in vain. *With haste.* [n: (AUG.)] And the life which seems the longer [0970D] passes quickly.

VERS. 34.-- *They sought him.* [n: (CASS. et AUG.)] Not those slain, but those left. Yet he speaks thus because they are one people, as of one body. *Early they came to him,* etc. [n: (CASS.)] Diligently, or before the light; whence: *It is vain for you to rise before the light.* The godly man prays in the light, the ungodly before the light. *They sought.* They returned, they came. [n: (AUG.)] All these things for the sake of gaining temporal goods and of avoiding evils: and so not God, but those things were worshipped.

VERS. 37.-- *Neither were they counted faithful in the covenant,* etc. [n: (CASS.)] He who truly believes does works: whence faith is so called because the things said are done; whence he says: *Nor faithful in his precepts.*



## PSALM LXXVII. (cont.)

[0971A]VERS. 38.-- *But he is merciful, and will be propitious.* [n: (AUG.)] Here the wicked promise themselves impunity, but there is mercy even for the wicked, yet before the judgment, because he makes the sun to rise upon the good and the evil. So he spared that nation also, that he might not root it out utterly, but it abides in the succession of those that come after, as Cain when he had received the sign, that none should slay him. *Their sins,* etc. [n: (AUG.)] Of the people, which is the same in the succession of the sons; as also afterwards *he took them away out of Egypt, and brought them into the mountain of his sanctification [n: (Gen. IV)]*, whereas not them, save in the succession. So here too, when it is said: *he will be propitious to their sins,* we understand that also to be fulfilled which is said by the Apostle: *The remnant shall be saved [n: (Rom. IV)]*, which shall believe in Christ, and this he foresaw here, when he adds: *And he scattered them not. And he abounded that [0971B] he might turn away,* [var: Vulg. Ps 77:38 *et non disperdet eos*, "and he will not destroy them"] etc., because: *He kindled not all his wrath,* etc. That is, he did not punish them as much as they deserved: because he forgave even this, that they slew the Son.

VERS. 39.-- *A spirit that goeth and returneth not,* etc. [var: Vulg. Ps 77:39 *spiritus vadens et non rediens*, "that goeth and cometh not again"] Therefore he calls them back by grace, because by themselves they cannot return: whence in the Proverbs it is said of the way of iniquity: *All that walk in it shall not return [n: (Prov. II)]* [var: Vulg. Pr 2:19 *Omnes qui ingrediuntur ad eam non revertentur*, "all that go in unto her shall not return"], namely by themselves, but by grace they shall be called back. This is the wandering sheep, which was brought back to the flock upon the shoulders of the loving shepherd.

VERS. 40.-- *How often.* [n: (CASS.)] Being about to tell the plagues of Egypt, wrought for their sake, by comparing their evils with the benefits of God, he says that they forgot, and that they tempted God. [n: (AUG.)] Because they are flesh, or a spirit that goes, how often then? as though to say, they provoked him many times.

[0971C]VERS. 41.-- *And they turned back,* etc. [n: (AUG.)] He repeats their unbelief, that he may call to mind what plagues God brought upon the Egyptians for their sake, whence they ought to have been not ungrateful.

VERS. 42.-- *From the hand of tribulation.* [var: Vulg. Ps 77:42 *de manu tribulantis*, "from the hand of him that afflicted them"] [n: (CASS.)] Tribulation is wont to be kept in memory, but not by them. Beautifully he sets the hand of God against the hand of Pharao, saying: *His hand and from the hand of [sic: tribu];* which differ greatly. [n: (AUG. et CASS.)] Mystical interpretations from this place.

VERS. 44, 45.-- *And he turned,* etc. *He sent upon them wrath,* etc. Here he begins to set forth the plagues wrought in Egypt for the deliverance of the Jews, and he sets down ten plagues, as in Exodus ten. Yet certain things are set down here which are not in Exodus, which we cannot interpret save figuratively, where also the things that were done are called parables, [0971D] as he is often wont not to follow the order of the history, but to add other things, or to change certain things for the sake of the signification. *Water* therefore *into blood* signifies to think carnally of the causes of things. *The dog-fly.* [var: Vulg. Ps 77:45 *coenomyiam*, the common fly] They are dog-like manners. *The frog.* Talkative vanity.

VERS. 46, 47.-- *Blight.* Which others interpret as rust, harms in secret, and is likened to a vice that hardly appears, such as to trust much in oneself. For a noxious breath works this in the fruits unseen: so in manners does hidden pride, when he thinks himself to be something who is nothing. *The locust.* Malice hurting with the mouth, namely by faithless testimony.

*Hail.* It is iniquity carrying off the goods of another; whence thefts, robberies, plunderings. But the waster himself is more wasted [0972A] by his own vice. *Frost.* The vice by which charity toward one's neighbour freezes in the darkness of folly as with the cold of night.

VERS. 48.-- *Fire.* Which in Exodus is not taken save with the hail in the lightnings, that it might hurt the fruits: which nevertheless is not read to have burned the possessions, signifies the monstrousness of anger, by which murder also is done. By the death of the cattle the loss of chastity is signified. Concupiscence, from which offspring arises, is common to us with the beasts: to have this tamed and ordered is the virtue of chastity. *The death of the firstborn.* It is the loss of the righteousness by which each man is sociable to the human race. The Egyptians were smitten with ten plagues against the ten commandments written on the tables. And here are ten plagues, though they be not set down in that order, but in place of three which [0972B] are in Exodus, that is the sciniphs, the boils, the darkness, which are not here. Three others are here which are not there, rust, frost, and fire. For such things are frequently varied for a fitting understanding. *Signs.* [n: (CASS.)] They are, upon hard hearts, as it were the traces of a stamp impressed. *Wonders,* which betokened certain things to come. *Thanis,* [var: Vulg. Ps 77:12 and 43 *Taneos*] a city, the humble commandment, which is necessary for us here, that in the time to come we may be raised up. *And he gave to the blight.* These things are not read in Exodus, therefore let them be expounded only by a mystical interpretation. *The locust.* [n: (AUG.)] Devouring one another, it signifies detractors, who out of envy gnaw the fruits of others. *Their mulberry trees with frost.* [n: (AUG.)] The mulberry is of a warm nature, by which charity is understood, which is killed by the frost. *To the hail* [n: (ID.)] The things the hail could not hurt, that is the soft ones, Exodus afterwards says were eaten [0972C] by the locusts. *He delivered up.* [n: (CASS.)] By the death of the cattle the fall of fools is shown.

[n: (ID.)] *To fire. Fire* is covetousness, which lays waste the possession, that is, the state of the mind.

VERS. 49.-- *The wrath of his indignation,* etc. [n: (CASS.)] The order of vengeance: first God is angry at their sins, and when they are not pricked in heart, then he abandons the malice of man in his own indignation, wrath and tribulation, that they may be afflicted by their own adversities who do not obey God, as it is said there: *Therefore he delivered them up unto a reprobate sense [n: (Rom. I)]*. The indignation of man belongs to swollen pride; wrath, to unspeakable audacity; tribulation, to confounded despair: then through the devil they are hurled headlong into unlawful assaults, stripped of the defence of God. *By evil angels.* [n: (AUG.)] By the judgment of God [0972D] these things were done, etc., *as far as* by evil angels therefore God punishes both good and evil men, but the good with bodily punishments only, the evil with punishments both bodily and spiritual.

VERS. 50.-- *Of his wrath.* [n: (AUG.)] There was a path of the wrath of God, by which with hidden equity he might punish the Egyptians; and for it God made a way, that he might bring them forth as out of hidden places, through evil angels, into open crimes, and so punish them openly.

VERS. 51.-- *The firstborn in Egypt.* [n: (AUG.)] The firstborn are the highest commandments, as: Thou shalt love the Lord thy God with thy whole heart; and when these are not kept we are bereaved of the offspring of reason.

VERS. 52.-- *And he took away his people as sheep, [0973A] and he led them like a flock in the desert.* [n: (AUG.)] Mystically. This is done within us also, where, being taught, we are carried over in mind out of the power of darkness into the kingdom of God, sheep in spiritual pastures. [n: (CASS.)] *And he took away.* In that he led those brought out of bondage into the land of promise; and again they offend.

VERS. 54.-- *And he brought them into the mountain of his sanctification.* [n: (AUG.)] Not the same men whom he took away, but those of the same name and nation; yet it signifies that many were converted to the Church, which he would rather have understood; she is the mountain which Christ purchased. *And he cast out the nations from before them,* etc. [n: (AUG.)] Or the malignant spirits. All which things Christ cast out from before the faithful; *and he divided them the land by lot.* By this it is signified that one and the same Spirit works all things in us, dividing [0973B] to every one according as he will.

VERS. 55.-- *In their tents.* [n: (CASS.)] In which sins reigned, he made virtues to reign.

VERS. 57.-- *Even as their fathers were turned,* etc. [n: (AUG.)] Throughout he speaks as though of the same men: yet now of those in the land of promise, whose fathers they were who embittered him in the desert. *A crooked bow,* etc. [n: (ID.)] The perverse intention of the mind, as against God, who had said: *Thou shalt not have strange gods [n: (Exod. XX)]* [var: Vulg. Ex 20:3 *Non habebis deos alienos coram me*, "thou shalt not have strange gods before me"], which he opens more fully. *Provoked unto wrath,* or, *into a crooked bow,* which is unbent with difficulty, and they themselves easily went back. Or, *a crooked bow,* which does not shoot from afar, but turns its darts upon itself, that is, the malice of the deceitful.

VERS. 58.-- *To jealousy.* Or they were jealous; [0973C] another reading, when they give the honour of God to another.

VERS. 59.-- *God heard.* [n: (CASS.)] Of the vengeance in the land of promise, which was turned back also upon the very men through whom he punished.

VERS. 60.-- *And he put away the tabernacle.* [n: (AUG.)] At that time of Eli the priest, when they were overcome by the Allophyli, and the ark of the Lord was taken, and they themselves laid waste with a great slaughter. For it is read in the book of Kings that there fell then of the people thirty thousand footmen. *He put away the tabernacle of Silo.* [n: (BEDA.)] Which was in Silo, etc., *as far as* but because they did not attend to it, nor made a tabernacle for God: therefore the Lord deservedly put it away. *He dwelt among men.* [n: (AUG.)] Since therefore there was nothing worthy in which he might dwell, he put away that which he had appointed not for his own sake, but for theirs. [0973D] [n: (CASS.)] So he forsakes our bodies, which the Holy Spirit inhabits while they are well treated.

VERS. 61.-- *And he delivered up.* [n: (CASS.)] For if the good too are delivered into captivity along with sinners, it is a discipline; they are not captives in mind. Or, delivered to the enemy, but free and joined to God. *Of their virtues.* [var: Vulg. Ps 77:61 *virtutem*, "their strength"] [n: (AUG.)] He diminished the liberty of choice, and took away the virtues in the wicked, which is not so in the good, although at times they are taken captive bodily along with the wicked.

VERS. 65.-- *And he was awakened.* [n: (CASS. et AUG.)] He ascribes the manners of men to the Lord, for the sake of explaining the matter [sic: *proptes*].

*As one asleep.* This is said in the person of the mad, who think thus, when he does not come to the aid of those in peril, that he is drunk with wine, when he does not come to their aid so [0974A] quickly. And in truth he does sleep for those who so madly think, and is a stranger, both to the negligent and to the unbelieving, who rouse him by no voice of praise or of work. But he is roused to vengeance by evil deeds, as here by the sins of those who set the ark among the idols. *As a mighty man,* because men are then quick to wrath, and mighty for feats of strength, when after a debauch they rise from sleep. [n: (AUG.)] *And he was awakened.* Christ, drunk with the wine of the passion, sleeping in the sepulchre, was awakened from death, as a mighty man in the resurrection. [n: (ID.)] *As one asleep.* He seemed to sleep when he gave his people into the hands of those whom he hates: where it is said to them: *Where is thy God?*

VERS. 66.-- *And he smote iniquity.* [var: Vulg. Ps 77:66 *percussit inimicos suos in posteriora*, "he smote his enemies on the hinder parts"] [n: (AUG.)] He shall be tormented with this punishment, etc., *as far as: reproach* indeed [0974B] *everlasting he gave to them.*

VERS. 67.-- *And he put away.* [n: (CASS.)] Of king David, and of the seat of the kingdom. [n: (AUG.)] He did not say, etc., *as far as* thus he proceeds from the beginning of the election of the Hebrew nation down to Christ.

VERS. 69.-- *As,* etc. [n: (AUG.)] Unicorns are they whose firm hope is raised up unto that one thing, of which it is said: *One thing I have asked of the Lord, this will I seek after. His sanctuary.* It is the holy people, a royal priesthood.

VERS. 70.-- *David his servant.* [n: (AUG.)] Who of Juda, etc., *as far as: the teeth* of the Church are they through whom she speaks, or through whom she passes the rest into her own body as though by eating. *A flock of shorn sheep* it is called, because it laid down the burdens of the world as fleeces, and laid at the feet of the Apostles the prices of the goods it had sold; [0974C] *coming up from the washing* of baptism, it brings forth twins, that is, the works of the twofold charity; whence: *there was not a barren one among them;* from these Christ was carried over, but he feeds among the Gentiles the flocks which are Jacob and Israel.



## PSALM LXXVII. (cont.)

VERS. 71.-- *Jacob. Israel his inheritance.* [n: (AUG.)] He repeats it: Unless it be said that now, according to this present state, Jacob is a *servant:* when he shall see face to face, an *inheritance.* Whence also he received the name: for Israel is so called from the contemplation of God. He carried him over to this end, that he might feed him, and so he did.

VERS. 72.-- *And he fed them,* etc. [n: (AUG.)] It seems perhaps, etc., *as far as* and he taught this which he did.

## PSAL. LXXVIII.

VERS. 1.-- *A psalm of Asaph.* [n: (CASS.)] The second psalm of lamentation. He prays Christ to come to the help of the nation which for its hardness is to be grievously [0974D] afflicted. We are admonished to rejoice at the good things of our neighbours, and to be afflicted at their calamities.

*O God, the nations are come.* [n: (ID.)] The first thing is the complaint, and there are numbered how great things Jerusalem bore in the times of Antiochus, when it was not destroyed, and by these the persecution of the Church is signified. [n: (AUG.)] *They are come.* He does not speak as to one that knows not, etc., *as far as* but now they are plainly fulfilled.

*Into thy inheritance.* [n: (ID.)] Of the people of Israel; the inheritance of God are the elect, but not the blinded; therefore these things can be said of the times after the ascension of Christ; but also before Christ had come, for then too there was an inheritance, where the Prophets were, and the saints led away into Babylon; or most of all under Antiochus. [n: (CASS.)] But if [0975A] any man, etc., *as far as* whose circuit is through the whole world, where the persecution also is.

VERS. 2.-- *The dead bodies.* [n: (CASS.)] Carcases, as though they raged not only against the living. To bury belongs to piety, to cast forth belongs to cruelty. *They have poured out.* [n: (ID.)] He heightens it, that he may move the judge.

VERS. 4.-- *We are become.* [n: (CASS.)] The glory of Jerusalem, wonderful in the whole world, is turned into *a reproach to our neighbours,* which is heavier than if it were to men far off, because these are always at hand. Or, the neighbours are the affections, the kinsfolk being now willing to believe, so that the martyrs say this.

VERS. 5.-- *How long, O Lord?* [n: (CASS.)] A prayer on his own behalf against enemies and insulting neighbours; as though they rage thus: But, O Lord, how long? [n: (AUG.)] He asks that he be not angry unto the end, that is, that [0975B] that laying waste may not continue, but that he temper his correction. By the name of *wrath* is understood the vengeance upon iniquity, by the name of *jealousy,* the exacting of chastity, that the soul may not commit fornication away from God. Note that this befalls men for their sins, though they be just, as the three children and the Prophets in the captivity bear witness. *As fire.* [n: (ID.)] Burn so long, until the gold of the virtues be perfectly purged, and the wood, that is the vices, be turned to ashes.

VERS. 7.-- *They have devoured.* [n: (AUG.)] By delivering them to the teeth of wild beasts. *They have laid waste,* by the overthrow of the city, which was stripped of its own citizens, or because they slew those put in the place of the citizens who had been slain; Jacob for the man, the man, that is, for the nation which offended. The patriarch is pleasing, that the memory of him may soften the sins of the people. *Jacob.* [n: (AUG.)] This is the inheritance spoken of above. *The place.* [n: (CASS.)] The city, where the temple was, to which they were gathered for the feasts.

[0975C]VERS. 8.-- *Of our iniquities.* [n: (BEDA.)] That is, those which we have contracted from our fathers, to whom, that is to say, correction is no longer owed, but damnation, unless the compassion of thy grace be at hand. For it is written: *The soul that shall sin, the same shall die.* And, *I will visit the sins of the fathers upon the children, unto the third and fourth generation [n: (Ezech. XVIII.)] Of old.* [var: Vulg. Ex 20:5 *visitans iniquitatem patrum in filios, in tertiam et quartam generationem*, "visiting the iniquity of the fathers upon the children"] (CASS.) He does not speak of past ones only, since in this time also certain Jews were following idols; but of the like of the ancient ones, in which they sinned through countless years. *Let them prevent us.* [n: (ID.)] Before the judgment which every man fears, because unless indulgence go before in the judgment, the guilty man is not acquitted. *Exceedingly.* Lest any man presume upon his deeds.

VERS. 9.-- *Help us.* [n: (CASS.)] In the war for the defence of the laws, because they trust not in their own powers, but in the powers of God. [0975D] *For the glory.* Not for our merit; that, being delivered, we may render honour to thy name. *For the glory of thy name.* Let it be ascribed to the goodness of the holy name, not to us, since it cannot be had by our own deeds. *Help us, O God.* By this he is neither ungrateful to grace, nor does he take away free choice. For he who is helped does something also through himself. *Deliver us.* [n: (AUG.)] He delivers, that is, he rescues from evils, since he both helps us to do righteousness, and is propitious to the sins without which there is no living here.

VERS. 10.-- *Lest perhaps.* [n: (AUG.)] That word customary with the faithless: Where is he? Either he is not, or he cannot; an insult not to be borne. *Let it be made known.* Either here iniquity is destroyed in those converted, which he had wished for before, or, at the end [0976A] they perish, which he says here not by wishing it, but by prophesying. [n: (AUG.)] Because the just man is not delighted at the punishment of his enemy, since he does not hate him, but at the divine justice, since he loves God; the unjust man does the contrary.

VERS. 11.-- *Of them that are in fetters.* [n: (AUG.)] By the corruptibility of the flesh, which weighs down the soul, and through which the persecutor sometimes prevails: these fetters none feel save those who groan being burdened; from these the Apostle desired to be loosed, and to be with Christ. Or, the fetters are the commandments of God, by which the saints are bound together, which, patiently borne, are turned into ornaments, whence: *Put thy foot into her fetters [n: (Eccli. VI)]*. [n: (CASS.)] *Of them that are in fetters,* he speaks according to the letter of those whom the Gentiles bound: who pray with groaning that the Church may not fail in her brethren, and so [0976B] the punishment consumes them. *According to the greatness.* [n: (AUG.)] For a thing so great followed among Christians as the enemies would never have believed. *The children,* because they were kindled by the example, not terrified. *Possess the children of them that have been put to death.* [n: (ID.)] This the holy martyrs always prayed, that their blood might not be unfruitful to those that came after, that the crop might grow from that whence it was thought that it would perish. [n: (CASS.)] A godly prayer, that he cast not away the children of those who were punished for him. *Possess,* that they be stained by no error.

VERS. 12.-- *And render.* [n: (CASS.)] Although he renders it bodily, yet it is the salvation of their souls. *Sevenfold,* it signifies the perfection of the heavenly gift, when we are filled with the Holy Spirit. *Into their bosom.* In the secret place of the mind, where conversion takes place. *Their reproach,* let them render praises for injuries: a glorious vindication, when God is praised by the mouth [0976C] of his enemies. Or *sevenfold.* By the sevenfold retribution he signifies the perfection of the punishment, as also of good things, when it is said that he shall receive sevenfold in this world: which is set down for all good things, as having nothing, and possessing all things; because in this number fulness is signified. Or *sevenfold,* for what is without end. *Their reproach,* [n: (AUG.)] Render this, that is, for that reproach give them up to a reprobate sense. For man, when he is given up to a reprobate sense, that is, in his inward sense, receives the desert of the punishments to come.

VERS. 13.-- *But we.* [n: (CASS.)] A conclusion of exultation, not of conquerors, but of sufferers. And a sweet conclusion of the lamentation. [n: (AUG.)] *But we.* These are the remnant gathered by Matathias. Or, generally, it is to be taken of every kind of the godly and of true Christians, [0976D] so that the Christian people also is mingled there; this is the flock for which he prayed, that he might not be angry any longer. [n: (ID.)] Or, *in the generation,* that is, in the world to come we shall declare it, not for the instruction of others, but for the honour that is due.



## PSALM LXXIX.

VERS. 1.-- *Understanding, for them that shall be changed.* [var: Vulg.
