Ps 79:1 *In finem, pro iis qui commutabuntur*, "Unto the end, for them that shall be changed"] [n: (AUG.)] For the better, etc., *as far as* a testimony of faithlessness. *Asaph.* Being enlightened [sic: *Illuminatur*] he treats of the coming of Christ and of his vineyard, a good testimony concerning the head himself and his body, the king and the people, the shepherd and the flock.

VERS. 2.-- *Thou that rulest,* etc. [n: (AUG.)] Here Asaph, enlightened, speaks of the coming of Christ, who converts [0977A] man, and makes firm the vineyard brought out of Egypt: this is the testimony for them that are changed. [n: (CASS.)] Above he was fearful, who was praying for the sins of the Synagogue: now he presumes, and to those changed for the better he gives a testimony. This psalm is the testimony of the Synagogue concerning the head himself, and his body. [n: (ID.)] A prayer for the first coming, through which the reparation of the human race is made. [n: (ID.)] Being finished, he makes an end. O God, O Son. *Thou that rulest,* spiritually, *Israel,* who see thee, *Give ear,* that is, pour the light of goodness upon us, that we may be bright from thee, who of ourselves are dark. *Joseph.* [n: (ID.)] Thou leadest the faithful people into the folds of God, as sheep to the pastures, of whom: *And other sheep I have that are not of this fold, and them I must bring, that there may be one fold and one shepherd [n: (Joan. X)]*. Each has its own proper word: Israel *is ruled,* [0977B] Joseph *is led,* that is, the devout people. *Be made manifest.* [n: (ID.)] He asks that he who was in the secret of majesty may appear before the Jews through the incarnation, as it had been promised, working in them these powers which are signified by these names, Ephraim, Benjamin, and Manasse. [n: (AUG.)] *Be made manifest before.* Known to the angels, be made known to men.

VERS. 3.-- *Before Ephraim.* [n: (AUG.)] That is, in the nation of the Jews, where these tribes are, namely before fruitfulness, and before the son of the right hand, that we also may be the fulness of knowledge through charity, which is God; and before Manasse, that is, the forgotten one, that thou mayest now come into his mind, according to that saying: *All the ends of the earth shall remember, and shall be converted.* [n: (ID.)] *Stir up.* He repeats, etc., *as far as* crucified through weakness, rise again through power. [0977C] [n: (CASS.)] *Stir up thy power* of miracles, etc., *as far as* whence: *God is faithful, who suffereth you not to be tempted above that which you are able,* etc. [n: (I Cor. X)].

VERS. 4.-- *Thy face.* [n: (AUG.)] This face was veiled to Asaph in the time of the weakness of Christ, and then Asaph was sick, not being able to look upon him veiled with a cloud: of which it is said: *God ascended upon a light cloud,* [var: Vulg. Is 19:1 *Ecce Dominus ascendet super nubem levem*, "behold, the Lord will ascend upon a light cloud"] and he shall enter, or shall go into, Egypt, that is, flesh free from sin; but he enlightened it when he rose again and ascended, and so we are saved.

VERS. 5.-- *How long wilt thou be angry?* [n: (AUG.)] Wrath passes from the servant, that he may not scourge, as is plain, but that he may not condemn: for wrath endures even toward those converted, but as a wrath that corrects. *For he scourgeth every son whom he receiveth [n: (Isa. IX, Heb. XII)]*.

VERS. 6.-- *Thou wilt feed* [n: (AUG.)] Here he sets forth the sufferings [0977D] which the saints are to suffer at the time of the coming. As though he said: Although I long for the coming of Christ, yet at that time we are to suffer many things, because *he fed,* etc. *In measure,* etc. [n: (AUG.)] According to thy strength, that thou mayest be instructed, not crushed; whence: *God is faithful, who suffereth you not to be tempted above,* etc. [n: (I Cor. X)]. Or, according to the measure of faults.

VERS. 7.-- *Thou hast made us a contradiction.* [n: (CASS.)] While our neighbours, who are of us, are against us. Our enemies mock us, from all of whom, that we may be saved, *O God of hosts,* etc.

VERS. 8.-- *O God of hosts.* [n: (AUG.)] It is the way of one who desires, to repeat often what he prays for with such prayers.

VERS. 9, 10.-- *A vineyard.* [n: (CASS.)] Of the vineyard, how it was [0978A] begun, and perfected. [n: (AUG. et CASS.)] *A vineyard out of Egypt,* as if to say, we shall be saved, but thou wilt destroy the Jews, for whom thou didst so much: and that not for a light fault, but because they did not know the coming of Christ. Or, we shall be saved because thou hast so foreshown it. *A vineyard out of Egypt.* But why wilt thou destroy? this is of the unbelieving. *Out of Egypt.* As he cast out those men from Egypt, so he cast the Church out of the darkness of vices: he *plants* the Gentiles in virtues, and *leads* them through this desert of the world. *The roots,* he makes the martyrs and others firm, and fills the world. [n: (CASS.)] *A vineyard out of Egypt,* through mystical figures, etc., *as far as* which has been so spread abroad through the world. *A guide,* while he leads her by a spiritual journey. *The roots,* the prophets, the Apostles, the martyrs and others, from whom, stretching out her *branches,* she takes and covers the world. *A guide of the way.* [n: (AUG.)] Or another [0978B] reading, etc., *as far as* and it is not another, but the same vineyard: thence the Apostles, thence also the other just men, although some branches were broken off.

VERS. 11, 12-- *It covered* [n: (CASS.)] The height and the breadth of the vineyard are here set forth. The vineyard is from above, which overshadows even the loftier mountains with life and doctrine, and makes them grow great. Vines that climb trees are called *bushes,* by which the lesser ones in the Church are understood. *Cedars,* not the proud, but those that are *God's,* are the martyrs, who are high in the Church, and these too the Church protects in her bosom. *The shoots,* the sons of regeneration, who flowed from the river Jordan, because there the rule of baptism began. *The mountains.* [n: (AUG.)] The prophets, etc., *as far as* likewise *they cover the cedars of God,* that is, the doctors. *The cedars of God.* [n: (ID.)] Because there are cedars [0978C] to be overthrown, namely the proud, who are the cedars of the world, but not of God.

VERS. 13.-- *Why.* He wonders why the defence of the Lord was withdrawn from such a one as this. [n: (AUG.)] *Why.* Because she was proud against the planter, and slew the servants sent to her, and at last the son and heir, the fence was destroyed, and the nations found her destroyed, and blotted her out. This Asaph first bewails, but not without hope: for he speaks now of those who direct their heart.

VERS. 14.-- *The boar.* [n: (AUG. et CASS.)] Vespasian, strong and savage, and hostile to the Jews, who among other creatures hold this beast unclean. *A singular wild beast.* Titus, who consumed the remnant with a terrible devouring, who is called *singular,* that is, proud. Or the boar and the *wild beast* are taken for the same thing, namely the Roman army. [0978D] And see by what sins man labours and perishes. *Singular.* [n: (AUG.)] Every proud man says: *I am, I am, and there is none other. The boar.* [n: (CASS.)] The devil, strong and fierce, *out of the wood,* because his thoughts are wild and trackless. *But thou, O Lord God of hosts.* Or continue it thus: So the vineyard was laid waste, but that the remnant may be saved, *O God of hosts, turn again,* and at the end of the last age serenely *behold,* he prays this remedy for those changed for the better.

VERS. 16.-- *And perfect thou that which thy right hand hath planted.* [n: (AUG.)] Set up no other, but perfect this one. For in this is the seed of Abraham: there is the root that bears the wild olive. Perfect it, *because blindness in part has happened in Israel, that the fulness of the Gentiles might come in, and so all [0979A] Israel should be saved [n: (Rom. XII)]* [var: Vulg. Rom 11:25 *donec plenitudo gentium intraret*, "until the fulness of the Gentiles should come in"]. *Upon the son.* [n: (CASS.)] Founded upon and [cj: *et*; read *te*, "thee"], etc., *as far as* but of the fire of good love it is said: *I am come to send fire on the earth, and what will I, but that it burn? [n: (Luc. XII.)]* [var: Vulg. Lc 12:49 *nisi ut accendatur*, "but that it be kindled"]

VERS. 18.-- *Let thy hand be.* [n: (CASS.)] A great sacrament, and a gift to God, because the salvation of Israel could be doubted so long, until Christ came: but then the promise was fulfilled, nor does the Church, joined to her spouse, depart from him any more. He says therefore: *Let thy hand be,* he prays for the working of the holy incarnation.

VERS. 19.-- *We depart not.* [n: (CASS.)] Any more, when *thou shalt quicken* the son of man: this is the profit that comes from him.

VERS. 20.-- *O Lord God of hosts, convert us, show thy face, and we shall be saved.* [n: (CASS.)] Great is the sweetness of this little verse, in which he sets forth the rule of the whole of religion. [0979B] For first God *converts* us, and then he shows us *before his face,* that is, he looks upon us with the light of his goodness: after which he at last *saves* us. This verse therefore the prophet repeats, because through it each man briefly implores what is profitable.



## PSALM LXXX.

VERS. 1.-- *For the winepresses.* [n: (AUG.)] In which there are three things, etc., *as far as* a psalm is sung for Asaph, that is, for the Lord's congregation. *Asaph,* etc. [n: (CASS.)] Because according to the history he speaks to the Jews, spiritually to Christians. [n: (AUG.)] First he says what is to be done by the elect, who are the oil, the wine, the birds.

VERS. 2.-- *He rejoiceth in God.* [var: Vulg. Ps 80:2 *Exsultate Deo adjutori nostro*, the plural imperative, "rejoice ye in God our helper"] Asaph, having set before himself the image of a certain solemnity, [0979C] stirs up the elect to the festival.

VERS. 3.-- *Take a psalm.* [n: (AUG.)] The words of God, that human acts may come together into one fellowship: for this is the peace of Jerusalem, this is the reckoning of what is given and what is received; whence the Apostle: *If we have sown unto you spiritual things, is it a great matter if we reap your carnal things [n: (I Cor. IX)]*.

VERS. 4.-- *Blow the trumpet.* [n: (AUG.)] Thus far, up to the division, these things are to be taken either according to the letter, simply of the Jews, or allegorically of Christians. [n: (AUG.)] *Blow the trumpet,* and declare, etc., *as far as* whence Isaias: *Cry, cease not, lift up thy voice like a trumpet,* etc. *On the new moon with the trumpet.* [n: (CASS.)] At the beginning of the month the Jews were bidden to sound the trumpet, that is, at the beginning of the new moon, which is also called the feast of trumpets, which they celebrated [0979D] in memory of that thing, that the Lord showed Abraham the ram sticking among the briers, which he offered up in the place of Isaac.

VERS. 5.-- *For it is a commandment in Israel; and a judgment,* etc. [n: (ID.)] Therefore the trumpet must be sounded, because the law was given to the Jews through Moses, and because he is to be their judge.

VERS. 6.-- *In Joseph.* [n: (AUG.)] The Gentiles are Joseph, etc., *as far as* where he ought to have had a heart.

VERS. 7.-- *From the burdens.* [n: (AUG.)] By what burdens: *hands in the basket,* that is, by servile work. Sins are servile works, according to that saying: *Whosoever committeth sin is the servant of sin [n: (Joan. VIII)]*. *They served in the basket. [n: (ID.)]* In the uncleannesses of sins, in earthly and miry works.

[0980A]VERS. 8.-- *In tribulation,* etc. [n: (CASS.)] So the Prophet: the Lord turns aside, saying, *In tribulation,* the benefits of God are reckoned to them, that the offence may be judged the heavier. *I heard thee in the secret place.* [n: (AUG.)] That with the heart thou mayest believe unto justice. *I proved thee,* that with the mouth confession may be made unto salvation. *Of the storm,* etc. Not in the storm of the sea, but of the heart: where he that is heard must be proved, in the water of contradiction: for he that already walks the way of God has men withstanding him in many ways. This is the water of contradiction, which the ancients felt, but now it is well dried up: for then the waters of the peoples were troubled, and resisted fiercely. Then to Samson, that strong man, that is, to Christ, going to take a wife from among the strangers, that is, the Church from among the Gentiles, there met a roaring lion, that is, the people of the Gentiles, but he tore him in pieces as a kid [0980B] of the goats: and because that roaring people was nothing but a sickly sinner. But when that fierceness had been slain in him, he no longer roars so; nay, in that very people we now find laws in favour of the Church, as it were a honeycomb in the mouth of the lion. *The water of contradiction.* Christ was so even in himself, as it was said of him when he was born: *This one shall be unto the ruin, and the resurrection of many, for a sign which shall be contradicted [n: (Luc. II)]* [var: Vulg. Lc 2:34 *Ecce positus est hic in ruinam et in resurrectionem multorum in Israel*, "behold, this child is set for the ruin and for the resurrection of many in Israel"], as the Jews contradicted the title of the cross.

VERS. 9.-- *Hear, O my people.* [n: (CASS.)] Diapsalma. Thus far of the oil of the winepresses, now of the lees, which is said by way of warning; here the reprobate are reproved. *I will testify unto thee.* The Lord himself makes himself the witness of the judgment of truth, both here and in the world to come, that none may doubt. Then he adds what he would have him understand: Israel, if thou wilt hearken to me; and by calling him Israel he signifies one familiar to himself, [0980C] and invites him to his commands. *In thee,* in thy heart, where truth, not falsehood, ought to be. *New,* lest the Word of the Father be thought to be under time. *A strange god,* one that is such in name, not in essence. *Strange:* It is well-nigh an equal crime to think God new, and to serve idols.

VERS. 10.-- *There shall be no new god in thee, neither,* etc. [n: (AUG.)] Some make themselves new gods, either idols, or a Christ unequal to the Father, and feign other such things in the heart: and so in all of them earthly thought is the temple of idols; the opinion is various, the vanity one. Therefore Samson bound together the tails of the foxes, that he might set fire to the harvests of the Philistines. Samson is interpreted *their sun,* namely of those to whom he himself shines, not of all: of whom the wicked shall say: *We have erred from the way of truth, and the sun of justice hath not shined upon us [n: (Sap. V)]* [var: Vulg. Sap 5:6 *justitiae lumen non luxit nobis, et sol intelligentiae non est ortus nobis*, "the light of justice hath not shined unto us, and the sun of understanding hath not risen upon us"]. And elsewhere: *He is the sun [0980D] of justice, and health is in his wings [n: (Malac. IV)]*. The foxes are the treacherous, and most of all the heretics, lurking in winding caverns, deceiving, stinking with their smell: who, although they seem to disagree with one another, yet agree in evil, that is, in their hinder parts, and are held by a like vanity, and burn the harvests, not of our own, because God knows who are his, but of the strangers, which are vessels of dishonour.

VERS. 11.-- *Who brought thee out.* God is pointed out by his own deeds.

VERS. 13.-- *And he let them go.* And although I filled the mouths of the Prophets, yet they were not heard by the lees, whence a fitting vengeance: because *and I let them go,* etc.

VERS. 15.-- *Perhaps for nothing.* [n: (CASS.)] If we shall resist God, [0981A] he himself does not contradict our enemies, nor does he lay the hand of defence upon them.

VERS. 16.-- *The enemies of the Lord,* etc. [n: (AUG.)] These things likewise the Prophet. *They have lied.* Not through men performing what was promised: this the pagans do not, because they promised nothing.

VERS. 17.-- *With the fat of corn,* etc. [n: (AUG.)] According to the letter, either of the manna, or of the body of Christ, or with a spiritual understanding.



## PSALM LXXXI.

VERS. 1.-- *A psalm of Asaph.* [n: (CASS.)] A psalm on the two natures in Christ, in which Christ, or the Prophet speaking, reproaches Asaph himself, that is, the Synagogue, which saw the Lord bodily, and yet did not know him. First he announces that God has stood, and [0981B] convicts the adversaries: man stands, God discerns. *God hath stood.* Whose part it is not to stand, who filleth heaven and earth, who is within all things not enclosed, outside all things not excluded; but briefly he indicates God as man. *He hath stood.* [n: (AUG.)] The Son, who was sent to the sheep of the house of Israel; but the spiritual presence of the Godhead, which, being everywhere, does not properly belong to the Synagogue, nor is varied by time. Why he stood there: *Behold, he judgeth gods,* [var: Vulg. Ps 81:1 *in medio autem deos dijudicat*, "but in the midst he judgeth the gods"] that is, according as he is God, that is, not by merits but by grace he distinguishes the vessels, some unto honour, some unto dishonour. But this he does standing in the midst, whence: *There hath stood one in the midst of you, whom you know not,* whence also here: *They have not known, neither have they understood,* but *the friend of the bridegroom standeth and heareth, and rejoiceth with joy. Of gods.* Whom afterwards he calls *sons of the Most High,* there: *I have said: you are gods, and [0981C] all of you to the sons of the Most High.* Whence also, *I have brought up children and exalted them, but they have despised me.* They are called sons not only by the grace of the New Testament, but by the grace of the Old, which delivered them out of Egypt.

VERS. 2.-- *How long.* [n: (AUG.)] This is the voice of him that discerneth: he sent the law and the Prophets, and you resisted; but how long? Did I send Prophets until the coming, and you afflicted them with injuries, or slew them, or consented to those who did this? But since it is not worthy even to speak to those who do such things, you who kept silence, and wished to imitate them, *how long do you judge iniquity, and accept the persons of sinners?* Is the heir also to be slain, who came for you, who for your sake willed to be without a father, as a fatherless child? who for your sake hungered and thirsted, as a needy man, who for your sake was made poor, when he was rich.

[0981D]VERS. 3.-- *Judge for the needy and the fatherless, justify the poor and the humble.* [var: Vulg. Ps 81:3 *humilem et pauperem justificate*, the two transposed] [n: (CASS.)] Diapsalma, he admonishes that one should understand Christ to be God, even if he was needy and poor, which he says that they did not do.

VERS. 4.-- *Rescue the poor.* [n: (AUG.)] He shows that not even those are guiltless who left Christ to the princes, since they were feared on account of their multitude, and could have freed them from the deed, and themselves from consent. [n: (CASS.)] For he who ceases to withstand when he can, consents.

VERS. 5.-- *They shall be moved.* [n: (CASS.)] Hence it is plain of whom he had spoken: this sign happened at the crucifixion. Or spiritually, earthly men were moved at that time, so great a miracle being seen; as the centurion, who said: *Indeed this was the Son of God.* [n: (AUG.)] *All the foundations shall be moved.* [0982A] As if he should say: through this blindness of Israel, *shall be moved* unto faith *all the foundations of the earth,* that is, the fulness of the Gentiles shall come in. Or, in the death and passion of the Lord the earth was moved. Or *the foundations,* that is, those made happy by abundance of earthly things, shall be moved. Or by wondering that a poor dead man is worshipped. Or by following after him, earthly things being despised. He adds *all,* because there is none who does not wonder, or is not converted. But the kingdom of earthly felicity is pride, against which the humble Christ came, reproaching them whom he wills to make high through humility, in these words: *I have said: you are gods,* you the good: *but you* the evil, *shall die like men.* Or he says to all: *you are gods,* that is, to all I promised heavenly things; *but you as men,* the first men, who sinned by persuasion, and you greater ones as the devil, who by pride [0982B] sinned.

VERS. 6.-- *I have said.* [n: (CASS.)] What was set before them? but they perished. *I have said: They have not known, neither have they understood;* yet *I have said.* All receive these promises, if they keep the commandments, that they may be sons, and of God: this to the just; *but you,* this to the wicked.

VERS. 7.-- *Like men.* [n: (AUG.)] As if: Whereas you are quickly to die, like men, you are not corrected thereby; but like the devil, who dies not in the flesh, you are lifted up, that you may fall: because through diabolical pride they envied the glory of Christ, and the humility of the cross became vile to them. But, that this vice may be healed, there is subjoined in the person of the Prophet: *Arise, O God,* etc., *as one.* The crowd which fell is called one, because it is alike in pride: You shall fall, but:

[0982C]VERS. 8.-- *Arise, O God.* [n: (AUG.)] That is, condemn these, because, these being cast off, thou shalt inherit. Or judge the world, because it is thine, and this is: thou shalt inherit.



## PSALM LXXXII.

VERS. 1.-- *A canticle of a psalm.* [n: (CASS.)] Because in things active divine contemplation is to be held. But he deters the wicked from the persecution of the people of God: while the good he admonishes unto patience. A psalm on the coming of Christ to judgment, where the good, who here suffer, shall be crowned: but the wicked, who persecute them, shall be tormented.

VERS. 2.-- *O God, who is like?* [n: (CASS.)] The fear of the judge to come being set forth, he describes the malice of the enemies. [n: (AUG.)] *O God, who is like?* Though a man, as others; though [0982D] judged, thou art reckoned with the wicked: who is like, when thou shalt judge in brightness? therefore then be not silent, thou who at the first wast silent, and didst restrain thy power, that thou mightest be held and slain. *O God, who is like?* This is not referred to the divinity, to which he thought that no man was to be compared. Therefore he says not: *Who is like to thee?* which would certainly be referred to the divinity; but he says, *Who shall be like to thee?* referring this to the form of a servant, in which he shall then be unlike the rest of men, when he shall appear as judge in glory; or this can be referred to the divinity according to what he says: *O God, who is like to thee?* in the power of the Godhead: for Christ, whom the wicked saw without, this one sees within, namely as almighty.

[0983A]VERS. 3.-- *For behold,* etc. [n: (AUG.)] He designates the last times of Antichrist, when the things which are now kept down by fear shall break out into free utterance: which utterance, because it was irrational, is called rather a noise than a speech. *They that hate.* [n: (CASS.)] Many, but one head, Antichrist: which is lifted up, that he may be called God.

VERS. 4.-- *Upon thy people.* They have lifted up their own head, but they have not brought down thy people.

[n: (AUG.)] *Upon thy people.* He says this derisively: for they cannot hurt the people of God, for whom God is, whence the Apostle: *If God be for us, who is against us? [n: (Rom. VIII.)]*

VERS. 5.-- *They have said, Come.* [n: (AUG.)] Behold the counsel, in which *they have devised vain things.* This is that noise, [0983B] which is not speech, but rattles emptily. *From the nation.* [n: (CASS.)] In the singular, because all Christians are one nation, since they are born from one font of baptism; or all the good, whom that Antichrist calls one nation: behold the persecution of Antichrist, who intends to take away utterly the Christian name as an evil thing.

VERS. 6.-- *They have ordained a testament.* [n: (AUG.)] For a testament, in the Scriptures, is not that alone which is not valid unless the testator be dead, but every compact also, and agreement which is valid between the living, is called a testament, as Laban and Jacob made a testament. So also the wicked have made as it were a compact among themselves, that with one mind they should persecute the good.

VERS. 7.-- *The tabernacles of the Idumeans.* He has set forth [0983C] with what zeal they have dealt maliciously: then he shows now who they are that deal maliciously, saying: *The tabernacles of the Idumeans,* etc. *Of the Idumeans, and the Ismaelites.* [n: (CASS.)] By these names he declares the enemies of Christ, who in the times of Antichrist are likened to so many evil men: because as these against Israel, so those shall then war against the Christians. *The Moabites* [n: (AUG., HIER.)] Whence the Moabites, whom Lot, being drunk, begot of his elder daughter. *The Agarens.* From Agar, who are also interpreted *proselytes.*

VERS. 8.-- *Gebal.* [n: (AUG., HIER.)] A vain valley. For *Ge* is valley [sic: *vallit*], *Bal,* vain. *Ammon.* Whence the Ammonites; Lot, being drunk, begot him of his younger daughter.

*Amalec.* The son of Eliphaz the firstborn of Esau, from whom the Amalecites.

VERS. 9.-- *Assur.* [n: (AUG., HIER.)] Literally Nabuchodonosor, [0983D] who was king of the Assyrians. *The Idumeans.* Earthly or bloody. *The Ismaelites.* Obeying themselves, not God, because they follow their own depraved thoughts. *Moab,* from the father, which is in evil. Good indeed was the father, whom the daughter used unlawfully, as the law also is good, if a man use it lawfully, not incestuously. *The Agarens.* Proselytes, that is, newcomers, who are not citizens, but creep in with an alien mind, and, an occasion of hurting being found, show themselves. *Gebal.* A vain valley, that is, deceitfully humble. *Ammon.* A troubled people, or a people of grief. *Amalec.* A people licking the earth. *Strangers.* Although by a Latin name they declare themselves aliens, and for this cause enemies, yet in Hebrew they are called *Philisthiim,* that is, falling by drink, as men drunken with worldly luxury. *Tyre.* In Hebrew *sor,* tribulation or anguish, according to that [0984A] saying. *Tribulation or anguish upon every soul of man that worketh evil. [n: (Rom. II.)] Assur.* [var: Vulg. Rom 2:9 *Tribulatio et angustia*, "and", not "or"] Depressing, or lifted up. *Lot.* Declining: the apostate angels, sons of declension, because by declining from the truth they departed into the retinue of the devil, whence in the Apocalypse: The dragon fell, and drew with him the third part of the stars, of whom the Apostle: *Our wrestling is not against flesh and blood, but against principalities and powers [n: (Ephes. VI)]*. [var: Vulg. Eph 6:12 *adversus principes et potestates*, "princes"]

VERS. 10.-- *Madian.* Declining judgment. *Sisara,* the shutting out of joy. *Jabin,* wise, of whom: *Where is the wise? where is the scribe? Cison,* where they were overcome, is interpreted hardness. *Endor,* where they perished, the fountain of generation. And carnal generation is understood, not those regenerated by the water of baptism. *Oreb,* dryness, *Zeb,* a wolf, *Zebee,* the victim [0984B] of a wolf, not of God. *Salmana,* the shadow of commotion. All these things suit the wicked, whom the people of God overcame in good. *Do to them as to Madian and Sisara, as to Jabin at the brook.* [n: (CASS.)] Of the vengeance upon the wicked; as all those were overcome in figure by the people of God, so he prays that these may be overcome in truth. *As Madian.* [n: (AUG.)] Namely those who decline judgment, not thinking themselves to be judged: and who shut out the judgment to come; and all these perished in Cison, and in the fountain of Endor.

VERS. 12.-- *Make their princes as,* etc. These things to all in common. But specially, *make their princes:* this he wishes for the soldiers of Antichrist, who are not to be converted.

VERS. 13.-- *Let us possess by inheritance the sanctuary of God.* [n: (AUG.)] God's: they invade it as their own.

[0984C]VERS. 14.-- *As a wheel, and as stubble before the face of the wind.* [n: (CASS.)] By this likeness he foretells their many calamities.

*Of the wind.* The lightness of sinners is compared to the wind, because it flits hither and thither.

VERS. 15.-- *Fire and a flame burning the mountains.* [var: Vulg. Ps 82:15 *Sicut ignis qui comburit silvam, et sicut flamma comburens montes*, "as fire which burneth a wood"] [n: (AUG.)] They are fitly said to be in the judgment, when both the dense woods of sinners and the mountains of pride shall be burned.

VERS. 17.-- *Fill their faces.* [n: (CASS.)] The punishments of the contumacious being prophesied, he prays for those who shall believe, who have not praisers of their crimes, but blamers, whom reproach often corrects. [n: (AUG.)] It is better to fall that one may repent, than to be proud under the hope of religion: whence the Prophet says: *And thou, daughter of Sion, shalt come even unto Babylon, [0984D] and there shalt thou be delivered [n: (Mich. IV)]*.

*Fill their faces.* This good and desirable thing would not be prophesied for them, unless among those enemies there were such as this shall be granted to before the judgment: because even now the head heals and washes the same body, but in part, not universally as at the end: and yet from thence some shall be converted, for whom he here prays.

VERS. 18.-- *Let them blush and be troubled,* etc. [n: (AUG.)] Here of the wicked, who shall not be converted, *Let them blush,* in the time to come for the evils which they have done. *And be troubled,* for the punishments which they shall suffer. And this *for ever and ever,* that is, unto eternity. *And be troubled.* Again he returns to those who are confounded in the present that they may not be confounded unto eternity; as if he should say: Let those be troubled unto eternity: but those seeking [0985A] the name of the Lord now, let them both be confounded for their sins wholesomely, and perish, in so far as they are wicked. And so let them know; or, let the wicked then be confounded, in soul and in body, and perish in soul beyond repair. And now let them know what they now will not. [n: (CASS.)] It is to be noted that Asaph has been brought in through the eleven preceding psalms, that the hardness of the Jews might be softened by the frequent voice of the Synagogue itself, or that they might believe the very name to which they seemed devoted. But in the aforesaid psalms he treated of the incarnation [sic: *psalmi*] of the Lord, and of the faith of the Gentiles, and of the judgment, by which the transgression of the Jews is refuted. Whence they are fitly eleven: for the number eleven is the number of transgression, because it both passes the ten, and does not reach the twelve, whence there were also eleven curtains of hair in the tabernacle [0985B] of the Lord, and the Apostles were in the number eleven, the most unhappy Judas being cast out at the time of the passion, when they failed from the faith, whence: *Thou hast appointed darkness, and it is night [n: (Psal. CIII)]*.



## PSALM LXXXIII.

VERS. 1.-- *Unto the end.* [n: (AUG.)] Nothing is here said of visible winepresses, whence it is plain that they are spiritual. The grape upon the vine, the olive hanging upon the tree, enjoy the free air, and neither is that wine, nor is this oil, before the pressing: so those whom God foreknew before the ages to be made conformable to the image of his Son, before they come to the service of God, enjoy in the world a delightful liberty. But when they come to the service of God, it is said to them: *Son, when thou comest to the service of God, stand in justice and in fear, and prepare thy soul for temptation* [0985C] [n: (Eccle. II)]. Behold the winepress: they are crushed, they are stripped in the coverings of carnal desires, as in husks; they are stripped of the old man, and they flow down into the storehouse of God: therefore the churches of the present time are called winepresses. But who among these are the sons of Core, that is, the bald, namely of Christ? For bald Eliseus, mocked by the boys, was a figure of Christ, who was crucified on Calvary. Therefore is the Christian pressed here, that he may seek the rest which is not here, and God becomes for him a refuge. For he who leans upon earthly things leans upon a staff of reed. *For the winepresses.* [n: (AUG.)] In the winepress the fruit of the grape or of the olive flows out: so in pressures the merits of the saints appear, which lay hidden in peace. [n: (CASS.)] The second psalm on charity. First, of the inestimable desire of the eternal house.

[0985D]VERS. 2.-- *How lovely.* Behold how great the warfare. *For the sparrow,* how secure.

VERS. 4.-- *For the sparrow,* etc. [n: (AUG.)] To two he renders two: *the heart* as *the sparrow; the flesh* as *the turtle,* to which he gives also *young ones:* with the heart we think upon God, as the sparrow flies to the house; with the flesh we do works. [n: (CASS.)] As the sparrow forsakes the woods, and rejoices when a house is found, where it fears no snares: so the soul rejoices, while it knows that a house is prepared for it in heaven. The spirit, which is as a sparrow flying on high, reaching towards God himself. *A nest, etc.* [n: (AUG.)] *A nest* for a time, *a house* true unto eternity. By the nest therefore he means faith, and the conformity of the sacraments of the Church, which nest the turtle seeks, that she may lay up her young. For she does not cast them anywhere, [0986A] but in the nest of the Church she does good works wholesomely, for which she is saved: wherein she differs from others who do works, but not in the nest of faith. For the pagans also do works, they clothe the naked, etc., and heretics do many things: but not in the nest of the Church, and therefore they are trodden down, they are not preserved. This flesh is the woman, by whom the sparrow is seduced, but she shall be saved by the bearing of works, if in the nest, that is, if she shall have persevered in faith. But God supplies that whereof the nest may be made, for he clothed himself with the hay of the flesh that he might come to thee. *Thy altars, O Lord.* [n: (CASS.)] Where souls feast at the heavenly banquet, where the soul may rejoice as in a house. [n: (AUG.)] And the flesh lays up its works, whence: Blessed are they who, etc., where he shows to what beatitude the warfare leads.

VERS. 5.-- *Blessed are they that dwell in thy house, O Lord; [0986B] for ever and ever they shall praise thee.* [n: (AUG.)] This the good have already, namely, that they dwell in thy house. There follows: *For ever and ever they shall praise thee.* This is the whole business of the saints there: there is no necessity there, which is the mother of all human actions, for whom do they do anything there, where none are in need; they have therefore and they act, whence they are blessed. But every blessed one is called blessed by having or by acting.

VERS. 6.-- *Blessed is the man whose help is from thee: he hath disposed ascensions in his heart.* [n: (CASS.)] By what help one may come to the house.

VERS. 7.-- *In the vale of tears.* [n: (CASS.)] The vale of tears is the humility of one repenting. [n: (AUG.)] But whence the weeping: because he afflicted us with the law, through which we knew that sin fights against the spirit, and we groan, [0986C] namely how we may come to the place which he has appointed, by his power and help assuredly. *In the place which he hath appointed. For he shall give a blessing.* Which cannot be set forth, which eye hath not seen, nor ear heard, that is, the kingdom to come, which God has disposed and ordained, that it may be come to by degrees.

VERS. 9.-- *O Lord God of hosts.* [n: (CASS.)] He prays and humbles himself for the obtaining of this help. *God of Jacob.* [n: (AUG.)] Whom thou madest Israel by appearing to him, so make me also Israel.

VERS. 10.-- *Look upon the face,* etc. For he himself speaks in us, whence the Apostle: *Do you seek a proof of him that speaketh in me, Christ!* And he is received in us, as he says, *He that receiveth you, receiveth me. Look.* The Father always looks upon Christ. [n: (CASS.)] But make [0986D] him to be looked upon by the nations [sic: *ventibus*] through the presence of the incarnation: or, thus by the face we become known to others. [n: (AUG.)] *Look* therefore *upon the face,* that is, make Christ known to all, that we may be able to go from virtue to virtue

VERS. 11.-- *One day.* Unchangeable, namely without rising and setting, to which yesterday does not yield, which tomorrow does not press upon. *Above thousands,* above the infinite present days, which have an end. For which cause *I have chosen,* though I might reign, *to be abject* in the opinion of men, *in the house,* in the Church, *Behold how sweet it is, to be pressed by the burden of the winepress.*

VERS. 12.-- *Because mercy.* [n: (CASS.)] Therefore I had rather be in the house of God than elsewhere, because in it he gives mercy, by which he justifies, after the truth of the promised [0987A] reward. But to whom? to those who walk in innocence.

VERS. 13.-- *O Lord of hosts, blessed is the man that hopeth,* etc. Thou who dost such great things, which cannot be told.



## PSALM LXXXIV.

VERS. 2.-- *Thou hast blessed, O Lord.* [n: (AUG.)] A psalm on the new blessing, etc., *as far as* of the turning away of iniquity.

VERS. 3.-- *Thou hast forgiven.* [n: (ID.)] He speaks of things to come as of things past.

VERS. 4.-- *Thou hast mitigated* [n: (CASS.)] Diapsalma, of the turning away of anger, that is, of punishment.

VERS. 5.-- *Convert us.* He asks what he knew to be still to come. As if: what I have said as past, I pray may be done. [n: (AUG.)] *Convert us.* I said prophetically things to come, as past; but here he prays for them as truly to come, saying, Convert [0987B] and turn away, as if he should say, I have said them as though done. And because I see things to come, and because they are not, I pray that they may be done.

VERS. 6.-- *Is it for ever.* He foresees that this punishment will pass away: *For as in Adam all die, so also in Christ all shall be made alive. Thou art angry.* [n: (AUG.)] The anger of God is every punishment which one suffers. As it was said to Adam when he sinned: *Cursed is the earth in thy work, and in labour and in the sweat of thy face shalt thou eat thy bread.* [var: Vulg. Gn 3:17 *maledicta terra in opere tuo : in laboribus comedes ex ea*; the second clause is Gn 3:19 *In sudore vultus tui vesceris pane*] Of anger therefore we are mortal and passible, but renewed in Christ. *From generation.* The first generation was mortal, of anger; the other shall be immortal, of mercy: but neither do we merit this mercy by being converted to God through ourselves, for he himself converts us. And thence he subjoins: *God converting,* as if [0987C] to say: as the quickening is from thee, so also the conversion.

VERS. 7.-- *O God, thou being turned.* [n: (CASS.)] Not extending, but God converting. First he gives the will of conversion, afterwards he leads to life. Or, O God, thou being turned from anger at the coming of the Son. *Thou wilt quicken us.* So long a time before it is had. And then, *Thy people shall rejoice in thee,* which before shall rejoice wickedly in itself.

VERS. 8.-- *Shew us, O Lord.* [n: (AUG.)] This the Prophet foretold by praying, etc., *as far as Blessed are the clean of heart, for they shall see God [n: (Matth. V)]*. [n: (CASS.)] *Shew.* Or this one asks that Christ be shewn to him spiritually only; he knew indeed that he was to come, but he asks that we may behold him with a pure heart, not with bodily eyes as the unbelievers: for the Jews saw and crucified him. And for the time to come: *Give us thy salvation,* that [0987D] is, Christ, to be embraced and possessed, that he be not taken away from us; and thus it is read partly of the present, and partly of the future. *Mercy.* [n: (AUG.)] Christ is called mercy, because he showed us mercy, teaching that whatever of good a man has is from God, and so man is not proud. But cleaving thus and enjoying God, he rejoices in him.

VERS. 9.-- *I will hear what the Lord speaketh in me.* [n: (CASS.)] Whence he knows, and by whom it is done, and what he himself bestows in coming. *Peace.* [n: (AUG.)] Christ. Peace toward God and toward the neighbour, and through Christ.

VERS. 10.-- *Near them that fear him.* [n: (ID.)] Everywhere demons were worshipped, etc., *as far as* immortal in our body now mortal.

[0988A]VERS. 11.-- *Mercy,* etc. [n: (ID.)] After he had said, etc., *as far as how truth is sprung out of the earth [n: (Luc. VIII)]*.

VERS. 12.-- *Truth is sprung out of the earth.* [n: (ID.)] Confession from man, that he may accuse himself, etc., *as far as* the justification of him that confesses is given.

VERS. 14.-- *Justice shall go before him.* [n: (ID.)] The first justice of man is, etc., *as far as* and visiting he shall set his steps in the way. *Shall walk.* It shall increase, or shall run before, preparing the way for him; and the Lord shall set his steps in the way made ready.

## PSALM LXXXV.

*A prayer of David.* [var: Vulg. Ps 85:1 *Oratio ipsi David*, "a prayer for David himself"] [n: (AUG.)] This psalm is the second of those which are entitled by the name of prayer, in which Christ, the Son of God and of man, one God with the Father, [0988B] one man with men, prays in the form of a servant, who is prayed to by us in the form of God. He prays for us as our priest; he prays in us as our head; he is prayed to by us as our God.

[n: (CASS.)] The prayer of Christ, of the faithful, etc., *as far as* he might restrain the boldness of foolish and inconsiderate rashness.

[n: (AUG.)] These things Christ says in us, and we in him.

[n: (CASS.)] He prays by ascending, whence he concludes: All the nations adoring.

VERS. 2.-- *For I am holy.* [n: (AUG.)] Christ properly holy, sanctifying, etc., *as far as* have mercy on him that cries, gladden him that is lifted up.

VERS. 5.-- *Meek.* [n: (ID.)] Bearing man, until he perfect him, etc., *as far as* when he is not yet perfect.

[0988C]VERS. 6.-- *Give ear.* [n: (CASS.)] See the order of the prayer, etc., *as far as* a hearing to my supplication.

VERS. 7.-- *Of tribulation.* [n: (AUG., CASS.)] When one cries more earnestly, etc., *as far as because there is none like to thee among the gods, O Lord.*

VERS. 9.-- *All whom thou hast made shall come and adore before,* etc. [n: (AUG.)] If there is a nation which God did not make, that one shall not adore. He announces the Church spread abroad everywhere, which is now fulfilled: And note that, as the Scripture says: *In a broad nation is the glory of the king, in the diminishing of the people is the ruin of the prince.* [var: Vulg. Pr 14:28 *In multitudine populi dignitas regis, et in paucitate plebis ignominia principis*, "in the multitude of the people is the dignity of the king"] There follows: *And they shall glorify the name.*

VERS. 10.-- *Thou art God alone.* [n: (ID.)] Or thou alone art great against those to come, who call themselves great and gods.

[0988D]VERS. 11.-- *Conduct me, O Lord, in thy way.* [n: (CASS.)] He prays for all who now adore, and speaks in his members. *O Lord, conduct,* etc. It is one thing to be led to the way, and another to be led in the way, lest one fall in it: everywhere men need to be helped, whether they be in the way, or outside it. But the way is Christ, who says: *I am the way, the truth, and the life. Let my heart rejoice.* [n: (AUG.)] In worldly fear the cause goes before, etc., *as far as* but he who fears, and does not hope, is cast down.

VERS. 13.-- *From the lower hell.* [n: (ID.)] By this we understand, etc., *as far as* these are opinions, not assertions.

VERS. 14.-- *O God, the wicked have risen up against me, [0989A] and the Synagogue of the mighty,* etc. [n: (CASS.)] Again he prays for himself, mention being made of his passion and of his glory, whence those for whom he prayed have much mercy and are rescued from hell.

VERS. 16.-- *Give thy command.* [n: (AUG.)] As if he should say: Let the time of patience pass, let the time of judgment come, whence not only Christ, but the saints also, shall sit as judges, who are *the sons of the handmaid,* that is, of the Church.



## PSALM LXXXVI.

VERS. 1.-- *A psalm.* [n: (CASS.)] Because it treats of the Church according to power, where it does works. *Of a canticle.* That from the worldly Jerusalem thou mayest be lifted up to the understanding that is from above, namely from action to contemplation. The Prophet preaches the heavenly Jerusalem to the sons of Core, which he commends [0989B] by its foundations, and its gates, and its fame, that their affection may be increased. *The foundations thereof.* A psalm on the heavenly Jerusalem: the Prophet, full of the spirit, turning many things over with himself concerning this city, of which he had as yet said nothing, breaks out into this: *The foundations thereof,* etc. The first praise is from the foundations; let the solidity of the whole city be noted. *Thereof,* is not referred to anything set down before: but to the meditation of the Prophet's heart, because he had never been silent about it in his heart, even if in his mouth.

VERS. 3.-- *Things said.* [n: (AUG.)] These are those things which the Lord foretold of it by the prophets.

VERS. 4.-- *I will be mindful.* [n: (ID.)] Diapsalma. The Lord, concerning the same Jerusalem. *I will be mindful of Rahab.* He shows what those glorious things are; as if he should say: These are they: in that city, says the Prophet in the person of God, [0989C] *I will be mindful of Rahab and of Babylon,* which do not belong to the people of the Jews. *Glorious things of thee,* where the Jew is not alone, but all the Gentiles. *Rahab.* She let down the scarlet through the window, that is, the Church of the Gentiles has upon its forehead the sign of the blood of Christ.

Whence: *Publicans and harlots shall go before you into the kingdom of heaven. Rahab,* [var: Vulg. Mt 21:31 *in regnum Dei*, "into the kingdom of God"] breadth or violence, the Gentiles, who wander broadly and violently through vices. To Babylon belong all the wicked, as to Jerusalem all the saints. *Behold the strangers.* I will be mindful. Why hast thou said this? *Behold the strangers,* that is, the Gentiles: and *Tyre,* the anguish of vices, or of penance.

VERS. 5.-- *Shall Sion.* According to another reading. *Mother Sion shall say, a man.*

[n: (AUG., CASS.)] As if he should say: Whence shall strangers come to [0989D] Jerusalem? Hear whence. There is a certain man, that is, Christ, who says *Mother Sion,* that is, the Synagogue is my mother according to the flesh; and through him they come; who is that man? He is the one who *was born in her* and founded her; he himself says, *mother,* to that city.

[n: (CASS.)] Or, he commends Sion spiritually, etc., *as far as* of what princes?

[n: (AUG.)] Of these, (according to another reading) *who were made,* namely princes, *in her,* that is, who were made princes; before they were in her, they were not princes, but the outcast things of the world: *God chose* indeed *the weak things of the world, that he might confound the strong.*

VERS. 7.-- *As of those rejoicing.* [n: (CASS.)] Diapsalma. He concludes in few words, touching the beatitude to come. [0990A] [n: (AUG.)] *As of those rejoicing,* all things being compared, he concludes: *As of those rejoicing. As,* because such shall the joy be as we do not know here, and therefore since it could not be expressed, he says, as he could, *as of those rejoicing.*

## PSALM LXXXVII.

VERS. 1.-- *Mahalath.* [n: (AUG.)] A choir, that the choir may answer the singer in harmony, etc., *as far as* for a certain man of the East is said to have been.

VERS. 2.-- *O Lord God.* [n: (CASS.)] He admonishes us to imitate the death of Christ. First he prays for help, reporting the contempt he has from his enemies: whence, praying in the form of a servant to the Father, he says: *O Lord.*

VERS. 3.-- *Let it enter in the sight.* [n: (ID.)] The power of pure prayer, which as a certain person enters to God [0990B] and performs its charge: whither the flesh cannot come.

VERS. 4.-- *For it is filled.* [n: (AUG.)] These affections of human infirmity, etc., *as far as* but tokens of human infirmity.

VERS. 5.-- *Counted.* Because I have borne not only grievous things, but reproachful ones also.

VERS. 6.-- *As the slain sleeping.* [n: (CASS.)] Because he also was wounded with a lance, and shut in a sepulchre, but he differs by that which follows, of whom thou art not yet mindful.

*In the sepulchre.* That is, lulled asleep in carnal pleasure, whence God does not rouse them.

*And they are cast off from thy hand.* [n: (AUG.)] For this is not referred to the sleeping, among whom are the just, [0990C] of whom although God is not mindful *as yet,* [var: Vulg. Ps 87:6 *quorum non es memor amplius*, "no more"; the gloss's *ut resurgant* rests on "as yet"] that they may rise again: yet of these it is said: *The souls of the just are in the hand of God [n: (Sap. III)]*. Or, *they have put me* by the truth of the thing, *In the lower pit,* that is, in the waters of hell, namely in darkness and in the shadow of death.

VERS. 7.-- *In the lower pit.* [n: (CASS.)] Or *of hell:* because by so abject a death he was taken away into the darkness of oblivion, whom no one shall seek. And *the shadow of death,* the place of the wicked, in which they are punished.

VERS. 8.-- *It is confirmed upon me.* [n: (ID.)] They thought that he had perished by the anger of God, and that God had brought all his indignations upon him, when he suffered him to be crucified.

VERS. 9.-- *Thou hast put far away.* [n: (ID.)] What his friends did in the passion.

[0990D] *Acquaintances.* Not those whom he knew: because then he knew all as God, but those to whom he was known.

VERS. 10.-- *All the day.* [n: (AUG.)] By *hands stretched out* is understood the continuance of good works, from which he never ceased. For on the cross that stretching out was not for even one whole day, nor can the whole be taken for the part when he adds *whole;* but because these works did not profit all, he adds.

VERS. 11.-- *Is it for the dead.* He means the dead in heart, for whom indeed the miracles of Christ are wrought, but they do not profit them: for wonders were wrought for those dead in body, when certain ones came to life again. And when the Lord pierced hell, and thence ascended a conqueror of death, a great miracle was wrought for the dead. *Is it [0991A] for the dead.* [n: (CASS.)] He shows to whom their own works do not profit. *Or shall physicians raise up,* etc. [n: (AUG.)] In the Hebrew not physicians, etc., *as far as: No man can come to me, unless the Father draw him.*

VERS. 12.-- *In the sepulchre.* Whence in the Gospel of such men: *You are like to whited sepulchres, which outwardly appear to men beautiful: but within are full of dead men's bones,* as if to say, so also you by hypocrisy and iniquity. *Mercy and truth.* [n: (AUG.)] Scripture loves to join mercy and truth: especially in the psalms.

VERS. 13.-- *In darkness.* The dead laid in the sepulchre, and the darkness, and the land of forgetfulness, are the same.

VERS. 14.-- *I, O Lord, have cried to thee.* [n: (CASS.)] The things which he endures in his own; behold the choir answering the precentor.

[0991B] [n: (ID.)] *And I as it were,* with those, with whom I am one.

[n: (AUG.)] *And I.* To these dead their works do not profit, etc., *as far as Why, O Lord, dost thou cast off.*

VERS. 15.-- *Why.* [n: (CASS.)] Behold how much he desires. *Why, O Lord.* He sets forth that the cause is to be sought: which is, that by the delay of good, and by adversities multiplied, by which the prayer is as it were repulsed, it may be kindled more ardently.

VERS. 16.-- *Lifted up.* [n: (ID.)] First with a swollen heart, afterwards, *Humbled.* Through confession. *Troubled,* that is, confounded for his sins, by condemning them [sic: *damnado*].

VERS. 17.-- *They have passed.* [n: (CASS.)] They did not happen gently.

[n: (AUG.)] Because it was not of the faithful, but of the unbeliever that it was said [0991C]: *The anger of God abideth on him [n: (Joan. III)]*. For he scourges the sons whom he receives, whence: *Whom I love, I rebuke and chastise [n: (Apoc. III)]*. And elsewhere: *I will strike and I will heal [sic: *Percutium*]: I will kill and I will make to live [n: (Deut. XXXII)]*. [var: Vulg. Dt 32:39 *ego occidam, et ego vivere faciam : percutiam, et ego sanabo*, the two members in the reverse order] [n: (ID.)] *They have passed and troubled.* Because one thing we already suffer from condemnation, and another we fear from eternal vengeance. *Thy terrors.* [n: (CASS.)] Which I suffer: because it pleased thee, not by my desert. [n: (AUG.)] The terrors of the judgment to come, which all flesh fears.

*They have troubled me.* According to the infirmity of the flesh. [n: (AUG.)] *They have troubled me,* sometimes, terrors: they so trouble the mind of one thinking, with evils hanging round about, that they seem like waters to flow about, and at once to surround on every side, whence they both seem to be friends, and desert after their manner, whence the Apostle: *All have forsaken me* [0991D] [n: (II Tim. IV)].

VERS. 18.-- *They have surrounded me.* [n: (CASS.)] Sins shut him in on every side with a copious flood.

*All the day.* [n: (AUG.)] Because at no time are evils wanting to the Church until the world be ended. But all these things are done, that the prayer of the Church may come before God until that salvation come, where there shall be nothing of evils.

VERS. 19.-- *Friend* [n: (CASS.)] Even those who are joined by a pious and faithful love.

*Neighbour and acquaintances,* etc. Kindred. Often have human offices been withdrawn from the just through fear.



## PSALM LXXXVIII.

VERS. 2.-- *The mercies.* [n: (CASS.)] This Psalm says many things concerning the [0992A] hope of Christians and the magnificence of the Lord, by which no one is led astray who prays worthily, which the true Israel so understands. First the Prophet promises that he will sing of mercy and truth.

*For ever.* [n: (CASS., REM.)] How does he *sing the mercies for ever,* and give thanks, who is not mindful of misery? And how is there full blessedness if the memory of guilt touches the mind? But we often remember sad things in gladness, and sorrows in health without sorrow, and thence we are the more glad and thankful; and so likewise the recollection of misery takes away nothing of blessedness.

*Truth.* The promise. In the mouth: it does not slip out of the mouth, but is turned over long in the mouth as something sweet.

VERS. 3.-- *For thou hast said.* Here he says: Whence does he know? and what are the mercies of God through Christ? [n: (AUG.)] [0992B] Which mercies, and what truth shall I sing? Behold.

*For thou hast said.* As if he should say: I, a man, say securely what thou, God, hast said, hast made manifest through the Spirit.

*Mercy shall be built up.* [n: (AUG.)] He repeats both, which he foretold. Because all the ways of the Lord are mercy and truth. Truth is not shown forth in the fulfilling of the things promised, unless mercy go before in the remission of sins. Finally, truth pertains to the Jews, to whom the promises were made, mercy to the Gentiles: as the Apostle says: *For I say that Jesus Christ was a minister of the circumcision for the truth of God, which was to be fulfilled: but that the Gentiles honour God for his mercy [n: (Rom. XV)]* [var: Vulg. Rom 15:8 has instead "to confirm the promises of the fathers"; the gloss's argument turns on the fulfilling, which it repeats two lines above].

*In the heavens.* They who declare the glory of God, through whom mercy is built up [0992C].

VERS. 4.-- *I have sworn to David my servant.* This man is secure, for this reason, because God has said it: more secure, because he has sworn. God's swearing is the confirmation of the promise, and out of both is made the man Ethan, that is, the strong one.

VERS. 5.-- *For ever.* [n: (CASS.)] The building up of mercy is not temporal, as the ruin of iniquity is temporal: for temporal iniquity is destroyed in certain men, that in them eternal mercy may be built up, whence the Lord says to Jeremiah: *Behold therefore I have set thee over the nations and over kingdoms, to root up, and to pull down, and to waste, and to destroy, and to build, and to plant. Unto generation.* [var: Vulg. Jer 1:10 reads "Behold I have set thee this day over the nations", with no "therefore"] [n: (AUG.)] For generation cannot, etc., *as far as* he shall sit, and then in the saints.

VERS. 6.-- *The heavens shall confess.* [n: (CAS.)] Diapsalma. [0992D] Secondly he describes the praises and the power of God in divers ways, as if to say, as thou hast said, so shall it be done. *They shall confess,* they shall praise.

*The heavens shall confess thy wonders.* For in every mercy shown to the lost, in the justifying of the ungodly, what do we praise but the wonders of God? Thou praisest because the dead have risen; praise the more because the lost have been redeemed. For thou seest a man who yesterday was a whirlpool of drunkenness, today the ornament of sobriety. For thou seest a man who yesterday was the mire of luxury, today the grace of temperance: yesterday a blasphemer of God, today a praiser of God. Thou seest a man who yesterday was a slave of the creature, today a worshipper of the Creator: these are the wonders which the heavens confess. *For thy truth also.* [n: (CASS.)] Or without *for.* Wonders, [0993A] in that he does and administers all things everywhere. Truth is in the Church where it is believed, *the Word was made flesh,* and that [there is] a Trinity, and before that, God.

VERS. 7.-- *For who.* [n: (AUG.)] It is to be noted that the heavens and the clouds are the same, namely the preachers. But heavens, that is, preachers, because of the splendour of truth. *Clouds,* which are cloudy because of the mortality of the flesh, and because they pass away, where also there are hidden things, which afterwards shall be made clear in the judgment. *Among the sons.* Either of those above, or of men. Let him not therefore be thought to be man only: for he is natural, we adoptive; he begotten, we created; he begotten from eternity, we made in time; he the only one, we many.

VERS. 8.-- *Above all.* [n: (ID.)] The angels, or men, who are his unequal attendants, who draw near to him by his own gift.

[0993B]VERS. 9.-- *To thee.* [n: (ID.)] Thou who hast made all things, etc., *as far as* to be tempted above that which a man can bear.

VERS. 11.-- *Wounded.* He was humbled by losing those whom he held [sic: *Humilitatus.*]; wounded by the smiting of the heart, wherein he was proud. *The proud one.* [n: (AUG.)] Namely the dragon, who was made in the sea to be mocked at, who through the humble Christ, in whom others are humble, are humbled; Christ humbled himself, that he might humble the proud one. For the proud one held the proud: the devil is wounded by the wounded Christ, he is not yet wholly dead. *The enemies.* The powers of the air, or the Jews dispersed through the world, or of men he took up some who are the spoils of the devil, and others thou didst leave, whom thou didst permit to go away after him.

VERS. 12.-- *Thine are the heavens, and thine is the earth, the world,* [0993C] etc. [n: (ID.)] Therefore he triumphed in heaven over the devil, because heaven is his; on earth over Judea, because the earth is his. Why then were they proud in what was another's?

*Thou hast founded.* Why then do they raise themselves up against him, whose is the compass of the world, and all the creatures which are in it?

VERS. 13.-- *The north.* The devil. *The sea thou hast created.* The world. Foolishly therefore do they raise themselves up against him by whom they were created. *Thabor and Hermon shall rejoice in thy name.* [n: (AUG.)] Mountains of Syria, and by them the Gentiles are understood, the whole from a part, by synecdoche.

*They shall rejoice.* At Christ's coming, allegorically. *Thabor,* the Jews, to whom the light first came. *Hermon,* the Gentiles, who were formerly accursed. *In thy name they shall rejoice,* the two walls being joined together [0993D] in the corner stone.

[n: (AUG.)] *Thabor.* The light coming from the highest light, namely, at whose coming the anathema of him, that is, of the devil, who held us in his error, is made. Which is: it is given by thee that we should be enlightened, that we should give the devil to anathema.

VERS. 15.-- *The preparation of thy seat.* [n: (CASS.)] The *seat* of God is eternal, but in the judgment it shall appear *prepared,* when he himself shall be seen to judge justly. Or in this present time men, by discerning and by holding to just things, prepare themselves as a seat for the Lord.

*Mercy and truth shall go before thy face.* From the likeness of a forerunner preparing a lodging for the Lord.

[0994A]VERS. 16.-- *Blessed is the people that knoweth,* etc. [n: (CASS.)] He knows, who places his gladness not in his own strength, but in the Lord's, or, who understands in the heart what he sings with the mouth: whence this reward follows, *in the light,* that is, they shall persevere in the contemplation of the Trinity.

*Jubilation.* Jubilation is a joy which cannot be unfolded in words, nor yet altogether kept silent. *O Lord, they shall walk in the light of thy countenance, and in thy name they shall rejoice.* [n: (CASS.)] He commends the grace, of which the jubilation is.

[n: (AUG.)] Or in thy good pleasure, that is, in Christ, therefore in him, because he took our flesh, in whom is all hope.

VERS. 20.-- *Then he spoke in a vision.* [var: Vulg. Ps 88:20 has the second person, "then thou didst speak" — as the gloss itself has it four lines below] [n: (CASS.)] Diapsalma. Thirdly, the promises of the Father concerning Christ. [0994B] The praise above is not doubtful, which the Father's authority confirms. [n: (CASS.)]

[n: (AUG.)] *Then.* When thou didst appoint the testament, or when thou didst say these things to me, *then thou didst speak to the saints,* to the rest of the prophets *in a vision,* for they did not speak unknowingly, as Montanus and certain other heretics have said.

VERS. 21.-- *David my servant, with my holy oil I have anointed him.* [n: (AUG.)] Strong of hand, an obedient servant, therefore in him I have placed help.

VERS. 23.-- *The enemy in him.* [n: (ID.)] The devil, whence: *The prince of this world cometh, and in me he hath not anything [n: (Joan. XIV)]*.

*The son of iniquity shall not go on to hurt.* Judas shall not go on, shall not add the effect to the will.

[0994C]VERS. 24.-- *From his face his enemies.* [n: (AUG.)] When his face looks upon sinners, they are separated from crimes, for which there is no place under his presence.

VERS. 25.-- *And my truth and my mercy shall be with him, and in my name shall his horn be exalted.* [n: (ID.)] Often mercy and truth are commended to us, etc., *as far as* where these meet one another.

VERS. 31.-- *But if they forsake.* Not only concerning those who persist, but also concerning those who fall, do I promise these things.

VERS. 33.-- *With a rod.* For who is a son? etc., *as far as: And with stripes,* etc.

*I will not scatter.* [n: (AUG.)] He himself does not take away his mercy from him who is indeed a Christian: but if a man is so wicked that he flees when his father draws near, he alienates [0994D] himself, the father does not cast him off.

VERS. 35.-- *Neither will I profane.* The promise would be profane, if it were unfulfilled. Is nothing sent into the vessels out of that net because of the bad fishes? The good fishes shall surely be sent, that is, those whom he has predestinated.

VERS. 36.-- *I have sworn once.* [n: (CASS.)] Man swears often, God *once,* who is not changed.

*In the holy one.* In the man Christ, in whom was to be fulfilled what I have sworn; or David, that is Christ, through holiness. *The seed,* Christians.

VERS. 38.-- *As the sun in the sight.* [n: (AUG.)] According to the soul, etc., *as far as* in heaven, that is, Christ.

*But thou hast rejected.* [n: (CASS.)] Diapsalma. Fourthly [0995A] he sets adverse things against the promises aforesaid, lest they be thought to be fulfilled in another than in the Christ who was then to come. *But thou hast rejected.* As if to say, these goods are for Christians, but not for the Jews, who have deprived themselves of the promise, and this is it: But thou hast rejected *thy Christ,* that is, David the anointed, from the kingdom for the sin which he committed against Urias concerning Bethsabee; *And thou hast despised,* or *hast spurned,* because he suffered many unworthy things; *thou hast deferred,* long in tribulation, *the testament,* the things promised to the Jews, they have been carried over to the Gentiles. [n: (AUG.)] *But thou.* Or, all these things concerning Christ and the Church: *thou hast rejected* to the gibbet *thy Christ,* and hast given him over to contempt. Or, thou hast deferred him from glory, for a time, or according to his members.

VERS. 40.-- *The testament.* The promise seems overthrown, and the saints are reckoned profane. *The hedges,* the prelates, [0995B] *thou hast destroyed,* while they are slain. *And thou hast made his strength fear.* They who strengthened others by teaching are now for a terror, while they are afflicted, and some are now plundered *by them that pass by the way,* which is Christ.

VERS. 44.-- *Thou hast turned away the help of his sword.* That is, preaching. *Thou hast not helped him in battle,* but hast given him over to sufferings. *Thou hast destroyed him from amendment,* etc., that is, certain clean ones have failed, and have been brought down to the earth. [var: Vulg. Ps 88:45 reads "from cleansing"] *Thou hast lessened,* as it is thought, while they are quickly taken away from life.

[n: (AUG.)] Why did God promise those things, etc., *as far as* that this promise might be looked for in others.

VERS. 45.-- *Thou hast destroyed him.* All these things came upon the Jews, yet Christ was not taken from them, nor the eternal [0995C] kingdom, but deferred. *Thou hast cast his seat down to the ground.* That is, their kingdom has been blotted out everywhere on earth, because there is now no prince from among them.

VERS. 46.-- *Thou hast lessened.* Because their kingdom would stand long, if it were under God. *Thou hast covered him with confusion.* Because he bears the reproach of unbelief. *Thou hast lessened.* [n: (AUG.)] They thought that they would reign for ever, but thou hast lessened the days of their kingdom, that it should not stand, according to their opinion.

VERS. 47.-- *How long, O Lord?* [n: (CASS.)] Diapsalma. Fifthly. Behold in whom the promises were fulfilled, namely in *the substance of David,* which lives, and delivered the soul, that is, in Christ. [n: (AUG.)] But, how long dost thou not raise up Christ? What *substance?* which is nothing, save through him. *Who is the man that liveth and shall not see death?* [var: Vulg. Ps 88:49 has the future, "that shall live"] [0995D] no one. In these things he now asks that help be brought to man, or especially to the Jews.



## PSALM LXXXVIII. (cont.)

VERS. 48.-- *Remember what my substance is.* [n: (ID.)] David says this, set in the flesh among the Jews, in hope in Christ. What *is my substance?* And if the Jews fail, *my substance* does not fail, the root does not wholly perish, whence Mary shall come: and of her shall come the seed to whom the promise was made. For of that people came the Virgin Mary, of the Virgin Mary the flesh of Christ, and that flesh is not a sinner, but the cleanser of sinners. *For hast thou in vain.* All the sons of men have gone into vanity, yet not *in vain didst thou establish* them: whence then dost thou cleanse them from vanity? through *the seed,* in which is *my substance;* concerning which he adds: *Who is the man?*

[0996A]VERS. 49.-- *Who is the man?* [sic: *hemo*] [n: (ID.)] This befits none, save Christ: for even if he finds those rising again, and they shall not see death any more: yet they themselves do not *deliver their own souls out of the lower hand,* [sic: *iuferiori*] but he does who *can lay down his soul, and take it again, from whom no one shall take it away.* [var: Vulg. Ps 88:49 has the singular throughout, "he shall deliver his soul out of the hand of hell" — the gloss's whole argument rests on the plural it prints] [n: (CASS.)] Or thus, no one shall live, or shall deliver his soul: and since this is so, *where are the mercies?* He now asks that the promises be fulfilled which he made to David.

VERS. 50.-- *Where are the mercies?* [n: (ID.)] Diapsalma. Sixthly, in the members also the promises are fulfilled, not temporally, but spiritually. And he speaks in the person of the Church.

VERS. 51.-- *Be mindful, O Lord.* [n: (AUG.)] That thou mayest repay; he seems to have forgotten, while he defers. *Of the reproach of the servants.* [n: (CASS.)] Brought upon the servants by many nations. [0996B] [n: (AUG.)] Which I (the Church) *have held,* that is, have borne. *In the bosom,* in the secret of the mind, because openly she did not dare to speak.

VERS. 53.-- *Blessed.* [n: (CASS.)] Against the curses of the nations. [n: (AUG.)] Blessed. An exclamation out of the brightness of Christ that has been seen.

## PSALM LXXXIX.

VERS. 1.-- *A prayer of Moses.* [n: (AUG.)] When the old life and the new, etc., *as far as* and he sees wonders out of the law of God.

[n: (CASS., REM.)] This psalm treats of the failing of the human race, which shall be taken away through Christ.

*O Lord, a refuge.* [n: (CASS.)] First, he sets forth an eternal refuge for man in time.

VERS. 2.-- *Before the mountains.* But lest he should seem [0996C] not to have been, before he was a refuge to us, he adds *before. The mountains, the earth.* [n: (AUG.)] Behold, every rational creature is distinguished [sic: *Elcce*] by this difference, that by the name *of mountains* is meant the loftiness of the angels; and by the name *of the earth* the lowliness of men: and therefore not unfittingly did he so distinguish the words, that he should say that *the mountains,* that is, the angels, *were made,* and that *the earth,* that is, men, *was formed. They should be made, or it should be formed.* To be formed, or fashioned, is wont to be said of bodies: to be made, out of nothing. *From the world.* He would more fitly say, etc., *as far as* but here *the world* is put for the eternal. *From the world.* Behold what eternity is a refuge to us, etc., *as far as: Turn not away, for a thousand years. Thou art.* Excellently does he say it, because he is unchangeable. He does not say thou shalt be, or thou wast, since the unchangeable essence of God has not known the past, or the future; whence it is said: *I am [0996D] who am.* And, *He who is hath sent me to you?* And: *But thou art the selfsame, and thy years shall not fail.* [sic: *aani*]

VERS. 3.-- *Turn not away.* [n: (CASS.)] Secondly, he sets out the infirmity of man, for which he asks that help be brought. *Unto lowliness.* That is, earthly concupiscence, etc., *as far as* who taught us to seek the things of heaven.

VERS. 4.-- *Yesterday's day.* [n: (AUG.)] He does not say, at least as tomorrow's, etc., *as far as* in anxiety, and trembling, as nothing [sic: *qnasi*].

VERS. 6.-- *In the morning as grass.* [n: (AUG., CASS.)] Let it be changed from the quality of the night.

*In the evening let it fall.* Cut down, or broken, at the last. [n: (CASS.)] *In the morning as grass.* I have said that the years are held, etc., *as far as* the youth which flowers and passes away.

[0997A] *In the evening.* Old age, when the vigour of a man grows cold. *Or, in the morning as grass.* A fitting comparison, as if to say, as grass is changed from the quality which it has in the night, to a worse one in the morning when the sun is risen: so is a man changed for the worse.

VERS. 7.-- *We have failed in thy wrath, and in thy fury we have been troubled.* [n: (AUG.)] Made mortal and liable to suffering, which nevertheless we fear to end by death, whence follows: *And in thy fury we have been troubled. The world.* Our depraved life. Or *the world,* those who come after. *In the enlightening of the countenance.* [n: (CASS.)] Those things are covered which have pardon: those are *enlightened* which are punished.

VERS. 9.-- *For all our days.* [n: (AUG.)] Our knowledge has failed, darkened in its reason by sin, or, the time of life has been shortened, and has almost *failed,* [0997B] because the days of our life have been brought down to fewness. *Our years as.* [n: (CASS., AUG.)] The misery and shortness of this life is expressed here by the likeness of *the spider,* which weaves empty webs, that it may catch flies by guile: whence as a spider, that is, we laboured among corruptible things, we wove empty things. [n: (CASS.)] Life has been shortened through the wrath of God, because they sinned in the presumption of a longer life. And we ourselves *have failed in thy wrath.* [n: (AUG.)] That is, our essence has failed through hardships. *The years . . . shall meditate,* they shall not work, because they pass without profit. Or, *they shall meditate* passively, that is, our years shall be reckoned *as a spider,* that is, as empty and useless.

VERS. 10.-- *Seventy.* [n: (ID.)] The appetite for earthly things which are done in seven days: if in virtues, eighty, that is, eternity is sought. *Years.* Or *the years,* [0997C] another reading. By which space of time they are called long-lived, who even up to *eighty* years are seen to have some strength. *Eighty.* Because even short of eighty years some are infirm, etc., *as far as* awaiting the redemption of the body. *Meekness.* It is clemency, if it be God's: a taming, if it be ours. For he himself, being meek through clemency, by taming us makes us meek, and so we shall be corrected.

VERS. 11.-- *Who I have known.* [var: Vulg. Ps 89:11 has the third person, "who hath known"] [n: (ID.)] The power of a man's anger is to kill the body, and afterwards to do nothing. God both punishes here, and afterwards sends into hell: and by few is his greater anger understood.

VERS. 12.-- *So make known thy right hand.* [n: (CASS.)] Thirdly, he prays and affirms deliverance. [n: (AUG.)] *Thy right hand.* He so *made known* Christ, etc., *as far as* [0997D] that he may set him at the right hand.

*And them that are learned in heart in wisdom. Or, fettered in heart.* Which is the same thing, because they who are taught in wisdom thrust the foot of the mind into the fetters of God, that they may not stray from his way. These God so made known in the New Testament, that for faith they should spurn all things which are held great, in the Old, and should seek in eternal death: attending to this he speaks, on behalf of those who suffer many things from the world for the sake of eternal things.

VERS. 13.-- *Turn thee.* As though his face were turned away from those in tribulation. *How long.* Elsewhere, a little. [n: (CASS.)] Not wholly, which profits the more, if some tribulation always admonishes us.

VERS. 14.-- *In the morning we have rejoiced in thy mercy, [0998A] and we have been delighted.* [sic: *delectatio*] [n: (AUG., CASS.)] Now it is night, in which we hunger: but then we shall be filled with the countenance, whence: *I shall be satisfied when thy glory shall appear.* And elsewhere: *In the morning I will stand before thee, and will see.* But now instead of it *we have been glad,* even in the evil days in which we say: *Look upon thy servants.*

VERS. 16.-- *Look upon thy servants.* What God does in himself, the Prophet prays may be done for his people: he prays, namely, that he may spare the sinning Jews, if not for their merits, at least for the sake of the works which he has wrought in them.

*And direct their children.* As if to say, even if they themselves have been scattered through iniquities [sic: *iniqui-tales*], at least the children, at the last, may be corrected by faith, who of themselves are depraved and unconvertible.

VERS. 17.-- *The brightness of the Lord our God upon us, [0998B] and work ye.* [var: Vulg. Ps 89:17 reads "and direct thou the work of our hands upon us"] [n: (HIER., CASS.)] Let the image of God, restoring, be imprinted on the mind. Or the brightness of God is upon us, while we are adorned by the impress of the cross, and bear the standard of triumph on the forehead. [n: (CASS.)] For the image is threefold, etc., *as far as* that I may remember thee, understand thee, love thee.



## PSALM XC.

VERS. 1.-- *The praise of a canticle.* Praise is human also: but this praise is of a canticle, because it is nothing if not divine. For this psalm is a hymn against the demons. The Prophet therefore says of any holy man whatsoever: *He that dwelleth in the help,* etc.

[n: (CASS.)] He shows that the temptations of the devil were overcome through humility by Christ, and by his own, who remain in the help of God, that we too through him may learn to overcome temptations, because he is the door through which [sic: *jauna*], if any man shall enter in, [0998C] he shall go in, and shall go out, and pasture shall come forth.

[n: (AUG.)] Christ is the door; to enter through him is to imitate him, not in godhead, and power, and miracles, but in that wherein he was made for us, that is, in the humility to which he calls, saying: *Learn of me, because I am meek and humble of heart.* Out of this psalm the devil tempted Christ. But Christ's temptation is our teaching, and following him we overcome by him.

*He that dwelleth.* [n: (CASS.)] He describes first the man who trusts in God alone, wholly walled about by the protection of God. *He that dwelleth,* that is, who sets all his hope always not in himself, but in God, shall be protected by God in every way. Note the correspondence of the words: for as he always *dwelleth,* so also shall he always *abide.* The one he affirms, namely that he *dwelleth;* the other he promises, [0998D] namely that he *shall be protected.*

*He shall abide in the protection of the God of heaven.* That is to say, that he turn not aside to the right hand or to the left, as in the Old Testament it was commanded to the children of Israel, that they should walk by the king's highway, not turning aside to the right hand or to the left. The king's highway is the way of Christ our king, who so ran the way of the world that he was neither enticed by prosperity, nor broken by adversity.

VERS. 2.-- *My God.* [n: (CASS.)] Lest thou think that after the upholding and the refuge nothing further is to be asked for, he adds: *In him will I trust,* lest at any time he presume of himself; and if Adam had done this, he would have stood well.

VERS. 3.-- *From the snare.* [n: (ID.)] Temptation, either by deeds, which is here called *a snare;* or by words, which is here called *a sharp word;* [0999A] as if to say, even though it seem light, yet it is a grave thing to be tempted by a word. *A snare,* the craftiness of spiritual or of carnal enemies; *a sharp word,* every saying which moves a man from the divine rules.
