[n: (AUG.)] *From the snare of the hunters,* that is, of the demons, who set their snares not in the way, Christ, but round about it, to the right hand or to the left. Into these some fall even through a sharp word, as they who will not be Christ's because of those who insult the Christian name, and they who fear the detractions of men, so that they cease from the way of God.

VERS. 4.-- *With his shoulders.* [n: (AUG., CASS.)] To him that hopes he promises the benefits of God. *The shoulders* are the workings of wonders, by which, as by arms, the power of God is shown. *The feathers* are the counsels of the Prophets, which carry men to the heavens.

[0999B] *He will overshadow.* That neither the sun of the godhead, nor the moon of the humanity, may scorch thee. *In the feathers* is the protection. *With his shoulders.* Elsewhere, *Between the shoulders.* [n: (AUG.)] That is, he places thee before his breast, etc., *as far as* that we may hope under his wings.

VERS. 5.-- *The truth.* [n: (ID.)] It is God's, who does not regard persons, nor mingle those who hope in themselves with those who hope in God, but discerns the humble or the proud. [n: (CASS.)] Or the truth is the incarnation of Christ, whence: *Out of the earth truth is sprung [n: (Psal. LXXXIV)]*, which both overshadows with the shoulders, and defends with the shield. As he has noted the kinds of defence, so he notes also the kinds of dangers, that the deliverance may be the more welcome.

*From the fear of the night.* From the likeness of a danger, whence only fear is had. *From the fear of the night.* [n: (AUG.)] He who [0999C] sins in ignorance, etc., *as far as: A thousand shall fall at thy side.*

VERS. 7.-- *They shall fall at the side.* [n: (CASS.)] Secondly, he says that the head of him who trusts in God, that is, Christ, lifted up into heaven, can be touched by none, who also protects the offspring on earth: but the presumptuous fall from him. Behold the certainty of the protection of him who trusts in God through Christ. *At the side.* The side of God is the crown of the just, who shall judge with him, whence it is said: *Send ye from your own side,* that is, they who hold counsel with you. The side of Christ, therefore, is the Apostles and their imitators, to whom he promised: *You shall sit upon twelve seats, judging the twelve tribes of Israel [n: (Matth. XIX)]*. The twelve seats are the perfection of the tribunal, who in which shall sit [cj: *qui quibus*; read *in quibus*, "in which"] all they who have left all things, and have followed Christ.

[0999D]VERS. 8.-- *But yet.* [n: (CASS., AUG.)] Although indeed it is allowed, thou permitting, that the wicked persecute thine own, yet not unpunished: with thine eyes he shall consider the evil which they willed, not the good which is done through those who know it not, it shall be repaid to them; and the holy man, who has the eyes of faith, sees them now exalted and thundering, and at the last struck by lightning: this he said lest the weak be moved. [n: (CASS.)] *But yet with thine eyes.* Even if they that fall should tempt thee with hard things, *yet.* Or, even if the demon does not draw near, which is through the wicked: yet nevertheless not what they do, but what they intend, *thou shalt consider.*

VERS. 9.-- *For thou art.* [n: (AUG.)] One Christ is head and body, of whom he speaks now as of the head, now as of the body, the person not being changed. Therefore [1000A] diligently mark where he treats of Christ according to the head, and where according to the members. *The Most High.* In the height is the refuge of God, deep in secret, that flight may be made from the wrath to come. For many so make for themselves the refuge of God, whereby they flee from temporal evils. *Thou hast made the Most High thy refuge,* that is, by rising again, by ascending, thou hast made the refuge high, that I may now hope to follow [sic: *speram*] whither thou hast gone before.

VERS. 10.--[n: (ID.)] *The tabernacle of God* is the flesh, etc., *as far as* this is rightly taken of Christ.

VERS. 11.-- *For to his angels.* This the tempter said to the Lord, when he tempted him: this he cast up against him, doubting of his Godhead. But Christ was tempted for our sake, that in him also we might learn to overcome the tempter.

[n: (AUG.)] So also he willed to be baptized by a servant, not for his own need, but for man's profit, that we should not disdain [1000B] to be baptized by our fellow servants.

VERS. 12.-- *In their hands they shall bear thee up.* [n: (ID.)] By the hands of angels Christ was lifted up into heaven, etc., *as far as* he who cannot be overcome.

VERS. 13.-- *Upon the asp.* After the human infirmity, the divine power is shown, which commands such creatures. And he treats of Christ according to the members. *And he shall trample.* [var: Vulg. Ps 90:13 *conculcabis*, "thou shalt trample"] [n: (AUG.)] The Church, which is wary of his cunning, treads upon the serpent. *The lion.* He rages openly against the martyrs.

The dragon lies in wait through the heretics, that he may corrupt the virgin of Christ, whence the Apostle: *I have espoused you to one husband, that I may present you a chaste virgin to Christ [n: (II Cor. XI)]*.

VERS. 14.-- *Because he hoped in me.* [n: (AUG.)] Thirdly, the words of God, by which he consoles the Church, to which he promises both defence here, and rewards in the world to come. [n: (CASS.)] [1000C] He gives charge to the angels concerning thee, saying: *Because in me.* The certainty of the promise, through the promise of God.

VERS. 15.-- *He shall cry to me.* [n: (AUG.)] There is no need to fear in tribulation, etc., *as far as* at that time let us labour the more to resist.



## PSALM XCI.

VERS. 1.-- *A psalm of a canticle.* [n: (AUG.)] Because of the weariness of minds, etc., *as far as* seeking the glory of God, not thine own.

VERS. 2.-- *It is good to confess.* [n: (CASS.)] First, he treats how the good man, being untroubled, keeps the sabbath of the mind, perceiving that all things are done well by God, which the foolish man does not know. [n: (AUG.)] The good things of the present, given to us by God, are a consolation: when they are lacking, it is justice. For God both consoles us, and amends us: in both, therefore, let us be [1000D] thankful to him, saying: *It is good to confess,* etc.

VERS. 4.-- *Upon the ten-stringed instrument.* [n: (ID.)] He sings a psalm who does good with cheerfulness. He with whom it is done in sadness does not himself do it: and he carries the psaltery, he does not sing upon it. *Upon the ten-stringed instrument.* If thou only speakest well, it is a canticle without the harp; if thou workest, and speakest not, it is a harp without the canticle; and therefore both speak well and do well.

VERS. 5.-- *For thou hast delighted.* [n: (ID.)] Whence the Apostle: *We are his own moulding,* [var: Vulg. Eph 2:10 reads "for we are his workmanship" — the word the gloss picks up two lines later in *In factura tua*] etc., *as far as: How great are thy works. In thy handiwork.* [n: (CASS., HIER.)] Both in the beauty of the fabric of the world itself, and in its obedience, and in its ordering, and in its causes, and in its effects, and in the works of the hands which I use [sic: *uior*], or which I marvel at.

[1001A]VERS. 6.-- *How great are thy works.* [n: (CASS., HIER.)] In substance, and in form, and in force, and in the rest. *Thy thoughts are exceeding deep,* by which thou hast made and dost govern such things.

VERS. 7.-- *The senseless man,* etc. [n: (CASS.)] Thou hast delighted me in thy works, but *the senseless man shall not know,* and the fool, who, if he knows, does not beware for himself, *shall not understand these things,* which he has said, or which he says afterwards. The highest kind of knowledge is to know that God bestows such great things as human sense is not able to attain to.

*The senseless man* is he who is empty of heavenly wisdom, and full of human craftiness; who does not know the works of God, because he thinks that all things are done by chance; *the fool* is a brute, who does not know even worldly things.

VERS. 8.-- *When they have sprung up.* [var: Vulg. Ps 91:8 has the future perfect, "when they shall have sprung up"] [n: (CASS.)] He asserts that the wicked shall [1001B] perish, etc., *as far as* the Jews thought that thou shouldst die by the common lot, that is to say.

VERS. 9.-- *But thou art the Most High.* [n: (AUG.)] Thou waitest from above out of eternity, etc., *as far as* not long-suffering, from the word eternal.

VERS. 11.-- *And shall be exalted.* [n: (ID.)] Even though they themselves shall perish, etc., *as far as* all heresies shall perish.

VERS. 12.-- *And hath looked down.* [n: (ID.)] And therefore even now he has looked down, and it shall hear from the truth within. *And from them that rise up.* [var: Vulg. Ps 91:12 reads "and among them that rise up"] [n: (CASS.)] Or, it shall hear at the last those that rise up against me, the malignant: when they shall rebuke themselves, saying, *What hath pride profited us, and riches? [n: (Sap. V.)] My ear.* [n: (AUG.)] Of him that stands at the right hand: it shall hear in the world to come this, namely, *Go, ye cursed, into everlasting fire.* [var: Vulg. Mt 25:41 reads "Depart from me, ye cursed"] From which evil hearing the just man shall not fear. Behold, this is [1001C] for these men; *what of the just? [n: (Matth. XXV.)]*

VERS. 13.-- *The just man as the palm tree.* They as hay. The just man as the palm tree, for its height, and because its root is rough, its foliage fair at the top and at the end, and it bears fruit late; so also does labour make the just man rough here, whom in the heights the beauty of immortality shall crown, and, as it were late, after long labours he shall receive the wage of his works. *The just man as the palm tree.* [n: (CASS.)] The third part. That the just flourish, so that by this promise they may devoutly rejoice. *The cedar.* [n: (AUG.)] The palm and the cedar, these trees do not wither in the summer, as hay does.

VERS. 14.-- *In the courts of the house.* [n: (CASS.)] *The court* is the beginning of the dwelling; *the house* is the inner part. *In the courts* therefore *they shall flourish,* that is, in the very beginning of the resurrection, when they shall hear: *Come, ye blessed of my Father [n: (Matth. XXV)]*.

VERS. 15.-- *They shall still be multiplied.* [n: (ID.)] The Church [1001D] shall the more begin to be most fruitful, when it shall have been brought to the end of the world: for then by the sufferings of the elect the number of the blessed shall quickly be filled up. And *old age* is here taken otherwise than above.

VERS. 16.-- *That they may declare.* [n: (ID.)] Here, or, when they shall receive the reward they shall declare that he is just, who fulfils to his own the things promised. *And there is no iniquity in him.* [n: (AUG.)] As that he should permit those to suffer here for his sake, whom he does not adorn there. *And he is upright,* who rewards, not unjust so as to defraud of the reward, but kind so as to heap it up.

## PSALM XCII.

*Unto the day,* etc. [var: Vulg. Ps 92:1 reads "on the day before the sabbath" — as the gloss itself has it in its own next words] [n: (AUG.)] On the day before the sabbath, etc., *as far as* which the sixth day signifies.

VERS. 1.-- *The Lord hath reigned,* etc. [n: (CASS.)] The praise [1002A] of Christ is sung here, etc., *as far as* from the praise of the house, there, *Thy house. Clothed with beauty.* [n: (AUG.)] Christ is beautiful in these things, etc., *as far as* the world which shall not be moved. *He hath put on beauty.* [n: (CASS.)] That is, comeliness, etc., *as far as* from the incarnation, when he taught men.

*He hath girded himself about.* He does not say, he girded, or he girded up, or he girded on, but he girded about. For we are girded when we are about to work, we are girded on when we are about to fight, we are girded up when we are about to go, we are girded about when we are about to minister. [n: (AUG.)] The girding therefore signifies works, etc., *as far as* because *better is he that overcometh anger than he that taketh a city. He hath girded himself about.* [n: (CASS.)] Ascending with the company of angels, or by strenuousness in working.

*For he hath established.* [n: (AUG.)] He has put on beauty and strength, that he might found the earth, whence he adds: *For he hath established.*

VERS. 2.-- *From then.* [n: (ID.)] Or from that time; it marks the time of grace, [1002B] and perhaps he understands this from the day before the sabbath, that is, in the sixth age of this world, in which the Lord came: but lest thou think that he then began, when he came born, he adds, *thou art from everlasting.*

VERS. 3.-- *The floods have lifted up their voice.* [n: (AUG.)] Because the disciples, hearing, etc., *as far as* of the world, which began to be angry, or to persecute. *The floods have lifted up their waves,* etc. [n: (CASS.)] This is wanting in others, etc., *as far as* he who does all things well, he adds: *The Lord is wonderful on high.*

VERS. 5.-- *Testimonies.* [n: (AUG.)] Because *the lifting up of the sea is wonderful,* [var: Vulg. Ps 92:4 has the plural, "wonderful are the surges of the sea"] etc., *as far as* and crowns out of the foretelling. [n: (CASS.)] *Testimonies.* Or thus, etc., *as far as* it contains, though in brief words.



## PSALM XCIII.

*A psalm of David* [var: Vulg. Ps 93:1 *Psalmus ipsi David, quarta sabbati.*, "a psalm for David himself, on the fourth day of the week"] [n: (AUG.)] To the weak, who also murmur [1002C] in heart, etc., *as far as* the whole teaching patience. *On the fourth day of the week,* on which day God made the luminaries, etc., *as far as* whence he admonishes to patience.

VERS. 1.-- *The Lord is the God of revenges.* Be not hasty to punish another, if he has hurt thee; even as thou wouldst not be willing to be punished at the asking of another whom thou hast hurt: and if he sins against thee, sin not worse by blaspheming God, nor wish for vengeance; which, if it comes, will perhaps find thee first.

*He hath acted freely.* [n: (AUG.)] In the time of his weakness, as when he taught in the temple, as one having power, because neither his truth nor his power can be withstood. And because it was so then, now *Be thou exalted, because, how long shall sinners.*

VERS. 4.-- *They shall utter.* [n: (CASS.)] He sets forth their sins, etc., *as far as* it goes on by way of exaggeration. *They shall speak, all that work,* etc. *They shall utter,* they [1002D] answer others, [n: (AUG.)] that is, they have what they may answer, etc., *as far as* the wicked speak, saying that God does not care for these things.

VERS. 6.-- *The widow and the stranger.* [n: (CASS.)] These God protects, etc., *as far as* without the devil for a father and concupiscence for a mother.

VERS. 7.-- *Neither shall he understand.* [n: (AUG.)] As it were, he will not attend to these things, he neglects these things, nor does he attend that he may see, God does not care for me, what I may do?

VERS. 8.-- *Understand ye.* [n: (CASS.)] The second part; against blasphemers, etc., *as far as* that he opens to his own friends his counsel, which is unknown to the wicked. *Ye foolish.* Who openly blaspheme, saying that God does not see. Or thus: You *unwise,* that is, ignorant, understand, you who by now, by living well, [1003A] are *in the people,* that is, in the number of the good. *And you foolish,* that is, improvident, and if you know, *at length be wise.*

VERS. 10.-- *Who correcteth the nations.* [n: (AUG.)] Daily, for the original sin, will he not rebuke for the things said above? Or, *Who instructeth.* [var: Vulg. Ps 93:10 *qui docet hominem scientiam*, "who teacheth man knowledge"] For the sake of which instruction God sent his Son, etc., *as far as* whence the Apostle: *We shall all stand before the judgment seat of Christ, that every one may receive, [n: (Rom. XIV)]*, etc. [var: Vulg. Rm 14:10 *omnes enim stabimus ante tribunal Christi*, with no such clause following; "that every one may render", *ut referat unusquisque*, stands at 2Cor 5:10]

VERS. 11.-- *The Lord knoweth.* [n: (ID.)] But thou knowest not the thoughts of God, since they are just; but when friends have been made, God brings forth his counsel, why he spares the wicked. *For they are vain.* The thoughts of such men are vain, in that they themselves of themselves cannot know it, but the Lord knows them to be vain.

VERS. 12.-- *Blessed is the man.* [n: (CASS.)] These are the vain, etc., [1003B] *as far as* all shall know God, *the heavens shall be folded up as a book. And out of thy law,* etc. [n: (AUG.)] From the law learn patience, *until there be dug,* it is not thine to know how, and why it is dug for him.

VERS. 13.-- *Until,* etc. [n: (AUG.)] The happiness of the wicked is a *pit;* the toil of the good is the father's scourge; happiness is a *pit,* because when God spares by impunity, a man is made lifted up, and when he thinks himself high, he falls. *Let a pit be dug.* Behold the counsel of God: therefore he spares the wicked, that *a pit may be dug for them.* It is still being dug, and thou already wishest to bury him: thou art too hasty. *A pit for the sinner,* etc. Not for every one, but for the proud, etc., *as far as* give thanks to him from whom thou hast it, and despise not another.

VERS. 16.-- *Who shall rise up.* [n: (CASS.)] The third part, where he says that there is a tossing for the just, and the wave of the world, [1003C] that is, evils through the wicked, so that being pricked they may cry to God, and that nothing else may please them, and then deliverance becomes the more welcome. [n: (AUG.)] Who *shall rise up,* as if he should say, etc., *as far as* but whence shall I conquer? *Unless because the Lord. He will stand with me,* etc. [n: (CASS.)] Patient in the battle line; but because he conquers who bears the violence; he loses who strikes.

VERS. 17.-- *Unless because the Lord.* [n: (CASS.)] By vain persuasions he could have been deceived, unless the Lord had delivered him.

VERS. 18.-- *He helped me.* Even as the Lord delivered Peter walking upon the water, that he might not be drowned, who said to the Lord: *Lord, if it be thou, bid me come to thee [n: (Matth. XXVI)]*.

[n: (AUG.)] That Christ by his own power, etc., *as far as* whence he subjoins, *thy comforts have gladdened my soul.*

[1003D]VERS. 19.-- *In the heart.* [n: (ID.)] He signifies wholesome thoughts, namely, that while he thought upon the eternal recompenses, he might despise temporal evils.

VERS. 20.-- *Doth there cleave.* [n: (ID.)] No wicked man sits with thee; and since these have nothing with thee, to what end then are they made? that thou mayest prick by means of them, and that they who are pricked may cry to thee, and at the last that these may perish; which things are shown in these three following verses. *The seat of iniquity.* [n: (CASS.)] It is the possession of the world, with love of it, which does not cleave to God, who commands his own to flee these things. *Who framest.* [n: (AUG.)] From this I understand that the wicked man does not cleave to thee, etc., *as far as* and that pain is a precept; but the other is unformed.

*Pain,* that is, a precept out of pain or toil, [1004A] etc., *as far as* and then the wave rises, in which he is stirred up.

VERS. 22.-- *And the Lord became my refuge, and my God the helper of my hope.* [n: (ID.)] Wherefore? because if thou wert not in peril, thou wouldst not seek a refuge: thou art pricked with pain, that thou mayest seek a remedy; the hope of the world departs and the hope of God comes.

## PSALM XCIV.

*Praise.* [n: (CAS.)] Devotion. *Song* signifies gladness.

VERS. 1.-- *Come, let us rejoice.* [n: (THEOD.)] King Josias was exceedingly pious, etc., *as far as* but the psalm is framed in the person of Josias, and of the priests of God. [n: (CASS.)] Concerning the time of grace, in which there is an entering into rest, the prophet, [1004B] describing the praises of God, first invites the Jews, whom he foresaw to be able to resist Christ.

VERS. 2.-- *His face,* etc. [n: (AUG., CASS.)] The sight of the judge to come, lest there be anything then in us to be sifted. *In confession.* [n: (AUG.)] Praising him, accusing ourselves, etc., *as far as* of which praise the psalm treats. *In psalms let us make a joyful noise.* [cj: *psalmi*; read *psalmis*, "in psalms"] What we say in psalms, or do, let us feel in jubilation.

VERS. 3.-- *Above all gods.* Or rather he calls men gods, over whom he is king, not over demons.

VERS. 4.-- *For in his hand,* etc. [n: (CASS.)] After the greatness of the Lord foretold, he comes to his clemency.

VERS. 5.-- *The sea.* [n: (AUG.)] The world, whose waves God confines within a bound, not suffering us to be tempted above [1004C] that which we are able [n: (I Cor. X)]; which waves also make increase for us, God working it, nor do the scandals hurt thee, which receive their measure from God.

VERS. 6.-- *Come, let us adore.* [n: (CASS.)] First he called the untaught to exultation: now, the glory of God being shown, he calls to penance, which is the harder. *Let us weep.* [n: (AUG.)] If perchance you are solicitous concerning the sins which have made you far off, weep: a tear extinguishes the flame of sin which burns in the conscience. *Who made us.* Behold great confidence: for of thyself thou canst fail, thyself thou canst not remake, he remakes thee who made thee.

VERS. 7.-- *And we are the people,* etc. [n: (AUG.)] Elegantly is the order of the words changed: He did not say the sheep of the pasture and the people of his hand, that thou mayest understand those very ones to be sheep who are the people, sheep of his hand, not those which from afar, because [1004D] they are, and sheep which we did not make, but he himself by his own grace made us sheep.

VERS. 8.-- *To-day if you shall hear the voice,* etc. [n: (CASS.)] But we are the people and the sheep. But to all these a condition is set forth, *if you shall hear his voice,* which voice he subjoins, *To-day. Harden not.* [n: (ID.)] Now Christ himself admonishes, lest they be hardened, lest it befall them as it did the fathers.

VERS. 9.-- *As in the provocation.* [n: (ID.)] The majesty of Christ speaking, etc., *as far as* the fault of the parents is recalled, that the vengeance may be feared.

VERS. 10.-- *Forty years.* [n: (AUG.)] By forty years he signifies all the ages, etc., *as far as* as he himself says: *I am with you unto the consummation of the world.*



## PSALM XCV.

[1005A]

*A psalm of David.* [var: Vulg. Ps 95:1 *Canticum ipsi David, quando domus aedificabatur post captivitatem.*, "a canticle for David himself, when the house was building after the captivity"] [n: (CASS.)] In the Old Testament it is read, etc., *as far as* this psalm does not treat of that actual one.

VERS. 1.-- *Sing ye to the Lord.* [n: (ID.)] He forewarned the Jews in the preceding psalm that, their hardness being laid aside, they should believe Christ; but because it was to abide, he now admonishes all the Gentiles. The second psalm concerning both comings. First he speaks to the prelates, that they themselves should sing and declare. *A new canticle.* [n: (AUG.)] Cupidity sings the *old,* etc., *as far as* whosoever shall have entered in, is without.

VERS. 2.-- *To the Lord, and bless.* [n: (HIER., CASS.)] Thrice to the Lord, for the distinguishing of the trinity of persons; because the praise of Christ is the honor of the Trinity. And there is [1005B] the figure epimone, that is, a frequent repetition of the same sentiment, which heightens it unto praise or unto blame.

VERS. 3.-- *Declare ye.* He speaks to the prelates, declare, because thus grows the building of the house. *From day to day.* [n: (CASS.)] The Old and the New Testament are days, which grow bright by the eternal sun, the one foretells, the other shows the thing done. [n: (HIER.)] He declares therefore from day to day, etc., *as far as* by which the feet of our affections are fenced from the thorns of the vices.

VERS. 4.-- *Great is the Lord.* [n: (AUG.)] Made little, because you are little, understand him great, and you shall be great in him.

VERS. 5.-- *He made the heavens.* [n: (ID.)] The apostles and the angels, whence if he made the heavens and the angels, then the apostles also, who command the demons.

[1005C]VERS. 6.-- *In the sight.* For such men, namely those confessing and just, behold him themselves, and are beheld by him. *Holiness and magnificence.* [n: (AUG.)] As if he should say: He who wishes to be magnificent and mighty, as the angels are, let him first be holy, which comes to pass from him who sanctifies: for of thyself thou canst not be holy. When therefore he sanctifies, a man is made holy, and so magnificent.

VERS. 7.-- *Bring to the Lord, ye kindreds.* [n: (CASS.)] The second part, where he speaks to the subjects, etc., *as far as* that none be excepted, native or stranger. *Glory.* [n: (ID.)] Because a man converses well, in whom God is glorified. *Honor.* When from good things thanks are rendered, whence, *There was not found who would give honor to God, but this stranger. [var: Vulg. Lc 17:18 *Non est inventus qui rediret, et daret gloriam Deo, nisi hic alienigena.*, "who returned and gave glory to God"] Into the courts.* [n: (ID.)] The *courts,* the prophets or the apostles, by whom there is a going to the Lord; from the [1005D] courts, that is, the prophets or the apostles, there is a coming into the court of the Catholic Church, where the majesty is adored. [n: (AUG.)] Or, Enter ye into the court, that is, into the doctrine of the prophets and the apostles.

VERS. 9.-- *Adore ye the Lord in the court,* etc. That is, in the Catholic Church, or by the *courts,* prophecy; by the *court,* the Gospel. *Let it be moved.* All being admonished, he desires that they be moved. As if he should say, enter in and adore, you that are good. *But the earth,* etc. *The whole earth.* [n: (CASS.)] Not some part of it; therefore, lest anyone say: *Lo, here is Christ to be there,* [var: Vulg. Mt 24:23 *Ecce hic est Christus, aut illic*, "Lo, here is Christ, or there"] he subjoins, *For he hath corrected the world of the earth,* etc.

VERS. 10.-- *Say ye among the nations.* [n: (ID.)] That he reigned and corrected and judged. *He hath reigned.* Another reading: [1006A] *He hath reigned from the wood [n: (Matth. XIII)]*. [n: (AUG.)] Of the cross, by which man passes over the *sea,* and becomes a *world* which is not moved.

*The peoples in equity,* etc. Others add, *And the nations in thy wrath.*

[n: (CASS.)] The peoples therefore, that is, the good, he shall judge in equity, but the nations, that is, the wicked, in his wrath.

VERS. 12.-- *Then shall they rejoice.* [n: (CASS., HIERON.)] *Then,* when he shall correct, etc., *as far as* because he is come, that is, he shall come to judge the earth. *Because he is come.* [n: (AUG.)] First he came in the Church, in the clouds, that is, in the apostles, and filled the whole world; whence: *From henceforth you shall see the Son of man coming in the clouds [n: (Matth. XXVI)]*: he who believes that coming shall rejoice in the last coming.

VERS. 13.-- *He shall judge the world.* [n: (CASS.)] He generally includes every [1006B] man, to be judged according to the equity of God. But how he is judged he sets forth by saying, *the peoples in truth,* because in the separation there shall be two peoples, to whom he shall give the sentence of truth, so that some go into life, others into punishments. [n: (AUG.)] By *the world* he means all the sons of Adam, etc., *as far as* the south.

## PSALM XCVI.

*A psalm for David himself.* [var: Vulg. Ps 96:1 *Huic David, quando terra ejus restituta est.*, "to this David, when his land was restored to him" — the clause this gloss expounds] According to the letter he touches here upon this, that when Absalom, the wicked son of David, was dead, who while he lived persecuted his father, the land was restored to David, and he reigned in peace, of which it is not here treated, but rather of the thing signified. [n: (AUG.)] David signifies Christ, whom the wicked sons, that is, the Jews, crucified, and abjured the kingdom to him [cj: *adjuraverunt*; read *abjuraverunt*, "renounced on oath"]. But *the land was restored to him,* when Judaea, which had crucified him, believed: [1006C] and the apostles, who had wavered, were confirmed: but other sheep also were led in from among the Gentiles.

Or, *the land was restored,* when his flesh rose again, after which resurrection all these things which are here sung were done.

[n: (AUG.)] Foreseeing these things in the spirit, the prophets rejoiced, etc., *as far as* for to the palate of faith the honey of the divine promise tastes sweet.

[n: (CASS.)] The fourth psalm concerning the first coming, by which the world was set free. He describes first the powers of the Lord, shows that all things are restored to him, and reproves the idolaters.

VERS. 1.-- *The Lord hath reigned.* [n: (CASS.)] The prophet, seeing the superstitions of the world abound before the resurrection of Christ, sets against them: *The Lord hath reigned.* [n: (AUG.)] [1006D] Who then is another to be worshipped? as it were, none; thereupon:

*Let the earth rejoice.* What remains to the idols? As it were, nothing.

*The islands.* The Churches of the whole world. *Many.* Not all, because of the assemblies of the heretics.

[n: (CASS.)] Thereupon he tells what manner of Lord he is in the first coming.

VERS. 2.-- *Clouds and darkness.* To the wicked, who are blind, he is cloudy and dark, to whom he shines with no brightness; but to the good, *Justice and judgment.* They who are corrected are his seat. Before his coming *fire shall go before,* because the wicked, kindled with wrath at the preaching of the prophets, took counsel of their slaying, and were inflamed unto indignation round about, because they were many, whereas the preachers were few.

[1007A]VERS. 3.-- *Fire shall go before him,* etc. [n: (AUG.)] As it were, they who are corrected are the seat of God, etc., *as far as* if they are wicked, unto consuming; if good, unto repairing.

VERS. 4.-- *His lightnings have shone forth.* [n: (ID.)] The clouds grew cheap in their human form, but a wondrous brightness appeared in their words or deeds, and thereupon the earth was moved.

VERS. 5.-- *They flowed down.* [n: (ID.)] Even if the pagan does not believe just now, yet he is overcome.

VERS. 7.-- *Let them be confounded.* This has now been done. [n: (AUG.)] For idolatry now ceases. And let them blush who adore stones, because they are dead.

VERS. 8.-- *Adore ye.* [n: (ID.)] The adoration of the angels therefore ceases, but they adore. The evil angels themselves wish to be adored, the good adore, and do not suffer themselves to be adored. Or at least by this example let idolatry cease.

[1007B] *I have heard and she was glad.* [var: Vulg. Ps 96:8 *Audivit*, "she hath heard"] [n: (CASS.)] Secondly, after the restoration of the land, an exultation of every kind, etc., *as far as* as the brethren rejoiced over Cornelius, whom Peter baptized. *And they rejoiced.* [n: (AUG.)] The apostles heard, who were brethren in Judaea, that the Gentiles had received the word of God, and they were glad and magnified God.

VERS. 9.-- *The earth . . . the gods.* [n: (ID.)] By *the earth* and *the gods* understand sinners and just men. Or, he signifies that he rules over things earthly and things heavenly.

VERS. 10.-- *The Lord keepeth.* [n: (AUG.)] If, when thou hast begun to hate the malignant, persecutions follow after, fear not, because the Lord keeps the souls of his saints, for the taking away of whom is the last kind of persecution.



## PSALM XCVII.

[1007C] *A psalm of David.* [var: Vulg. Ps 97:1 *Psalmus ipsi David.*, "a psalm for David himself"] [n: (CASS.)] The third psalm of those which treat of both comings: in which there is matter of hope through the mercy of the first coming, and of fear through the judgment of the second: these things, even if they are often repeated, are yet always new by reason of the varied setting forth of the words and of the sentiments.

VERS. 1.-- *Sing ye to the Lord.* [n: (CASS.)] First he speaks of the showing forth of the saving right hand.

*He hath saved.* [n: (AUG.)] It is a greater thing that he raised up the whole world from eternal death, than that he raised the dead. *To himself.* Many are saved bodily, but to themselves, not to him: they who, having received bodily health, grow wanton, but they who by faith are healed within, are healed to him, or save themselves.

VERS. 2.-- *Salvation . . . justice.* [n: (AUG.)] He who saves and justifies. *He hath revealed.* Things to come were foretold under a veil by the [1007D] prophets: but the veil has been taken away, since *truth is sprung out of the earth.*

VERS. 3.-- *Of mercy and of truth.* [n: (AUG.)] *Mercy* is that whereby he promised, *truth* follows upon this, because he rendered it. But to whom? *To Israel,* that is, to him who now by faith sees the truth, afterwards by sight.

VERS. 4.-- *All.* [n: (ID.)] Because Christ was not given to certain men only.

VERS. 5.-- *Rejoice ye.* [n: (CASS.)] Secondly he says that in all ways he is to be praised and declared, because the judge is come.

VERS. 6.-- *With ductile trumpets and with the voice of a trumpet of horn.* [n: (AUG.)] Of brass, which are drawn out by beating, etc., *as far as* as the horn cleaves to the flesh, and surpasses it. [1008A] [n: (HIERON.)] With ductile trumpets, of silver, after the manner of the Jews. Mystically, drawn out under the hammer of the whole earth, that is, the devil. [n: (AUG.)] Tribulation is the beating; advancement, the drawing out: such a trumpet was Job, whence he sounded as a trumpet, saying: *The Lord gave, the Lord hath taken away.*

VERS. 7.-- *Let it be moved.* [n: (ID.)] That is, let the sea of the law be made sweet, the wood of the cross being set therein. *And the fulness thereof.* All the sacraments of the law, etc., *as far as* but *the rivers,* etc. *The earth.* [n: (CASS.)] That is, every sinner.

VERS. 9.-- *The world of the earth.* All earthly men. *In justice.* [n: (AUG.)] Because they did not merit mercy. *The peoples.* [n: (CASS.)] The faithful out of the Jews and the Gentiles, who shall receive things equal to their merits.

## PSALM XCVIII.

[1008B]

VERS. 1.-- *A psalm of David.* [var: Vulg. Ps 98:1 *Psalmus ipsi David.*, "a psalm for David himself"] [n: (AUG.)] The prophet describes Christ the king giving good things, etc., *as far as* but the veil has been taken away, since *truth is sprung out of the earth, and justice hath looked down from heaven [n: (Psal. XLVIII)]*. *The Lord hath reigned* [n: (CASS.)] Concerning the power and the kingdom of Christ, that he may be adored in the man. He shows Christ reigning, and (whosoever may resist) high above all, whence he admonishes to yield to him. *Let the peoples be angry.* [n: (CASS.)] A derision. It can safely be granted that they be angry, because they are not going to hurt any further: he speaks by permitting, not by commanding. *He sitteth upon the cherubim.* [n: (ID.)] Against him the earth can do nothing, even though he himself was on the earth where he suffered the wrath of the people. *Cherubim.* [n: (AUG.)] *The fulness of knowledge* [1008C] is its interpretation, which is charity. The fulness of the knowledge of the law, which is the seat of God, shall be in thee also, if thou hast charity, against which the angry can do nothing. For what is the earth against heaven?

VERS. 2.-- *The Lord in Sion.* [n: (ID.)] If thou dost not understand what is *upon the Cherubim,* see this, that he is *great in Sion.* Sion is the soul which beholds God. *Great in Sion.* [n: (CASS.)] Who the Lord is, he sets forth: *In Sion* born. *Great,* lest he grow cheap on account of the passion. Or, yet the Lord is great in the Sion of the Jews, and above the peoples of the Gentiles.

VERS. 3.-- *Great.* [n: (AUG.)] It was little when they were angry, that is, made known among few, but now enlarged in all the earth. *Terrible.* [n: (ID.)] Because even if he is preached as crucified, he is the judge to come, and he shall not err [1008D] in judgment. *And the honor of the king.* [n: (CASS.)] He sets forth how they ought to confess. *The honor of the king,* is the glorious preaching, *judgment* requires discernment; he is to be worshipped not with a light will, but with fixed deliberation, which is from him; whence he subjoins: *Thou hast prepared directions.* Or *the honor of the king* is the damnation of the guilty, whereby the glory of the king is increased.

VERS. 4.-- *In Jacob.* [n: (AUG.)] Not only the patriarch, but the people whom he signifies. *Thou hast done.* These things, which another could not do.

VERS. 5.-- *Exalt ye.* [n: (CASS.)] The second part admonishes to adore him, because his fathers *were.* As it were, because he does these things for us, therefore *Exalt ye.*

*The footstool.* [n: (AUG.)] *The earth is the footstool of his feet,* says [1009A] Scripture, etc., *as far as* in whose honor it adores the footstool. *Of the feet.* [n: (CASS.)] *The feet,* the unfailing stability of the Godhead. *Holy.* That thou mayest distinguish the body from the Godhead, even though it be one person.

VERS. 6.-- *Moses.* [n: (CASS.)] A priest, because if not victims, he offered the vows of the people, and made supplication for the people.

VERS. 7.-- *A pillar.* [n: (CASS.)] The strength and the beauty of the house. In this appearance he spoke, which announced the Church, or the flesh, but then in a cloud, now openly through the Son: *He spoke to them out of the cloud.* [n: (ID.)] To them therefore then in a cloud, who now speaks to us in the footstool, that is, openly in the flesh assumed. *His testimonies and precepts.* [n: (ID.)] A testimony is a signification set down by certain signs of a preceding thing: whence often in the law it is said: *This shall be to you for a testimony.* The precepts [1009B] are as the precepts of the law given through Moses. [var: Vulg. Ps 98:7 *praeceptum quod dedit illis*, singular, "the precept which he gave them"]

VERS. 8.-- *Thou wast merciful to them.* [n: (AUG.)] It is not said save of sins, merciful in forgiving, merciful also in avenging, behold, even though these were so great, yet they had somewhat of sin; whence he adds, *upon all their inventions.*

*Avenging.* Lest human elation should creep upon them; whence: There was given me a sting of my flesh *[n: (II Cor. XII)] Inventions.* Otherwise, affections. [n: (AUG.)] Which God alone knew in their hearts; whence it was a punishment to them to bear with those among whom they were, for whose sins they grieved as for their own, through charity. For it was not, as is thought by very many, a punishment to Moses not to enter into the land of promise, into which the wretched also entered; but a figure, that they who were under the law, and were unwilling to be under grace, should not enter into rest.

## PSALM XCIX.

[1009C]

VERS. 1.-- *A psalm in confession.* Confession is twofold; one is of praise, the other of sins. The confession of praise befits the perfect, the confession of sins those who are beginning [sic: *inicipientibus*]; whence in this psalm the prophet admonishes to confession. [n: (CASS.)] This psalm teaches both to praise God unceasingly, and unceasingly to bewail our sins: because neither in evils is one to despair, nor in good things ought the mind to be lifted up. First he enjoins upon the perfect the chief kind of confession, that is, praise.

*Rejoice ye.* [n: (AUG.)] If the voice rejoices outwardly, etc., *as far as* and in what order and how wonderfully he made it. *All the earth.* Admonishing to praise. Not one corner of the earth does he [1009D] exhort: but as he knows the blessing to have been sown everywhere, so from everywhere he requires jubilation. *Serve ye in gladness.* [n: (AUG.)] This servitude is not of bitterness, nor of indignation, but of happiness and of liberty. Let charity therefore make thee a servant, because truth has made thee free. Whence the Lord: *If you shall abide in my word, you shall be my disciples indeed: and you shall know the truth, and the truth shall make you free [n: (Joan. VIII)]*. [var: Vulg. Jo 8:31 *Si vos manseritis in sermone meo*, "in my discourse"]

*In gladness.* Because they are set free; not murmuring, as in the desert: whence he subjoins the reward.

VERS. 2.-- *Enter ye into his sight.* It is a great thing to rejoice in the sight of so great a one. *Enter ye in.* [n: (CASS.)] Secondly, to those about to enter, the second kind of confession; nor let it be doubted, because mercy is with him.



## PSALM C.

[1010A]

VERS. 1.-- *A psalm for David himself.* [n: (CASS.)] This psalm contains the form of perfect sanctity, as the hundredth number is a perfect one.

*For David himself.* That the whole of what is here taught be applied to the virtues of Christ, nothing to an earthly king. Here we are taught to seek good things and to refuse evil, which is the perfect converse of life.

*Mercy and judgment I will sing.* [n: (CASS.)] Here Christ sings, and we in him. First he sets forth concerning mercy and judgment. *Mercy and judgment I will sing.* These two are joined in a mutual fellowship: in them he briefly includes all the works of God. And here is set before us the cause why he so bears himself, as he afterwards subjoins. [n: (AUG.)] Let no one presume upon mercy unto impunity, [1010B] because there is judgment for him; let no one who has been changed for the better fear judgment, because mercy goes before. These are always together in him: but through the times they are distinguished according to their effects, mercy now, judgment in the time to come.

VERS. 2.-- *I will sing and I will understand.* [n: (ID.)] This the singing grants, that I may understand with the mind what lies not open to the eyes, that is, that I may believe that the Lord will come, whom this man so greatly desires, who will come in the unspotted way. [n: (CASS.)] Because he passed through the world without sin; or, I will sing and I will understand, being in the unspotted way, when, etc. Or, *I will sing,* I will work cheerfully, and through this I shall understand. *When wilt thou come to me.* In the second coming, or in the death of each one. Or, *when wilt thou come to me,* through the flesh in the first coming, because [1010C] in the unspotted way. *I walked about.* [n: (CASS.)] Secondly, he sets forth what the unspotted way is. [n: (AUG.)] *I walked about.* What is the unspotted way? It begins from innocence, and in it is consummated; and there is no need of many words: Be thou innocent, and thou hast fulfilled justice. But in two ways a man does harm, namely if he makes another wretched, and if he abandons the wretched. He is innocent who harms neither himself nor another. [n: (CASS.)] *I walked about.* As if he should say: I sought not transitory things, but I ran the way of the world, being in the midst of thy house. Innocence has this power, that it makes a man dwell in the midst of the house of God, as an honorable example to others.

VERS. 3.-- *I did not set before me.* [n: (CASS.)] He sets forth innocence by parts. First, because he has nothing with the wicked, he says: *I did not set before me,* down to, *mine [1010D] eyes.* After which, that he has a part with the just, and that he drives the wiles of the devil out of his heart, there, *in the mornings.* [sic: *mututinis*] [n: (AUG.)] *I did not set before me.* As it were: *I walked about in innocence:* in this indeed (the whole Christ says it,) namely that *I did not set before me,* that is, I did not love an unjust thing.

VERS. 4.-- *It did not cleave.* [n: (CASS.)] He sets forth the transgressions. *A perverse heart,* he who transgresses in heart; the man turning aside, the heretic who dissents from the Catholics: and him who strays from the right paths I did not acknowledge, because even if they are sealed with the sacraments along with us, yet they are not true men.

VERS. 5.-- *The proud.* [n: (AUG.)] And Jesus feasted, etc., *as far as* whence: *Blessed are they that hunger and thirst after justice [n: (Matth. VIII)]*.

VERS. 6.-- *Mine eyes.* Here he pursues the other part of innocence, namely, that he has a part with the just. As if he should say: With such men thou didst not eat, whence then wast thou fed? *Of the earth.* [n: (AUG.)] Those living on the earth, where there are so many enticements, whence a greater crown. Or [n: (CASS.)] those who are throughout the whole world. *That they may sit with me.* [n: (AUG.)] That they may abide with me in the truth of faith, or share with me in the eternal kingdom, or judge with me.

VERS. 7.-- *He shall not dwell in the midst of my house.* [n: (CASS.)] That is, of the Church. Even if he is for a while borne with for the sake of correction, he does not stand out by merits, he can do nothing; he is not grain in my people, but chaff. *He shall not dwell,* this befits priests most of all, that they dwell not with the depraved.

VERS. 8.-- *In the morning.* [n: (CASS.)] The third part, where he shows [1011B] what the end of the wicked is. And here Christ speaks. *In the morning.* After the wicked have been shunned, the just man, returned to himself, adds that he drove the suggestions of the demons out of his heart *in the morning,* that is, when at the beginning of the temptation they begin to appear, as it were in the twilight. *I put to death.* By prayer, whereby they ought to be cast out, lest they grow. *Sinners.* The demons, who make all flesh to sin. *The city of the Lord.* The soul of the just man, whence the devil is scattered and removed. *All that work iniquity.* That is, the insinuations or the injections of the devil.

[n: (AUG.)] He has now recounted that none clave to him but the good [cj: *et*; read *ei*, "to him"], etc., *as far as* this befits the just man also, who foresees this to be to come, and therefore so bears himself.



## PSALM CI.

VERS. 1.-- *A prayer of the poor man.* [n: (AUG.)] According to [1011C] the form of a servant, etc., *as far as* the bridegroom on account of the head, the bride on account of the body. Christ, poor in us, and with us, and for our sake, here prays in the person of one repenting, who acknowledges his own abasement and the cause of his abasement, and who asks to be set free from sins and restored to his former state. He admonishes, therefore, that a man mark his own misery, and seek the mercy of God.

VERS. 2.-- *O Lord, hear my prayer.* [n: (CASS.)] The fifth psalm of penance; the fourth that is called a prayer. The first part, a deprecatory bid for a hearing.

*And the cry.* The prayer grew into a cry. *Let it come,* let not the clouds be in the way, which are in the way of the wicked. [n: (ID.)] An epimone through three verses, that is, a frequent repetition of the thought.

VERS. 4.-- *For they have failed.* [n: (CASS.)] The second part, in which is the mournful prayer; in which is set forth [1011D] what the poverty is, what the tribulation. He now discloses whence he cries. *For they have failed. My days have failed like smoke.* [n: (AUG.)] For when Adam was lifted up, the days [sic: *deis*] fail, the strength of the mind is drawn in. Misery can move a merciful judge. *My bones are grown dry like fuel.* The strong, whose frying-pan are they that are scandalized, etc., *as far as* for a man is already being fried, while he fears that he shall burn.

VERS. 5.-- *I am smitten as grass, and my heart is withered.* [n: (ID.)] Made mortal. *Grass* is green, but when smitten it easily feels the hurt: so the flesh of man, green in the law of God, when cut down by that same law withers away.

VERS. 6.-- *From the voice of groaning.* [n: (CASS.)] The nature of them that sin is shown, because while sin is sought after, [1012A] the contemplation of God is not had. *My.* [n: (AUG.)] Not of those who groan over earthly and not over spiritual things, etc., *as far as to bear the weak.*

VERS. 7.-- *Like a pelican,* [n: (AUG.)] He sets down three birds, three places, etc., *as far as* who, if not among men, is outside on high, saying to them that sleep in the night.

Or, the sagacious sparrow flees to the heights of the virtues, where he is safe from the snares. He who is solitary or single, on account of charity (which makes him one out of many), is upon the roof, or upon the building, on account of the height of faith, that he may not come down to take anything out of the house, that is, out of the flesh.

*In a dwelling-place.* [n: (ID.)] Or in ruined walls, which are commonly called ruins, where the walls stand without a roof, where there dwells the night-raven, a bird that loves the night.

VERS. 9.-- *All the day.* [n: (CASS.)] Or, the whole may be taken in the person [1012B] of the just man. As if he should say, etc., *as far as* or, in my abasement I was refreshed, so much did it please me.

VERS. 10.-- *Ashes.* [n: (AUG.)] By ashes and weeping the penitent are signified. For ashes and haircloth are the arms of penitents. *Drink.* [n: (CASS.)] Temporal delight. Or, I mingled with tears the longing for my homeland because of the delay; or the drink of the Scriptures, which recall ancient things.

VERS. 11.-- *From the face of wrath.* [n: (CASS.)] Wrath is vengeance; indignation is a movement of the soul, by which we are indignant even with our sons. Wrath is called an onset against those who are to be punished, and these do not fall upon God. But it is antropopathos.

*Of wrath.* Whence the Lord also says: *The wrath of God abideth upon them that believe not [n: (Joan. III)]*. He does not say, shall come; but *abideth,* because it is not taken away, the wrath in which he was born.

[1012C]VERS. 12.-- *My days have declined like a shadow.* Because I have declined from the true day, my declining days are like myself.

VERS. 13.-- *But thou,* etc. [n: (CASS.)] The third part, where the certainty of obtaining pardon is shown. Now he that is fallen rises up to prophesy, now the penitent lifts up his head.

[n: (AUG.)] The memorial is a promise to be remembered; from thee is our restoring, because thou art mindful of us, or we of thee.

VERS. 14.-- *The time is come.* [n: (AUG.)] Because God willed all things to be done in order, and appointed a time for this, wherein the opportunity is fitly ordered.

VERS. 15.-- *The stones thereof.* [n: (AUG.)] There are stones in Sion, etc., *as far as* hence it is that, when the Holy Spirit had been sent, many of them believed who had crucified the Lord.

[1012D]VERS. 17.-- *In glory.* [n: (AUG.)] He who was first seen in weakness in that Sion; whence: *We saw him having neither beauty nor comeliness [n: (Isa. LIII)]*.

VERS. 18.-- *The prayer.* This is done in the building of Sion: they pray, they groan who build, who are one poor man and many poor; whence in the title it stands in the singular, here in the plural. But he is called one because of the truth of the Church's peace, and many because of her breadth.

VERS. 19.-- *Let these things be written.* [n: (CASS.)] He who above was foul with penance, now adorned with the honour of a prophet, commands that these things which he has said be established by the testimony of Scripture, that the age to come may learn them. *In the generation.* [n: (AUG.)] These things, when they were being written, did not so profit the generation [1013A] of the Old Testament; they profit another, that is, the new.

VERS. 20.-- *Because he hath looked forth,* etc. [n: (AUG.)] After the groans of one grieving, now there are words of consolations, now the poor man rejoices. And because he said above: *The Lord hath built up Sion;* and again: *Let these things be written;* now he declares both; as if this is what he said was to be written, *because he hath looked forth.*

*He hath looked forth.* He speaks as of things past what were yet to come.

VERS. 21.-- *Of them that are in fetters.* [n: (AUG.)] The fear of God: these are the fetters, etc., *as far as* and when they are loosed, they are turned into glory. [n: (CASS.)] They that are fettered, the saints, etc., *as far as* whence their discourse runs together into the Church universal. *That he might loose the children.* [n: (AUG.)] But we are the children of those slain men, we who are loosed from the knots of sins [1013B] and from the chains of desires.

VERS. 23.-- *He answered him in the way of his strength.* [n: (CASS.)] The fourth part, where, after the certainty of pardon, he says that from his own brevity he passes over to eternity. [n: (AUG.)] *He answered.* Because: I speak, that I may declare the name of the Lord and his praise in Jerusalem, etc., *as far as* he does not despise, but renders fruit. *He answered him.* That is, he believes that Christ has risen. But because he foresees that some will say, there was indeed once a Church gathering all into one, but now it ceases.

*The fewness,* he says, *of my days:* not the eternity of those days where I shall be, but the temporal days, so long as I shall be in this world. *Declare unto me.* Because of those who say that the Church now is not. That utterance is not idle, that petition is not empty. For the way itself, that is, Christ, [1013C] has thus declared: *I am with you unto the consummation of the world [n: (Matth. XXVIII)]*. The whole time unto the end is that scantiness of days: because everything that comes to an end is scant, that from thence there may be a passing over into eternity. *Declare* and:

VERS. 24.-- *Call me not back in the midst of my days.* Let it not so come to pass, as the heretics say, etc., *as far as* but they are nothing but halves, if the eternal ones be not added.

VERS. 25.-- *In the way of strength.* [n: (AUG.)] In the faith by which he believes that Christ has risen. Christ was the way of strength after the resurrection, when, being now not weak but strong, he calls the Church into heaven. The praise of faith is not that it believes him dead, which the Pagan also believes, but that it believes him glorified.

[1013D]Or, the Christian religion is briefly defined when it is called *the way of strength.*

Or, in generation of generations. There is a generation of generations which passes not away, gathered out of all, that is, the saints. In it shall be the years of the Lord, which do not pass away, that is, the eternity of God, which is the very substance of God, having nothing mutable.

VERS. 26.-- *In the beginning thou, O Lord.* As if: call me not back, because I tend toward eternity, which he expounds, saying: *In the beginning thou, O Lord, didst found the earth.*

VERS. 27.-- *They shall perish.* [n: (AUG.)] The heavens themselves shall perish; of those nearest the earth this is certain; whence Peter also: *But the earth and the heavens which now are, by the same word are kept in store, reserved unto fire [n: (II Petr. III)]*. Of the higher ones [1014A] it is not certain, but if they remain, they remain not in themselves, but in God.

VERS. 28.-- *And as a covering thou shalt change them.* Understand the heavens, which cover all things, which also shall be changed. *And thy years shall not fail.* [n: (AUG.)] Which shall be in the generation of generations. But shall we be able to be there? Assuredly, because *the children of thy servants shall dwell.*

VERS. 29.-- *The children of the servants.* [n: (AUG.)] As the children entered the land of promise, etc., *as far as* with all the newness of the children.

*The children.* A cheerful conclusion. The children of the prophets and of the apostles, not to speak of those themselves.

*The seed.* Works, whence eternal fruit.

*Shall be directed.* Because nothing crooked enters in.



## PSALM CII.

[1014B]

VERS. 2.-- *Bless, O soul.* [n: (AUG.)] After the prayers of the poor man, the proclamations of one rejoicing. All who are in Christ are as it were one man, who rouses and exhorts his own soul to bless the Lord, wishing to define why he is to be blessed, lest any be found slothful thereto. Cry with the voice, if there is a man who [sic: *qut*] hears; without voice, if there is no man to hear, because God hears. [n: (CASS.)] By his own benefits he exhorts men to bless God. [n: (AUG.)] The prophet, speaking in the person of the just man in general, according to the higher power of the soul, that is, the reason, exhorts the soul to bless, saying: *Forget not.* Let not the delight of sin be before thine eyes, but the condemnation and the remission of God: think over all thine evils, [1014C] because as many as they are, so many are the good retributions of God. *Retributions.* Not bestowings. Man bestows evils for goods, and God requites him with goods for evils. He bestowed when he made me, he requites when he justifies.

VERS. 3.-- *Who forgiveth.* [n: (AUG.)] This he does in the sacrament of baptism. *Who healeth all thy diseases.* This he does in the life of a faithful man, while the flesh lusts against the spirit, so that we do such things as we would not; and those languors of the old state are healed from day to day, as newness increases, out of the faith which works through love.

VERS. 4.-- *Who redeemeth thy life from corruption.* This is done in the last resurrection of the dead. *Who crowneth,* etc. This is done at the judgment, where, when the king shall sit upon his throne, about to render to every man according to his works [1014D], who shall boast that he has a heart glorious and clean from sin? Therefore it was necessary to make mention of mercy and of compassions in that place where dues would seem to be so rendered that there should be no room left for mercy.

VERS. 5.-- *Thy youth like the eagle's.* [n: (AUG.)] So great a likeness is set down, etc., *as far as* whose mouth old age has closed, who have forgotten to eat our bread.

VERS. 6.-- *He that doeth.* [n: (AUG.)] The second part, where he admonishes men to bless like [cj: *par*; read *per*, "through"] those things which were done for the men of old, and are done for those that come after. *Mercies.* Which they shall obtain who love their enemies, etc., *as far as* for after *mercy* he adds concerning judgment, saying, *And judgment.*

[1015A]VERS. 7.-- *To Moses.* [n: (AUG.)] By Moses, all the saints, etc., *as far as* through whom he gave the law, that the beloved [cj: *dilectum*; read *delictum*, "the offence"] might abound.

VERS. 11.-- *For.* [n: (ID.)] The heaven is on every side about the earth, whence there is even for them that sin light, air, breath, rain, whence fruit: without heaven the earth comes to an end, so is the protection of God over them that fear him.

VERS. 12.-- *How far distant,* etc. [n: (ID.)] When sin is forgiven, sins set, grace rises, sins are in their setting; turn thyself away, therefore, from the setting of sins, and stretch toward the rising of grace, and thou risest.

VERS. 14.-- *He hath remembered,* etc. [n: (ID.)] Or a second [cj: *secundam*; read *secundum*, "according to"] other reading: *Remember that we are dust.* As if he should say: *He knew our frame,* that is, our weakness: which frame, being turned [1015B] to God, says: *Remember that we are dust,* that is, let thy mercy persevere toward us, lest we forget thy grace. Or, *Man is as grass,* etc. For man goes forth as *a flower,* etc., *as far as The mercy of the Lord. He shall flourish forth* [n: (CASS.)] That is, at the most he shall thus flourish or flourish forth, that is, shall shed his flower. And so it is either intensive or privative.

VERS. 16.-- *For the spirit shall pass in him. And he shall not abide.*

VERS. 17.-- *And justice.* [n: (AUG.)] Mercy and justice are a retribution. He says, *upon the children's children;* many just men have no children, much less children's children, but the *children* are works; the *children's children* are the wages of works.

VERS. 19.-- *The Lord hath prepared his seat in heaven.* [n: (CASS.)] He proclaims the judgment to come, lest they that sin grow insolent; [1015C] as if: and thou oughtest to be mindful, so as to act, because the Lord is in heaven.

VERS. 21.-- *Bless the Lord, all ye his angels, ye mighty.* [n: (ID.)] In the third place he admonishes the heavenly beings and all other creatures to bless. *Bless the Lord,* etc. He speaks by way of congratulation, not by way of command or of admonition. *His ministers.* [n: (AUG.)] Bless ye, etc., *as far as* that what he has in his mouth he may show forth in his work.



## PSALM CIII.

VERS. 1.-- *A psalm of David.* [n: (CASS.)] Likewise God [cj: *Deus*; read *Deum*, "to bless God"] admonishes men to bless, first through heaven and earth and their adornment. [n: (AUG.)] Although indeed all things here may be devoutly taken according to the letter, etc., *as far as* in all things the spiritual sense is to be sought. *Bless the Lord, [1015D] O my soul,* etc., *magnified.* [sic: *mugnificatus*] In knowledge, because thou art always great by nature, who thinkest [cj: *putes*; read *potes*, "thou canst"] not to grow or be lessened, because thou art perfect; *Confession,* that is, thou hast on every side creatures which confess thee, and which are comely and full of light. Or, comeliness is in the essence of God, and light on every side. [n: (CASS.)] Or it signifies the incarnation, through which the confession and the glory of the heavenly majesty became known, and men confessed him, who are light by knowledge. [n: (AUG.)] Thou hast put on confession, that is, the Church, etc., *as far as* and a garment without spot or wrinkle.

VERS. 2.-- *Stretching out the heaven thus.* [n: (CASS.)] What things confess him? Heaven, earth, sea, with all their adornment; of each of these in what follows. [n: (AUG.)] *Stretching out [1016A] the heaven like.* According to the letter, the heaven, as it were a roof, etc., *as far as* afterwards at the end, like a book, Scripture shall be folded up.

VERS. 3.-- *Who coverest.* [n: (ID.)] According to the letter they are waters, etc., *as far as* whose upper parts are the two precepts of charity. Or thou who *coverest with waters,* that is, with the spiritual sense, the surface of Scripture; or by spiritual gifts thou fortifiest their charity, that nothing may be able to separate them. *Upon the wings of the winds,* etc. Wind is a movement of the air, etc., *as far as* but the charity of Christ surpasses the charity of all.

VERS. 4.-- *A burning fire.* [n: (CASS.)] That is, the seraphim, who are the higher order, thou makest thy ministers, whence in Isaiah: *There flew to me one of the seraphim,* who are the higher order.

VERS. 5.-- *Who hast founded the earth upon its own [1016B] stability.* [n: (ID.)] Therefore the Holy Spirit in this psalm speaks things difficult according to the letter, that we may seek spiritual things.

VERS. 7.-- *At the voice of thunder.* [n: (ID.)] By terrible winds the waters shall be dried up; the wicked shall fear at the threatening of the judgment.

VERS. 8.-- *They ascend.* [n: (AUG.)] The wicked, as mountains, etc., *as far as* that the Church may be free to preach. [n: (CASS.)] God permits the proud, etc., *as far as* that they may no longer take possession of the world.

VERS. 10.-- *In the valleys.* [n: (AUG.)] From the humble, made subject to grace, even if they are mountains by spiritual height, flow the fountains of the Holy Spirit, who rests upon the humble. *Between the midst.* [n: (ID.)] That is, between the midst, etc., *as far as* and such a mountain as this is not to be heard.

[1016C]VERS. 11.-- *All shall drink.* [n: (ID.)] All were in the dish let down thrice to Peter, etc., *as far as* that the Church may be gathered. *Wild asses.* [n: (ID.)] Great untamed asses of the woods, etc., *as far as* or the proud Jews shall put off drinking until the last time.

VERS. 12.-- *Over them the birds of the air.* [n: (ID.)] Not all birds abide, etc., *as far as* others on the left hand are condemned.

VERS. 15.-- [sic: *VERS. 15.*] *Of the fruit of thy works.* [n: (ID.)] Let no man therefore glory in his own works, but *he that glorieth, let him glory in the Lord,* for by the grace of God the earth is filled. Thus therefore grace is given freely. If it is rendered to the works of men, it is called a wage.

VERS. 14.-- *For the service of men.* [n: (ID.)] Whence *whereas I was free from all, I made myself the servant of all,* etc., [1016D] *as far as* to whom he gave an example of taking necessary things from those subject to them. *In oil. To every man is given the Spirit unto manifestation:* [var: Vulg. 1 Cor. 12:7 *Unicuique autem datur manifestatio Spiritus ad utilitatem*, "to every man the manifestation of the Spirit is given unto profit"] grace, therefore, and a certain brightness, etc., *as far as* that the face may be made glad with oil. *And bread.* Bread, therefore, and wine are the same thing. For this bread and wine is justice, is truth, is Christ: and both the one and the other gladdens and strengthens the heart.

VERS. 16.-- *Which he hath planted.* [n: (ID.)] Why does he leave the brightness of the world to those planted by himself? Because there is the coot, a bird which dwells in the waters; these are they who abide in the grace of baptism. Or, the heron is a most rapacious bird, and such as these Christ does not reject.

VERS. 17.-- *There the sparrows.* [n: (ID.)] Small flying things, etc., *as far as* that they may build their nests in the cedars of Libanus. *The heron's* [1017A] *house.* [n: (AUG.)] *Is the leader of them,* that is, of the sparrows, or of the cedars, which nevertheless cannot be said in Latin, because cedars are of the feminine gender; and this in no way holds according to the letter, for how is the house, that is, the nest of the coot, which is a bird of the sea or of the marsh, the leader of sparrows? But by this he hints that spiritual things are to be sought. And if the sparrows build their nests among the rich of this world, etc., *as far as* but they shall not perish, because the rock is their leader. [n: (CASS.)] Or the coot is a bird which dwells in the waters; these are they who abide in the grace of baptism, whose house is baptism. And it is as if he should say: Who are the sparrows? Those who are baptized. And this is it, *the coot's house,* that is, baptism, which is the house of the regenerate, *is the leader of them,* that is, of the sparrows, that is, of the Christians.

[n: (HIER., CASS.)] The heron: a most rapacious bird, etc., [1017B] *as far as* because he himself, being converted, converted many others to the faith. *For the harts: a rock.* [n: (AUG.)] The harts are great spiritual men, etc., *as far as* this is that *from which* (as was said above) *the birds of the air give their voices.*

VERS. 19.-- *He hath made the moon.* [n: (CASS.)] After earthly things he passes over to the heavenly, which nevertheless act for the earth, that is, serve it. After so many kinds of Christians, which he signified by the animals, and after the aforesaid ordering, ascending he adds concerning Christ and the Church, namely how the Church here in time waxes and wanes, as though she made the settings and risings of the true sun.

VERS. 20.-- *Thou hast appointed,* etc. *And night was made* in them, that is, the dark obstinacy of the mind. *And these shall pass through.* [n: (AUG.)] Not, as above, the converted, the Gentiles, but the demons, who at the time of the passion found the hearts [1017C] of the wavering apostles; but when Christ rose again, because they cannot prevail in all, they abide *in the dens,* that is, in the hearts of the obstinate: so, when Christ sets in some man, evils grow strong; when he rises again, they flee. *The setting.* The sun so sets that it may rise: shall he then that sleeps, that is, Christ, not go on to rise again? He set because he willed it, no man takes away his life; when he set, darkness came upon the apostles, their hope failed.

VERS. 21.-- *The young lions,* etc. [n: (AUG.)] There are also princes of the demons, as the whelps too are contemptible demons, which in the darkness seek to seduce souls; the greater lion always *goeth about seeking whom he may devour [n: (I Pet. V)]*, and they are fed with the errors of men.

VERS. 22.-- *The sun is risen,* etc. [n: (CASS.)] After [1017D] he has said what the setting of the true sun did, he adds concerning the rising.

VERS. 24.-- *With possession.* [n: (AUG.)] Elsewhere, *creature.* [n: (ID.)] Through Christ the Father, etc., *as far as* yet the wood was given, upon which we might sail across.

VERS. 25.-- *This great sea,* etc. [n: (CASS.)] The second part, where he says: How he leads the Church across the sea of this world to the shore of the homeland. *Creeping things,* etc. [n: (AUG.)] Evil spirits, whose snares creep.

VERS. 26.-- *This dragon which thou hast formed.* [n: (ID.)] Besides these animals, etc., *as far as* he was made greater in malice. *For mocking.* [n: (ID.)] It is said in Job *(Job XL)* that Satan is mocked by the angels of God, who are set over the powers of the air, that they may not hurt us.

VERS. 27.-- *Meat.* [n: (ID.)] The dragon's food is earth, [1018A] because it was said to him: *Thou shalt eat earth all the days of my life [n: (Gen. III)]*. [var: Vulg. Gen. 3:14 *cunctis diebus vitae tuae*, "all the days of thy life"] Because they that love earthly things are the devil's food. But the food of the just man is the word of God, whence, *Not in bread alone doth man live, but in every word that proceedeth from the mouth of God [n: (Deut. VIII)]*.

VERS. 28.-- *Thy hand, all things shall be filled with good.* [n: (CASS.)] Or the hand is the power which abundantly fills all things.

VERS. 29.-- *But when thou turnest away.* [n: (AUG.)] Many, being filled with good, have ascribed it to themselves; but God, willing to prove to them that they have it not from themselves but from God, so that with the good they may have humility, sometimes troubles them, turns away his face, and they fall into temptation.

VERS. 30.-- *Send forth thy spirit.* [n: (CASS.)] The dust is called theirs, because from themselves they have it that they are [1018B] dust. But the Spirit is the Lord's: behold the medicine for those returned into dust.

VERS. 32.-- *To tremble.* [n: (AUG.)] Better is the trembling of humility than the confidence of pride. Whence the Apostle: *With trembling work out your salvation [n: (Phil. II)]*. *The mountains.* [n: (ID.)] Those who were proud, boasting that all things came from themselves. Even as it happened on mount Sina.

VERS. 33.-- *I will sing.* [n: (CASS.)] The third part, where he promises that he will sing always. As if he should say: These foretold things the Lord has done, and these being beheld. *In my life.* [n: (AUG.)] Present and to come; of hope and of sight. Now hope is our life, afterwards it shall be eternal. The life of mortal life is the hope of immortal life. *As long as I am.* [n: (ID.)] Because in him I am without end, I will sing psalms as long as I am, lest any other act of that life be supposed than to sing psalms: [1018C] because he who is praised cannot come to be wearisome, it does not become wearisome to praise him; but he is ever loved, ever praised.

VERS. 34.-- *Let it be pleasant.* Another reading: *Let my disputation be sweet to him.* [n: (AUG.)] For the disputation of man with God is the confession of sins. Whence Isaiah [n: (Isa. I)]. *Take away the wickednesses from your souls, say ye to do well, judge for the fatherless, and come and let us dispute, saith the Lord.* [var: Vulg. Isa. 1:16-18 *auferte malum cogitationum vestrarum ab oculis meis … discite benefacere … et venite, et arguite me, dicit Dominus*, "learn to do well … and come and reprove me"] Thy disputation, therefore, is the sacrifice of thy humility, which is sweet to God.

VERS. 35.-- *Bless the Lord, O my soul.* [n: (ID.)] He repeats what stood at the beginning of the psalm: *Bless the Lord, O my soul,* hinting by this that the end of good men is like the beginning.



## PSALM CIV.

[1018D]

VERS. 1.-- *Alleluia.* [n: (CASS.)] Here for the first time *alleluia,* nor was it set down before this by anyone; and no tongue has presumed to change it. *Alleluia,* praise ye. *Ia* is the name of the invisible God, which belongs to him alone. Through twenty psalms, which have this title, the great works of God are told, and great is the exultation in them. [n: (CASS.)] Here the prophet commends the good among that people of Israel, who together with other believers are the true seed of Abraham, the children of the promise.

*Give glory to the Lord.* [n: (AUG.)] The first psalm to which alleluia is prefixed, etc., *as far as* he subjoins what temporal things the mercy of God gave them.

VERS. 2.-- *All things:* [n: (CASS.)] all things; there is nothing that shame should [1019A] keep silent. Or, all the things which they shall tell are wonderful, for no one can relate them all.

VERS. 3.-- *Be ye praised in the name,* that is, rejoice that you are called Christians from Christ.

[n: (AUG.)] Be ye praised: he does not forbid being praised, if they are good; if they are not, then not save in the Lord, who makes men good. Be ye praised in the name. Which elsewhere is, *In the Lord shall my soul be praised:* what he adds there, *let the meek hear and rejoice:* here too in a manner, *let the heart rejoice,* as if he should say,

VERS. 4.-- *The face,* etc. [n: (ID.)] That is, the presence, etc., *as far as* the wicked man can fear the presence which the good man loves. [n: (HIER.)] From the martyr Hippolytus, etc., *as far as* there about to take his Church from among the Gentiles.

VERS. 5.-- *Remember, and the rest.* [n: (AUG.)] Seek [1019B] him, etc., *as far as* if it is a great thing to seek what God is, at least behold the things which he has made. *The wonders,* etc. [n: (ID.)] Little by little he ascends from visible wonders to judgments: thence to the everlasting testament, where there is both precept and promise.

VERS. 7.-- *In all the earth.* [n: (ID.)] Think not that the sons of Abraham are in one nation according to the flesh, because in all the earth are Jacob and Israel, to whom he makes known his judgments, but not to another nation.

VERS. 8.-- *For ever,* [n: (ID.)] that is, unto eternity, whence afterwards, unto an everlasting testament. Which testament, if it be taken as the Old on account of the land of Chanaan, of which it follows. How is it everlasting, since that earthly inheritance cannot be everlasting? *Of the testament.* For that is called a testament which [1019C] is promised, and the teaching in which the promise is declared, and for the keeping of which it is rendered. [n: (ID.)] The promise is what we ought to receive: the commandment, what we ought to do. This is faith, that the just man live by faith, and to this faith an inheritance [sic: *haere ditas*] is promised. *Abraham, Isaac and Jacob.* Behold three patriarchs, whose God he calls himself in a special manner: *I am the God of Abraham, the God of Isaac, the God of Jacob.* Behold a threefold authority.

VERS. 10.-- *Unto an everlasting testament,* [n: (ID.)] that is, that from the very word fulfilled, etc., *as far as* and the son abides in the house for ever.

VERS. 11.-- *The land of Chanaan,* [n: (CASS.)] that is, that which is signified by it: for he gave this land also as a figure of the eternal inheritance [n: (I Joan. IV)]. [n: (AUG.)] Fittingly the eternal inheritance is called the land of Chanaan, [1019D] because from it servants are driven off and sons received.

VERS. 12.-- *When they were in number.* [n: (CASS.)] What supports of temporal life he bestowed upon the fathers and upon their sons, as well in flesh as in faith, not that they should cleave to these things, but should refer them to the eternal good, which is, that being drawn by them they should seek out the justifications and the law of God.

VERS. 13.-- *From a kingdom.* [n: (AUG.)] Not that the kingdom of Israel then was, but that it was to be.

VERS. 15.-- *Touch ye not.* [n: (ID.)] These words, although they are not in the history, God nevertheless spoke them either secretly in the hearts of men, or by an angel. *The anointed,* [n: (ID.)] because even then they were Christians, though secretly, whence *Abraham rejoiced that he might see my day; he saw it and [1020A] was glad [n: (Joan. VIII)]*. For no one, either before or after, was reconciled to God apart from this faith, *for there is one mediator, Christ [n: (I Tim. II)]*.

VERS. 16.-- *And he called.* [n: (AUG.)] The fourth part, where he begins to narrate, etc., *as far as* he commanded that to be which already was in his disposition. *So he called, and all the staff of bread was broken [n: (Gen. XXXVII)]*.

VERS. 17.-- *A man.* [n: (ID.)] Joseph, sold by his brothers, was afterwards exalted in Egypt for his justice. *Joseph.* [n: (ID.)] If Joseph was both humbled and exalted, etc., *as far as* but *seek ye first the kingdom of God, and all things shall be added.*

VERS. 18.-- *In fetters.* [n: (ID.)] We do not read of Joseph's fetters in Genesis, but what was there passed over is to be believed, since the Spirit says it in the psalm. [1020B] [n: (CASS.)] And although he found favour with the keeper of the prison, yet he was first in anguish. There follows. *The iron pierced,* etc. As if, besides the evils of the body, *the iron,* that is, the tribulation of hard necessity, which was in his burning and anxious soul, *pierced through,* etc. Or according to another reading, *his soul passed through the iron,* that is, the tribulation of hard necessity. The same sense as the former.

VERS. 23.-- *In the land of Cham,* etc. [n: (AUG.)] Cham the father of Chanaan, from whom the Egyptians draw their origin.

VERS. 24.-- *And he increased his people,* etc. [n: (ID.)] The fifth part. Behold, he sets forth briefly what was done in Egypt, which afterwards he relates more fully. He tells also how he strengthened his people over their enemies.

[1020C] *And he strengthened him.* By the signs of Moses, namely; for before this the male offspring of the children of Israel were being killed, and they themselves were worn down in the making of bricks. But afterwards, by a mighty hand and by the signs and portents of the Lord, they were made men to be feared and honoured.

VERS. 25.-- *He turned.* [n: (ID.)] He begins to relate how this was done. He turned: not that God is the author of evil, but he uses evils well; while he increased the prosperity of Israel, they were turned to envy, because they were already evil. *That they should hate.* Envy is the hatred of another's prosperity.

VERS. 26.-- *He sent.* He who could do all things by himself, that he might teach with what moderation he had done these things. *Whom he chose, him.* [n: (ID.)] It would have been enough to say, whom he chose: but nothing [1020D] is to be sought in this, that he added, *him,* but it is the custom of the divine manner of speech, for the sake of explanation.

VERS. 27.-- *The words of his signs.* [n: (ID.)] By which it was preached by them, etc., *as far as* he wrought therefore through them words not of voices, but of signs or of wonders.

VERS. 28.-- [n: (ID.)] Or: *They provoked his words,* which is plain. Or, Moses and Aaron did not provoke his words, because they bore patiently with those men, namely the Egyptians, even the hardest of them, on account of the commandment of the Lord, until all things which the Lord had appointed should be done upon them. *Darkness,* etc. [n: (CASS.)] They suffered it among the plagues.
