But here he says that their hearts were *darkened,* who despised the precepts of God. Blindness is a fitting [1021A] beginning of the plagues, without which they would not have incurred the rest.

VERS. 29.-- *He turned their waters.* [n: (AUG.)] The second plague, which in Exodus is the first, which signified that he would turn the waters of the Red Sea also, and that unto death for them.

VERS. 30.-- *The earth brought forth.* [n: (AUG.)] He turned their land into frogs, because they were so many that this is fitly said by way of hyperbole.

VERS. 31.-- *He spoke, and there came the dog-fly,* etc. [n: (ID.)] For in his word they were without time, before they came to be in time through that same word: or through angels, or through Moses he in a manner spoke, that it should be done then, when it was to be done.

VERS. 32.-- *He made their rains,* etc. [n: (ID.)] It is like to that: *and their land gave forth frogs.*

[1021B]VERS. 34.-- *The locust and the bruchus.* [n: (ID.)] The locust and the bruchus are one plague, because the one is the parent, the other the offspring.

VERS. 36.-- *And he struck.*[n: (Id. )] Although there are ten plagues, etc., *as far as* who could not despise the wage of their labours, though a temporal one.

VERS. 37.-- *And he brought them out,* etc. The sixth part, according to Cassiodorus and Augustine. Above, the justice of God told what evils it brought upon the enemies of the Israelites: now he subjoins what temporal goods the mercy of God gave them, that being helped here they may seek the law of God. [n: (AUG.)] *And he brought out*: God said to Abraham, etc., *as far as* then there follows the second benefit, *And [sic: *Ft*] there was no one weak, and the rest.*

VERS. 38.-- *Because the fear of them fell upon [1021C] them.* They feared Israel, lest he should consume what was left of them.

[n: (AUG.)] Here was fulfilled what he said above, and he strengthened him over his enemy.

VERS. 39.-- *He spread a cloud for their protection.* And this is the second part, according to Augustine, where he subjoins what temporal goods the mercy of God gave them, the evils having been told above which the justice of God brought upon their enemies.

[n: (AUG.)] Now he begins the benefits which were done for them as they journeyed in the desert.

VERS. 40.-- *They asked,* [n: (AUG.)] the good, for the support of the weak. *The quail: and he filled them with the bread of heaven:* which is also flesh, because here he speaks not of the embittering of the wicked, but of the faith of the elect: they themselves are to be understood [1021D] to have asked, whereby the murmurs of the embittered might be held down.

VERS. 42.-- *For he remembered his holy word.* [n: (ID.)] By all these benefits God commends in Abraham the merit of faith, whence he adds: *for he remembered.*

VERS. 44.-- *And he gave them.* [n: (AUG.)] Not that they should worship him for the sake of these things, but that they should refer them to the eternal good, that is, *that they may keep his justifications. They possessed,* etc., not that these things are to be held for the highest good, but that they may be free for those things whereby the highest and eternal good may be acquired, not that in them they should melt away in luxury or grow torpid.

VERS. 45.-- *The justifications,* etc. [n: (CASS.)] They are where [1022A] there is a plain and absolute precept, as, *Thou shalt love God and thy neighbour,* which are to be kept. The law is of those things which are to be kept not bodily but spiritually: and therefore it is to be sought out, because it is a shadow of things to come. *The law.* As concerning its circumcision and the other matters, which are to be diligently investigated, because they are spoken mystically.



## PSALM CV.

VERS. 1.-- *Alleluia. Alleluia.* Some say that one *alleluia* belongs to the end of the preceding psalm, and the other to the following. For Jerome hands down that this rule is to be held. To whose assertion Cassiodorus also agrees. But the greater number assign it, wherever alleluia stands, to the psalm that follows: and sometimes once, sometimes twice; as the Lord in the Gospel now said *amen,* now *amen amen,* for the greater certainty [1022B] of the matter. With these Augustine agrees, especially since no codices of the Greeks have alleluia at the end of a psalm.

*Give glory to the Lord.* [n: (AUG.)] This psalm is joined to the preceding, etc., *as far as* he admonishes therefore to a twofold confession, of praise and of sins.

[n: (CASS.)] The Hebrew, having left the perfidy of his fathers and being turned to the mercy of God, persuades others to be turned to the same mercy, praying that they may be joined to the well-pleasing people, which was to be gathered at the coming of Christ.

VERS. 2.-- *Who shall declare.* [n: (CASS.)] He that asks for mercy is filled with the consideration of the divine works, and marvelling says: *Who shall declare?*

VERS. 3.-- *Blessed are they that keep.* [n: (AUG.)] Or because [1022C] there follows, etc., *as far as* judgment and justice, because *Blessed are they that,* etc.

VERS. 5.-- *To see,* etc. [n: (AUG.)], that we may see thee in that goodness which thou bestowest upon the elect, that is, by thy grace let us see, lest we remain blind. *That thou mayest be praised:* it is strange that in very many codices, etc., *as far as* because he is not praised save on account of thee.

VERS. 6.-- *We have sinned.* The second part, where he enumerates the sins of the fathers which they committed in Egypt, and yet says that they were delivered by God.

*Iniquity.* [n: (CASS.)] A third time he says the same, that the confession may appear pure; a man is loosed the more quickly, the more keenly he condemns himself.

VERS. 7.-- *They understood not.* [n: (AUG.)] That is, what thou didst will to give them through those things, that is, not a temporal but [1022D] an eternal good; which is awaited through patience, and therefore they, being impatient, murmured, and wished to be happy in temporal things. [n: (CASS.)] There are two things which commend benefits, understanding and memory, and he reproves both of these in them. And first the understanding, when he says: *they understood not the wonders. They provoked, going up.* [n: (AUG.)] Fearing God and despairing, etc., *as far as* whence it deserved to obtain among the others the privilege or the princedom of the kingdom.

VERS. 9.-- *As in the desert.* [n: (ID.)] Because where there were depths, it was made by dryness like a desert where there are no waters.

VERS. 10.-- *He redeemed them from the hand.* [n: (ID.)] At what price? As if he should say, at none; but it was done as a figure of baptism, [1023A] in which we are redeemed from the hand of the devil by the blood of Christ; whence also he said *the Red Sea,* because by its colour blood was figured.

VERS. 12.-- *And they believed in.* [n: (AUG.)] Behold the hard hearts of the Jews, which believe not before but after miracles: it had been better to believe him who promised.

VERS. 13.-- *They quickly did, forgetting.* The third part, where he sets forth the sins which they committed after the going out: nor yet did the mercy of God fail them, he being appeased through Moses. Or rebuke, by the justice of God, taking vengeance upon certain of them through Moses. [n: (CASS.)] *They quickly did.* With the utmost speed they were changed, etc. [sic: *ete.*], *as far as* for God put off giving, that he might prove them.

VERS. 14.-- *They lusted a lust in the desert.* [n: (AUG.)] Such a manner of speech, as *they praised,* etc. [n: (CASS.)] [1023B] The excess of the desire is shown by the doubling of the word.

VERS. 15.-- *Fulness.* This is not that fulness of which it is said. *Blessed are they that hunger and thirst after justice, for they shall be filled [n: (Matth. V)]*.

*Into their souls.* [n: (AUG.)] The animal nature, which is sustained by food: whence also in the Gospel: *Is not the soul more than the meat?* Or, souls, that the will, which had asked in despair, might be overcome by abundance; after fulness there follows sedition, whence he adds.

VERS. 16.-- *And they provoked.* [n: (CASS.)] Canvassing for honour by envy, Moses in the camp, and Aaron the holy one of the Lord: this provoking was for the priesthood which Aaron had, and his sons.

VERS. 17.-- *The earth was opened.* [n: (ID.)] The quality of the punishment inflicted declares the praise of God, who has avenged his own; they are devoured by the earth [1023C] who savoured of earthly things.

VERS. 18.-- *In the synagogue.* [n: (ID.)] For Core and two hundred and fifty with him, wishing to have the priesthood of Aaron, took censers, to burn incense, and therefore were consumed by divine fire. *Sinners.* [n: (AUG.)] He does not say sinning, which belongs to a good man too: but *sinners,* men exceedingly wicked and laden with heavy burdens of sins.

VERS. 19.-- *And they made.* [n: (CASS.)] Not enduring the absence of Moses, while he himself tarried on the top of mount Sinai to receive the law, they made a calf. *Horeb.* It is interpreted *Calvary,* where the crucifixion took place, etc., *as far as* this the impatience of the fickle did, as all evils are born of it.

VERS. 20.-- *In the likeness.* [n: (AUG.)] He does not say into [1023D] a likeness, but in a likeness. And it is such a manner of speech as above, *and they believed in his words.*

VERS. 21.-- *They forgot.* [n: (CASS.)] Great forgetfulness indeed in the memory, if not the deliverance.

*Terrible things.* Because they afflicted their adversaries, and showed to these men whom they ought to fear.

VERS. 23.-- *Had stood in the breach.* [n: (AUG.)] Saying: *If thou forgive them not, blot me out of thy book.* How much avails the intercession of the saints? Moses, secure concerning the justice of God, by which he could not blot him out, obtained mercy for those who would justly have perished.

*That he might turn away his wrath.* Concerning the water of contradiction and the immolation of the sons, whereat God was the more vehemently angered, and yet afterwards had mercy. *And for nothing.* The fourth [1024A] part, where he likewise enumerates yet other sins, and says that God was appeased through Phinees.

*They believed not his word.* [n: (AUG.)] They despised that which was signified by her, etc., *as far as* they did not await his counsel.

*In his tents.* [n: (CASS.)] They murmured, because this befalls not them that labour but the idle, whence the greater odium of the crime grows.

VERS. 26.-- *And he lifted up his hand over them,* etc. [n: (CASS.)] This justice of God is set first, that the mercy which follows may be the more glorified, which mercy is shown in what follows, where God is said to be appeased by the intervention of Phinees.

VERS. 28.-- *And they were initiated to Beelphegor.* [n: (ID.)] Or, they were consecrated, as though worshipping only for a time: [1024B] a graver excess and a more vehement wrath: they devoted their sons to the demons. [n: (AUG.)] *And they were initiated,* before he should say, etc., *as far as* he delivered him whose destruction was to be the greater.

VERS. 30.-- *And Phinees stood up.* [n: (CASS.)] With a firm mind, etc., *as far as* because he who acts well, prays.

VERS. 31.-- *And it was reputed,* etc. [n: (AUG.)] God, who knew with how great charity for the people that was done, reputed this to his priest unto justice, not only so long as there is a generation, but even unto eternity.

VERS. 32.-- *And they provoked,* etc. [n: (CASS.)] The fifth part, where he treats of the water of contradiction and of the immolation of the sons, whereat God was the more vehemently angered, and yet afterwards had mercy. As if: they so provoked him in their own devices, as was said above; now [1024C] again they have provoked him.

VERS. 33.-- *And he made a distinction with his lips,* etc. [n: (CASS.)] This miracle, etc., *as far as* he did not hold the confidence he ought to have held. [n: (AUG.)] *And he made a distinction.* As if: God could not do this, etc., *as far as* yet he was not estranged from the kingdom of the grace of God.

VERS. 35.-- *The nations.* Those seven peoples which possessed the land of promise.

VERS. 36.-- *And it became to them a stumbling-block.* [n: (CASS.)] The land of promise, given as a reward, was turned to ruin for those who transgressed, because by conformity with the nations they became idolaters.

VERS. 37.-- *And they sacrificed.* [n: (AUG.)] Although that history does not relate these things, yet this prophet cannot lie. [n: (CASS.)] If not in the first, then in the following [1024D] age was done what David relates.

VERS. 38.-- *And slain.* [n: (AUG.)] A figurative manner of speech, the container for the contained, that is, men; for they themselves were slaying their own souls by sacrificing their sons, and they were defiled in their works. [var: Vulg. Ps. 105:38 *Et infecta est terra in sanguinibus*, "and the land was defiled with blood"]

VERS. 39.-- *In their own devices.* [n: (ID.)] Not that they had invented them first, but because they imitated others. Which is plainer in the Greek, both in these places and above; whence others also render it, pursuits, or affections, or pleasures.

VERS. 40.-- *He abhorred.* [n: (CASS.)] That is, he turned away from them: and to be turned away from is the utmost of all evils, whence ruin follows, *and he delivered.*

VERS. 41.-- *And he delivered them,* etc. [n: (ID.)] Behold, they who gloried in idols lie under the reproach of servitude, [1025A] because they serve their enemies, which is a hard thing. *They that hated.* It is grievous to serve one who has been offended, who is appeased by no compliance.

VERS. 43.-- *Often he delivered,* etc. [n: (CASS.)] A twofold mercy; for above he set out first the benefits of God, and yet their iniquity does not cease; he brought in vengeance, and again he has mercy on the afflicted.

*In his own counsel.* [n: (AUG.)] The counsel of man is evil to the man himself, who seeks the things that are his own, not the things that are God's.

VERS. 45.-- *Of the testament.* [n: (CASS.)] The cause that Israel did not perish for its debt is the New Testament, wherein is the coming of the Lord, who was promised to Abraham. *It repented him.* [n: (AUG.)] Because he changed what seemed about to destroy them. [n: (CASS.)] Then he repents, when he sees us repent, and he is such to us as we are to him. [n: (AUG.)] God does nothing as by a sudden [1025B] counsel, but what he foreknew from eternity. But he is said to change that which, according to the causes of things, would seem to follow, when other causes intervene on the contrary part. But both the causes, and what should come to pass, he unchangeably foresaw.

VERS. 46.-- *Unto mercies.* [n: (CASS.)] Because of the diverse gifts: prophets, apostles, martyrs and confessors were of the Jews, whom he gave unto mercies: and this *in the sight of all, they had taken them,* [var: Vulg. Ps. 105:46 *in conspectu omnium qui ceperant eos*, "in the sight of all that had taken them captive"] that is, of the Gentiles, that the Gentiles might marvel at them, who before had despised them. Or, made free before the demons, under whom they had been captives.

VERS. 47.-- *Save us.* [n: (CASS.)] The sixth part. Now filled with the abundance of the praise of God, he prays for that which he saw was to come, that the Church may be gathered out of all nations, [1025C] and may sing eternal praises. [n: (AUG.)] *Save us,* as if: Thou hast given mercies in the sight of all, etc., *as far as* there follows. *And gather us from among the nations.*

VERS. 48.-- *Blessed be the Lord.* [n: (CASS.)] This is the praise which he desires that they, being gathered, should sing; this the Church now sings, glad for so great a good. *From everlasting unto,* etc., that is, from eternity unto eternity, because he shall be praised without end: this is the third consummation of the body of Christ in immortality, which was prayed at the beginning of the psalm: *Remember us,* O Lord, *in the good pleasures of thy people,* to see, to rejoice, that thou mayest be praised with thine inheritance.


## PSALM CVI.

*Alleluia, alleluia.* [n: (AUG.)] This psalm commends [1025D] the mercy of God, etc., *as far as* the things which the Apostle set down, although they signify the same.

VERS. 1.-- *Give praise to the Lord.* [n: (ID.)] This psalm teaches what we are through ourselves, etc., *as far as* he first admonishes the redeemed to give praise. *Give praise.*

[n: (CASS., AUG.)] Of which there are seven divisions, etc., *as far as* in the seventh he treats of heretics, there, *And they were made few.*

VERS. 3.-- *And from the sea.* [n: (CASS.)] For the south he puts the sea, understanding thereby the ocean of that quarter; and this can be taken for any quarter of the world whatever.

VERS. 4.-- *They wandered.* [n: (ID.)] The second part, where is the first temptation, etc., *as far as* fourthly, confession for having been heard, that is, praise.

[1026A]VERS. 5.-- *Hungry.* [n: (AUG.)] He signifies the curious of the world, who sought out the truth by divers opinions, and found it not. *Their soul,* etc. God commends himself, because it is expedient for us; we fail that we may ask, and may love him who comes to our aid.

VERS. 6.-- *And he led.* [n: (AUG.)] As it were, he rescued them from ignorance, showing the way, Christ. Behold, he freed them from error; but in what manner he freed them from the hunger and thirst wherewith they burned, he will say afterwards; but he sets confession first.

VERS. 8.-- *Wonderful things.* [n: (HIER.)] As it were, in a wonderful manner of redemption were the mercies given.

VERS. 9.-- *Him that is hungry.* [n: (CASS.)] Him that desires to know, or him that desires not to know, but fails through too great fasting, as is wont to befall one worn out [1026B] by too great hunger or by infirmity, so that he desires not to eat, but fails through too great fasting.

*Sitting.* [n: (AUG.)] The third part, where the second temptation is set down, etc., *as far as* let us also consider that we can do this.

VERS. 11.-- *Because they exasperated.* [n: (ID.)] This is another thing, attempting yet again, etc., *as far as* whence he was wroth with them.

*The counsel of the Most High.* That they who were proud might be humbled in straits, which was a wholesome counsel, that the disease of the world might be cured by a contrary remedy.

VERS. 12.-- *Nor was there any who.* [n: (ID.)] For when God withdraws his aid, thou canst labour against vices, but not conquer; and when thou art pressed by thine own depraved habit, the heart will be humbled, that thou mayest learn to cry out, whence he subjoins: *And they cried out.*

[1026C]VERS. 13.-- *And they cried out.* [n: (CASS.)] From a like cause the same sentence as above.

VERS. 16.-- *Brazen gates.* [n: (ID.)] The brazen gates are the vicious customs of men, which suffer not those shut in to go out.

*Bars.* Unclean spirits, who bolt the gates. He puts brazen and iron for strength, but the power of God breaks them.

VERS. 18.-- *All meat.* [n: (AUG.)] The fourth part, where the third temptation is set down, which is weariness. They who above were languishing with hunger are in like manner endangered by loathing; lest therefore thou think men secure through satiety, since they die of loathing also. And this is *all meat,* etc.

[n: (AUG.)] After he has come from ignorance to truth, from error to the way, from the hunger of wisdom to the word [1026D] of faith, and after he has overcome the difficulties of evils, so as to live without complaint, there remains weariness, so that sometimes neither to read nor to pray delights him; he has escaped unskilfulness and concupiscence, but he dies of weariness.

VERS. 23.-- *They that go down the sea.* The fifth part, etc., *as far as* if the whole ship is in peril.

VERS. 24, 25.-- *They have seen the works of the Lord.* [n: (AUG.)] Or *they saw,* etc., *as far as* he expounds their storms, *he spoke, and there stood. In the deep. He spoke, and there stood,* subjoining, *the spirit of the storm.* [n: (ID.)] The deep is the heart of men, whence the winds break forth, and there arise tempests of seditions and dissensions.

VERS. 27.-- *As a drunken man.* [n: (ID.)] They stagger, because their prosperity or their adversity is greater than their strength. [1027A] *And all their wisdom was swallowed up.* They appear restlessly wise when they speak, when they read; but when the tempest comes, all human counsels fail; what is to be done is not seen. It remains therefore to cry out, that God may make their heart firm, lest it be tossed about by temptations.

VERS. 32.-- *In the Church.* [n: (AUG.)] Behold whence he has spoken all these things, and where all that is foretold is done.

VERS. 33.-- *He hath turned rivers.* [n: (CASS.)] The sixth part, thus far: how he makes even the evils of enemies to profit. [n: (AUG.)] *He hath turned rivers.* Consequently the Church is commended, etc., *as far as* whence also the benefits of God toward the Church are openly shown.

VERS. 34.-- *In saltness.* [n: (CASS.)] Salt moisture, where it prevails, takes away fruitfulness.

[1027B] *From the malice of them that dwell.* [n: (AUG.)] Because of the malice of those who inhabited that land, that is, the tabernacle of the Lord, with deceitful minds.

VERS. 37.-- *And they sowed.* [n: (CASS.)] They sow fields and plant vineyards who purify their bodies by a heavenly institution, that they may bear the fruits of good [sic: *honorum*] works.

VERS. 38.-- *Exceedingly:* [n: (CASS.)] for "greatly," or because many are called, but few chosen.

VERS. 39.-- *And few.* [n: (AUG.)] The seventh part, where somewhat is said also of heretics, etc., *as far as* they were not of the fewness of the elect. *Afflicted.* [n: (CASS.)] Either those who persevere under heretics, or heretics by the snares of the devil. Sorrow and tribulation to those who forsake the author of salvation.

[1027C]VERS. 40.-- *It was poured out.* [n: (AUG.)] Or, contempt was poured out, etc., *as far as* and what has he done even concerning those that were led astray? Behold.

VERS. 41.-- *He helped the poor man.* God could have suffered them always within, but we should not have profited by them. But when they are separated, and disquiet us with their questions, there is born in us a zeal of inquiry and an example of fear, so that, *He that standeth, let him take heed lest he fall.* And what is done concerning those led astray, in the poor man that is helped? *And he set as sheep.* Thou didst think him one poor man; but this poor man is many households, many churches: yet he is also one Church, one sheep. So great are these mysteries, long hidden, and therefore sweetly found.

VERS. 42.-- *The upright shall see and shall rejoice.* [n: (AUG.)] [1027D] Behold whither the Psalmist tends: to this, namely, that he may show that the good are exalted through the mercy of God, as the wicked are crushed through pride.

VERS. 43.-- *Who is wise, and will keep these things?* [n: (ID.)] The conclusion, as if he should say: since the Lord helps the poor man, therefore: *Who is wise? The mercies.* That he delivers from error, etc., *as far as* and the unjust shall stop his mouth.

## PSALM CVII.

VERS. 1.-- *A canticle of a psalm.* [n: (AUG.)] From the last parts of the two, etc., *as far as* that the words of the prophets might be fulfilled. [n: (CASS.)] Here are many of the same things as above, etc., *as far as* and they are well joined together, which in no wise disagree. [n: (ID.)] In the first there is treated the flight [1028A] of David, etc., *as far as Canticle* belongs to divine things, *Psalm* to actual works.

VERS. 2.-- *Prepared.* [n: (CASS.)] The sixth psalm concerning the two natures in Christ. *My heart is ready.* First Christ gives thanks, because, taking upon him the passion, he rose again into glory. Secondly, what salvation has been wrought for the whole earth, there, *That they may be delivered.*

VERS. 7.-- *That they may be delivered.* [n: (ID.)] The second part: what salvation has been wrought for the whole earth over which is the glory; here also the lowliness of the humanity and the power of the majesty are shown.

*Save.* That they may be delivered in the time to come, here save them, lest they fall. Or, he asks that he himself be saved in the resurrection, that the faithful may be delivered, who by such a sign perceived his Godhead. As it were: that I may sing and give praise, *Save [1028B] me* in the resurrection by thy right hand, that is, by the Word, who is the arm of the Father, that *so thy beloved may be delivered.* The order of the words is inverted. For he says: Save me and hear me, and save me.

VERS. 8.-- *I will rejoice.* [n: (AUG.)] Or, I shall be exalted: above, I shall be glad, but here, I shall be exalted, which agrees well enough, because gladness comes of exaltation. And here Christ speaks in the person of the Church.

VERS. 9.-- *And Ephraim.* [n: (ID.)] Ephraim is mine, that is, mine is the fruit-bearing, which is the taking up of my head. This fruit-bearing is from this, that the head took me up, or I the head. [n: (CASS.)] Or Ephraim, that is, fruit-bearing, is the taking up of my head, because when my head took up the crown of thorns, it bore fruit beyond all others: for Christ gained the whole [1028C] world.

VERS. 11.-- *Who bringeth me into the strong city.* [n: (ID.)] As man, Christ asks, that he may answer; he denies that this can be done by man. The city. Hell, which none could open. The Idumeans are the Gentiles, to whom he became known after the resurrection: these things could be done by the Godhead alone.

VERS. 12.-- *Thou hast cast us off, and thou wilt not go forth.* [n: (ID.)] He says "us" in the plural, because here Christ suffers together with his members; above, speaking in the singular, "he will bring me." Seek the expositions of all these things in the ninth psalm.


## PSALM CVIII.

VERS. 1.-- *Unto the end, a psalm.* [n: (AUG.)] A prophecy concerning Christ, etc., *as far as* and the things which are expressed concerning Judas, as that: *And his bishopric let another [1028D] take.*

[n: (CASS.)] The fifth of those which treat more at large of the passion.

VERS. 2.-- *O God, praise.* [n: (CASS.)] He admonishes men to turn aside from the malice of Judas, etc., *as far as* Christ therefore, according as he is man, praying to the Father says: *For the mouth of the sinner.* [n: (ID.)] A comely diversity, because the wicked are not silent in their reviling.

VERS. 3.-- *Without cause.* [n: (AUG.)] As equity pleases the godly of itself, so does iniquity the ungodly.

VERS. 4.-- *In return for this, that me.* [n: (ID.)] They do more hurt who detract from Christ in his members, because they slay the souls of them that would believe, than they who slew his flesh, soon about to rise again.

[1029A]VERS. 5.-- *And they set [sic: *posueruent*] against.* [n: (AUG.)] There are six differences, etc., *as far as* which is good for evil, which is, God the judge does. [n: (CASS.)] In three ways is every sin contracted, etc., *as far as* which Christ did when he prayed for the Jews.

VERS. 6.-- *Set thou over.* [n: (AUG.)] The second part, where he begins to prophesy what they shall receive for their impiety. Above, of many, etc., *as far as* whose person Judas bore. *Set thou a sinner over him.* As of Judas, so of them.

VERS. 7.-- *When he is judged, may he go out condemned.* Because, abiding in wickedness, they treasure up wrath to themselves. *Prayer unto sin:* because it is not made through the Mediator.

VERS. 8.-- *Few days.* According to the kingdom, which [1029B] did not stand long after. *The bishopric,* that is, Christ (who was sent to them), the people of the Gentiles received.

VERS. 9.-- *Children fatherless:* the kingdom, as a father, being lost; or God the Father. *The wife,* that is, the people. *Widows,* the kingdom being lost.

VERS. 10.-- *Let it be transferred,* as was done in the captivity. *Let them beg,* of the kings of the Gentiles.

VERS. 11.-- *All his substance,* that is, his life, punishing each single thing of sin; their debts are not forgiven, because in Christ alone are they forgiven. *And strangers,* that is, the devil and his angels. *Let them plunder his labours,* Because they lay up treasure in heaven [cj: *thesaurizant*; read *non thesaurizant*, "lay not up"].

VERS. 12.-- *And let there not be for him,* etc. That is, there is no helper for him to whom Christ is wanting. *Nor let there be any to have mercy.* I say not for the having of temporal things, but of eternal.

[1029C]VERS. 13.-- *Let his children be given over to destruction* everlasting. Therefore, because they remained in *one generation,* that is, because they were not regenerated. *And let the name be blotted out.* That is, of the earthly Jerusalem, which is in bondage with her children, which slew the prophets.

VERS. 15.-- *And let it perish from the land of the living,* that is, the Church. *Their memory,* because, though they were natural branches, so they became.

VERS. 16.-- *Because he remembered not.* By doing penance.

*To shew mercy.* Upon the members of Christ.

VERS. 18.-- *Cursing, and it shall come to him: and he would not have blessing.* [n: (AUG.)] Sins delight for a season, but they are called a cursing, because they prepare torments.

VERS. 19.-- *Wherewith he is covered.* He seems to say here, [1029D] etc., *as far as* let him be always ready, whence here he says *he is girded always.*

VERS. 20.-- *With the Lord.* [n: (ID.)] They detract from Christ especially who say that he is not of the Father, or that he is less than the Father. *Evils against the soul.* Saying that he could not rise again when he would.

VERS. 21.-- *And thou, O Lord.* [n: (CASS.)] The third part, where he prays, etc., *as far as* help me, according to the man. *For thy name's sake.* For by no works, etc., *as far as because thy mercy is sweet.*

VERS. 22.-- *Because needy and poor.* [n: (ID.)] The neediness and poverty of Christ is the infirmity out of which he was crucified.

[1030A]VERS. 23.-- *I am taken away as the shadow when it declineth.* [n: (CASS.)] As night comes of the declining shadow, so death comes of mortal flesh.

VERS. 24.-- *It hath been changed.* [n: (AUG.)] The flesh of Christ therefore has been changed, etc., *as far as because Jesus was not yet glorified. Because of the oil,* that is, spiritual grace, whence Christ is named.

VERS. 25.-- *They moved their heads.* [n: (CASS.)] Madmen threaten by the shaking of the head. [n: (AUG.)] They moved their heads, because they saw him hanging on the cross, but not yet when the flesh was changed.

VERS. 26.-- *Help me, O Lord.* [n: (CASS.)] He asks as man, he grants as God.

VERS. 27.-- *This is thy hand.* [n: (AUG.)] Christ is this hand, and he is the Word with God, and he made it, when the Word was made flesh. For he was without [1030B] time according to the Godhead, and it was made in time according to the flesh.

VERS. 28.-- *They shall curse, and thou wilt bless.* One is the cursing of the wicked; God, when he blesses, does what he says.

VERS. 29.-- *As with a diploide,* etc. Others interpret, for diploide, a double cloak, that is, let them be confounded within and without, that is, before God and before men.

VERS. 30.-- *Exceedingly in my mouth.* *Exceedingly* is wont to be put for more than is due, but also for greatly. *In the midst.* [n: (AUG.)] That is, in common, or in the heart.

VERS. 31.-- *Because he hath stood at the right hand of the poor.* The devil at the right hand of Judas, etc., *as far as* but that he might save the soul [1030C] from them that persecute it.

## PSALM CIX.

VERS. 1.-- *A psalm of David.* [n: (CASS.)] This psalm is the seventh, etc., *as far as* whatsoever [sic: *quiquid*] is contained in either Testament. [n: (CASS.)] The prophet exhorts men to be made subject to Christ. First the prophet relates the words of the Father concerning the Son, etc., *as far as* this belongs to the humanity.

*The Lord said.* [n: (CASS.)] After our manner, that man may be instructed. *The Lord to the Lord.* Distinguish the persons, and receive one nature and one power. *Sit thou.* After labour, rest, and reign together, according as thou art man. *At my right hand.* Among my better and hidden goods, until thou reign manifest. And this is, *until I make thy enemies,* etc. [n: (AUG.)] *Sit thou,* not only on high, that thou mayest have dominion over the exalted, but also in secret, etc., [1030D] *as far as* if he sees not what he believes. *A footstool for thy feet:* from a similitude, wherein also full subjection is marked. By the feet, eternal stability: this daily in them that are converted.

VERS. 2.-- *The rod of power.* Now the kingdom of Christ begins out of Sion, that is, from the Jews, whence he subjoins, *The rod of power,* etc. *Out of Sion.* The prophet speaks not of that kingdom of Christ, etc., *as far as and remission of sins throughout all nations, beginning at Jerusalem.*

VERS. 3.-- *With thee the beginning.* [n: (CASS.)] Secondly the Father declares the nature of the Godhead, etc., *as far as* when in the resurrection they shall be resplendent, as the angels of God. [n: (AUG.)] [1031A] Already he has dominion, already there is power; but *in the day* of his special power, etc., *as far as* in what manner the Father is with him the beginning. This then, but now. *From the womb,* that is, *as* from my secret and hidden substance, etc., *as far as* one who may offer himself, whence there follows, *He hath sworn,* etc. [n: (CASS.)] Or *With thee the beginning:* this the Father says; as it were, this I grant to thee more than to another, because thou art the Son by nature. [n: (AUG.)] Or it can be taken as of the person of the prophet speaking, etc., *as far as* with the Holy Ghost, who makes the saints resplendent.

VERS. 4.-- *The Lord hath sworn.* [n: (CASS.)] Thirdly the prophet treats of the humanity of Christ, etc., *as far as* hence it is plain that the victims pass away according to the order of Aaron. *It shall not repent him: thou art a priest, for ever.* [n: (AUG.)] Upon God no repentance falls: for he knows [1031B] what he does, nor does he change his counsel; but by the word "repentance" is signified the change of things falling out otherwise than one hoped. For in what manner, when it repents thee to have done anything, thou grievest for the deed which thou hast done.

VERS. 5.-- *Kings* [n: (AUG.)] Who wish, or wished, to blot out the name of Christ.

VERS. 6.-- *He shall crush,* that is, he has rendered them weak, etc., *as far as* and now *he shall judge* among the Gentiles, that he may fill *ruins,* not the upright. *He shall judge among nations.* [n: (CASS.)] As it were, he breaks kings for their pride: he judges peoples for their common sins: both, that they may be humbled unto conversion. Or,

VERS. 7.-- *Of the torrent.* [n: (CASS.)] Of the passing and troubled passion. In the way, whereby men go to another dwelling.

[1031C] *He shall lift up the head.* Whence: *For this cause he exalted him, and God gave him a name which is above every name, that in the name of Jesus every knee should bow, of them that are in heaven, on earth, and under the earth, and that every tongue should confess that the Lord Jesus Christ is in the glory of God the Father.*


## PSALM CX.

*Alleluia.* [n: (CASS.)] The first psalm through all the letters of the alphabet, etc., *as far as* thirdly he affirms the same men redeemed, and the New Testament abiding, there, *Redemption.*

VERS. 1.-- *I will give praise.* [n: (AUG.)] Here there is no confession of sins, but of praise: the one mourns, this rejoices: the one shows the wound to the physician, this gives thanks for health. *In the council.* Not rashly, but out of reason, namely [1031D] for things eternal, as all the just do. [n: (CASS.)] *In the council of the just.* Here never among all the just, where the wicked are mingled with them, but they are seen already to sing in the fellowship to come, caught up by hope to the things above. *In the council,* which is, when they judge with the Lord. *In the congregation,* when they are gathered together out of the whole.

VERS. 2.-- *Great works.* This is the matter and the cause of the confession. Great are the works of the Lord, in that he makes a godly man out of an ungodly. *Sought out.* [n: (AUG., CASS.)] To which nothing is like, while he suffers the devil to rage, etc., *as far as* not: *for his justice remaineth for ever and ever. Unto all the wills.* [n: (AUG.)] Nothing surpasses the will of God, who, even if he wills that thou sin not, yet does not prevent it. If he wills to spare the penitent, he wills also to punish the impenitent; [1032A] so concerning man God always fulfils his own will, because man does nothing out of which God does not work what he wills.

VERS. 3.-- *Confession and magnificence is his work.* [n: (CASS.)] As it were: I will give praise, because great are the works of the Lord. Any works soever are also great, if in them there be seen the intention and the will, which is the *confession* of sins, and the *magnificence* of good works: and *justice,* that is, the reward which is justly given.

VERS. 4.-- *He hath made a remembrance.* [n: (ID.)] Secondly he says that the faithful are filled with a plentiful gift, promising the coming of the Lord, that the inheritance may be eagerly sought. [n: (AUG.)] *He hath made a remembrance of his wonderful works.* Humbling this man and exalting that, etc., *as far as* what shall he receive, he is glorified [cj: *glorificatur*; read *glorificatus*, "glorified"], in the things to come?

[1032B]VERS. 5.-- *He hath given food.* [n: (CASS.)] He makes confession and magnificence, etc., *as far as* that all things may be fulfilled. *He will be mindful,* etc. [n: (AUG.)] Nor did he give the whole who gave a pledge, etc., *as far as* they enter through the piercing straits of the passion.

VERS. 6.-- *The power of the works.* [n: (CASS.)] The power of the works is that the blind see, etc. Which things he made known to the Christian people. *That he may give them the inheritance,* this was the intent of the miracles, that they might believe.

VERS. 7.-- *That he may give them the inheritance of the Gentiles.* [n: (ID.)] As it were: although he gives them the inheritance, yet they shall suffer many things. *The works of his hands.* [n: (ID.)] That is, of his power. *Truth,* while he restores to the converted what was promised: *Judgment,* which he threatens to the ungodly. [n: (AUG.)] *Truth,* which the holy martyrs hold, and for which they are also judged [1032C] here; *and judgment,* whereby in time to come they shall judge those by whom they are here judged. [n: (ID.)] *The works of his hands are truth and judgment.* Let *truth* be held by those who are judged, etc., *as far as* this is the inheritance of the New Testament.

VERS. 8.-- *All the commandments are faithful.* [n: (CASS.)] Because they keep those who keep them, they are not lies. *Confirmed.* Not sent forth in vain, as human words are: *Made,* not only spoken, *in truth,* because all are fulfilled.

*In equity.* [n: (AUG.)] Because all things are done with the balance of justice.

VERS. 9, 10.-- *He hath sent redemption.* [n: (CASS.)] Thirdly he affirms the redeemed, and the New Testament consecrated by eternal grace; or. *His name: the beginning of wisdom.* [n: (ID.)] That is, the Son, who in his humanity is *holy,* [1032D] in the power of the Godhead *terrible.* Let the advocate therefore be loved, let the judge be feared, lest thou be negligent, lest thou despair.

The fear of the Lord. [n: (ID.)] The dread of judgment is the gate of conversion, if the discernment of love be added to it. *His praise continueth.* The intent of the psalm, whence Alleluia is prefixed, began with praise and ends in praise: the faithful people, delivered from the devil and giving thanks, has expressed what is to be done here also by the devout, and to be accomplished in heaven: here because he delivers, there because he crowns.

## PSALM CXI.

*Alleluia of the return.* [n: (AUG.)] Aggeus and Zacharias were long after David, etc., *as far as* it may be made firm eternally. [n: (CASS.)] The perfection of this building, etc., *as far [1033A] as* as though men delivered [sic: *liberavi*] from captivity and glad because of the good things which they have.

VERS. 1.-- *Blessed is the man.* [n: (CASS.)] Here the Psalmist shows, after the deliverance, etc., *as far as* thirdly he affirms that adverse things befall the ungodly. *That feareth the Lord.* The fear of the world makes men wretched, but the fear of God makes a man desire those things whereby he is blessed. *In the commandments.* This the fear of God does, that he should will. And he says not "he shall do," etc., *as far as* and *the earthly habitation presseth down the mind that museth upon many things.*

VERS. 2.-- *Mighty.* [n: (AUG., CASS.)] Behold the rewards of that blessed man. *His seed.* That is, the reward of a good work; *Shall be mighty upon earth,* not upon this earth, where he is a laughing-stock, but upon the heavenly. Or even here in this present time, wherein it is less seen. *Seed,* the works of mercy, whence comes the everlasting harvest. *Mighty,* because with a very small thing, even as with a great one, the kingdom of heaven [1033B] is bought. *The generation.* Not of the flesh, but of imitation. Or the works of the upright. *The upright* are they who resist not the Father when he corrects, and who believe him when he promises, and who believe not that their works have perished when no requital is made them in time. For there are those who do those good things while hoping here for a reward from God, or wishing to please men, which is not the blessed man: and yet,

VERS. 3.-- *Glory and wealth in his house.* That is, in the secret of the heart, where he dwells with the hope of eternal life, where holy riches and eternal glory are laid up. *Justice.* This is his glory, these are his riches, which nevertheless he has from God. *It remaineth for ever.* As it were, he is not blessed for a time.

VERS. 4.-- *There hath risen up.* [n: (CASS.)] Secondly he treats of the coming of the Lord, etc., *as far as* that he may fortify it beforehand with firm allegations [1033C] and assertions in the judgment. Or,

VERS. 5.-- *He sheweth mercy.* [n: (ID.)] He who gives to the needy; *He lendeth,* he who grants a loan, to receive afterwards as much again.

VERS. 7, 8.-- *His heart is ready to hope in the Lord, his heart is strengthened, it remaineth,* etc. [n: (AUG.)] It is plain therefore that this man seeks not his own things, but the things which are Christ's; he bears labours patiently, he awaits the promises with confidence. [n: (CASS.)] Or, if any man threatens evils, he fears not, but is ready.

VERS. 9.-- *He hath distributed.* He hopes not to fear, because he has distributed. *He hath given to the poor.* [n: (AUG.)] Behold, this man was buying what he did not see, but that other was keeping a treasure for him in heaven, who vouchsafes to hunger and to thirst in the poor upon earth. *His justice remaineth for ever and ever.* [n: (CASS.)] [1033D] He gave what was his own, and lived justly and godly; or, he who gives what is his own works justice. *His horn shall be exalted in glory.* Because he is humble here, there he shall have power.

VERS. 10.-- *The sinner shall see.* [n: (ID.)] Thirdly he affirms that contrary things befall the ungodly. By the good things of the blessed he exhorts, by the evils of the enemies he terrifies, and this psalm is an instructor of the faithful.

*He shall be angry, he shall gnash with his teeth and pine away, the desire of the wicked shall perish,* saying: *What hath pride profited us, and the boasting of riches? [n: (Sap. V)]*


## PSALM CXII.

*Alleluia* [n: (CASS.)] Because he is worthily to be praised who by the merit of humility takes up the Gentiles, he expounds the title of the psalm. [1034A] For Alleluia, as we have said, is interpreted "praise ye the Lord," in which manner the psalm also begins.

VERS. 1.-- *Praise ye.* [n: (ID.)] First the Prophet admonishes the devout to praise continually, and everywhere to preach. Secondly he himself does what he admonishes others to do, that is, he praises God there: *Who is as the Lord [n: (I Cor. XIV)]*? Admonishing therefore unto praise, he shows and says who and whom, and how long, and where, and why they should praise. *Children.* Not in sense, but in malice, and chiefly in pride, that is, the humble; whence, *Be not children in sense, but in malice be children, that in mind ye may be perfect.* [n: (AUG., CASS.)] Praise the Lord, ye children, the simple and the pure, who ought to praise.

VERS. 2.-- *Let the name be.* [n: (CASS.)] Behold, he has said who and whom they ought to praise; then he subjoins how long we praise [cj: *laudamus*; read *laudandus*, "is to be praised"], namely always. *Let the name of the Lord be:* [1034B] lest ye think that I command praise for an hour, etc., *as far as* by the Gentiles the carnal, by the heavens the spiritual.

VERS. 3.-- *From the rising of the sun.* [n: (AUG.)] Here he says that he ought to be praised everywhere.

VERS. 4.-- *Above the heavens.* [n: (ID.)] The Apostles, through whom he reigns among the Gentiles, and is; therefore I invite to praises, because he is to reign above all.

VERS. 5.-- *Who is as.* [n: (CASS.)] Secondly he himself does what he admonishes others to do, that by the deed also he may teach others. *Who?* None that can of himself. *In the high places,* etc. In the angels and the saints: and although he is high, he despises not the lowly.

VERS. 6.-- *The lowly things.* These are they who by his gift are made high, and in them he sits. *He beholdeth,* the lowly one by one. *In the earth,* the saints, whom he so beholds as, on the contrary, he hates the proud.

[1034C]VERS. 7.-- *Raising up from the earth:* from the labour of the body. *Out of the dunghill,* he is lifted up over whom the vices of the flesh have no dominion.

VERS. 9.-- *Who maketh to dwell.* [n: (CASS., AUG.)] He expounds where he raises up and lifts up, etc., *as far as* out of the mammon of iniquity by works of mercy. *The barren woman.* [n: (AUG.)] Namely Sara, that is, the Gentile Church, which was barren before, whereby she is signified by Sara. Her, I say, he makes to dwell in the house, that is, in the Church of the Jews, among the Jews themselves, that in the corner-stone the two walls may be joined together, and there may be made one fold. *Of children.* [n: (ID.)] In whose person these things are said, etc., *as far as* since I know myself to be without children, and a widow.

## PSALM CXIII.

[1034D] *Alleluia.* [n: (CASS.)] Here again the Alleluia is set down from another occasion, etc., *as far as* as becomes clear in the exposition of the psalm.

VERS. 1.-- *At the going out.* [n: (AUG.)] Here things past are not related, but things to come are foretold, because in those deeds things to come were signified: and therefore not all the things which were done there are spoken of here, but certain of them otherwise, lest he be thought to be recounting things past. [n: (CASS.)] First the Prophet tells what wonders God gave to the Hebrews in figure, and to Christians in spirit. Secondly he asks why these things were done, there, *What ailed thee, O sea?* Thirdly he reproves the empty idols of the Gentiles, and commends the religion of God, there, *the idols of the Gentiles.*

*At the going out.* As it were, when Israel, that is, the house of Jacob, went out of Egypt, that is, from a barbarous people.

[1035A] *Barbarous.* [n: (AUG.)] That tongue is barbarous which praises not God.

VERS. 2.-- *It was made.* [n: (CASS.)] As that people, when it went out of Egypt, etc., *as far as* they are able to resist vices, and this.

VERS. 3.-- *The sea saw,* that is, the sinners of the world. *Jordan,* they who by divers desires snatch men into the great sea, that is, into the embittering malice of this world; but these things cease at the coming of the Lord, being as it were turned backward.

*The sea saw.* [n: (AUG.)] This is here said otherwise than it was done. There it is read to have stood on that side from which the waters were flowing down, nor is anything said there of the mountains skipping; but both the things done there and the things said here are figures.

[1035B]VERS. 4.-- *Rams.* [n: (ID.)] Who with the horns of both, etc., *as far as* every knowledge lifting itself up against Christ. *The hills.* [n: (CASS.)] From tilling, the middling sort: for these things are not according to the letter; whence also by the sea and by Jordan, that is, by the ancient deeds, doubt not that those things are signified which today are done spiritually.

VERS. 5.-- *What ailed thee.* [n: (ID.)] Secondly he asks why these things were, that he may stir up wonder. And he resolves it: *At the face of the Lord.*

VERS. 7.-- *At the face.* [n: (ID.)] One answer for two, because one author both made the ages to be astonished, and made his own, that is, the mountains and the hills, to rejoice: for at the appearing of Christ men were moved from their own superstition to the worship of God.

*At the face of the God of Jacob.* And as though one should ask, who is the Lord? [1035C] The God of Jacob, not a new one; who makes them wrestle.

VERS. 8.-- *Who turned the rock.* [n: (ID.)] The hard hearts of the Jews, unto the water of baptism.

*The crag.* The Gentiles, that they also may pour forth the watering fountains of preaching.

[n: (AUG.)] That is, himself, hard before, while he was not known, etc., *as far as* he might overflow upon all that believe, saying: *If any man thirst, let him come to me and drink.*

VERS. 1.-- *Not to us.* [n: (CASS.)] When he had said that God gives good things, etc., *as far as* truth, while he renders the rewards, or punishes the ungodly. Therefore, Give.

VERS. 2.-- *Lest at any time:* this the ungodly often lay to the charge of the good when they are afflicted.

*Mercy.* [n: (AUG.)], that is, by restoration.

*Truth,* that is, by the promise fulfilled.

[1035D] *Where is God.* [n: (ID.)] This the Gentiles are wont to say, who have visible gods: because we worship an invisible God.

VERS. 3.-- *In heaven.* [n: (ID.)], that is, in Christ the man, according to that: *God was in Christ, reconciling the world to himself.* Or, *in heaven,* [n: (CASS.)] that is, mighty above all creatures.

*He hath done all things whatsoever he would.* Whereby he appears almighty.

VERS. 4.-- *The idols of the Gentiles.* [n: (ID.)] Thirdly he proves the idols empty, and shows how profitable the religion of God is. And here is a perfect demonstration in dispraise and in praise. [n: (AUG.)] The power of the true God having been shown from his works, he passes to the visible gods of the Gentiles. *Silver.* [n: (ID.)] But because God made this, he says, *The works [1036A] of the hands of men:* they venerate that which they made thereof, for God created the matter; but the folly of men added the form.

VERS. 5.-- *They have mouths.* [n: (ID.)] They were formed by hand, etc., *as far as* and they served the creature rather than the Creator.

VERS. 7.-- *They shall not cry.* [n: (ID.)] After speaking (which is proper to man) he adds this which is common, etc., *as far as* and an idol wanting life and sense.

VERS. 8.-- *Like unto them.* [n: (ID.)] See the worship that should shame them, when he says *like,* which indeed they are; or let them understand themselves to be like unto them.

VERS. 9.-- *The house of Israel.* [n: (CASS.)] Here he shows the profit of the Christian religion.

VERS. 10.-- *The house of Aaron.* [n: (AUG.)] But lest that should seem to be already a thing possessed by the greater which to the lesser is [1036B] a hope, he adds, *The house of Aaron.*

VERS. 12.-- *Mindful.* [n: (CASS.)] That he might convert them, and he blessed the converted through the same.

VERS. 14.-- *Upon you.* [n: (AUG.)] Because the number of teachers increased.

*Upon the children.* Because the number of followers also increased. Therefore ye both, fathers and sons, are blessed of the Lord, who made you *heaven* in the greater, *and earth* in the lesser; or who made every creature.

VERS. 15.-- *Blessed be ye of the Lord.* [n: (CASS.)] He who above wished to be blessed, now with confidence says, *Blessed,* which belongs to God, that ye may serve him. Truly *of the Lord,* because even *the heaven of heaven,* that is, Christ, is unto the honour of the Lord; or,

VERS. 16.-- *The heaven of heaven,* that is, the empyrean, he gave [1036C] to the Lord Christ, according as he is man. *But the earth.* This lower earth he gave. *To the children of men.* That in it they may purify themselves; or the people to the preachers; whence: As the Father hath sent me, and I will send you [n: (Joan. VI)].

*The heaven of heaven.* [n: (AUG.)] He speaks of those whose minds, etc., *as far as* whom the weak cannot yet see without a support.

VERS. 17.-- *Nor all they that go down.* [n: (CASS.)] Or, not all they that go down; because the saints also went down, but not into the lower place.


## PSALM CXIV.

*Alleluia.* This title is often repeated, because the praise of God is both the dignity of works and the price of labours, that is, it is both the merit and the reward. [n: (AUG.)] The Prophet, delivered from the pit of sins and set upon the rock, [1036D] first gives thanks because he was heard, and says that he has called upon him against all perils.

This psalm is the consolation of the faithful against the sorrows of the world.

VERS. 1.-- *I have loved.* [n: (ID.)] This the sheep says which had strayed, the son returned, the pilgrim of this world.

[n: (CASS.)] *I have loved.* Not for the temporal goods given to me, as the unbelievers do, but *because,* etc. God is loved even by unbelievers because he gives good things; whence: *I will praise thee when thou shalt do well to him [n: (Psal. XLVIII)]*. [var: Vulg. Ps 48:19 *confitebitur tibi*, "he will praise thee," in the third person, which is what the gloss's argument requires] He is loved perfectly, him [cj: *eum*; read *cum*, "when"] the mind is not moved by adverse things, but is kindled by hope: which manner he afterwards expounds, *I have loved.* Not because he made me a king, or gave me prosperity, but because he hears my prayer in straits.

VERS. 2.-- *And in my days.* [n: (AUG.)] Namely of my misery, which days I made for myself by sinning, which are full of labour and sorrow, that is, of the old rottenness.

*I will call upon.* Which is not without faith; whence the Apostle: *How shall they call on him in whom they have not believed? [n: (Rom. X.)]* When therefore he says, *they shall call upon,* note that the lover is not idle. In which, *They have compassed me,* etc. Or, in the days of salvation, that is, in the time of fulness; whence: *Behold, now is the acceptable time,* etc. [n: (II Cor. VI)].

VERS. 3.-- *Sorrows.* [n: (CASS.)] Sins, whence come the sorrow and the death of the soul; or eternal sorrows, and this shall be in hell. *The perils of hell.* [n: (AUG.)] For, steeped in carnal pleasure, I did not perceive the evils that compassed me about.

VERS. 4.-- *Trouble and sorrow.* [n: (ID.)] As it were: those things first compassed me about, but afterwards I found trouble [1037B] and sorrow, that is, outward affliction, to be profitable.

VERS. 5.-- *O Lord, deliver.* [n: (CASS.)] Secondly he cries again, that being delivered he may come to rest. *The Lord is merciful.* [n: (AUG.)] Because he first inclined his ear to me and called me.

*Just.* Because he scourges again. *He hath mercy.* Because he receives unto blessedness. Nor ought that to be so bitter which he scourges, as that is sweet which he receives, because the Lord keeps the little ones, whom, when they are grown, he seeks for heirs.

VERS. 7.-- *Turn thee.* [n: (ID.)] Not by thy merits or by thy strength, but because the Lord hath done well to thee.

VERS. 8.-- *From death, the eyes.* [n: (ID.)] Death is unbelief; whence: *Let the dead bury their dead;* and again: *The dead shall not praise thee, O Lord [n: (Psal. CIII)]*.

[1037C]VERS. 9.-- *I will please.* [n: (ID.)] He said, etc., *as far as* and this in the land of the living, who live eternally.

## PSALM CXV.

*Alleluia.* This psalm is fitly entitled with the voice of praise, because in this psalm are the words of the martyrs, who were strong in confession and obtained the rewards of God.

VERS. 10.-- *I have believed,* etc. [n: (CASS.)] First the benefits of God are recalled; and while they doubt what worthy thing they might render for them, the chalice of the passion presents itself, which is given by God. Secondly they call themselves servants, and sons of the handmaid, that is, of the Church, there: *O Lord, for I,* etc. It is a true and fitting order, first to believe, afterwards to speak: lest one not yet believing should dare to speak, but that confession should follow faith. But lest confession be ascribed to the strength of man, he says [1037D] that this is proper to man, that he is a liar: which he would not easily know, unless he had been carried by an excess of mind to the things above. For it is a great thing if a man marks that he is unwise. And since man has nothing good of his own, what is there to be rendered again? *I have believed.* [n: (AUG.)] He says not: *I have believed and I have spoken,* because he did not only believe the other things which faith requires, but together with them he believed also both what reward he ought to hope for by speaking, and what punishment he ought to fear by keeping silence; whence the love of speaking is put in from the reward, and the necessity is laid on from the punishment. *For which cause.* For he believes not perfectly who speaks not what he believes; that other servant is slothful, or fearful, but this one is faithful: what he received, he lays out and gains withal, and therefore enter thou [cj: *intra*; read *intrat*, "he enters"] into the joy of his Lord, who says: *Well done, [1038A] good and faithful servant, enter into the joy of thy Lord [n: (Matth. XXV)]*.

VERS. 11.-- *In my excess.* Or, in my ecstasy. [n: (AUG.)] And ecstasy is here spoken of, etc., *as far as* that *every man is a liar.*

VERS. 12.-- *What shall I render to the Lord.* [n: (ID.)]? This man, affrighted, looks back upon his own infirmity, and sees that he must not presume of himself. For of himself man is a liar, as Peter also, when he presumed, was found a liar: but it is the power of God, if he does not believe. Seeing this wrought in himself by the grace of God, he subjoins, *which he hath rendered to me.* He says not, for all the things which he hath bestowed. What goods of man, therefore, went before, that of all the gifts of God it might be called not a bestowing, but a rendering again? What had gone before but sins? God therefore renders to men [1038B] good things for evil, having suffered for them, whereas men render evil things for good.

VERS. 13.-- *The chalice of salvation.* [n: (ID.)] The passion, from a likeness, etc., *as far as* which is said afterwards.

[n: (CASS.)] *The chalice,* because it is drunk under measure. Whence, *God is faithful, who suffereth you not to be tempted above that which ye are able [n: (I Cor. X)]*. *Of salvation,* because it is propitious unto eternal salvation.

VERS. 15.-- *Precious in the sight of the Lord is the death of his saints.* [n: (AUG.)] He who suffered first grants that his own sufferings be imitated; and therefore: *Precious in the sight of the Lord is the death of his saints.* He bought it with the blood which he shed for them, that they might not doubt to die for him. Bought therefore at this price, he confesses his own condition: *O Lord, for I,* etc.

[1038C]VERS. 16.-- *O Lord, for,* etc. [n: (CASS.)] Secondly they call themselves servants, and sons of the Church, because there is no place of true sacrifice outside the Catholic Church, lest any of the heretics should think his martyrdom to be pleasing to God. *O Lord.* He wonders that he has received such things as the Lord received for the redemption of the world. And to increase the wonder he repeats: *I am thy servant. The son of the handmaid.* [n: (AUG.)] Of the heavenly Jerusalem, free from sins, the handmaid of justice. But they who are outside the Church are not sons of the handmaid, even though they call themselves servants (because they have the name of Christ) and martyrs, as the heretics do. *The son of the handmaid,* Not a schismatic. Or, as a handmaid brings forth not for herself, but for the Lord; so I too in my death shall bring forth some for thee, as also in my life.

VERS. 17.-- *Thou hast broken my bonds.* [n: (CASS.)] By the power [1038D] of martyrdom the bonds of sins are broken, because all things are washed away by the blood. This verse is believed by some to be of such power that a man's sins are forgiven, if at the end of his life it be said with a threefold confession.

[n: (ID.)] Who *precious. O Lord, to thee will I sacrifice;* speaking to God, he affirms what he had said above.

*I will sacrifice.* [n: (AUG., CASS.)] And because thou hast broken them, to thee will I sacrifice, as one that is thankful, *The sacrifice of praise.* So that:

VERS. 18, 19.-- *I will pay [sic: *reddum*] my vows.* Not everywhere, but *in the courts,* that is, in the Catholic Church, not in that of the heretics. *Of the house of the Lord.* He shows which it is.

*In the sight of all the people.* [n: (CASS.)] He ought to be praised publicly who was willing to suffer for all, that the more may be edified. *I will pay my vows.* Myself, who am the image [1039A] of God, which must be rendered to God, etc., *as far as* and here he names Jerusalem more openly. [n: (AUG.)] But lest [sic: *ue*] any think that by Jerusalem the Jews alone are signified, who glory in this name, let him hear the following psalm [cj: *Psalmus*; read *Psalmum*, "the following psalm"]: *Praise the Lord, all ye nations.* These are the courts of the Lord, for that is the people.

## PSALM CXVI.

*Alleluia.* [n: (CASS.)] A short psalm, the first of all that is an atom, that is, without division: for by reason of its brevity not to be received [cj: *recipi*; read *recipit*, "it admits"] division, but it surpasses all others by the compendiousness of its utterance, and it is as it were the point of the psalms, out of which the divers kinds are formed. A wondrous variation against weariness, to speak wholesome things, now in few words, now at large.

VERS. 1.-- *Praise the Lord, all ye nations.* [n: (CASS.)] That none out of the whole world be passed over. [n: (AUG.)] And [1039B] these are those courts of the house of the Lord, and that whole people, whereof he treated in the preceding psalm. Not the Jews alone (who attempt to arrogate it to themselves) do I say, praise ye him. And praise ye together. [n: (CASS.)] that the praise of all may be brought into one, which belongs to the Catholic Church.

VERS. 2.-- *Confirmed.* [n: (CASS.)] Because what he promised through the prophets he fulfilled by the coming of Christ; and he made his mercy firm, that it may never be moved, because he who gave it keeps it. *It remaineth for ever.* [n: (AUG.)] Whether in those things which he promised to the godly, or in those which he threatens to the ungodly.



## PSALM CXVII.

*Alleluia.* [n: (CASS.)] The fifth psalm concerning the first coming, adorned with many figures, has verses of one ending, some of one beginning, some intercalary, and ornaments of this kind. The faithful people, loosed from sin [1039C], etc., [sic: *ete.*] *as far as Blessed is he that.*

VERS. 2.-- *Let him now say.* [n: (ID.)] As I admonish you, so do you admonish one another; or, *let him say,* that is, let him confess.

*Because for ever.* Behold four verses of one ending.

VERS. 5.-- *Out of tribulation.* [n: (ID.)] He sets an example from himself, that he may invite others to mercy.

VERS. 6.-- *The Lord is my helper.* [n: (ID.)] The same beginning in two.

VERS. 7.-- *I shall look down.* Elsewhere, *I shall see.* [n: (ID.)] Here, that I may become safer and more wary. *My enemies.* Those to be damned.

VERS. 8.-- *It is good.* [n: (ID.)] Secondly, he says that trust is to be placed in God alone, through whom he escapes the enmities of the Gentiles, and [d: et] comes to the true life.

VERS. 9.-- *In princes.* [n: (AUG.)] In the angels, etc., [1039D] *as far as* he who makes them good according to their measure.

VERS. 10.-- *All nations.* [n: (CASS.)] Behold whence he delivers from hidden or from open tribulation: either by not suffering one to fall, or by revealing from the fall [cj: *revelando*; read *relevando*, "by raising up"].

VERS. 11.-- *They that compassed me about,* etc. [n: (AUG.)] As it were rendering the reason whence he is able to overcome so many things, he tells what he first suffered in the head.

VERS. 12.-- *They surrounded me,* etc. [n: (ID.)] What was done by men who knew not, the Holy Spirit foretells with mystical subtlety. Judah not knowing, they made God, by his passion, the concern of all, and sweeter to us: as bees, while they think to lay up for themselves, make honey for others. *And they were enkindled.* Or this is better understood of the Church. For after the passion of the Lord the Jews also were enkindled against the Church of the Gentiles, like fire in [1040A] the thorns of sins [sic: *pecatorum*]. For these, that is, sins, they burned up in the faithful people by the fire of persecution; and wishing to harm it, they profited it. *Because I was avenged.* While, being converted, malice being extinguished in them, they were joined to the Christian people; or, when, being judged in the end, they shall feel the judgment of truth without mercy.

VERS. 13.-- *Being thrust at,* etc. [n: (CASS.)] As when good men are given over to punishments, that they may fall from the faith, and the Lord upheld. [n: (AUG.)] Because he to whom the Lord is strength and praise falls as little as the Lord falls: but those fall who wish to be strength and praise to themselves.

*I was thrust at and overthrown,* etc. After he has shown that he delivers by not suffering one to fall, he shows also that he delivers by raising up from the fall.

VERS. 14.-- *He is become my salvation.* [n: (ID.)] [1040B] He is not made what he was not to himself; but he is salvation to them converted, which he was not to them turned away.

VERS. 17.-- *I shall not die.* [n: (ID.)] The Church says it, since the persecutor thinks that by a slaughter of funerals he can blot out the Christian name, whereas he rather increases it.

VERS. 18.-- *Chastising.* [n: (CASS.)] But why he bore so many things, he shows: *Chastising,* whatever they do is the chastisement of the Lord. Let them claim nothing to themselves; he is not given over to death whose vices are purged. [n: (AUG.)] Often a father amends his sons by worthless servants, when for those he prepares punishment, for these an inheritance. And what the inheritance is may be known from this: *Open ye the gates.* What within? *Having entered I will confess.*

VERS. 19.-- *Open ye.* [n: (CASS.)] In the third place he desires that the gates of Jerusalem be opened to him, where he speaks also of the corner [1040C] stone. Open, O prophets, who bear the keys of the kingdom of heaven. *The gates.* [n: (CASS., HIER.)] They who have entered through these gates of doctrine praise truly; outside the Catholic Church there is no praise of God. [n: (CASS.)] All these gates lead to one, which is Christ, through whom the just enter into heaven; whence: *I am the door, through which if any man enter in, he shall be safe [n: (Joan. X)]* [var: Vulg. *per me si quis introierit, salvabitur*, "by me, he shall be saved"]. And elsewhere: *No man cometh to the Father, but by me [n: (Joan. XIV)]*. [n: (AUG.)] Behold, he has made mention of both worlds: and because this gate has appeared as Christ, *I will confess to thee,* giving thanks with gladness, *because thou hast heard* at the coming of the Son; for the just of former times were asking for the coming. *The gates of justice.* That is, the gates of death, etc., *as far as* that is, through it unto life.

[1040D]VERS. 22.-- *They that build.* [n: (ID.)] The Jews alone, worshipping God, were building, the rest being destroyed through idolatry; but they did not finish, because they despised Christ.

VERS. 23.-- *This is done by the Lord.* Or, *This is done by the Lord for him,* according to another reading. [n: (ID.)] That is, unto the honour of the Father.

VERS. 24.-- *This is the day.* This is the jubilee year, figuratively observed under the law, which is called *remitting,* or *beginning,* because then bondmen were set free, possessions were restored to their owners: and we on this day have been redeemed from the servitude of sin, to whom an eternal inheritance has been restored. [n: (CASS.)] *This is the day.* Although he made all days, this one singularly, on which the Lord came, on which it is fitting to rejoice, because the devil lost his right and man lived again; and because this is so,

[1041A]VERS. 25.-- *O Lord, save,* etc. Behold the desire of a great petition. *Save,* from the ruin of the world. *Give prosperity,* that these sorrows may be turned into eternal joys.

VERS. 26.-- *Blessed,* etc. [n: (CASS.)] Fourthly he urges that the courts of God be frequented, rejoicing in the coming of Christ. [n: (AUG.)] Cursed therefore is he that comes in his own name, etc., *as far as* he who shall be cursed. [n: (CASS.)] *Blessed.* Praise for the coming; and, O ye other Christians, we the elders, as it were priests, *have blessed you,* which profits much, because *we have blessed you out of the house of the Lord.*

*Out of the house.* [n: (ID.)] That is, the Catholic Church, etc., *as far as* because glad is the confession that follows benefits.

VERS. 27.-- *Appoint a day.* [n: (AUG.)] The great, who go beyond, who with the mind touch the Word, God with God, etc., *as far as* that is, that he may attain unto eternal joys.

[1041B] *A solemn day.* In the old law [n: (Deut. X.)] the Pasch, Pentecost, the feast of tabernacles, three chief solemnities, in which it was commanded that no man should appear empty in the sight of the Lord. He is empty who has not Christ in himself, who is the foundation, which no man is able to change. Hence it is that on those three solemnities the faithful people offers a coin by an offering of the hand, signifying the inward offering. For on a coin there is the name and image of the king. Let each one therefore offer a coin spiritually, that he may have in himself the *name* of the king, that is, of Christ, being called a Christian from Christ; and may show forth to God the *image,* that is, the soul, marked with the light of his countenance. *The horn of the altar.* [n: (CASS.)] Or by the *altar* is understood the sacrament of the body and blood, etc., *as far as he shall not have life everlasting.*

[1041C]Or, the *altar* is understood to be the mind of man; whence in the law it was commanded [n: (Exod. XXVII)] that an altar be made four-cornered and hollow: four-cornered, because the human mind ought to be fortified with four virtues, prudence, justice, fortitude, temperance; and the same ought to be hollow to receive the word of God and the fire of love. For swelling drives back what is poured in; whence in Leviticus it is commanded [n: (Levit. I)] that the little bladder of the throat and the feathers be cast away, when a turtle-dove or a pigeon is offered. The bladder signifies the puffing up of pride; the feathers, empty searchings into lofty things; cast these away therefore, that thou mayest be a hollow altar.

VERS. 28.-- *Thou art my God.* And that we may speak more openly, this is the sense of these words: Let not this feast day upon earth, on which the lamb was slain, suffice: [1041D] but let it be appointed in frequent celebrations, until with minds exalted we come to his inward Godhead, where we shall sing praises thus: *Thou art my God,* etc. And so, as he began with praise, so he ends with it.

VERS. 29.-- *Give praise to the Lord.* From the beginning which we forsook, unto the end to which we return, there is nothing more wholesome, which may delight, than the praise of God

## PSALM CXVIII.

*The exposition of this psalm, as being exceedingly long, we omit. See the Enarrationes on the psalms of Ambrose, Cassiodorus, Augustine, and from these the Glossa ordinaria on this place has been expressed word for word.*



## PSALM CXIX.

VERS. 1.-- *A canticle of degrees.* [n: (CASS.)] The beatitude [1042A] which the foregoing psalm sang in a united variety, etc., *as far as* by these degrees therefore one ascends and descends with the unstumbling foot of the mind. [n: (AUG.)] *A canticle of degrees.* The name of degrees is ambiguous among us, etc., *as far as* and the height of his own Godhead. The first degree, to go out from the evils of the world, and to renounce all vanity. [n: (CASS.)] The beginning of advancement is to leave carnal vices and to make supplication to the Lord; whence in the old law, when a Gentile bondman was bought, his nails were pared and his hair was shorn [n: (Deut. XII)]. Because vanity must be put off on every side, that a man may be made over to the service of God. Here, from the renunciation of the world, one comes by certain degrees of merits to that perfect and eternal charity which is set at the summit.

*Unto the Lord when I was in trouble.* [n: (CASS.)] First, beginning [1042B] to ascend, because he finds many besetting him, he asks to be delivered from their tongues: secondly, he complains of the mingling of the wicked, *Woe is me.*

VERS. 2.-- *O Lord, deliver,* etc. When a man begins to ascend, that is, when he purposes to advance, the world being despised, cleaving to God alone, he begins to suffer the tongues of adversaries, that is, of detractors, and (what is graver) of those who, as though counselling him, turn him away from salvation: he who does not suffer these does not yet endeavour to advance. Being about to ascend, therefore, he prays first against them: *O Lord, deliver my soul from wicked lips.*

VERS. 3.-- *What shall be given to thee,* etc. [n: (CASS.)] After the manner of one thinking overmuch he hesitates, and seeks a remedy. *What shall be given to thee, or what shall be added?* He has set down two words, *to be given and to be added;* arrows are given for arms, coals are [1042C] added as it were for consolation.

VERS. 4.-- *Arrows.* [n: (AUG.)] The heart is shot through with the words of God, etc., *as far as* that there may be a clean place for the building of God.

VERS. 5.-- *Woe is me.* [n: (CASS.)] Secondly he complains of his sojourning and of the mingling of the wicked. Having received the arrows and the coals, being made wheat, he is not yet in the barn, but on the threshing-floor where much chaff is trodden; seeing therefore many evils which he had not seen before, he cries: *Woe is me.* To no man do the wicked appear, unless he has been made good; *For when the blade was sprung up, and had brought forth fruit, the cockle appeared. Prolonged.* Though the time be short, to desire it seems exceedingly long. *Cedar.* [n: (ID.)] The son of Ismael, from whom the Saracens are descended, who would more truly be called Agarenes from Agar: but from Sara, the worthier and the free woman, they have claimed a name for themselves, and both of these are read to have dwelt in [1042D] tents. The tents of Ismael are rightly called Cedar: Cedar belongs to Ismael. [n: (AUG.)] Isaac therefore, etc., *as far as* if now the evil cannot be separated from the good, they must be borne with.

## PSALM CXX.

VERS. 1.-- *A canticle of degrees.* [n: (CASS.)] The second degree, to seek the divine defence, against which nothing is shown to be able to prevail. [n: (AUG.)] For he upon whom, as he goes out of the world, dangers press, ought to demand the divine help, etc., *as far as* Such is this man, who lifts his eyes to God. [n: (CASS.)] First he lifts his eyes and prays: secondly, he affirms what he prayed for, showing the power of the help obtained.

*I have lifted up mine eyes.* [n: (ID.)] He who cried out as one bowed down, [1043A] now taking breath, lifts his eyes to the holy intercessors.

VERS. 3.-- *Neither let him slumber.* [n: (AUG.)] If faith sleeps in thee, Christ sleeps in thee.

[n: (CASS.)] This is said after human custom; as when a weary watchman slumbers, he suffers theft.

VERS. 4.--[n: (AUG.)] *Behold he shall not slumber.* Christ slumbers, etc., *as far as* but lest thou still suppose this of some man, he subjoins, *The Lord keepeth.*

*He that keepeth Israel.* [n: (AUG.)] He who cannot see the face, etc., *as far as* and for this man Christ does not sleep.

VERS. 5.-- *The Lord keepeth.* [n: (CASS.)] The second part, where he affirms what he had prayed. And as if one should ask, Who is that keeper? he subjoins, *The Lord keepeth,* etc. [n: (AUG.)] It is not without cause, etc., *as far as* the love of God, namely, and of the neighbour.

[1043B]VERS. 6.-- *By day the sun shall not burn thee, nor the moon by night,* etc. [n: (ID.)] God is the sun, etc., *as far as* or *the moon,* that is, the Church, will not suffer thee to be burned in the night of tribulation. [n: (HIER.)] Or *by day* prosperity is understood, etc., *as far as* so now he keeps us both in prosperity and in adversity.

VERS. 7.-- *May the Lord keep thy coming in.* [n: (CASS.)] He concludes all: to good beginnings may he join good endings. [n: (AUG.)] *May the Lord keep thy coming in.* He keeps those who enter, etc., *as far as* now unto eternity nothing shall hurt.

## PSALM CXXI.

VERS. 1.-- *A canticle of degrees.* [n: (CASS.)] The third degree. A great joy: to be conversant in the Church with a pure mind, and worthily to serve God as a soldier in this pilgrimage: whence we sigh for the heavenly Jerusalem, to [1043C] which we are lifted by the two wings of love.

*I rejoiced at these things,* etc. First he rejoices that he has been admonished to go to Jerusalem, where the saints, secure, shall judge him [cj: *eum*; read *cum*, "with"] with the Lord. Secondly, being about to ascend, he wishes for an abundance of peace: *Pray ye for the things that are for the peace,* etc.

VERS. 4.-- *A testimony to Israel.* [n: (AUG.)] That is, in whom it is known that they are truly Israel. For when the grains are with the chaff, the whole is thought to be chaff; but when they shall be separated, then shall there be a testimony to Israel.

VERS. 5.-- *Seats.* [n: (ID.)] That is, the just, in whom God [cj: *Deum*; read *Deus*, "God sits"] sits, etc., *as far as* let all therefore who are to be questioned beware for themselves.

VERS. 6.-- *Pray ye.* [n: (CASS.)] The second part, where he speaks to the judges, wishing them the abundance of peace which he proclaimed out of love of the brethren. *To them that love.* [1043D] [n: (AUG.)] He who loves in another the good which he himself cannot have, it is reckoned to him as though he had it; whence: If charity has not what to give, let it give a cup of water; and as much is reckoned to him as to Zacchaeus his many gifts [n: (Matth. X)]. The means are unequal, but the charity is not unequal.

VERS. 7.-- *Let peace be made.* [n: (ID.)] Charity is a virtue strong as death, etc., *as far as* he subjoins: *Because of my brethren.*

*In the towers.* [n: (ID.)] That is, in thy high places.
