The judges are few, but many shall pertain to each one of them, whom they shall receive into everlasting tabernacles, and then shall there be *abundance in the towers;* but God is their whole abundance, both of those who receive and of those who are received.

VERS. 9.-- *Because of the house of the Lord God.* Or thou mayest so read it [1044A] that this verse is the exposition of the preceding. As it were, *I spoke peace of thee, for neighbours, brethren,* namely: *I have sought good things for thee, because of the house of the Lord our God.* The sense of these words is not changed. *For thee.* O thou, heavenly Jerusalem, because thou art the house of God, and so in this [d: hoc], that I love thee, I love God.

## PSALM CXXII.

*A canticle of degrees.* [n: (CASS.)] The fourth degree, that amid whatsoever straits one must steadfastly presume upon the Lord, until he take pity and hear. He who above lifted his eyes to the mountains, now lifts them to God.

VERS. 1.-- *To thee have I lifted up.* [n: (ID.)] First the voice of the troubled looking to God, not to other patronages; secondly, he prays for mercy against those who insult him. [n: (AUG.)] *To thee have I lifted up.* Whither should he lift them, save whither he tended in ascending? [1044B] this man does not regard himself, as the proud man does who pleases himself: for he who is displeasing to himself, such a one pleases God; and he to whom God is pleasing is himself displeasing to himself. *I have lifted up.* The ascent therefore is in the affection, etc., *as far as* but he who is a pilgrim and desires his country.

VERS. 2.-- *Behold as the eyes,* etc. [n: (CASS.)] The servant lifts his eyes to the hands of his master, when he is in want or when he is beaten, because both are in the master's power. *As the eyes of the handmaid.* [n: (AUG.)] *The servant* is the people; *the Lord,* Christ; *the handmaid,* the Church; the mistress, the power of God and the wisdom of God. And though we are now sons and friends by grace, yet we are servants by creation, because every creature serves God. [n: (CASS.)] He gives a similitude [sic: *similttudinem*] from the manservant and the handmaid, the master and the mistress, lest the feminine and weaker sex suppose itself excepted.

[1044C]VERS. 3.-- *Have mercy.* [n: (ID.)] The second part, where he prays for mercy against those who insult him. The servant who is beaten, having lifted his eyes to God, breaks forth into the needful cry: *Have mercy.*

*We are filled.* As when it is said: What dost thou believe, madman; dost thou see what thou believest? Has anyone returned from the dead, and told thee what is done there? Behold, what I love, I see and enjoy.



## PSALM CXXIII.

VERS. 1.-- *If it had not been that.* [n: (CASS.)] First the holy martyrs, mindful of how great things they have escaped, ascribe them to mercy alone; secondly, they give thanks that they have been delivered.

VERS. 3.-- *Perhaps they had swallowed us up alive.* They live who know that an idol is nothing. They are dead who think it to be something. Therefore the dead are swallowed up, who [1044D] think that the evil which they do is a thing to be done; but the living are they who indeed know that what they do is evil, but being overcome by persecution pass over into the fellowship of the ungodly, and prefer the sweetness of this life to God, as certain men did who for fear of death sacrificed to idols. [n: (AUG.)] Mark also that Peter, that is, the Church, swallows up many, but not alive. To whom it is said: *Slay and eat. [n: (Act. X.)]* [var: Vulg. *occide*, "kill"] For he eats none, that is, sets none among his members, save one slain, that is, one renouncing the devil. He therefore is slain as to evil, but quickened as to good, who is eaten by Peter. The contrary befalls him whom the devil swallows down.

VERS. 4.-- *Water.* [n: (AUG.)] The sinful people (and it is from the likeness of the Egyptians): which water flows as a torrent [1045A] with violence, but quickly passes; whence he subjoins: *a torrent. --Perhaps* the Latins set down, as best they can, for the Greek ἄρα in the place of doubting, which is more intelligibly said: *thinkest thou, has our soul passed through?* that is, the greatness of the peril scarcely suffers this to be believed.

VERS. 5.-- *Water insupportable.* [n: (AUG.)] Without substance, as are vices, and sins, and the sweetness of this life, etc., *as far as* and might bring back to the father the prodigal son worn out by long need.

VERS. 6.-- *Blessed be the Lord.* [n: (CASS.)] The second part, where the saints give thanks that they have been delivered and that the snare has been broken.

VERS. 7.-- *Our soul.* [n: (AUG.)] Or, as when someone makes a great noise, etc., *as far as* which is as it were a snare. *Of them that hunt.* [n: (ID.)] The hunters are [1045B] the persecutors; the bait in the trap, the sweetness of this life. *The snare is broken.* [n: (CASS.)] The martyrs being slain, there remains no sweetness of life whereby they might be taken again: but they themselves were not broken along with it, nay rather delivered. *Broken.* [n: (ID.)] As if he should say: because it was of iron; not torn apart, like tow: so great was the fortitude of the passion, so great the power of him that delivered.

## PSALM CXXIV.

VERS. 1.-- *A canticle.* [n: (CASS.)] The sixth degree: the confidence of the most faithful Christian, which he has in the Lord, is compared to the most solid mountains, that he may not be jealous of those who are happy in this world. *They that trust.* [n: (AUG.)] This psalm teaches, etc., *as far as* who are these? what manner of Jerusalem is that? he briefly describes it.

VERS. 2.-- *The mountains round about,* that is, the angels, the prophets, [1045C] the apostles are its wall: whence Isaias: *And there shall be in it a wall and a bulwark [n: (Isa. XXVI)]*; but lest thou remain among the mountains, he adds, and the Lord round about. *Let there not then be hope in the mountains,* but in the top of the mountains: whence Isaias: *In the last days there shall be a mountain on the top [n: (Isa. XXI)]*, etc. *They that trust.* [n: (CASS.)] First he affirms, etc., *as far as* because they are both many and are one. *Mount Sion.* [n: (HIER.)] Christ, on whom the Church is founded: that mountain, according to the letter, shall be shaken at the end of the world.

VERS. 3.-- *The rod of sinners.* [n: (AUG.)] For they bear rule sometimes for the discipline of the good, but this shall not be always, as the prophet here plainly says: and therefore the just do not fail, because they know the time to come, in which Christ, appearing in his brightness, shall gather [1045D] before him all nations, and shall divide them as a shepherd divides the kids from the sheep, and shall set the sheep on the right hand, the kids on the left. *The lot.* [n: (CASS.)] That is, the just chosen by lot, or who are the lot and the inheritance of God.

VERS. 4.-- *Do good, O Lord.* [n: (ID.)] The second part, where, the people being confirmed, he prays for what he knows shall come to pass, that prosperity may come to the good, vengeance to the wicked.

VERS. 5.-- *But such as turn aside.* [n: (ID.)] By a like sentence also do they perish who turn aside from the commands of the prince, and they who strive to will evil works; those failing, together with those who compel them to fail. *Peace upon Israel.* [n: (AUG.)] By one name is called the paternal inheritance, which is Christ. For he is our peace, who has made both one, who shall give us peace above peace.

## PSALM CXXV.

[1046A]

VERS. 1.-- *A canticle of degrees.* [n: (CASS.)] The seventh degree, in a figure of the Old Testament, prophesies through Christ who is to come the return from captivity, as it were from Babylon: whence now consolation, and a sowing that more may be reaped. [n: (AUG.)] Those redeemed by the blood of Christ and turned back from captivity sing these things, while they are pilgrims, groaning by the way. Sion is Jerusalem, eternal in the angels, captive in men. *In the turning.* [n: (CASS.)] First the greatness of the deed is shown, with the greatness of the joy: secondly he prays for the turning from captivity still to come, there, *Turn again, O Lord. In the turning.* [n: (AUG.)] Or, when he shall have turned, etc., *as far as turn again, O Lord, our captivity. As.* [n: (ID.)] It is not always set for a likeness, but [1046B] for a property, as here; whence it is also said: He did as a just man, which he truly is; so these are made *as men comforted,* that is, they rejoiced, as receiving consolation in the resurrection of Christ, who went before that we might follow.

VERS. 2.-- *Our mouth.* [n: (CASS.)] The secret of the heart, where joys first are, which afterwards break forth by the office of the tongue. [n: (AUG.)] *Our mouth.* Of the heart: thence proceeds the evil and the good which cleanses or defiles us; whence not: *That which proceedeth out of the mouth, defileth [n: (Marc. VII)]* [var: Vulg. Mc 7:15 *quae de homine procedunt, illa communicant hominem*; Migne's wording is that of Mt 15:11]. Not of the mouth of the flesh, but of the heart. For thou art not unclean if thou namest thefts and other evils: but while thou thinkest of them, even if thou keep silence. *Among the Gentiles.* [n: (ID.)] Not the Gentiles, because not all believe. *He hath magnified,* because they see them work miracles also; a firm testimony from an adversary. [1046C] [n: (CASS.)] And he has truly magnified, lest thou doubt because of the words of the Gentiles.

VERS. 4.-- *Turn again, O Lord.* [n: (ID.)] The second part. He prays for the turning which is to come, in which there shall be a sowing in hope of joy. [n: (ID.)] The redemption of the coming of the Lord having been foretold, they pray again that their own sins be forgiven. In the general remission they rejoice, and they pray pardon for themselves. *Turn again, O Lord, our captivity.* [n: (ID.)] There was none according to the letter in the days of David, but under the devil the world was captive, etc., *as far as* it is not therefore to be given over, because *he that soweth in tears.* [n: (THEOD.)] He calls Jerusalem the south, etc., *as far as* that they may be constrained, and make their way after the manner of a torrent.

VERS. 5.-- *In tears.* [n: (AUG.)] Spiritual sowing is always in tears, whence: *Blessed are they that mourn.* But they [1046D] who sow in the flesh, sow with gladness: but afterwards weeping: because *mourning taketh hold of the end of joy.* [n: (ID.)] Zacchaeus, *Behold the half,* etc., *as far as: He that shall give a cup of cold water shall not lose his reward [n: (Matth. X)]*.

VERS. 6.-- *Going.* [n: (CASS.)] By the advancement of a better life. *Casting,* sending on before them into that world. *Their own,* not of rapine, but of their own labour; *coming,* to the judgment. This is common to all, but to joy none come but the good. *And they wept.* [n: (AUG.)] Because they were wretched, etc., *as far as* he wishes that many be sick.



## PSALM CXXVI.

VERS. 1.-- *A canticle of degrees of Solomon.* [n: (CASS.)] The eighth degree, etc., *as far as* and in himself, as the corner stone, he built up the Church of two walls, of the Jews and [1047A] of the Gentiles. [n: (AUG.)] Here is added *of Solomon,* because it was he who built the temple, in a figure of Christ the peacemaker, who built the Church of two walls. Lest therefore thou shouldst understand that Solomon, he says, *Unless the Lord build.* In him is hope to be placed, but in no man: because even that Solomon fell who built the temple. [n: (CASS.)] First, he admonishes how they that ascend ought to bear themselves, namely that they learn that the building and the keeping are from none but God. And exaltation in the world to come is only after humiliation. Secondly, he tells what the inheritance is, and how it is gathered, there, *Behold the inheritance. Unless the Lord.* [n: (ID.)] As if he should say [sic: *dieeret*]: now Jesus has come, he has brought back from captivity: now the city Jerusalem is being built; but let them that ascend learn that the building and the keeping are from none but God, and exaltation [1047B] only after humiliation in the world to come; for although they are glad of the grace of the New Testament, yet in so great a gift no man ought to presume. *The house.* [n: (AUG.)] To this every man ascends who advances: from this every man falls who fails; by loving God thou ascendest, by loving the world thou fallest. For these are the songs of lovers, and of men burning with a certain holy desire. *They have laboured.* [n: (ID.)] All labour in this building who preach the word of God in the Church. *The city.* [n: (CASS.)] He passes from the house to the city, that presumption be made neither in individuals nor in a small gathering. And although house and city, according to the metaphor which is drawn from different things, are diverse: yet the Church is one, which both signify.

[1047C]VERS. 2.-- *Before the light.* [n: (AUG.)] If ye rise before the light rises; the light is Christ, after whom we must rise, not before; as he does who sets himself before him: wishing to be lofty here, where he was humble: as also the sons of Zebedee: but the resurrection, that is, the exaltation, follows the sitting, that is, the humiliation, as our light Christ rose lofty after death. *Ye shall have sat.* Sitting sometimes signifies the honour of judging, but here humiliation. *Ye that eat the bread of sorrow.* [n: (AUG.)] He eats the bread of sorrow who groans in this pilgrimage, that is, in the vale of weeping refreshes himself out of his affliction, saying: *My tears have been made my bread day and night [n: (Psal. XLI)]* [var: Vulg. *Fuerunt mihi lacrimae meae panes*, "my tears have been my bread"]. *Sleep.* [n: (CASS.)] Temporal death, in which is a secure rest.

VERS. 3.-- *Behold the inheritance.* [n: (ID.)] Secondly, what the [1047D] inheritance is and how it is gathered. As if he should say, the beloved shall rise after sleep. *The fruit of the womb.* [n: (AUG.)] The womb of the Church, etc., *as far as* because he acquired all men through the obedience of his passion, *whence, ask of me, and I will give thee the Gentiles for thy inheritance [n: (Psal. II)]*.

VERS. 4.-- *As arrows.* [n: (ID.)] But whence so great an inheritance was made: because the Lord is mighty: when he shot forth the arrows, the apostles, mightily from the bow of holy Scripture, those that were shot forth reached unto the ends of the earth; and such are the *children of them that were shot forth,* as were they that were shot forth. *The children of them that were shot forth.* [n: (ID.)] The children of the apostles; to whom it was said, *Shake off the dust from your feet [n: (Matth. XVIII)]*. But they so appear, etc., *as far as* who were sent by the hand of God unto the ends of the earth.

## PSALM CXXVII.

[1048A]

VERS. 1.-- *A canticle of degrees.* [n: (CASS.)] The ninth degree is, that the peacemaker be chastely feared, who alone exalts, keeps, and builds. First he enumerates the good things of him that fears; secondly he wishes the good things of him that fears. *Blessed are all they that fear the Lord.* It is to be known that there are four fears, etc., *as far as* of which the Prophet here says: *Blessed are all they that fear the Lord.*

VERS. 2.-- *The labours of the hands.* [n: (ID.)] And note that he began from the plural, saying: *Blessed:* but because all are one in Christ, in the singular there follows, *The labours of thy hands.* [n: (AUG.)] He says the labours of the fruits, when he ought to say: The fruit of the labours; but because they are taken up with joy even in the labour, for the sake of hope, they themselves also give delight, and are even now food; but more delightful [1048B] shall their fruit be, when the saints, coming [cj: *veniens*; read *venient*, "shall come"], *carrying their sheaves with joyfulness.*

VERS. 3.-- *Thy wife.* [n: (CASS.)] Although this might be taken according to the letter, etc., *as far as* it pertains to the New Testament. [n: (AUG.)] Or by *wife* the Church is understood, and the speech is directed to Christ, when *thy* and *thine* are said; nor yet is the voice or the person changed, because of the unity of the head and the body. For Christ is one man, both head and body, and in the head each one of the faithful has this.

VERS. 4.-- *He shall be blessed.* [n: (CASS.)] Where blessing is, all good things flow together unto the joy of sweetness.

*He shall be blessed.* [n: (ID.)] The second part, where out of piety he asks that they that fear be blessed; here he wishes good things for him that fears, which, namely, are not common to the wicked.

[1048C]VERS. 5.--Or, *The Lord.* [n: (AUG.)] Of the Jews, Out of Sion, etc., *as far as* which they cannot so do.

## PSALM CXXVIII.

VERS. 1.-- *A canticle of degrees.* [n: (CASS.)] The tenth degree, that he who fears chastely may bear all things for his sake, which is now expedient for the Church among the wicked.

*Often have they fought against me.* [n: (AUG.)] The wicked, although they hear the word of God, etc., *as far as* that they bear so many wicked men. [n: (CASS.)] Showing first what things he has borne, he comforts the weak. Secondly he treats of the reprobation of the persecutors, there, *The Lord is just. Often have they fought against me.* As it were: O sons, do not marvel at these things, etc., *as far as* because the Church grows in adversities.

VERS. 2.-- *For indeed they could not against me.* [n: (AUG.)] [1048D] Namely, prevail, so as to draw me into consent unto sin.

VERS. 3.-- *Upon the back.* [n: (CASS.)] What the wicked cannot do publicly to all, they attempt to urge secretly upon single persons, so that they who would be seen coming from the front may not be felt from behind. *They have lengthened out iniquity.* Elsewhere, *they have put their injustice far from me.* [n: (AUG.)] Who, although the wicked are with the good in the world, even if they be also in the Church: yet lest any suppose iniquity to stand beside justice, although I bear them, nevertheless their iniquity is far from me; let no one therefore fear the wicked mingled with him, who are far off.

*The Lord is just.* [n: (CASS.)] The second part, where he treats of the reprobation of the persecutors.

VERS. 4.-- *They that hate Sion.* [n: (ID.)] Whom not only open persecutors hate, but also they who enter [1049A] feignedly. For they who will not keep the word of God hate the Church.

*Let them be as grass upon the housetops.* [n: (CASS.)] The wicked are compared to grass upon the housetops, etc., *as far as* but in neither is there fruit for the wicked.

[n: (GREG.)] The bulrush is said to wither before all herbs, etc., *as far as* it loses, as it were, the appearance of greenness, etc.

VERS. 6.-- *Which before it be plucked up.* [n: (AUG.)] They are not yet finished in the judgment of God, and already they have not the sap of greenness.

VERS. 8.-- *And they have not said.* [n: (CASS.)] It was a custom among the Hebrews that a wayfarer should bless those at work: whence it is here said conversely, that to those who work in vain no blessing is given.

[1049B] *And they have not said.* Whom the angels shall not gather, to whom the apostles have not preached. And this is what he says:

[n: (AUG.)] *And they have not said,* etc. Chiefly among the Jews it was the custom to say to any whosoever were at work: *The blessing of the Lord be upon you. We have blessed you in the name,* etc. [n: (CASS.)] They bless the good, not in their own name, but *in the name of the Lord:* from whom are all things, not from them.



## PSALM CXXIX.

VERS. 1.-- *A canticle of degrees.* [n: (ID.)] The eleventh degree, etc., *as far as* a glad conclusion is set down with the certainty of pardon, there, *Let Israel hope in the Lord.*

*Out of the depths.* [n: (AUG.)] He who feels himself in the deep, etc., *as far as* as the Scripture says: *The sinner, when he shall have come [1049C] into the depth of vices, contemneth.* [var: Vulg. Prov. 18:3 *impius, cum in profundum venerit peccatorum, contemnit*, "the ungodly … the depth of sins"]

VERS. 3.-- *Who shall abide it.* [n: (ID.)] Because well nigh all human life is barked at round about by sins, and every conscience is accused by its own thoughts. That heart is not chaste which presumes upon its own justice. Let it therefore presume upon the mercy of God, *For with him there is propitiation,* whence he subjoins:

VERS. 4.-- *For with thee there is propitiation.* As it were: In what hope dost thou cry? Because there is mercy in thee, wherefore he speaks thus: Or join it thus.

[n: (AUG.)] No man can abide it, etc., *as far as* one hope therefore there is: *For with thee there is propitiation,* etc., *For thy law's sake,* [n: (ID.)] I have waited for the *law* of mercy, etc., *as far as* and thou hast given this law in the very necessity.

[1049D]VERS. 6.-- *The morning.* [n: (ID.)] Let him hope in the morning watch who hopes for nothing else from God than immortality. *From the morning watch,* etc., *as far as* thence in the law the head and the tail of the victim were offered to the Lord. *Let Israel hope in the Lord.* [n: (CASS.)] The second part, where is the glad conclusion: Secure concerning himself, he preaches that they should hope always in the Lord. As it were, as his soul has hoped, so let Israel hope.

VERS. 8.-- *And he shall redeem Israel from all his iniquities.* [n: (ID.)] Here he has now said what that one shall do, whom he called merciful and plentiful.

## PSALM CXXX.

VERS. 1.-- *A canticle of degrees.* [n: (CASS.)] The twelfth degree is humility, which he here [1050A] shows that he has within and without. To humility therefore he exhorts.

*Lord, it is not.* [n: (ID.)] First, setting himself forth as humble, he brings in punishment for the man that is not humble; secondly, by his own example he exhorts to hope. Showing therefore that he is humble within and without, he says. *Neither have I walked.* [n: (AUG.)] I was not proud, I would not, as it were, walk in wonderful things, etc., *as far as: The greater thou art, humble thyself in all things.* [var: Vulg. Sir. 3:20 *Quanto magnus es*, "howsoever great thou art"]

VERS. 2.-- *If I was not humbly minded.* [n: (ID.)] Nourished in milk, which is the Word made flesh, that so I might grow up to the bread of angels, to the Word which is with God, but not.

*I have exalted,* as the heretics do. Who, if they have in any way begun something, or have seemed to grasp it, thereupon grow proud; and while they are still tender upon their mother, and are carried in her hands that they may grow, they who were carried in the womb [1050B] that they might be born: they are parted from the milk before the time, whence they are quenched.

*So the reward.* [n: (ID.)] He binds himself with a curse, etc., *as far as* so shall God reveal that wherein he is otherwise minded, *because he resisteth the proud, and giveth grace to the humble.*

*As one is weaned.* For as five ages are recounted, etc., *as far as* because God was man; but not changed into man.

VERS. 3.-- *Let Israel hope.* [n: (CASS.)] The second part, where he concludes, admonishing the rest by his own example.




## PSALM CXXXI.

VERS. 1.-- *A canticle of degrees.* [n: (ID.)] In the thirteenth degree, now humble and meek, etc., *as far as* whence in Isaias: *Upon whom shall my Spirit rest, [1050C] but upon the humble and the quiet, and him that trembleth at my words?* [var: Vulg. Is 66:2 *ad quem autem respiciam, nisi ad pauperculum, et contritum spiritu, et trementem sermones meos*, "but to whom shall I have respect, but to him that is poor and little, and of a contrite spirit"]

*Remember, O Lord, David, and all.* [n: (ID.)] First, showing the vow of building a house, he prays that he may fulfil it. Secondly, he shows the house built, there, *Behold, hear.* Thirdly, he tells the promises of God made to Sion when built, there, *The Lord hath sworn.* Willing therefore to build a house for the Lord, he says in the person of the whole Christ, the head: *O Lord,* etc. *David.* [n: (AUG.)] According to the letter, meek, in that he rendered not evil for evil to Saul.

*As he swore and vowed a vow.* That is, that he may fulfil what he promised: let no man presume to render by his own strength what he has vowed. And the Church has vowed in meekness that she may be the house of God, because nothing more pleasing can be offered to God. And it is a house if it seeks not its own, but rejoices in [1050D] what is common: from things of one's own come evils, whence he says: *If I shall enter.* He knew that he was hindered by what was his own, lest a place for God should be made.

VERS. 3.-- *If I shall enter* as far as *till I find out a place for the Lord.* The letter is left hanging, and it is an aposiopesis, as if he said: If I shall do this or that, let me never hereafter be believed. As it were, David swore to the Lord: *If I shall enter into the tabernacle of my house,* etc. [n: (CASS.)] Or, in the person of Christ, from that place: *If I shall enter into the tabernacle of my house,* namely of the heavenly habitation. The tabernacle is the heavenly habitation. *If I shall go up,* etc., to the right hand of the Father, where is the bed, that is, rest and the end of labours.

VERS. 4.-- *Sleep* [sic: *Sumnum*], the rest of the three days' death. [1051A] Then in other words he repeats the same. *And to my eyelids slumber. And rest to my temples* until the holy Church be founded by preaching. It is an aposiopesis, as it were, let me be believed no more.

*And to my eyelids.* [n: (AUG.)] There is he who sleeps not, but slumbers: now he is drawn back from earthly things, now he is rolled back into them, often he nods his head.

VERS. 5.-- *And rest.* From the rest of the temples sleep comes to the eyes, because for them that are about to sleep the temples begin. When therefore something temporal begins to give delight unto sin, the temples are weighed down; but give not thyself to that delight, and sleep comes not to the eyes, by this thought, if, as though rubbing thy forehead, thou shakest off sleep.

*I shall find:* [n: (ID.)] he says it, because he sought long.

[1051B] *A place for the Lord:* he sought it in himself, who rests upon the humble and the quiet. And a man is a place of God if he forsakes his own things; or at the least, if he loves not himself, but God.

*A tabernacle.* [n: (ID.)] As yet we groan. In the house we shall praise, because groaning belongs to them that are on pilgrimage; praise to them that already abide in the fatherland. *To the God of Jacob.* He says that he has paid, as above he said that he had promised.

VERS. 6.-- *Behold we have heard.* (CAS.) The second part, where the house is shown built, and where he also prays for those who are to be exalted, and that the Jews be not utterly strangers. *It in Ephrata,* [n: (AUG.)] or *it,* etc., *as far as* where were the brambles of idolatry.

[n: (CASS.)] Behold, hear, namely that the Lord was to be born in Ephrata Bethlehem, where the Lord was born: it was heard [1051C] that he should be born in Bethlehem, but for the faith of the Gentiles he was found *in the fields of the wood.*

VERS. 7.-- *We will go in.* [n: (AUG.)] We too, by persevering in our warfare, have nevertheless gone in (or shall go in) into his tabernacle, the tabernacle of the God of Jacob: to this house belongs he who is framed together with others in charity as with living stones. He therefore enters who loves, and he who enters is made the house of God. *In the place.* The house of God is where he ought to be adored: apart from which God hears not unto life eternal. He belongs to the house who is framed together with others in charity. Or the place in which the feet of the house of the Lord stand is Christ, in whom they persevere.

VERS. 8.-- *Arise.* [n: (ID.)] And it is said to him who says, *I have slept troubled:* Arise from death, into thy rest, [1051D] that thou be troubled no more, because *death shall no more have dominion.*

VERS. 9.-- *Priests.* [n: (ID.)] He takes the same men for priests and for saints, namely all the faithful. Or, by *priests* he signifies the prelates of the Church; by *saints,* the faithful that are subject. The ark therefore, which he had prayed might arise, he distinguishes by the parts of the Church.

*Priests,* as in the Old Testament the priests are adorned with divers ornaments, so the priests of the Gospel with divers virtues: these are the ornaments which God requires. For what the pontiffs of the law bore upon their garments, the priests of the Gospel ought to have in their hearts.

VERS. 10.-- *For David the servant's sake.* [n: (CASS.)] *Remember:* [1052A] And well do I say *for David's sake,* because *the Lord hath sworn to David.*

*For the sake of:* then in the person of David himself entreating God the Father.

[n: (AUG.)] For because Christ turned away his face from the Jews unto the Gentiles, the Father is entreated for the remnant, that they may be saved, which was fulfilled in the apostles and in many others. And he says: *for David's sake,* as if he said: Remember me also, that for my sake thou cast not off the Jews utterly.

VERS. 11.-- *He hath sworn.* [n: (CASS.)] The third part, where he tells the promises of God made to Sion when built, that is, to the Church. And David speaks in his own person or in the person of the whole Christ. *And he will not make it void* (otherwise) *he will not repent.* [n: (AUG.)] God is said to repent, etc., *as far as* [1052B] for thou doest it because thou hadst erred: he does it, and delivers. *Of the fruit of thy womb.* He could have said, of the fruit of thy loins, etc., *as far as* because those whom David ruled temporally, Christ rules spiritually.

VERS. 12.-- *If they shall keep.* [n: (ID.)] They seem to earn merit, etc., *as far as* this is not changed. *And their children.* [n: (CASS.)] *If they shall keep the testament. They shall sit upon thy seat,* that is, they shall be partakers of thy blessedness: that as thou art of kingly power, so they also, according to their measure, and this, *Unto the age of the age,* that is, unto eternity.

VERS. 14.-- *This is my rest.* [n: (ID.)] So does God love us, that he says that he himself rests, if we rest in him.

VERS. 15.-- *The widow.* [n: (ID.)] The soul is a widow [1052C] which understands itself forsaken of all help save of God alone. *With bread.* God is the bread, who in the flesh made himself milk.

VERS. 16.-- *Her priests.* Or a distinction is to be made between priests and saints, as above. And it is, *Her priests,* that is, the prelates, *I will clothe with salvation,* that is, with Christ, that is, that they may be honourable for teaching. *And the saints,* that is, those subject. *They shall rejoice with exceeding great joy* over Christ.

*They shall rejoice with exceeding great joy.* [n: (ID.)] Because they are clothed with salvation, etc., *as far as* who dwells in him; therefore he has exalted [sic: *exaltaxit*] securely.

VERS. 17.-- *The horn of David, I have prepared a lamp for Christ.* [n: (ID.)] The spiritual height of the Church, etc., *as far as* before the death and resurrection of Christ.

[1052D]VERS. 18.-- *I will clothe with confusion,* etc. [n: (CASS.)] This the Son will do, to whom all judgment is given. But note that the Father, who here speaks, does what the Son does.

## PSALM CXXXII.

VERS. 1.-- *A canticle of degrees.* [n: (ID.)] The fourteenth degree: charity, which makes brethren to dwell in one, whence blessing and life. *Behold how good.* And this psalm is an atom, etc., *as far as* here therefore the just man, rejoicing over the dwelling together of brethren, says: *Behold how. Behold how.* [n: (AUG.)] That verse, most famous in the whole world, has as the trumpet of the Holy Ghost gathered together them that were divided: it has brought forth very many monasteries. [n: (CASS.)] *Behold.* As though more were set forth by the showing than could be unfolded by the tongue. *To dwell in [1053A] one.* [n: (AUG.)] This concerning all Christians, but it properly befits monasteries, from which nevertheless the blessing comes down to the rest, as also to those brethren of Judaea, of whom it is said: *They had one heart,* and *one soul.*

VERS. 2.-- *As the ointment.* [n: (THEOD.)] The priestly oil, etc., *as far as* it perfects the odour of perfect virtue. *The beard.* [n: (AUG.)] Behold the determination, that is, upon the apostles and the other primitive saints, who first strove in faith, but could not be overcome, because they had already gone before the head that had suffered [cj: *praecesserunt*; read *praecesserat*, "the head had gone before"]. *Aaron.* The priest who offered up himself, etc., *as far as* but they could not be overcome. From them. *To the skirt.* [n: (CASS.)] Or by the *skirt,* which is the edge of the garment, etc., *as far as* the profit of concord reaches even to them that are subject.

VERS. 3.-- *As the dew.* [n: (ID.)] *Dew* is thin water, whence the earth brings forth, and it signifies grace, whereby sinners are converted. *Hermon.* [n: (AUG.)] According to the letter a mountain beyond Jordan, etc., *as far as* the rest are taken up, who are *in one* in heart.




## PSALM CXXXIII.

VERS. 1.-- *A canticle of degrees.* [n: (CASS.)] The fifteenth degree: the charity of the world. And this is the third psalm concerning the charity of God, of which he here treats, as above of the charity of the neighbour. There is not one grace whereby God is loved, and another whereby the neighbour. But because grace has divers acts, and by it divers things are loved. He admonishes therefore unto the loving of God. First, he invites in the plural to bless God; secondly, he wishes them to be blessed, there, *May he bless.*

*Behold,* is the word of one pointing out. And to whom he makes this pointing out he determines: you, *ye servants of the Lord.*

*Ye stand.* It belongs to servants not to sit in the house of their lord. Or, it is understood of the same men: and by the *house* the Church is understood, by the *courts,* the breadth of charity. For in charity, which extends even to enemies, there is breadth; in hatred, narrowness. As though he said: do ye *bless, who stand,* that is, who persevere, *In the house of the Lord,* that is, in the churches; and *who stand, In the courts of the house of our God,* that is, in [sic: *iu*] the breadth of charity, where no man is straitened.

VERS. 2.-- *Lift up your hands.* As Moses lifted up his hands while the people of Israel fought against Amalec, *Lift up,* I say, *unto the holy places:* that with such intention ye may work the eternal good, for otherwise it avails not.

[1053D] *May the Lord bless thee.* The second part, where he wishes the faithful to be blessed. As though he said, *bless ye,* and that ye may be able to do this, *May the Lord bless thee,* with the blessing which is *out of Sion.* Who is able, because he it is who made heaven and earth. [n: (CASS.)] *May he bless.* Above he exhorts many to bless God, but he himself blesses one only, because, though they be many, yet they are in one, and to unity blessing belongs. Whence one was healed in the pool, he who went down first. Be thou therefore in one, and the blessing shall come unto thee.

## PSALM CXXXIV.

VERS. 1.-- *Alleluia.* [n: (CASS.)] After the degrees, which lead unto eternity, the alleluia is set, that the Church may enjoy the praises of God, for whom such a gift is prepared. [1054A] *Praise ye.* First, by a demonstrative address he admonishes, etc., *as far as* he subjoins who ought to praise and why: *Praise ye, servants.*

VERS. 3.-- *Praise ye the Lord,* etc. [n: (AUG.)] If all things are praised because they are good, etc., *as far as* in whom the whole seed of Israel is understood, the whole Church is thought of.

*Sing ye to his name,* that is, praise him for this, that he did what the reality of his name requires, when, being incarnate, he gave himself to men to be tasted.

VERS. 4.-- *Jacob.* [n: (ID.)] He is interpreted "wrestler," and signifies those who wrestle against spiritual wickednesses. *Israel for a possession.* According to the letter. The other nations the Lord set under angels to be guarded: Jacob he chose for himself, that he might till him as a field, sow him, possess him, keep him.

[1054B]VERS. 6.-- *All things whatsoever.* [n: (ID.)] As though he said: He is great in essence, great in works, etc., *as far as* but the spirit of man is unknown, whence, behold, it is said.

VERS. 7.-- *Who bringeth forth winds out of his treasures. Who slew the firstborn.* [n: (ID.)] Faith, whence we begin, by which, when it is living, a man advances unto better things; Egypt, affliction: whosoever therefore afflicts the Church and makes scandals, though he be called a Christian, loses faith.

*From the end.* Either from the lowest part of the earth, or from the compass of the ends of the earth. *Lightnings and rain.* [var: Vulg. Ps 134:7 *fulgura in pluviam fecit*, "he hath made lightnings for the rain"] The lightning works terror, the rain gladness. He therefore who terrified with the lightning refreshes with the rain.

VERS. 8.-- *Who slew.* [n: (ID.)] This he did among men for his own cup's sake. If he has told what things thou shouldst love, [1054C] hear also what things thou shouldst fear. *From man even to beast.* [n: (CASS.)] This he often repeats, etc., *as far as* which he destroys in the prudent and in the simple.

VERS. 10.-- *Mighty kings.* [n: (ID.)] The spirits of the world: who are slain, while they cannot do in us what they will.

VERS. 11.-- *Sehon king of the Amorrhites,* etc. This was done under Moses: of this it is read in the book of Numbers, that when they forbade the children of Israel to pass through their borders, they were smitten by him, and the people of Israel possessed their land.

*Chanaan.* [n: (ID.)] Humiliation, an evil one, namely, when the holy fall away from honour.

VERS. 12.-- *And he gave their land for an inheritance.* [n: (ID.)] That is, the heavenly blessedness, which [1054D] the demons forsook through pride, he gave to the faithful. This is the true land of promise, which is more truly called an inheritance, which is in peace, than that actual land, which the children of Israel often lost. *The inheritance of Israel.* [n: (AUG.)] The inheritance: they are the nations of Israel, that is, of the faithful people, whom they feed with tribulation, teaching patience; or which Israel cultivates by conforming them to himself.

VERS. 13.-- *Unto generation and generation.* [n: (ID.)] One generation there is, whereby we are born again through baptism, another whereby we shall rise again: God has not forgotten to call in the one, to crown in the other.

VERS. 14.-- *For he will judge.* [n: (CASS.)] The cause of praise, because the Lord saw the people for whom he did so much, to whom he sent prophets, [1055A] and afterward the Son also. But because they were hard, *he will judge* them in the time to come, *and in the servants,* that is, in the faithful, *he will comfort,* [var: Vulg. Ps 134:14 *et in servis suis deprecabitur*, "and he will be entreated in favour of his servants"] when he shall render the eternal rewards. Behold, the gradation of praise is brought through unto judgment. [n: (AUG.)] Or, thus. Therefore concerning the present, etc., *as far as they may see, and they who see may become blind.*

VERS. 15.-- *The idols.* [n: (CASS.)] The second part: after he had filled himself with the praises of God, he refutes the worshippers of idols by mocking them, because the perfect praise of truth is to destroy falsehood; above the same things were said, but repeated they increase shame.

VERS. 16.-- *They have a mouth, and they shall not speak.* [n: (AUG.)] Those outward things the craftsman could make; but he could not give a spirit, nor hearing to the ears, nor light to the eyes, nor voice to the mouth.

[1055B]VERS. 18.-- *Let them become like unto them.* [n: (ID.)] Knowing these things they mock the idols, etc., *as far as* neither do they perceive the good odour of Christ.

VERS. 19.-- *House of Israel, bless ye.* [n: (CASS.)] This is the third part, where, after the idols have been mocked, he admonishes all orders to celebrate the praises of God: as it were: Let the worshippers of idols become like unto them. But you, who are *the house of Israel. House of Aaron, bless ye.* [n: (AUG.)] The prelates are the house of Aaron: the ministers, the house of Levi; the peoples, all that fear; all generally are the house of Israel: let us all with one voice say: *Blessed.*

VERS. 21.-- *Blessed be the Lord.* [n: (ID.)] Or according to the interpretation of the names, etc., *as far as* where we shall see him in eternal peace.




## PSALM CXXXV.

VERS. 1.-- *Alleluia.* [n: (AUG.)] This psalm contains the praise of God, where, although many things are said in the praise of God, yet chiefly here the mercy of God is commended. [n: (CASS.)] The divers beginnings of the verses, etc., *as far as* so is the divine presence at hand if it be sung purely. *Give praise to the Lord.* [n: (ID.)] First, he sets forth the magnificence of God and the founding of the whole world [cj: *orbi*; read *orbis*, "of the whole world"]. Secondly he tells what things he did in Egypt and in the nation of the Jews as a figure of us, there, *Who smote.* Thirdly, he treats of Christians, there, *and he gave their land for an inheritance. Give praise to the Lord.* [n: (AUG.)] Let us now praise, etc., *as far as* through mercy is eternal blessedness.

VERS. 2.-- *Give praise to the God of gods.* He calls men gods, to whom the word of God came in another psalm, where he says, *I have said, You are gods and sons of the Most High,* [1055D] etc. And they are not therefore called gods because all are good: for if all were good, he would not say in the same psalm: *but in the midst he judgeth gods,* that is, he distinguishes them; of whom also he subjoins: *Give praise to the Lord of lords.*

VERS. 3.-- *Who doth wonders.* [n: (ID.)] As it is everywhere set at the end, etc., *as far as* therefore *give praise, for his mercy endureth for ever.*

VERS. 4.-- *Great wonders alone.* [n: (ID.)] Because he does certain things through men or angels, and certain great things he himself alone, which also he sets forth first.

VERS. 5.-- *Who made the heavens.* [n: (ID.)] The whole creation therefore he made by himself, etc., *as far as* whence, *Thou hast made all things in wisdom, O God.*

[1056A]Or typically, *The heavens in understanding,* that is, the spiritual saints, to whom he granted not only to believe, but to understand divine things. But the simple, who hold fast only a firm faith, as though beneath the heavens, are called *earth;* who, because in the baptism which they received they stand fast as it were *upon the waters* by a firm faith, as it were *upon the waters* he established the earth.

VERS. 6.-- *The earth above the waters.* [n: (ID.)] But whereby the heavier earth is borne by the lighter waters, etc., *as far as* so the Lord sat above the well, that is, beside it.

VERS. 7.-- *Who made the great lights.* [n: (ID.)] that is, he conferred the gifts of graces upon his faithful.

VERS. 9.-- *The moon and the stars for the power of the night.* [n: (CASS.)] It can also be said otherwise, mystically, etc., *as far [1056B] as* that through these each of the lesser may give light.

VERS. 10.-- *Who smote Egypt.* [n: (ID.)] The second part, where he tells what things he does in Egypt and in the nation of the Jews under a figure of us. Now he begins upon the things which he did through men or through angels. *With their firstborn.* He smote the firstborn of Egypt, etc., *as far as* but God shuts them out from himself.

VERS. 12.-- *With a mighty hand.* [n: (ID.)] The hand of God is his invincible action, which comes from the arm, that is, from his whole singular power.

VERS. 13.-- *He divided the Red Sea into parts.* [n: (ID.)] According to the letter *into parts* twelve, for the number of the tribes, that each tribe might have its own ways to go by; typically, thus by divers ways there is a passing out of the world unto God. By the *Red Sea* the world is understood.

[1056C]VERS. 15.-- *And he shook off Pharao.* [n: (ID.)] He shook off the devil, with his ministers, as dust, which is quickly shaken off. In this quickness both the power of God is shown, and those earthly defilements are pointed out which we are wont to shake off. *Pharao.* [n: (ID.)] Fitly by Pharao the devil is understood: Pharao kills the males, keeps the females alive; so the devil stifles the virtues, nourishes concupiscence.

VERS. 21.-- *And he gave their land for an inheritance.* [n: (CASS.)] The third part, where he comes down to Christians, setting forth the benefits given to them, that thus thou mayest know one author of all things.

VERS. 22.-- *To Israel his servant.* He takes by *Israel the servant* Christ, etc., *as far as* he gave no price [1056D] to the Egyptians.

VERS. 25.-- *He giveth food.* [n: (THEOD.)] From his singular Providence, etc., *as far as* in the Gospels he says: *Behold the birds of the air, which neither sow, nor gather into barns, and your heavenly Father feedeth them. To all flesh.* [var: Vulg. Mt 6:26 *Respicite volatilia caeli, quoniam non serunt, neque metunt, neque congregant in horrea*, "behold the birds of the air, for they neither sow, nor do they reap, nor gather into barns"] [n: (AUG.)] To every kind of men, of Israel and of the Gentiles, not as the manna to the Jew alone.

## PSALM CXXXVI.

*A psalm of David.* [n: (CASS.)] The third psalm concerning the lamentation of Jerusalem, etc, *as far as* but this one concerning the captivity brought about by Nabuchodonosor. [n: (AUG.)] Two cities there are, mingled together in body, etc., *as far as* who in mind, in desire are citizens of Jerusalem.

VERS. 1.-- *Upon the rivers.* [n: (CASS.)] First, that they should be led captive [1057A] long afterward, etc., *as far as* he begins with the desire of the fatherland, saying: *Upon the rivers,* etc. *While we remembered,* [n: (AUG.)] In a hostile land sweet is the remembrance of the fatherland, etc., *as far as* both are of Babylon.

VERS. 2.-- *On the willows.* [n: (ID.)] Which are watered by the rivers of Babylon, etc., *as far as* but by deferring we hang them up. [n: (CASS.)] The force of grief is heightened when the delight wherewith they were wont to console themselves is hung up.

*For there they asked us.* [n: (BASIL.)] And hence they began no small profit [cj: *coeperunt*; read *ceperunt*, "they took"], etc., *as far as* so that they were even willing to hear our songs.

VERS. 3.-- *The words of the songs.* [n: (AUG.)] Or, of the canticles, as, what does Christ profit? or, what is that other life? But here, among those who are full of evils, we ought not [1057B] to strike the instrument, that it should sound, but to hang it up. [n: (ID.)] The devil craftily seeks occasion to mock, etc., *as far as* and he bound himself with an oath, saying: *If I forget thee,* etc.

VERS. 4.-- *How shall we sing.* [n: (ID.)] Care must be taken lest any man, fearing to displease such men, court their friendships, and begin to take delight in Babylon.

VERS. 5.-- *My right hand.* [n: (ID.)] Whatever thou doest for eternal things, the right hand works; if thou aim also at another thing, the left hand has mingled itself in; and delighting in it as in the right hand thou losest eternal things.

VERS. 6.-- *Let it cleave.* [n: (CASS.)] Let me become dumb in the praises of God, which is the consolation of weeping and of miseries. *My tongue.* [n: (AUG.)] The canticle of Jerusalem is our tongue, the canticle of the world a barbarous tongue. He is dumb [1057C] to God who has forgotten Jerusalem.

VERS. 7.-- *Remember.* [n: (CASS.)] The second part, where the turning to God is made against the enemies. *Remember, in the day* of the separation, that they may be punished. *Of the children of Edom.* [n: (AUG.)] The children of Edom, that is, the Idumeans, were a hindrance to them that rebuilt Jerusalem; or, in the destruction they were joined with Babylon, whence he says, *The daughter of Babylon, miserable. Edom morally* is carnality, which seduces us unto unlawful motions. *Edom.* [n: (ID.)] Esau, all the carnal; Jacob, the spiritual; the elder, the carnal man, the younger, the spiritual, because first indeed that which is animal, afterward that which is spiritual. All the carnal are enemies to the spiritual, whence he says: *Remember,* deliver us from them. *Empty it out.* [n: (CASS.)] From the likeness [1057D] of a cistern, out of which, if the water be drawn, the bottom is reached. But what does it profit even to take away the life of such men, for whom, in the foundation (which is Christ), there is life eternal? while the wicked think to hurt, they give crowns.

VERS. 8.-- *The daughter.* [n: (AUG.)] By succession the city of the wicked is made the daughter of Babylon, as the Church, that is, Jerusalem, the city of the good, is by succession the daughter of Sion. [n: (CASS.)] *The daughter of Babylon* morally. It is the flesh, which confusion, etc., *as far as* they dash them against the rock, Christ, that being broken they may perish.

VERS. 9.-- *Blessed is he that shall take.* [n: (AUG.)] What retribution? that as Babylon smothers those born in the world, while they are little, with the errors of their parents and of the world: so they who are now grown up, the knowledge of God being given them, dash the little ones of Babylon [1058A] to pieces, that is, the desires as they are being born, before they receive strength; thus they are more easily overcome, but the greater ones also must be slain against the rock.




## PSALM CXXXVII.

VERS. 1.-- *A psalm to David himself.* [n: (CASS.)] The captive, who had wept, as though Jerusalem and the temple were rebuilt, and as a fellow-citizen with that part of Jerusalem which was not captive, that is, with the angels, rejoices and praises first the benefits given him. In the person therefore of the Church, which is in the things above in hope, awaiting to be there in reality, he says, *I will confess.*

*I will confess to thee, O Lord.* First, he commends the benefits given him: secondly, he tells how these are spread abroad unto all, there, *Let them confess to thee, O Lord,* etc. *In the sight.* [1058B] [n: (AUG.)] In praising I set before myself the angels, to whom I look to be made equal in dignity hereafter, as I am now. But these in the praise of God set before themselves nothing else but himself. *In the sight* therefore *of the angels,* in that purity wherein the angels behold thee, that in thee alone they may rest, I *will sing to thee.*

VERS. 2.-- *For thy mercy.* [n: (ID.)] *All the ways of the Lord are mercy and truth:* There are no other ways by which he came to us, there are no others by which we may come to him: let us render these to God, mercy in succouring, truth in judging. *Above every holy name of thine.* [n: (CASS.)] Or above thy holy name, when thy worship became known to us.

VERS. 3.-- *In the soul.* And because in the soul a carnal multiplication can take place, he adds, *strength,* as though: I seek nothing [1058C] else in the soul but strength.

VERS. 4.-- *Let them confess.* [n: (ID.)] The second part, where it is said [cj: *dici*; read *dicit*, "he tells"] how these good things are to be spread abroad unto all. As it were, after my example: *Let them confess,* etc. *All the words of thy mouth.* [n: (AUG.)] Which have now been revealed everywhere, and sent unto all nations, etc., *as far as* and thus, the fleece being dried, *they heard all the words,* etc.

VERS. 5.-- *Great is the glory.* [n: (ID.)] If they are humble, they love and sing this; for if they exalt themselves, let them fear what follows. *For the Lord is high. He looketh on the low things.* [n: (CASS.)] Here is not the human condition, where the humble man is despised and the proud man regarded; but the Lord regards the humble.

*The high he knoweth afar off.* [n: (AUG.)] The proud man does not this, etc., *as far as* mourning this sojourning, whence [1058D] it is subjoined.

VERS. 7.-- *If I shall walk in the midst of tribulation.* Otherwise he does not quicken, save *in the midst of tribulation,* whence, *Blessed are they that mourn, woe to them that laugh.*

*Upon the wrath.* [n: (ID.)] This very thing, that they are angry with me, is thy chastisement upon them, that is, thy punishment. For to be angry is to them a punishment, not [sic: *noc*] only a fault. While therefore thou permittest this, thou stretchest forth the hand of vengeance upon them all.

*Thy right hand.* God has not a right hand or a left, but the right hand of God is called eternal happiness, the left, temporal happiness. Sometimes therefore he does not save his saints in the left hand, but he always saves them in the right.

[1059A]VERS. 8.-- *He will repay.* [n: (AUG.)] What I cannot do, etc., *as far as* in whom the princes of the world found no sin, wherefore they should kill him.




## PSALM CXXXVIII.

VERS. 1.-- *Unto the end, a psalm of David* [sic: *Ddvid*]*.* [n: (CASS.)] A psalm full of the depth of mysteries, etc., *as far as* veiled in the prophets, manifest in the Gospel. [n: (AUG.)] The whole Christ speaks, as one entire man, etc., *as far as* as two in one flesh. *Lord, thou hast proved me.* [n: (CASS.)] First, concerning the knowledge of God, etc., *as far as* fourthly, how the wicked do him no hurt, there, *If thou wilt kill, O God,* etc. *Lord, thou hast proved.* [n: (AUG.)] Now restored in Jerusalem, etc., *as far as* this is the person of the whole of human nature returning.

[1059B]VERS. 3.-- *Thou hast understood.* [n: (CASS.)] The foreknowledge of the Godhead is here shown, etc., *as far as* because the manhood assumed cannot be made equal to the divine substance. *From afar off.* [n: (AUG.)] While I am still on pilgrimage, before I come to the fatherland, as the father ran to meet the younger son returning, because he understood his thoughts from afar off, because he had said within himself, worn out with want [sic: *egestare*]: *I will arise and go to my father.*

*My path,* namely the evil one, by which I went, forsaking my father.

*And the line.* (Otherwise) the boundary, how far I came: far indeed had I gone, but thou wast there. He speaks as a fugitive caught, the vengeance of God following after him.

VERS. 4.-- *And all my ways:* before I went in them; and thou didst permit me to go in labour, that I might return if [1059C] I would not labour: this I confess. *For there is no guile in my tongue:* [var: Vulg. Ps 138:4 *quia non est sermo in lingua mea*, "for there is no speech in my tongue"] now I have justified in thy grace.

VERS. 5.-- My *last things,* when I fed the swine, *The old things,* when I asked for the portion of the substance: this according to the parable. But mystically, *The old things,* when through the ancient sin [sic: *pecatum*] we fell: *The last things,* the penalty, when we came into this mortality, which is the last, if we are willing to return; otherwise the eternal remains. *Thou hast formed me,* unto labours, unto which we are all born. *Thou hast laid.* (Otherwise) Thou hast made heavy the hand, the avenging hand, weighing down the proud man wholesomely, that it may raise up the humble man, in the hand made heavy.

VERS. 6.-- *Wonderful,* and incomprehensible [sic: *incomprehensibllis*] to me. *Thy knowledge is become.* It is not easy for me to contemplate thee, whom in my pride I forsook, as it was before, when I was with thee, when I said, give me the portion of the substance. [1059D] *From me,* that is, from my sin.

*It is strengthened,* (otherwise) it has prevailed. *And I shall not be able* to attain to it of myself. [n: (AUG.)] In Exodus it is read that the Lord, etc., *as far as* I am not hidden from thee, whose boundary thou seest.

VERS. 7.-- *Whither shall I go by thy spirit?* [n: (CASS.)] The second part, where he now determines that he must not flee [sic: *fungiendum*] from him, but to him, from him angered to him appeased, which shall be through him with whom the world is full: *For the Spirit of the Lord filleth all things. Whither shall I flee from thy face?* [var: Vulg. Wisd. 1:7 *Spiritus Domini replevit orbem terrarum*, "the Spirit of the Lord hath filled the whole world"] he turns himself this way and that, as though seeking a place of flight.

VERS. 8.-- *If I shall ascend into heaven, thou art there,* etc. [n: (AUG.)] If I shall lift myself up, I find one to repress me; if I shall be proud of my own justice, *Thou art there,* whose justice it truly is. *If I shall descend,* if by sinning I shall come, *Into hell,* [1060A] or into the deep, and unwilling to confess: I shall say, who sees me? *Thou art present,* to avenge. He found a counsel, but thus shall I be able to flee from him angered.

VERS. 9.-- *If I shall take wings,* the two wings of charity, which I had lost.

*Wings.* [n: (CASS.)] Which are of my own power, in [cj: *in*; read *id*, "that is"] is the efficacy of the Godhead, whereby he raised the body out of the sepulchre. These he took *at daybreak,* before the light, that is, at the time of the resurrection: and *he dwelt in the uttermost parts of the sea,* that is, above the widest end of the world. *And if I shall dwell in the uttermost parts.* [n: (AUG.)] In mind, at the end of the world, that I may not love the world, but its end, in which there may be rest for me. But he himself also leads thither, whence he subjoins:

VERS. 10.-- *For even there shall thy hand.* But considering the length of the way, he subjoins:

[1060B]VERS. 11.-- *And he said, perhaps darkness shall tread down,* etc. The iniquity of the world abounds, whereby the wings of charity can grow cold. And because in the night I despaired of crossing the sea, the night was made a light to me: There came a woman, the wisdom of God, who had lost the groat wherein is the image of the king, that is, man: she lighted from herself a lamp, which is of clay, that is, flesh, and the night being lit up she found the groat.

*And he said.* [n: (AUG.)] I deliberated.

*Perhaps thee.* By darkness he means that lower part of the rational creature, pressing upon him as an adversary, namely wicked men or the flesh, whence he fears: And it is, *Darkness,* that is, the lower rational creature holding the left-hand way.

*They have trodden me down.* [n: (ID.)] So I feared, but *the night [1060C] was made a light, in my delights,* [var: Vulg. Ps 138:11 *et nox illuminatio mea in deliciis meis*, "and night shall be my light in my pleasures"] that is, to those who, being before night, shall be enlightened in delights. *Because thee.* Sinners.

VERS. 12.-- *They shall not be darkened,* etc., *and the night,* by the sinners that dwell there. *As the day,* that is, the saints out of the Jews. *As his darkness, so also his light,* because in so far as he acknowledges, in so far he pardons. But whence is this to me? *because thou hast possessed my reins. Because darkness.* [n: (AUG.)] He who confesses not his sins darkens his own darkness; but if he confesses, God enlightens. *As his darkness.* Of our night, that is, the miseries of human life, etc., *as far as* whom the light does not lift up.

VERS. 13.-- *Because thou hast possessed my reins.* [n: (ID.)] Whence is this difference in thee? because the inward possessor [1060D] holds not only the heart, that is, the thoughts, but the reins also, that is, the delights: that the light of night may not delight, but the light of his own wisdom.

VERS. 14.-- *I will confess to thee.* [n: (CASS.)] The third part, where he tells how great is the exultation and the multiplication for him that flies back to God, God enlightening him and possessing his reins. *My soul shall know exceedingly.* [var: Vulg. Ps 138:14 *anima mea cognoscit nimis*, "my soul knoweth right well," in the present tense] [n: (AUG.)] Now my soul knows greatly: before, wonderful was *thy knowledge from me, and I could not attain unto it;* but now I can, because thou hast enlightened the night, hast possessed the reins, hast received me out of the womb, that is, because thy grace came to me and enlightened me.

VERS. 15.-- *My bone is not hidden from thee, which thou hast made in secret.* [n: (ID.)] Os from osse, not from ore, etc., *as far as* whence the Apostle: *As sorrowful, yet always [1061A] rejoicing.* [n: (BASIL.)] Again he speaks of the foreknowledge of God, and either says that all things are his secrets, or that other thing which he says, namely the workmanship and the fashioning. *My substance in the lower parts of the earth.* [n: (AUG.)] In the flesh, and yet the bone yields not to the evils of this lower place, etc., *as far as* let them not therefore despair.

VERS. 16.-- *Thy eyes did see my imperfect being, and in thy book all,* etc. [n: (ID.)] He who has hitherto spoken in the person of the body, here now speaks in the person of the head. *Days shall be formed.* (Otherwise) *They shall wander through the day.* The day here abiding was still Christ, etc., *as far as* because the apostles were made leaders and shepherds of the Church. As it were, deservedly I strengthened them, etc., *as far as* in the promise it was said to Abraham: *I will multiply thy seed as the stars of heaven and as the sand of the sea.*

[1061B]VERS. 18.-- *I am still with thee,* etc. As before death, which the Jews thought they had taken away. Or, *still,* that is, in death itself, which is not seen of a dead man. Or, though the resurrection be a sign of the Godhead, yet *I am still* hidden from the many, *with thee,* that is, as thou art.

[n: (AUG.)] *I am still with thee.* Not yet with them by knowledge, because they do not yet acknowledge him, because they did not acknowledge Christ soon after the resurrection. Or, through all this time I am with thee in secret at the right hand of the Father, before he be revealed in that brightness wherein he is to come.

VERS. 19.-- *If thou wilt kill, O God, the sinners,* etc. [n: (CASS.)] The fourth part, how he is not hurt by the wicked. [n: (AUG.)] *If thou wilt kill.* He subjoins what he suffers meanwhile, [1061C] etc., *as far as* in the judgment he will say openly: *Amen I say to you, I know you not, depart from me.* [var: Vulg. Mt 7:23 *numquam novi vos : discedite a me*, "I never knew you: depart from me"; the opening words are those of Mt 25:12 *Amen dico vobis, nescio vos*]

VERS. 21.-- *Have not they that hate thee, O Lord.* As it were, *If thou wilt kill, Have I not hated them that hate thee?* Them will I also slay.

[n: (AUG.)] As it were, heretics not understanding, etc., *as far as* how then am I defiled by being mingled with them?

*Prove me, O Lord.* [n: (ID.)] Hitherto concerning heretics and others following them, separated from the Church; now he treats also of the wicked, or of feigned men joined to himself, whom the Church suffers, and groans over. As it were, heretics separate themselves and others, etc., *as far as* but thou, O God, *Prove me and know.*



## PSALM CXXXIX.

[1061D]VERS. 1.-- *Unto the end. A psalm of David.* [n: (ID.)] A complaint, a groaning, the prayer of the body of Christ set among the wicked, not yet having the good in fact, but in hope, where there is full exultation. He admonishes us to pray to God against the evils of the world.

VERS. 2.-- *Deliver me, O Lord, from the man.* [n: (CASS.)] First he sets forth who the enemies are, what evils they stir up, setting prayers against them. Secondly, he says what help he has against evils, there: *I said to the Lord:* Thirdly, he discloses what the evils of his enemies are, there: *The head.* The Church therefore, labouring among the wicked, cries out. *From the unjust man.* [n: (AUG.)] He is unjust who does harm; whence, when he had said, *From the evil man,* as though expounding it, he added, *From the unjust man.*

[1062A]VERS. 3.-- *Who have devised.* [n: (CASS.)] He pursues in what manner the wicked persecute, for they assail the good in three ways, namely with the tongue, the hand and the heart: and he puts the heart first, saying, *Who have devised,* as if he were to say, Deliver me from the people of the wicked. [n: (AUG.)] Who, although for the most part they put forward good things upon their lips, have nevertheless devised iniquity in the heart.

VERS. 4.-- *They have sharpened.* He shows in what manner they persecute with the tongue.

*Like that of a serpent.* [n: (ID.)] In the serpent there is cunning and the guile of doing harm: therefore also it creeps, having no feet, that it may not be heard when it comes; its motion is smooth, but not straight: so the wicked creep to do harm, having a hidden venom beneath a smooth motion, whence he adds, *The venom of asps is under their lips.*

[1062B]VERS. 5.-- *Keep me, O Lord.* [n: (CASS.)] He adds concerning the persecution which is done by the hand, that is, by deed. Keep me, because he deceives through many things which he cannot guard against beforehand.

[sic: VERS. 9.]-- *The proud.* [n: (AUG.)] By this name he has briefly set forth the whole body of the devil: hence it is that they both call themselves just and are unwilling to confess; every proud man is also envious, as the devil is, who envied man that which he himself had lost. *And they have stretched out cords for a snare,* etc. Whence Isaias: *Woe to them that draw sins as a long garment,* [var: Vulg. Isa. 5:18 *Vae qui trahitis iniquitatem in funiculis vanitatis, et quasi vinculum plaustri peccatum*, "that draw iniquity with cords of vanity"] because they add sins to sins, etc., *as far as* and he be cast into the exterior darkness.

VERS. 7.-- *I said to the Lord.* [n: (ID.)] Diapsalma. The second part, what help there is against evils, as if he should say, In so many ways do the wicked assail, and what is the remedy amid [1062C] so many and so great evils?

*I said to the Lord: My God.* Great confidence: though he is the God of all, yet he is properly the God of those who enjoy him.

*Hear, O Lord, the voice of my supplication.* [n: (ID.)] The voice of men and their soul, etc., *as far as* not as the men of the Lord, who buy with a purse, thou with blood.

VERS. 8.-- *O strength of my salvation, thou hast overshadowed my head.* [n: (ID.)] Thou who givest the strength of my salvation, etc., *as far as* however much the enemy may rage.

VERS. 9.-- *Deliver me not, O Lord, from desire.* [var: Vulg. Ps. 139:9 *Ne tradas*, a prohibitive subjunctive] [n: (ID.)] The devil sets forth gain, etc., *as far as* save out of thy desire.

VERS. 10.-- *The head of the compassing.* [n: (CASS.)] Diapsalma. The third part, where he says what the evils of his enemies are. [1062D] [n: (AUG.)] As if he should say, Thy shadow covers me, etc., *as far as* or they are overcome out of their own assertion. *The labour of their lips shall overwhelm them.* [n: (CASS.)] That is, their false and lying assertion shall overwhelm them, because they are overcome out of their own false assertion.

VERS. 11.-- *They shall fall upon them.* [n: (AUG.)] Or coals of fire upon the earth, etc., *as far as* whence the Apostle: *To some we are the odour of life unto life, to others the odour of death unto death [n: (II Cor. II.)]*

VERS. 12.-- *A man full of tongue.* [n: (ID.)] Whosoever wills to be directed, etc., *as far as* in outward things there is necessity, not will. *The unjust man,* etc., *unto destruction.* Which does not befall the needy man, who, if he is taken by evils, is yet not taken unto destruction; God scourges every son whom he receives. And although [1063A] the good be for the present afflicted, yet *they shall confess* amid grievous things, which is the more praiseworthy: *And they shall dwell,* therefore nothing is feared in the world.

VERS. 14.-- *But yet.* [n: (CASS.)] The conclusion of the psalm, where he says that if the good have been delivered, and avenged of their enemies, they do not nevertheless give it to themselves, but to God, whence, and they shall dwell with him.

*They shall dwell with thy countenance.* Because they shall see God as he is.

## PSALM CXL.

VERS. 2.-- *Let prayer be directed.* [n: (CAS.)] Let *prayer* be *incense,* etc., *as far as* he who repents at the end.

*The lifting up of my hands,* etc. [n: (AUG.)] Let all understand this of the Head, etc., *as far as* he adds it from the voice of the body.

[1063B]VERS. 3.-- *Set, O Lord, a watch.* Note that he asks, he sets [cj: *apponit*; read *apponi*, "to be set"] a watch upon the heart as well as upon the mouth: there is need of a twofold defence, that neither may an unjust thought have savour, nor may a depraved will break out into foolish words.

*A door.* [n: (AUG.)] From the likeness of him who stands about the door, that he may not enter or go out to whom it is not lawful.

VERS. 4.-- *Incline not.* [n: (CASS.)] As if: Set a door to this end, that he may not be excused: for this greatest vice has dominion over men, whence they repent the more slowly; whence in the book of Kings: *As the sin of soothsaying is it to rebel, and as the crime of idolatry to be unwilling to acquiesce [n: (I Reg. XV)]*.

*To make excuses for excuses.* [n: (ID.)] It is the custom of the shameless that, when caught, they first excuse themselves by certain falsehoods, and then, convicted of these, they seek others, imputing their vices to the time [1063C] or to necessity, lest at any time they should yield to the truth.

*I will not communicate.* [n: (ID.)] Or, *I shall be coupled.* Men are said to be coupled when two are bound together, so that the one hinders the other, that flight may not be free. I shall not be coupled after the likeness of the flesh. *With the elect.* [n: (AUG.)] The elect are those who justify themselves, despise the rest, as the Pharisee did [n: (Luc. XVIII)]; and defend their sins by whatever means. With such men this man does not wish *to be coupled,* that is, associated, lest he be hindered by them.

VERS. 5.-- *He shall correct me.* [n: (CASS.)] The second part, where this faithful man, now on the contrary corrected by the just man, spurns false praisers, being about to profit the wicked by word and by manners, wherein also he asks to be kept.

*But the oil of the sinner,* etc. [n: (ID.)] A feigned love, [1063D] which softens minds away from the rigour of truth toward hurtful things.

VERS. 6.-- *Judges.* [n: (AUG.)] The powerful, the learned, who judge concerning manners, etc., *as far as* whom all worship.

VERS. 7.-- *They shall hear.* [n: (ID.)] With the ear of obedience, not like Adam, etc., *as far as* those nearest to a curse.

*The fatness of the earth.* [n: (ID.)] There are [sic: *quae dam*] contemptible things from which nevertheless the earth receives its fatness, etc., *as far as* who are the bones and the firmament of the faithful or of the faith.

VERS. 8.-- *Near hell.* [n: (ID.)] He says *near hell,* not, in hell: because although they die by the common lot, they do not nevertheless go down into hell as the wicked do, but eternal life is given them. *For unto thee, O Lord.* [1064A] [n: (CASS.)] The frailty of man having been spoken of, he takes refuge in the divine remedies.

Take not away from thy good things, but let my hope be always in thee: and let my eyes ever be intent upon thee. For this man does not ask against that which the saints will, namely to be snatched from the world and to be with Christ.

VERS. 9.-- *Keep me from the snare.* By snares and scandals he briefly notes the evils of the whole world. Snares are the flattering bonds, the desires of the flesh; but while the devil does not prevail through these, he strives with scandals, which he stirs up by means of the restless.

Or otherwise. And by those setting the snare he means open persecutors: but by those working iniquity he means those consenting to them, who fail through fear of persecution. And the sense is: Take not away my soul, but [1064B] rather, *Keep me from the snare* of persecution, *which they have set for me.* [n: (AUG.)] This the persecutors do, who set the bait of this life as it were in a trap. If the bird loves this bait, it falls into the trap: because through fear of death it succumbs to persecution. *Keep me.*

*From the scandals of them that work iniquity.* That is, from the scandal of those who, by consenting to them, fall, and so work iniquity. As if he should say: Keep me from the persecutors, who terrify, and from those who have fallen.

VERS. 10.-- *They shall fall in the net.* Words fitted to the comparison, since he does not wish to be coupled with these.

They shall fall from vice into vice, but

*I am alone until I pass.* Alone, that is, because he does not walk with the worst. *Alone.* [n: (AUG.)] The same that he says, etc., *as far as* so also other ancients.

[1064C] *I pass.* As a wayfarer of this world, who is taken by no pleasure.



## PSALM CXLI.

VERS. 1.-- *The understanding of David.* [n: (CASS.)] This psalm is the fifth of those which are entitled by the name of a prayer, where no other things are said than those which have been said in other psalms, but the same. [n: (AUG.)] But because by a repetition of this kind provision is made for the common infirmity, they are not repeated to no purpose, that things repeated may the better be committed to memory; whence it is written in the Proverbs: *A desirable treasure rests in the mouth of the wise man [n: (Prov. XII)]*, [var: Vulg. Prov. 21:20 *Thesaurus desiderabilis, et oleum in habitaculo justi*, "a desirable treasure, and oil in the dwelling of the just"] because the wise man ponders what he has heard; he ruminates upon it by going over it again; but the foolish man swallows it down, [1064D] that is, he does not ponder it, he gives it over to forgetfulness; and these are those clean animals in the law which chew the cud; those are the unclean. David, fleeing from Saul, lay hid in a cave, of which nevertheless there is no question here, but of the thing signified. For David signifies Christ. The cave, the flesh, or the world, or the sepulchre. David therefore, that is, Christ, with his members in the world, or Christ in the flesh, or in the sepulchre.

VERS. 2.-- *With my voice I have cried to the Lord.* First the prayer against evils is set down, secondly he treats of the end of evils, there: *I have cried to thee, O Lord.* He admonishes us therefore to pray. And in the person of the whole Christ, as of one crying to God out of a cave, he says: *I have made supplication.* [n: (AUG.)] That which he says, *I have cried, he expounds, I have made supplication.* As if he should say: My cry [1065A] is a supplication, not a murmur, not a blasphemy.

VERS. 3.-- *In his sight.* [n: (AUG.)] The Lord is about to repay, pray in secret; whence in the Gospel: *But thou when thou shalt pray, enter into thy chamber, and having shut the door, pray to thy Father who sees in secret,* etc. [n: (Matth. VI.)]

VERS. 4.-- *And thou hast known.* [n: (ID.)] This he adds, lest those hearing, *my spirit hath failed,* should suppose him cast down. As if he were to say: If they suppose me cast down, because my spirit has failed, thou seest me standing, whose Spirit gives me life.

*Paths.* Of justice, which they do not see.

*In the way.* Many are the ways, because many are the precepts: one is the way, because all are brought back to one, that is, to charity.

[1065B]AUG.) *In the way,* according to their desire; beside the way, according to the truth. For Christ is the way, who does not suffer them to set a snare *in the way,* so that we should have nowhere to go; yet he suffers them to set it beside the way, lest we turn aside from the way.

VERS. 5.-- *I looked to the right hand.* [n: (AUG.)] Which the wicked did not see; and this is, *and there was none that knew.* [n: (CASS.)] For when thou sufferest all things, who knows what thou art regarding? [n: (AUG.)] He who attends to the right hand, sees; he who attends to the left, is blinded. Or, the apostles were not standing at the right hand, because they were scattered by a carnal fear, but I stood fast.

*Flight hath perished.* As much of the body as of the mind. *And there is none that seeks.* [n: (CAS.)] Because there is no consolation, but he was left alone to the passion. *Flight hath perished.* [1065C] [n: (AUG.)] He says that he is shut in on every side. *Flight hath perished,* because he does not flee in mind, though sometimes in body. He who is shut in does not flee in body, because he cannot. More strongly [cj: *Fortius*; read *Fortis*, "the strong man"] does he not flee in mind, who does not will it. *Flight hath perished* for the man shut in or for the strong man; flight indeed, whence it is said in the Gospel: *He sees the wolf coming and flees [n: (Joan. X)]*. Where the Lord reproves the hireling. But there is another flight, of the body, which the Lord permits, saying: *If they shall have persecuted you in one city, flee into another [n: (Matth. X)]*. [var: Vulg. Mt 10:23 *Cum autem persequentur vos in civitate ista, fugite in aliam*, "when they shall persecute you in this city"]

*And there is none that seeks the soul.* The soul is sought either by persecutors or by those who love it. So flight perishes in two ways, both for the man taken captive and for the strong man.

VERS. 6.-- *I have cried.* [n: (CAS.)] The second part, where he treats of the end of evils. *My hope.* Because I choose thee out of all. *Portion.* Humbly and in common with others he says that a portion [1065D] is his in the land of the living, whereas the whole is his. This appears from the fact that he said to the thief: *This day thou shalt be with me in paradise:* which is the land of the living everlastingly.

VERS. 7.-- *From them that persecute me.* For the devil through his vessels, that is, through the children of unbelief, works persecution of the body, that within there may come about the ruin of the heart.

VERS. 8.-- *Bring forth out of custody,* etc. [n: (AUG.)] Speaking in the person of the Church, he sets forth from what he is to be delivered. Otherwise, *out of the prison.* Because in corruption (which is the punishment of the body) he is straitened: after which straits in the house of God the whole life shall be confession, that is, the praise of God; now the prison hinders him from confessing. This prison or cave may be called the world, where all things are vain: whence he asks [1066A] that the soul be led forth, which suffers many things there, etc., *as far as* but its corruption, and temptation, is the prison.



## PSALM CXLII.

*A psalm of David.* This psalm is the seventh of the penitential psalms, which are therefore seven, that by the same number by which we have sinned we may wash away our sins. [n: (CAS.)] For as we sin in the week of this world, so by this number of psalms the remedy of penance is set against our sins. In which psalms the form is such, that it is begun from tears, and the end is in joy, lest any despair of pardon. [n: (AUG.)] As Absalom did to David, so Judas to Christ, false brethren to his body; or unlawful motions and action.

VERS. 1.-- *O Lord, hear,* etc. [n: (CAS.)] First [1066B] he takes his exordium; secondly he narrates his own evils, and what he has done against them, there, *because he has persecuted;* thirdly the supplication is set down, confirmed by reasons, there, *hear speedily;* fourthly the joyful conclusion is set down, there, *thy spirit.* He admonishes to penance, and to prayer, that we should pray.
