In the person of the whole Christ he says: *O Lord,* etc. *In thy truth.* [n: (CAS.)] This is not against the humility which was asking to be heard: but it is the truth and justice of God, that he should not dispute with the one asking, that he should not judge him who condemns himself; whence it is added: *and enter not,* he did not ask for the justice of judgment. [n: (AUG., CAS.)] *In thy truth, in thy justice.* Or he calls justice and truth that which spares the penitent, and absolves those who condemn themselves, and he takes *truth and justice* for the same thing. And the sense is, *in thy truth, and thy justice,* that is, according to the justice and [1066C] truth which thou grantest to the penitent and to those who accuse themselves.

VERS. 2.-- *And enter not into judgment.* [n: (AUG.)] They enter into judgment with the Lord who establish their own justice, which this humble man does not do; whence the prophet: *Why will you contend with me in judgment? You have all forsaken me, says the Lord. With thy servant.* It is unworthy to contend with a servant, not to say with a friend: and if thou sayest 'friend', and I 'servant', a fugitive I return, I seek peace, *I am not worthy to be called thy son.*

VERS. 3.-- *Because he has persecuted.* [n: (CAS.)] The second part, where there is the narration of the evils which he suffers, and what he has done against them.

VERS. 6.-- *I have been mindful.* [n: (AUG.)] This the members say, etc., *as far as* but thou hast begun not to be. *I have [1066D] meditated.* Because assuredly all things are good, wherein he perceives that whatsoever of good is in him, is from God. *In the works of the hands.* As if he should say: I have sought the craftsman in the work, and in all created things the creator: thy creature has been made a spectacle for me.

*I have been mindful of the days.* [n: (CAS.)] This man, calling to mind that he made heaven and earth, and that he did good to the ancients, forgets the miseries of the world. And out of those things which God has made he consoles himself.

VERS. 7.-- *Speedily.* [n: (CAS.)] The third part, where the supplication confirmed by reasons is set down. *The spirit hath failed.* [n: (AUG.)] Let thy Spirit fill me, because mine has failed: already I am poor in spirit. For in whom his own spirit lives, he is proud in his own spirit, and is lifted up [1067A] against God. Whence elsewhere: *Thou shalt take away their spirits, and they shall fail [n: (Psal. CIII)]*. *Into the pit.* He goes down into the deep who loses even confession, as one who sets before himself licence to sin without hope of pardon, the reins of iniquity let loose. Such a man does not hear God saying: *Turn to me, and I will turn to you [n: (Zach. I)]*.

VERS. 8.-- *In the morning.* [n: (CAS.)] After the night of sins. [n: (AUG.)] It is morning, when sins are loosed. *Make the way known to me.* This God has done, etc., *as far as* we shall come to the pure light, to the Word in the beginning with God. *The soul.* (CAS.) As a vessel I have brought it to the fountain: fill it therefore.

VERS. 10.-- *Thy good spirit.* [n: (CAS.)] The fourth part, where the joyful conclusion is. *For thy name's sake, [1067B] O Lord, thou wilt quicken me in thy equity.* The grace of God is here commended: because he says that he is quickened not out of merits, but out of the name, which is Jesus; yet along with this the grace of equity shows mercy, because although God does all things freely, it belongs nevertheless to his justice to justify the suppliants whom he sees the demons assail.



## PSALM CXLIII.

VERS. 1.-- *A psalm of David to Goliath.* [var: Vulg. Ps. 143:1 *Psalmus David. Adversus Goliath*] [n: (CAS.)] David, the whole Christ, etc., *as far as* and so his head is cut off with his own sword.--David slew Goliath, Christ the devil, etc., *as far as* whence spiritually the following psalm is entitled *praise.*

*Blessed.* [n: (CAS.)] A triumphal psalm, etc., *as far as* praying to be delivered through him, there, *O God, a canticle.*

[1067C] *Who teaches my hands.* [n: (AUG.)] That is, who through the Holy Spirit, etc., *as far as* that which follows. *My mercy.* [n: (CAS.)] Because it is tedious to explain the causes of many things, he touches them briefly in single words, etc., *as far as* because with contempt and under wonder it is added, *O Lord, what is man, that,* etc. [n: (AUG.)] *My mercy.* Because thou grantest me to be merciful, etc., *as far as* forgive therefore, and I forgive; give, and I give.

VERS. 3.-- *That thou makest account of him?* [n: (AUG.)] Great is that which God esteems, and holds of such worth that for it he gave his Son, for whom he even becomes bread, who is the bread of angels: for God does not so esteem man as man does, who buys a horse more dearly than a man.

VERS. 4.-- *He is become like to vanity: his days [1067D] as a shadow.* [n: (ID.)] When man was created, he was made like [sic: *simlis*] to truth [sic: *veriteti*]; like to vanity by sinning. All transitory things are vain, in comparison with the truth that ever abides. [n: (CAS.)] That is vain which, falling away from its own substance, vanishes: as smoke, as a shadow. So man himself also wastes away, whence he adds, *His days,* that is, his notorious life, that is, his transitory life, so passes away as a shadow.

VERS. 5.-- *O Lord, bow down.* [n: (AUG.)] The humble David, in the person of the Church, full of grace, presuming upon God, fighting here calls upon the help of God. As if: we are in a wrestling, but let us not fail. *O Lord, bow down.* [n: (CAS.)] *O Lord.* He discloses the same thing, etc., *as far as* and the form of God took upon it the form of a servant.

[1068A]VERS. 7.-- *Send forth the hand.* [n: (CAS.)] He turns his words to the Father: *Send forth the hand,* that is, Christ; *from on high* where he is with the Father, that he may put on flesh. [n: (AUG.)] Or he treats of the second coming in the person of the Church. As if he should say: So do now. *Send forth thy hand from on high,* that is, Christ from heaven unto judgment. For the Lord shall come in the voice of an angel, and in the trumpet of God he shall descend from heaven. Then in the person of the Church he adds: *Take me out,* namely, by that hand. *And deliver,* etc., as in the letter.

VERS. 9.-- *O God, I will sing to thee a new canticle.* [n: (CAS.)] The second part, where, as though he whom he was entreating had come as victor and deliverer, he promises to sing to him in the New Testament and in the Old: and he prays to be delivered by him from those who think beatitude to lie in earthly things. [1068B] *A canticle.* [n: (AUG.)] By this other name he signifies the law, which he signified by the five stones.

VERS. 10.-- *With the sword.* [n: (AUG.)] Those which above he called waters, the same he here calls a sword; whence here also he adds what he added there: *And rescue me from the hand of strange children. Malignant.* Of which: *Their tongue is a sharp sword [n: (Psal. LVI)]*. There is also a kindly sword, of which the Lord says: *I came not to send peace, but the sword [n: (Matth. X)]*. This is the sword twice driven [cj: *actus*; read *acutus*, "sharpened"] [n: (Apoc. XIX)], mighty with either edge, namely of the Old and of the New Testament, by which all rottenness is cut away from the members of Christ.

VERS. 12.-- *Whose sons.* Here he sets forth who the strangers are, and what their right hand of iniquity is, and in what manner they have spoken vanity. [n: (AUG.)] He enumerates their felicity: [1068C] this is their right hand, this is the vanity which they speak, while they call these things beatitude. [n: (CASS.)] *Whose sons.* Or, mystically these things can be understood from that place, etc., *as far as* but they have sprouted with new errors.

*Daughters.* [n: (CASS.)] Which have not the strength of constancy.

*Decked out.* Not beautiful by nature, but decked out by men. These daughters are the thoughts or the preachings of those who have not the true Church, but a likeness of it.

VERS. 13.-- *Storehouses.* Hearts made for divers things: whence they breathe out the worst belchings, which stink.

*From this into that.* Unstable, because they have not the root [1068D] of truth.

VERS. 14.-- *Their sheep fruitful.* According to the letter, because he wishes to show that they cleave to perishable things. As if he should say: Their flock is fruitful, while they themselves are barren in faith; whence mystically their disciples, these are the fruitful sheep, because they themselves beget many others in error. *In the goings out.* Not in the goings in, because all the good which they seem to have goes out from them, who are soon to perish. *Oxen.* Not I labour [cj: *laboro*; read *labore*, "with labour"] lean, but fat with idleness, like those who abound in riches, him [cj: *eum*; read *cum*, "whereas"] they themselves are fasting from truth. Or mystically, oxen, the masters, abound in earthly things. *The fence.* Which are of stones alone in gardens but or in vineyards; nor does it itself fall down for them, therefore they are prosperous.

[n: (CAS.)] *Neither passage, nor cry,* turbulent, [1069A] interrupts their quiet, etc., *as far as* which leads to death.

VERS. 15.-- *Blessed.* He does not blame them on this account, because they abound in these things, but because they hold them for a right hand, that is, for beatitude: and this is the vanity which they have spoken. In these Abraham also, and Isaac, and Jacob abounded. Happiness is a left hand, that is, temporal, mortal.



## PSALM CXLIV.

VERS. 1.-- *Praise, for David himself.* [n: (CAS.)] This psalm contains briefly the things which in the divine Scriptures are spread abroad at large, etc., *as far as* lest any should be able to err in his praise:

[n: (AUG.)] God fills the saints with the Holy Spirit, etc., [1069B] *as far as* and therefore let his spirit in his servants praise him, because he makes himself to be praised.

[n: (CAS.)] As there are seven psalms of penance, so also seven spirits, according to that sacrament wherein the Lord commanded Moses to set seven lamps in the tabernacle, shining with perpetual light.

*I will extol thee.* [n: (CAS.)] First the prophet promises endless praise; secondly, he says that all shall accomplish the praise of God in nine ways, there, *Generation and generation;* thirdly, he explains the same at greater length, there, *compassionate.*

*For ever and ever.* [n: (CAS.)] He who does not begin here shall be dumb in the world to come.

VERS. 2.-- *Every day,* etc. [n: (ID.)] He who here every day does so, shall deserve in the eternal day to praise with continual exultation.

[1069C] [n: (AUG.)] He blesses now *every day,* that, when the one day without ends [cj: *fines*; read *fine*, "without end"] shall have come, he may go from praise to praise, as from virtue to virtue. *For ever, and for ever and ever.* [n: (CAS.)] Of which praise elsewhere: *They shall praise for ever and ever [n: (Psal. XXXVIII)]*, because they will always love him whom they will always see, since nothing is more delightful than that vision.

VERS. 3.-- *Exceedingly.* [n: (CAS.)] In one word he has given us to consider that which cannot be fully grasped: and therefore never cease from praises.

VERS. 4.-- *Generation and generation.* [n: (CAS.)] Secondly, he promises that all shall accomplish the praise of God in nine ways. *Thy works.* [n: (AUG.)] If we fail in his greatness, let us at least praise the worker from his works, the founder from the things founded, the creator from his creatures. [n: (CAS.)] [1069D] Nine are the works: power, magnificence, holiness, wonders, strength, the greatness of terrible things, memory, justice, which shall be explained hereafter: from these, as from topics and seeds, all praise is drawn, in the manner in which all arguments are taken from a small number of topics. *And thy power.* [n: (AUG.)] For works do not praise, save that they may announce the strength of the worker. But certain eloquent men praise the creature, forgetful of the Creator. But holy praisers, if they love the works, praise the worker the more: and on account of these they love him. If in things made there is felt any beauty, usefulness, strength, it is the more praised in him who made it. If thou praisest the works, thou praisest thyself also, who art the work of God. And this occurs: *Let not thy own mouth praise thee, lest thou [1070A] seem arrogant [n: (Prov. XXVII)]*. [var: Vulg. Prov. 27:2 *Laudet te alienus, et non os tuum ; extraneus, et non labia tua*, "Let another praise thee, and not thy own mouth"] But it is not arrogance, because thou praisest God in thee, not thyself.

VERS. 6.-- *And the power.* If he did not threaten, there would be no correction [sic: *corectio*]; but if he did not allure, there would be no exhortation. He is therefore both lovable and terrible. They shall declare therefore the power of him that disciplines and punishes, if they preach the eternal kingdom, they do not keep silent concerning the eternal fire.

VERS. 7.-- *They shall belch forth the memory of the abundance of thy sweetness.* [n: (AUG.)] He himself through his abundant sweetness is first mindful of us, by calling us, by preventing us with his grace, and thence we of him.

*They shall belch forth,* he says, not, they shall eat.

[n: (AUG.)] He eats who learns, he belches forth who teaches: yet he belches forth that which he eats. This memory, because it is exceeding sweet, is to be eaten and to be belched forth. So then [1070B] eat, that thou mayest belch forth.

VERS. 8.-- *Compassionate and merciful.* [n: (CAS.)] Thirdly he explains at greater length the things which in the second place he set forth briefly. As if: Deservedly in these things they shall praise, because *compassionate,* etc. The parts of praise are set down here which were set down above, but they cannot be explained one by one: for who could fully explain his works or his power? [n: (AUG.)] *Compassionate and merciful.* To those to whom he has given that pardon. *Patient.* To those to whom he has not yet given it, but waits that he may give it.

VERS. 9.--[n: (CAS.)] *Tender mercies.* He begins from the works, that he may be called compassionate while he restores the fallen. *Merciful,* by nature, even while he creates. *Patient,* this he takes from the second topic of praise, namely from power, which he praises, not because he has hung the lands, restrained the seas, painted the heavens with stars, etc., but because he bears with [1070C] sinners, that they may be converted. *And his tender mercies.* [n: (AUG.)] For if thou takest away thy evil works, and there remain in thee nothing but his works, his mercy will not let thee go: but if thou dost not let go thy own works, his severity will be upon thy works, when he shall say: *Go, ye cursed, into everlasting fire [n: (Matth. XXV)]*. [var: Vulg. Mt 25:41 *Discedite a me maledicti in ignem aeternum*, "Depart from me, ye cursed"]

VERS. 10.-- *Let all thy works confess to thee, O Lord.* [n: (AUG.)] Let them praise as do those things which have not the sense of praising, which are the matter of praise: because while thou inquirest, thou findest a great power in them, which is from God.

*And thy saints.* [n: (CAS.)] Everything that is confesses either by itself, or through rational creatures. Or the saints, that is, angels and men. This concerning holiness: for this is the holiness of God, that the saints should owe him confession.

[1070D]VERS. 11.-- *The glory of the kingdom.* [n: (ID.)] It is taken therefore from the wonders. As if: How wonderful is he who made heaven and earth, and filled them with divers good things.

VERS. 12.-- *That known.* [n: (ID.)] This is taken from the power of terrible things, etc., *as far as* to whom, being magnificent, nothing is compared.

VERS. 13.-- *Thy kingdom,* etc. [n: (ID.)] He praises from the perpetuity and the effect of dominion. And this is taken from greatness: if it is great to rule for a time, it is greater to have no term of dominion.

*The Lord is faithful in all.* [n: (ID.)] This is taken from the abundant sweetness; whence above: *The memory of the abundance of thy sweetness. Faithful,* because his coming fulfilled the gifts. To abundance also belongs [1071A] that he lifts up those who fall, raises up the crushed; whence the remembrance of sweetness is had, and is belched forth in his praises.

VERS. 14.-- *Who fall down.* [n: (CAS.)] From their own evils, all of whom God lifts up. Or, from these temporal things, as the saints do, like Job, who lose something in the world and are made abject, and are falling: but all these he confirms, because seven times the just man falls and rises again; but the wicked are weakened in evils [n: (Prov. XXIV)].

*And he lifts up all that are cast down.* [n: (AUG.)] Those belonging to him, because he resists the proud.

VERS. 15.-- *The eyes of all hope in thee, O Lord.* [n: (ID.)] He treats of justice at greater length, that he who hears of the merciful one may fear the just one: and it belongs to justice that he should satisfy those who hope, bodily or spiritually. For food is not always to be given, but in due season: [1071B] for to many who ask it is wholesomely deferred. *And thou givest them their food in due season.* [n: (ID.)] The physician therefore gives the sick man what is fitting, etc., *as far as* blessing God at all times.

VERS. 16.-- *Thou openest the hand.* [n: (ID.)] Although sometimes thou dost not give, yet thou givest opportunely: thou deferrest indeed, thou dost not take away.

VERS. 17.-- *Just.* [n: (CAS.)] By the very name he discloses justice, that it may be manifestly shown that this praise is concerning justice. *Ways* are dispositions and wills, which are all just, even if he scourges.

*In his ways, and holy in all* [sic: *omnibns*] *his works.* It is to be known that *Ways* pertain to disposition, works to effect: these are divided according to us. But with him, to will is the same as to do: because [1071C] from his will things have their being.

VERS. 18.-- *He is near to all that call upon him.* [n: (CAS.)] Propitious, who is everywhere, and this concerning justice.

VERS. 19.-- *He will do the will of them that fear him.* (ID.) He concludes concerning justice: for it is just both to keep those who love him and to destroy sinners.

VERS. 21.-- *Praise.* [n: (CAS.)] All things having been expounded, there follows a brief conclusion, in which he concludes that which cannot be explained.



## PSALM CXLV.

VERS. 1.-- *Alleluia.* [n: (CAS.)] This voice, as it were saving musics [cj: *musicas*; read *musica*, "a saving music"], like a heavenly harmony, which delights not men only but angels also, setting forth the praise of God. And it is fitly prefixed to this psalm, [1071D] where the just man exhorts his soul to the praise of God: because in adversities the soul is sometimes troubled, whether it will or no. Whence above: *Why art thou sad, O my soul, and why dost thou disquiet me?* But that he may take away her disquiet, he suggests joy out of hope, if not out of the thing itself: *Hope in God,* etc. [n: (AUG.)] This soul, lifted up by hope, praises God here. [n: (CAS.)] First, he counsels himself, as it were all men, to praise, lest prayer be made among vain things; and as though acquiescing in the counsel, he affirms that he will praise there where he shall be able to praise fully. Secondly, he admonishes others not to hope in man, there: *Trust not.* Thirdly, he teaches that all hope is to be placed in the Almighty, there: *Blessed,* etc.

VERS. 2.-- *Praise the Lord, O my soul.* But [1072A] it is asked, by whom, and to whom, is this counsel given? If it be said that the flesh counsels the soul, this does not seem to be true, since counsel is wont to be given by the better and the worthier to the inferior: but the flesh is inferior to the soul. For the soul, although it be blamed because it is a sinner, is nevertheless called better by nature than the body that is praised in its own kind, because a spiritual nature excels the body, which it moves, rules, endows with sense, and apprehends: and being itself unbounded, it is near to the substance of God. So gold, blamed because it is foul, surpasses lead in purity, praise [cj: *laudatio*; read *laudato*, "that is praised"]: so too a bad man surpasses the best of horses. Counsel therefore is not given to the soul by the flesh, however greatly it be subdued and made peaceable: which nevertheless is dead because of sin. Nor yet does the mortal flesh counsel the immortal soul, because it is always beneath the soul; but the higher part of the soul, [1072B] that is, the mind, which thinks upon the divine wisdom, cleaving to it and sighing after it, sees certain of its own parts troubled by the motions of the world, and hurried away to outward things by the desire of earthly goods: whence it calls it back to things inward and higher. Exhorting itself therefore according to its sensuality, it says: *Praise the Lord, O my soul.* For what pleases thee in this world? Divided among so many loves of the things of the ages, gather thyself to thy Author: here is peace and security for thee. Then the soul answers the mind, as one weighed down here and not able to leave off, as is meet. *I will praise in life.* Here in death I shall praise but thinly, because the body *is a load upon the soul, and the earthly habitation presseth down the sense that thinketh upon many things [n: (Sap. IX)]*; here hope feeds us, life is in the world to come.

*In my life.* [n: (CAS.)] When I shall have cleaved to him perfectly, from [1072C] whom to be parted, as now befalls, is death: but hope consoles, in which, living after a sort, it praises.

*As long as.* Not that at some time he shall not be, because he shall be for ever, and therefore truly long.

VERS. 3.-- *Trust not,* etc. [n: (CAS.)] The second part. The counsel being given and confirmed, where he shall be able to praise fully, he admonishes them, as though set far off, and in the meantime while evils last (by which the soul is troubled), not to hope in man, but in God. [n: (AUG.)] Or, as one set in mind at the end of the world: he teaches them not to love things soon to end in place of things eternal; as if by my example also, *trust not,* etc. *In the children of men.* [n: (ID.)] In one son of man is salvation, and in him do ye hope. Not because he is a son of man, but because he is the Son of God. Not for that which he took from thee, but for that which he kept in himself.

[1072D]VERS. 4.-- *His spirit shall go forth,* etc. [n: (CAS.)] First he cleanses hearts by forbidding what is hurtful, that afterward he may aim at what is saving by admonishing to wholesome things.

VERS. 5.-- *Blessed is he whose God is the God of Jacob.* [n: (CAS.)] The third part, where he teaches that the whole hope is to be placed in the Almighty, whom he describes from his works, that the Gentile world, so often confuted, may be still. [n: (AUG.)] Hurtful things being removed, he teaches what belongs to salvation, namely, in whom hope is to be placed. *His helper is his hope.* [n: (CAS.)] Of that Jacob himself he was so the helper, etc., *as far as* that is, of this true Jacob, as of that one.

VERS. 6.-- *Who made heaven and earth.* [n: (ID.)] Because the worshippers of demons also have hope in their god, he determines in whom hope ought to be, that is, in Christ, and this [1073A] he shows even to the end of the psalm, describing him through all things. *All things that are in them.* Lest thou think any other to be the creator, or that there exists a creator even of the smallest things.

VERS. 7.-- *Who keepeth truth for ever.* [n: (CAS.)] Christ, who is the truth; every man is a liar. *He executeth judgment.* These two pertain to justice. Christ the judge avenges those who suffer for his name, and gives food to them that love him. *For them that suffer wrong.* [n: (AUG.)] Not every trouble is a wrong. What thou sufferest by right is not a wrong, as is that which the robber and other wicked men suffer. It is one thing therefore to suffer wrong, another to suffer punishment. For a man suffers punishment by right, but wrong is contrary to right.

VERS. 8.-- *The Lord looseth them that are fettered.* [n: (CAS.)] This [1073B] belongs to the abundance of his sweetness.

*The Lord lifteth up them that are cast down.* Whom the devil casts down, the Lord lifts up.

*He loveth the just.* [n: (CAS.)] Whom he sets in eternal blessedness.

VERS. 9.-- *The fatherless and the widow he will receive.* [sic: *suseipiet*] [n: (AUG.)] The whole Church of the Gentiles is come as a stranger to the fathers, a daughter not by flesh but by imitation.

*The fatherless* are all who are in pilgrimage, whose father is absent; and every faithful soul is a *widow* while the bridegroom is absent: whence they desire the more both the father and the bridegroom, and God protects them, not seen but desired.

VERS. 10.-- *The Lord shall reign for ever.* [n: (CAS.)] This is of his magnificence.

[1073C]He shall reign in us when our hearts shall be firm in him, which is not yet.



## PSALM CXLVI.

VERS. 1.-- *Alleluia.* [n: (AUG.)] Here likewise he speaks of the praise of God, where the prophet admonishes men to praise him that gathers Israel.

*Praise ye the Lord.* [n: (CAS.)] First he shows whom he gathers, and in what manner. Secondly, after these things, he rebukes them as still doubting and wavering, showing whence they may be able to praise God, namely if they shall have been humble, there, *Sing praise.--Praise ye the Lord.* [n: (AUG.)] This is said to all the Gentiles. And as though we should ask in what manner we shall praise, he brought forward the cause of praise, *for a psalm is good.* [n: (CAS.)] In the praise of God the act itself is the reward which shall be in time to come; therefore is he to be praised, because [1073D] he builds up, and thus, because *the dispersions* [sic: *disper iones*] *of Israel,* that is, of the faithful, out of the whole world at the end of the age he *shall gather* into his kingdom. And here is praise from his wonderful works. [n: (AUG.)] This is the whole reward of them that praise, etc., *as far as* let us for the present be exercised in this praise of good works.

*Let it be pleasant to our God,* etc. It is not enough to sing psalms unto this end, that praise may please God, but there is need that we please him also by cleanness of life.

VERS. 2.-- *The Lord buildeth up Jerusalem.* [n: (AUG.)] Or, he has taken *Jerusalem* for the Church universal, etc., *as far as* whom the Father sent to redeem our captivity by the price of his blood.

VERS. 3.-- *And he bindeth up.* From the likeness of a physician, etc., *as far as* and the Lord binds up their bruises. [n: (HIER.)] [1074A] Even as that Samaritan who was going down from Jerusalem to Jericho [n: (Luc. X)], when he saw the wounded man lying in the way, bound up his wounds. But concerning those who have not bruised their heart it is said by Isaias: *There is no plaster to lay on, nor oil, nor bandages [n: (Isa. I, secundum LXX)]*. The bandages of God are the sacraments of time, by which for the present we have consolation; and these shall then be taken away when health shall be perfect.

VERS. 4.-- *Who numbereth the multitude of the stars, and calleth them all by their names.* [n: (CAS.)] Not strange names, as the astrologers who name Jupiter and Venus. Or, figuratively, of the saints, because to each he gives his proper gift; or, all are in his familiarity, from the likeness of a lord who knows the names of his own household servants.

[1074B]VERS. 5.-- *Of his wisdom,* etc., [n: (AUG.)] The measure itself, and the number and the weight, etc., *as far as* see what follows, *but humbling sinners.*

VERS. 6.-- *But humbling sinners.* [n: (AUG.)] For they who find fault with things intelligible, therefore savour of earthly things, thinking bodily concerning the incorporeal God. These things he does to those who wish to mock the law before they know it. Because therefore it is a great thing to advance to things intelligible and spiritual, let us be meek, let us knock and do that which follows.

VERS. 7.-- *Sing praise.* So shall a man come to understanding, so shall the image be reformed unto God. *Sing praise.* [n: (CAS.)] Secondly, out of the things aforesaid he rebukes and urges them as still wavering, showing whence they may be able to understand, if they be humble and not proud. As if to say: And that ye may be able [1074C] to understand, *sing praise,* etc.

VERS. 8.-- *Who covereth the heaven.* [n: (CAS.)] He calls to mind the works through which his power is shown. For unto this end he willed certain things in the Scriptures to be obscure, that the servants of God might have somewhat which by interpreting they should pour upon the ears and hearts of men, who receive from the clouds of God the fatness of spiritual gladness.

*To the service of men.* To the preachers, who serve men. Whence the Apostle says: *But we ourselves your servants through Jesus Christ [n: (II Cor. IV)]*. [var: Vulg. 2 Cor. 4:5 *nos autem servos vestros per Jesum*, "your servants through Jesus"] These sow spiritual things and receive from *the high ones of the world hay and grass,* that is, temporal things.

VERS. 9.-- *And to the young of the ravens.* [n: (AUG.)] For all things that are given to the Church by the rich are hay.

[1074D] [n: (ID.)] The Jews, who called themselves alone just and all the Gentiles sinners. And truly our fathers were *ravens,* but we do not so remain: yet rightly are we called, and are, the young of ravens. The raven calls not upon God, but we being let go do call. [n: (CAS.)] The young of ravens are said to feed upon dew, and by the benefit of their age they know not their fathers' food, that is, the stench of carcasses: so the innocent children of the pagans, who call upon God.

VERS. 10.-- *In the legs of a man.* In the composed bearing [cj: *In composito*; read *Incomposito*, "in the uncomposed bearing"] of the presumptuous and bold. For by a bold man he denotes a presumptuous one. In the legs is noted an uncomposed gait, *in the legs of a man.* Or, according to another reading, *in the tabernacles of a man,* that is, in the conventicles of heretics, *shall it be well pleasing to him.* Note that he says not [1075A] in the tabernacles of God, but *of a man,* thereby designating the boldness of heretics.

VERS. 11.-- *Upon them that fear him,* etc. [n: (AUG.)] He joined hope to fear, because fear avails not without hope of pardon: for Judas, fearing but despairing, hanged himself with a halter. He therefore that fears God, let him hope also in him. And thou shalt appease him if thou hope in his mercy, and for the rest so beware of sinning that for things past thou entreat that they may be forgiven thee.



## PSALM CXLVII.

VERS. 12.-- *Alleluia.* This psalm works the love of the Jerusalem to come. It is said to be of Aggeus and Zacharias, who in the captivity prophesied the restoring of Jerusalem, which, as Jeremias foretold, was accomplished after seventy years, [1075B] that is, after the rolling round of the whole of time, which is carried on in seven days.

*Praise, O Jerusalem.* [n: (CASS.)] This whole psalm proclaims the power of God. First, it tells the good things of the Jerusalem to come; secondly, it tells what good things are given here to the saints, there: *Who sendeth forth.* [n: (AUG.)] The prophet, being as yet set in captivity, etc., *as far as* we who have heard him thus pouring it forth.

VERS. 13.-- *He hath strengthened the bolts of thy gates.* [n: (AUG.)] Note that the door is shut, the prudent virgins being admitted and the foolish shut out, so that now none may go out or come in. And fitly after the admonition to praise he subjoins concerning the shut gates: for with the gate of Jerusalem shut she praises securely, because none has gone out for whom she should grieve; none comes in whom she should fear, whence scandals should be.

VERS. 14.-- *With the fat of corn.* [n: (ID.)] With the bread that came down from heaven: [1075C] in the world he is shut up in flesh, known by faith alone; then shall he be seen as he is.

VERS. 15.-- *Who sendeth forth his speech.* The second part subjoins what gifts are given here to the elect, through which the truth of that which is to come may be known.

*To the earth.* [n: (AUG.)] That he may be known on earth. For unless to send [cj: *mittere*; read *mitteret*, "he were to send"] his word to the earth, by which we are weighed down and hindered from our returning, we should not be lifted up to the fat of corn that satisfies. For in the earth, where we labour weary, faint, sluggish, cold, that is, in the desert, he forsakes us not, but manna: *who sendeth forth his speech,* etc.

*Swiftly runneth.* [n: (AUG., CASS.)] Or even unto swiftness, that is, from God, so that it is swiftness itself, than which nothing could be said more choicely or more expressly.

He says not that the word is swift, that it be swift with a swiftness [1075D] of which it partakes more or less: but than swiftness itself, whereby all other things are called swift, nothing is swifter. And again, that which is swift, as a horse is swift to a man, is not so everywhere: but swiftness itself is everywhere, it is not in a part. To the Word of God it belongs not to be in a part, but to be everywhere through himself. This is that wisdom *which reacheth from end to end mightily,* yet not by motion of place, but by immobility. And of the spirit of wisdom also it is said, *driven, moveable, sure, undefiled;* this swiftness effects, that it be both always everywhere, and nowhere held shut in. But who is sufficient to think upon these things? Yet there is hope of laying hold of the Word, because he has predestinated us.

VERS. 16.-- *Who giveth snow,* etc. [n: (AUG.)] To the earth. For charity growing cold, etc., *as far as* and while he calls these [1076A] to penance, *he scattereth mist like ashes.*

[n: (ID.)] *He scattereth mist like ashes,* etc. Ashes pertain to penance, etc., *as far as* and who has predestinated and has called to penance. According to the letter: God scatters snow like wool, mist like ashes, he makes crystal like morsels of white bread. For crystal is a certain kind of thing after the manner of glass, but it is white: and it is said to be snow hardened by ice through many years, so that it cannot be dissolved.

*His crystal,* etc. That is, the obstinate, who are not dissolved easily as snow is.

VERS. 17.-- *He sendeth as morsels,* etc. Otherwise, *as crumbs of bread,* that they may feed others, that they may preach the truth which they assail. All together, one bread; singly, [1076B] morsels; whence: *Being many, we are one bread in Christ [n: (I Cor. X)]*. [var: Vulg. 1 Cor. 10:17 *unus panis, unum corpus multi sumus*, "one bread, one body, we being many"] Paul was crystal, hard and shining: hard, because he persecuted the Church; shining, because he was skilled in the law. But God dissolved him, and he was made bread, who taught the truth. But unless he dissolve,

*Before the face of him, of God, who shall stand?* Otherwise, shall subsist. [var: Vulg. Ps. 147:6 *ante faciem frigoris ejus quis sustinebit*, "before the face of his cold who shall stand?"] [n: (AUG.)] It is cold when he forsakes, calls not, pours not in grace: and who else shall dissolve it? None. Whence the Apostle: *Who shall deliver me from the body of this death? [n: (Rom. VII.)]* As if he said: I grow cold, none dissolves me, unless the grace of God *send forth his word.*

VERS. 18.-- *He shall send out his word, and shall melt it.* [n: (CASS.)] For when the word of God comes, neither cold, nor darkness, nor hardness can despair. For they are melted healthfully, and pour out streams of spiritual benefit [1076C] to others whereof they may drink, according to that saying: *All you that thirst, come to the waters [n: (Isa. LV)]*.

[sic: VERS. 16.]-- *Jacob . . . Israel.* [n: (AUG.)] Or the same thing is signified by Israel, etc., *as far as* because he was not sent but *to the sheep that were lost of Israel [n: (Matth. XV)]*. [var: Vulg. Mt 15:24 *domus Israel*, "of the house of Israel"]

*His judgments.* [n: (ID.)] Judgment begins at the house of God, etc., *as far as* subjoining three other psalms of praise, that in this work the graces of the Trinity may shine forth.

## PSALM CXLVIII.

VERS. 1.-- *Praise ye the Lord from the heavens.* [n: (CASS.)] By the mention of a few he exhorts the whole creation to praise God. There are certain things which [1076D] have a spirit of praising God in that affection wherewith God pleases them: certain have no understanding, but because they themselves are good in their own order, and are referred to the beauty of the whole, they praise not of themselves, but by them God is praised through those that consider them: and so after a sort they praise, because from them the praise of God is born in us, whereto the prophet here admonishes us.

VERS. 5.-- *For he spoke, and they were made.* [n: (CASS.)] This against those who say that the world is coeternal with God; there follows: *He commanded.* By his word, and they were created. First he said, *made,* afterward *created.* For make we also can, but not create. He therefore alone creates, which is proper to the Godhead. Praise from his works

[1077A]VERS. 6.-- *He hath established.* From his power he is almighty, whose works are not changed.

*Unto the age, and unto the age of the age.* [var: Vulg. Ps. 148:6 *Statuit ea in aeternum, et in saeculum saeculi*, "he hath established them for ever, and for ages of ages"] [n: (CAS.)] And though the heaven be new, yet they are said to be well established by God, which are made new through Christ and remain bettered. And here is praise from his power.

VERS. 7.-- *Praise ye the Lady [cj: *Dominam*; read *Dominum*, "the Lord"].* The second part, where, the heavenly things being enumerated, he descends to earthly things. Turning therefore to them, as exhorting us that by the consideration of them we may praise God, which things considered lay open the praise of God, he says: O ye that are of the earth. *All ye deeps.* [sic: *ubyssi*] [n: (AUG.)] The depth of the waters, etc., *as far as* these therefore, that is, he exhorts us to praise by the consideration of them.

VERS. 8.-- *Which do his word.* This therefore [1077B] because it seemed to certain men that God ruled the higher things, but despised these lower ones, and that they were ruled by chance; but these also God does, and in these least things he is praised, and alleluia is sung.

VERS. 13.-- *Confession.* [n: (CAS.)] Above he spoke first of heaven, afterward of earth; here of all together. And he takes by heaven all higher things, and by earth all lower things. [n: (AUG.)] *His confession above heaven and earth.* All things confess him in themselves or in others; the beauty of all things is the voice of them that confess, in those who consider them, that is, let those who consider praise him, both from earthly and from heavenly things, than which there is nothing better.

VERS. 14.-- *He hath exalted the horn of his people.* [n: (ID.)] Behold what Aggeus and Zacharias prophesied. Now the horn of his people is lowly [1077C] in tribulations, but when the Lord shall come, it shall be exalted.



## PSALM CXLIX.

VERS. 1.-- *Alleluia.* [n: (CAS.)] After all the creatures which in the foregoing psalm he admonished to praise God, for that they were created by him, here more properly and more especially he invites Israel to a new canticle for the newness wrought in it, which is the more wonderful working of God: this is the sanctification of Israel, whereby out of the old it was made new.

*Sing ye to the Lord,* etc. [n: (CAS.)] First in many ways he admonishes them to praise the King who is kind and bountiful [sic: *benficus*] to them. Secondly he treats of the joys of the saints, there, *They shall rejoice.* [n: (AUG.)] He that would sing this canticle, let him love things eternal, [1077D] that he may be made new. For the old man sings an old canticle of earthly things. The new man sings a new one of spiritual things; a canticle, namely, of peace, a canticle [sic: *eanticcum*] of charity and of gladness. For whosoever follows the old animosity, and not the new charity, sings not a new canticle.

*His praise in the Church.* [n: (CAS.)] Here he determines where and by whom this canticle ought [sic: *debat*] to be sung.

VERS. 2.-- *In their king.* [n: (ID.)] That is, in Christ, who is king and priest. [n: (AUG.)] As king he fought for us from the cross, overcoming the devil: as priest, he offered himself for us, finding nothing in men that he might offer for them.

VERS. 3.-- *In choir.* [n: (ID.)] Because there is no dispersion, [1078A] but a most dear unity; then shall the rejoicing be of the timbrel and the psaltery, not the act of the thing itself.

VERS. 4.-- *For the Lord is well pleased.* [n: (ID.)] In the first framing of things we read that all things pleased God, etc., *as far as* or when he shall make them equal to the angels.

VERS. 5.-- *The saints shall rejoice.* [n: (ID.)] The second part, where he treats of the joys of the saints and of their power. *In glory.* It is the frequent praise of good deeds, wherein the just rejoice in their beds, that is, in their conscience, therefore because they have so good a Lord, who gives grace to sinners and eternal life upon their merits. The fool rejoices without, in the tales of men; to the saints this is the manner of joy, that good things be referred to him who gave them.

VERS. 6.-- *Exaltations.* [n: (ID.)] The strength and power of God is preached in the glory of the saints. In their beds, [1078B] in the divers mansions, is the glory of the saints. Whereof the Lord says: *In my Father's house there are many mansions [sic: *mahsiones*]. [n: (Joan. XIV.)]*

*In the throat.* They who in heart and in tongue cease not to praise him from whom they have things eternal.

*Swords.* Receiving the power of the word of God, they shall judge with the Lord, sitting upon twelve seats; and then shall truly be wrought *vengeance and rebuke among the peoples,* then shall the kings of the earth be bound: whence it is free for us to go out [cj: *nos*; read *non*, "not"]. And the nobles shall bear iron fetters, as heavy to carry, and such as cannot be broken: and so shall be wrought the judgment written, that is, approved by the angels. Whereof the Apostle: *Know you not* [sic: *Neseitis*] *that we shall judge angels? [n: (I Cor. VI.)]* And lest thou think that the apostles alone judge, he adds, *this glory,* that is, the power of judgment, is to all [1078C] his saints, that is, to the perfect, who for Christ left all earthly things.

[n: (AUG.)] *The exaltations of God in the throat.* For so do they rejoice in their beds and in their throats, that is, in heart and tongue, that they ascribe not to themselves that they are good, but to God, from whom they received what they are, and hope for that which they are not yet.

VERS. 8.-- *To bind.* With these bonds of the commandments and precepts of God are bound the kings of the Gentiles, the nobles of the world, by the weak and contemptible of this world, whom the Lord chose that he might confound all the strong things; and he filled these same with the Holy Ghost, and gave them two-edged swords, that they might turn the hearts of nobles to the fear of Christ, and that, worldly nobility [1078D] being despised, they might choose poverty and nobility in Christ. For he willed to profit the emperor by a fisherman, not the fisherman by an emperor.

[n: (AUG.)] By these he binds kings and nobles in iron [sic: *ferrcis*] fetters, that they should not go forward to unlawful things.

## PSALM CL.

VERS. 1.-- *Praise ye the Lord.* [n: (CAS.)] Of the praise of God in his saints, who being glorified praise him without end. The city of God is admonished, that is, the unity of all the saints, to sing with mouth and with mind. In this is all psalmody brought to its summit, which is sung with nuptial sweetness.

*Praise ye him in his firmament.* [n: (AUG.)] There is power in [1079A] the firmament, etc., *as far as* who, honouring one another, give praise to God.

[n: (CAS.)] To whom the prophet in the beginning showed the form of their conversation, now, being received into that Jerusalem, he says that in them God is to be praised, who gave all things. *In the firmament,* because by his own death he overcame the devil, and led believers to heaven. In his powers or mighty acts, because all things are subject under his feet.

VERS. 2.-- *According to the multitude of his greatness.* How is this, since of his greatness there is no end? but this is said to the spiritual and to the saints. And since there is no end of his greatness, he is praised according to it. God, since he is praised without end. But in what manner he ought to be praised, he tells by mystical similitudes even to the end of the psalm: let *the trumpet* sound loud to the King, let *the psaltery* sing [1079B] to God: *the harp* with the rest to the Bridegroom. For the trumpet is terrible, and is either taken up in war, or made ready at the coming of kings; the psaltery sounds from above, the harp from below: all these things, being compared to human acts, are there said figuratively to sound forth the praises of God.

VERS. 4.-- *On strings and organ.* [n: (CAS.)] *Strings* he puts for every musical instrument which sounds by the stretching of strings. *The organ* is as it were a tower framed of divers pipes, sounding by the blast of bellows; and that thou mightest take all these [1080A] things of men, he comes to the mouth of man: and expounding what it is to praise *on strings and organ* he subjoins.

*Praise ye him on cymbals,* etc. *Cymbals* well sounding are our lips, which being tuned to the heart sound well in the praises of God, wherein is the likeness of cymbals, and they render a harmony, and therefore are they rightly among musical instruments. [n: (CAS.)] Cymbals well tinkling are very small bowls of mingled metals, which render a shrill sound. But because he would have all things understood spiritually, he concludes with a fitting end:

VERS. 6.-- *Let every spirit,* angelic and human, *praise the Lord.* Not flesh and blood, not the habit of the world: but that which is more sublime in the nature of things, [1080B] which savours of heavenly things, which lays hold of things eternal. A brief and perfect knowledge, that all things should praise God spiritually; in these is the perfection of all music. For there is breath in the trumpet, striking in the harp and in the cymbals, voice in the choir. By which musical instruments is signified the spiritual harmony, which is perceived not by the ears, but by a pure mind.

[n: (AUG.)] There are three kinds, etc., *as far as* it is the voice of life eternal: *Let every spirit praise the Lord.*
